# Gita Govinda

## Foreword

Jayadeva was a poet born in 1170 CE. (Caitanya Mahaprabhu was born in 1486, over three hundred years later.) Some say he was born in Kenduli Sasan, a village in Orissa, while others claim he was born in Kenduli in Bengal. From the evidence at the present time, it seems he was born in Orissa. It seems certain that he taught Sanskrit in Orissa, and wrote his famous poem Gita Govinda at Puri. In any case, not much is known of his life in detail.

His main work is Gita Govinda, depicting the love of Radha and Krsna. The famous description of the ten *avataras* is included in it at the beginning. Through this work, the ten *avataras* became well known. Krsna is not included as one of the ten *avataras*, but is regarded by Jayadeva the source of all the *avataras*. This thus supports the conclusion of Srimad Bhagavatam as explained by the Gosvamis hundreds of years later. Caitanya Mahaprabhu during his stay in Puri would recite verses of Gita Govinda in remembrance of the pastimes of Radha and Krsna.

Gita Govinda became a major cultural influence in Orissa, and spread to Andhra, Tamil Nadu and Kerala, and had an influence upon the local dance forms of those states. As well Gita Govinda became a popular subject of painting in Rajasthan around 1600 CE. William Jones published an English translation of the work in 1792 CE. It has since been translated in the major languages of the world. It is regarded as an example of the finest Sanskrit poetry.

The various portions of the work are set to specific *ragas* (selection of notes) and *talas* (beats). However various editions of the work have different ragas and talas for a particular section. The renditions at the present time often use different *ragas* and *talas*.

There are twelve chapters, each titled after Krsna in his various moods. Each chapter is divided in several *prabandhas*, usually having eight verses with a chorus recited with each verse. There are twenty four *prabandhas* in the work.

Prabodhananda Sarasvati has written a commentary on this work for the *Gaudiya sampradaya*. His commentary often gives various meanings for the same statement, as does Jiva Gosvami in his commentary on *Bhagavatam*. The alternative meanings are justified by the grammar and suitable for the scene being depicted. The alternative meaning is partially due to the nature of Sanskrit language, where compound words and elision of vowels may create ambiguity in the meaning, and where phrases could modify different words in the sentence, and where one word often has various meanings.

His commentary sometimes uses dialogues to supply elements which will help explain certain actions Krsna or Radha in the poem itself. This is a technique used by Jiva Gosvami and Visvanatha Cakravarti in their commentaries on *Bhagavatam* as well.

Though the commentator quotes material *kama-sastras*, it should be understood that these quotations are only supplied because of some similarity between material and spiritual activities, as is the case with material and spiritual *rasa*.

## Chapter One. Samoda-damodarah - Joyful Damodara

### Text 1.1

*meghair meduram ambaram vana-bhuvah syamas tamala-drumair*  
*naktam bhirur ayam tvam eva tad imam radhe grham prapaya \|*  
*ittham nanda-nidesatas calitayoh praty-adhva-kunja-drumam*  
*radha-madhavayor jayanti yamuna-kule rahah-kelayah \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*meghaih* - “With rain clouds; *meduram* - dense; *ambaram* - the sky \[is\], *vana-bhuvah* - The forest ground; *syamah* - \[is\] dark; *tamala-drumaih* - with tamala trees, *naktam* - \[It is\] night; *bhiruh ayam* - This person \[is\] trembling; *tvam* - You; *eva* - alone; *tat* - for this reason; *imam* - this boy; *radhe* - О Radha; *grham* - to home; *prapaya* - should take; *ittham* - Thus; *nanda-nidesatah* - because of Nanda’s order; *calitayoh* - \[they are\] leaving; *praty-adhva-kunja-drumam* -endowed with paths, groves, and trees; *radha-madhavayoh* - of Radha and Madhava; *jayanti* - exist eternally (or exist preeminently); *yamuna-kule* - on the banks of Yamuna \[River\]; *rahah-kelayah* - the private pastimes (or amorous play).

**Перевод:**

**\[Nanda ordered\] “O Radha! Take this boy home. He is trembling, and it is night. The sky is dense with rain clouds and the forest ground is dark because of the tamala trees.” Radha and Madhava are leaving \[to the bank of Yamuna\] because of the order of Nanda and their private pastimes exist eternally on the bank of Yamuna which is endowed with paths, groves, and trees.**

**Комментарий:**

The author Jayadeva offers a prayer for auspiciousness by remembering his protecting deity (Radha and Madhava), so that  
**ПРОПУЩЕНА СТРАНИЦА ИЛИ ДВЕ...**

the beloved). The *sthayi-bhava* is *srngara*, the king of all *rasas*, in the form of *vipralambha* (separation), characterized by desire, hatred and jealousy. This will be displayed along with its parts and subparts.

*karanany atha karyani sahakarini yani ca \|*  
*ratyadeh sthayino loke tani cen natya-kavyayoh \|\|*

*vibhavanubhavas tavat kathyante vyabhicarinah \|*  
*vyaktah sa tair vibhavadyaih sthayi bhavo rasah smrtah \|\|*

**In drama and poetry, the causes, effects, and assistants of** ***rati*** **and other \[secondary\]** ***sthayi-bhavas*****, are known as** ***vibhavas*** **(causes),** ***anubhavas*** **and** ***vyabhicari-bhavas*****. The** ***sthayi-bhava*** **manifests along with these and the combination is known as** ***rasa*****. (*****Kavya-prakasa*****)**

These are described by Bharata Muni. *Vibhavanubhava-vyabhicari-samyogad rasa-nispattih*: *rasa* arises from the combination of *vibhava*, *anubhava* and *vyabhicari-bhavas*. (*Natya-sastra*)

In this way the author establishes the main *rasa*. Some who do not understand his intention may feel that the father’s order will cause fear in the production of *srngara-rasa*, and that Krsna is an infant and Radha is proud. But these misconceptions should be rejected. The *rasa* established by the author is something else. The previous explanations are correct.

**Nanda-nidesatah (because of Nanda’s order)**

*Nanda-nidesatah* can also mean any of the following:

The couple went from Nanda or the couple went from Vraja.

1\. Or they went from the special place within Vrndavana which gave bliss (*nanda*) where they had met.

In these three cases, they left on Krsna’s instruction. He called out to her with pain while sighing: “O Radha! I’m trembling since I cannot give you up.” In this case the hero is obedient (*anukula*) and the heroine is independent (*svadhina-bhartrka*). Their characteristics will be described later.

The first half of the verse (first two lines which describe Nanda’s order) describes desire in separation and the second half describes union in conjugal *rasa*.

### Text 1.2

*vag-devata-carita-citrita-citta-sadma*  
*padmavati-carana-carana-cakravarti \|*  
*sri-vasudeva-rati-keli-katha-sametam*  
*etam karoti jayadeva-havih prabandham \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vag-devata* - By the goddess of speech (spiritual Sarasvati); *carita* - extraordinary topics (or deeds or pastimes); *citrita* - was created (or decorated); *citta-sadma* - the abode of whose heart; *padmdvati-carana* - at the feet of Padmavati \[Radha\]; *carana-cakravarti* - the best dancer; *sri-vasudeva* - of Sri \[Radha\] and Vasudeva \[Krsna\]; *rati-keli katha* - the topics of amorous pastimes; *sametam* - containing; *etam* - this; *karoti* - writes (or makes); *jayadeva-kavih* - the poet Jayadeva; *prabandham* - work.

**Перевод:**

**The poet Jayadeva, whose heart is the abode of extraordinary topics created by spiritual Sarasvati, and who is the best dancer at the feet of Radha, writes this work containing the amorous pastimes of Radha and Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

The author, whose heart is absorbed in the proper object of worship, promises to make a work filled with the highest *srngara-rasa* by describing the pastimes of the Lord who is attractive to all people having knowledge of *rasa*.

**etam karoti jayadeva-kavih prabandham (The poet Jayadeva writes this work)**

The word *jayadeva* means he who reveals (*deva*) Krsna (*jaya*). He reveals Krsna to most fortunate persons by causing them to engage in worship of the Lord intimately. This Jayadeva who is a poet (*kavih*) writes this work. Or *jayadeva-kavih* means the person (*jayadeva*) who is enlightened by Krsna and is thus wise (*kavih*).

Or *jayadeva* means the Lord who is victory (*jaya*) and sportive (*deva*) - Bhagavan Svayam. Then Jayadeva-kavih means the poet who is named after the Lord known as Jayadeva. Or Jayadeva can mean he who rejoices (*deva*) by the victory of Radhika (*jaya*) \[and *jayadeva-kavih* means the poet who is named after this\].

*Jayadeva-kavih* also means “Krsna and the poet” The poet and Krsna have written this work which decisively binds up (*prabandham*) the hearts of the listeners.

**sri-vasudeva-rati-keli-katha-sametam (containing the amorous pastimes of Radha and Krsna)**

The cause of the work is explained as follows. Sri refers to Radha or one who worships (from the root *sri*) Krsna to obtain *rasa*. Or it means she who is worshipped (*sriyate*) by Krsna. In any case, *sri* refers to Radha. Vasudeva means Nanda since Vasudeva literally means he who shines (*deva*) among the Vasus (*vasu*). While explaining the previous life of Nanda, it is said *drono vasunam pravah*: Drona, the best of the Vasus, along with his wife Dhara, spoke to Brahma (SB 10.8.48). Or Vasudeva means he who shines (*deva*) with all wealth (*vasu*). The son of Vasudeva (Nanda) is Vasudeva - Krsna. This name suggests that Krsna is endowed with qualities such as complete wealth. Or *vasu* can also indicate ‘residence’ and thus *vasu* means those who live in Vrndavana, the *gopis* (so Vasudeva means the Lord of those who live in Vrndavana, the *gopis*). Or *vasu* can mean that which makes everything fragrant by the perfume of his pastimes. The suffix *u* is added to *vas* to make a noun from a verb.

*Deva* means one who shines with all qualities like beauty, or one who illuminates everything with his beauty. Or it can mean he who conquers all by his skill. Or it can mean he who, enjoying *srngara-rasa*, causes taste in his devotees. Or it can mean he who gives joy to his devotees by his pastimes. Or it can mean he who is delighted by his devotees. The root *div* means to play, to try to conquer, to shine, to praise, to enjoy, to become intoxicated, to dream, to move and to be beautiful. Thus *deva* means the person who possesses the highest pastimes, qualities, conjugal *rasa*, and enjoyment. He who is both *vasu* and *deva* is called Vasudeva.

Jayadeva has written this work endowed with topics of amorous pastimes (*rati-keli-katha*) of Radha (*sri*) and Krsna (*vasudeva*). He has written this work endowed with descriptions of pastimes of the couple which are performed because of great *anuraga* (*rati*).

**vag-devata-carita-citrita-citta-sadma (whose heart is the abode of extraordinary topics created by spiritual Sarasvati)**

How does one have qualification for the knowledge and conversations of the couple, whose beauty and other qualities are most inconceivable and who are astonishingly attractive? (A rhetorical question)

(In the following two paragraphs, the commentator gives multiple interpretations to the first line of the verse. In fact, he does this a lot throughout the book. The Sanskrit texts are given in brackets to show how the words are taken in each interpretation.)

\[First line of the verse answers.\] The spiritual Sarasvati (*vag-devata*) made (*carita*) the house of the poet’s heart (*citta-sadma*), colorful (*citrita*) with extraordinary poetry. The poetry is endowed with qualities like sweetness and purity. His heart is mentioned as a house, since it is the abode of the greatest wealth of various poetic forms describing the Lord’s various qualities; also it gives comforting shelter to all the devotees who need to rest.

However it is said *yato vaco nivartante*: words cannot describe the Lord (*Taittirlya Upanisad* - 2.4.1). The descriptions (*vak*) of the Lord’s form and qualities, which are the highest human goal (*devata*) and normally cannot be described, have appeared (*carita*) on the poet’s heart (*citta-sadma*), and because of that appearance, the abode of his heart has taken a form of eternal bliss (*citrita*), which is astonishing. Or the meaning is as follows. The poet’s heart is a residence (*citta-sadma*) which has become capable of expressing in words (*citrita*), the pastimes (*carita*) of Krsna, who is the most worthy of worship and who is dear to Radha (*vak-devata*). It is implied that other residences are decorated with various other types of writing whereas this poet’s residence, which is supremely private, is decorated with the direct appearance of the Lord and with the Lord’s personal writing of the verses. This story is well known among the devotees.

**padmavati-carana-carana-cakravarti (the best dancer at the feet of Radha)**

How did the poet become such a vessel of the Lord’s mercy? \[The second line of the verse answers.\] *Padmavati* means she who has a lotus in her hand. *Padma* becomes *padma* according to the rule *saradinam ca* (*Panini* 6.3.120). This word refers to Radha endowed with all auspicious qualities. *Padma* also means having beauty which controls the Lord. *Cakravarti* means the best; the poet is the best, because he has received Radha’s mercy. So, the meaning is, the poet is the best dancer (*carana-cakravarti*) at Radha’s feet. He is skillful at worshipping Radha at all times by dancing, etc. Or the poet is like a wheel (*cakra*), because he dances (*carana*) \[continuously like a rotating wheel\] at the feet of Radha. This indicates the great *bhakti* of the poet. *Tad-yoga-yukta hi ganayanti na laukikam*: those deeply absorbed in meditation do not speak in common language \[Hence the implied meanings in the verse, which are shown in the commentary, are justified\].

The meaning according to common language is ‘he is the chief of those who serve the feet of Radha.’ This indicates the poet’s absorption in *srngara-rasa* and his qualification for writing a work on *rasa*. It is said:

*srngari cet kavih kavye jatam rasa-mayam jagat \|*  
*sa eva ced a srngari sarvam virasatam vrajet \|\|*

**If a poet appreciates** ***srhgara-rasa*****, his work makes the whole world full of** ***rasa*****. If the poet does not appreciate** ***srhgara-rasa*****, he makes the world devoid of** ***rasa*****. (*****Dhvanyaloka vrtti*****)**

*Citra* means a picture or composition, according to *Amara-kosa*. *Carana* means an actor. *Kavi* means a poet or a sage.

### Text 1.3

*yadi hari-smarane sarasam mano*  
*yadi vilasa-kalasu kutuhalam \|*  
*madhura-komala-kanta-padavalim*  
*srnu tada jayadeva-sarasvatim \|\|3\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yadi* - If; *hari-smarane* - remembers Krsna; *sarasam* - in full of love (in *madhurya-rasa*); *manah* -\[your\] mind; *yadi* - \[and\] if; *vilasa* - for playful (or coquettish gestures); *kalasu* - pastimes (or sixty-four types of arts); *kutuhalam* - \[you have\] eagerness; *madhura* -sweet; *komala* - easy to understand (or delicate); *kanta* - pleasing; *padavalim* - a series of verses; *srnu* - hear; *tada* - then; *jayadeva-sarasvatim* - Jayadeva’s eloquence.

**Перевод:**

**If you have devotion to Krsna in** ***madhurya-rasa*****, if you have eagerness for the playful pastimes, then please hear this collection of verses eloquently composed by Jayadeva, which are sweet with** ***rasa*****, easy to understand and pleasing.**

**Комментарий:**

**yadi hari-smarane sarasam mano**

О devotees! *yadi hari-smarane mano* (if your mind remembers Krsna) means “if you have bhakti for the youthful son of Nanda who steals the hearts (*hari*) of all the girls of Vraja, and for no one else.” It is said:

*etah param tanu-bhrto bhuvi gopa-vadhvo*  
*govinda eva nikhilatmani rudha-bhavah*

**Among all persons on earth, these cowherd women alone have attained prefect bodies, for they have achieved** ***mahabhava*** **for Govinda, the source of all forms of the Lord. (SB 10.47.58)**

*bhaktir asya bhajanam tad ihamutropadhi-nairasyenamusmin*  
*manah-kalpanam etad eva naiskarmyam*

***Bhakti*** **is worship of the Lord, concentrating the mind on him, renouncing all material desires for enjoyment (*****upadhi*****) in this world and the next. It destroys all** ***karmas*****. (*****Gopala-tapani Upanisad*****, 1.15)**

The word *sarasam* indicates *srngara-rasa*.

**yadi vilasa-kalasu kutuhalam**

If you have eagerness (*kutuhalam*) for the pastimes (*kalasu*) containing coquettish gestures (*vilasa*) of Radha and other *gopis*.

*tat-kaliko visesas tu vilaso’nga-kriyadisu*

***Vilasa*** **refers to actions particular to the time, involving movement of the limbs. (*****Dasa-rupaka*****)**

It is said:

*skhalati bahulam pada-nyase vilokini... nyatah*  
*sthiram api tadambhojam prayati vidhunanam \|*  
*smitam api mudha vyarthah krodho vrthaiva camatkrtir*  
*dayita-savidham yantyas tanvya vilasa-raso’py ayam \|\|*

**The sweetness of** ***vilasa*** **(activities particular to time) of the slender woman, who is going towards her lover, is as follows. \[She is\] glancing, \[and because of that\] she stumbles excessively while walking. \[Then\] she begins to shake a stationary lotus, and \[she thinks\] “Smiling is useless. Anger is useless. Astonishment is useless.”**

The first two lines of the text describe the devotion of the devotees of the Lord. *Kala* means a phase of the moon and pastimes according to *Visva*.

The word *kalasu* can also refer to the sixty-four types of actions in *rati* which are as follows:

*hasitam gitam pathitam nrtyam tatha vina venu-vadyam*  
*talah pustaka-jnanam ayuso vedah \|*

*anga-samvahanam caru-darsanam copalaksanam \|*

**srnu tada jayadeva-sarasvatim**

If you have devotion for the Lord and have eagerness for the pastimes involving the *gopis*, then (*tada*) please hear (*srnu*) the poem written by Jayadeva. Or *srnu tada* means, please understand with reverence (srnuta), О people with great piety (*da*)!

**madhura-komala-kanta-padavalim**

This poem of Jayadeva is a series of verses (*padavalim*) which are sweet (*madhura*), very soft (*komala*) since they are easily understood, and pleasant (*kanta*) since they can be sung. The words *madhura*, *sukumara* (a synonym for *komala*) and *kanta* are explained in the following verses:

*madhuram rasavad vaci vastuny api rasasthitih \|*  
*yena madhyanti dhimanto madhuneva madhuvratah \|\|*

***Madhura*** **means filled with** ***rasa*****. It is situated in words and objects. The intelligent people take joy in this sweetness just as bees take joy in honey. (*****Kavyadarsa*** **1.51)**

*suprayogair mahac-chabdair yuktam aspasta-sandhibhih \|*  
*sukumarartha-samyuktam sukumaram pracaksate \|\|*

**A work is called soft (*****sukumara*****) when it is easy to understand because of eloquent words, when it is used properly, and when it is without elision of vowels. (*****Lilaravinda*** **2)**

*janmanah srotr-visadam prahlada-janakam bhavet*  
*sabda-bandhanuyogena tat kantam iti kirtitam \|\|*

**The father of Prahlada gives lamentation to the listener from his birth. However, by skilful use of words, the story becomes pleasing (*****kanta*****).**

There are two types of sweetness (*madhura*): that which takes shelter of sound (*sabda*) and that which takes shelter of meaning (*artha*). Similarly there are two types of softness (*komala*): of sound and of meaning (easy to understand). There are also instances where words may be easy to understand such as “death, the end of fame”, but are still harsh in meaning. Pleasantness (*kanta*) \[has also two types:\] through sound and through meaning. Hearing skilful verses pleasing to the listener takes shelter of sound. When the meaning is sweet, it creates intense *rasa*. *Rasa* means loving relationships like conjugal *rasa*.

These verses (verses 1-3) have shown the subject matter, the goal, the *sambandha* and the qualification. *Rahah-kelayah jayanti* (May the private pastimes remain glorious) in verse 1, indicates the subject matter. The goal is attraction for the qualities of Radha and Krsna, who are endowed with all qualities. The *sambandha* (relationship of subject to the verses) is that the poem (*pratikapadaka*) describes only its intended subject - the pastimes of Radha and Krsna (*pratipadya*). The heart steeped in this *rasa* is the qualification, as explained in this verse. In this way the four *anubandhas* have been shown.

### Text 1.4

*vacah pallavayaty umapati-dharah sandarbha-suddhim giram janite jayadeva eva saranah slaghyo duruha-druteh \| srngarottara-sat-prameya-racanair acarya-govardhana-spardhi ko’pi na visrutah sruti-dharo dhoyi kavi-ksamapatih \|\|4\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vacah* - Words; *pallavayati* - extends; *umapati-dharah* - Umapati-dhara; *sandarbha-suddhim* - Excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornaments (or the purity of the arragement); *giram* - in its expression (or of poetry); *janite*- knows; *jayadeva* -Jayadeva; *eva* - alone, *saranah* - Sarana; *slaghyo* - \[is\] praiseworthy; *duruha-druteh* - because of \[his\] cryptic meaning and rapidity; *srngarottara* - in which excellent *srngara-rasa* (or in which *srngara-rasa* is preeminent); *sat-prameya-racanaih* - Because of \[his\] truly exemplary compositions; *acarya-govardhana* - with Govardhana Acarya; *spardhi* - who can compete; *ko’pi na* - no one; *visrutah* - is known, *sruti-dharah* - \[Is able to\] remember by hearing only once; *dhoyi* - Dhoyi; *kavi-ksamapatih* - the king of poets.

**Перевод:**

**Umapati-dhara writes profusely and Sarana writes quickly with cryptic meaning. No one can compete with Govardhana Acarya who writes of excellent srngara-rasa as an example for all. Dhoyi, the king of poets, remembers by hearing only once. But Jayadeva knows excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornament.**

**Комментарий:**

The poet Umapati-dhara, a minister of Laksmana Sena, writes extensively; but his writings are devoid of suggestive meaning; they don’t have attractive qualities of sound or meaning. This is hinted at in this verse. The poet Sarana is praiseworthy in composing verses  quickly but they are difficult to understand. His poetry is without qualities like clarity (*prasada*) and faulted with abstruse meanings. No one is known to compete with Govardhanacarya in writing exemplary poetic works, in which there is excellence of *srngara-rasa* or predominance of *srngara-rasa*. But he cannot write works endowed with qualities like sweetness, since he is preoccupied with material *rasa*. The word *acarya* is used with mockery, since Govardhana was not an *acarya* of the best spiritual poetry, for he gave material instructions. The kingly (*ksama-patih*) poet named Dhoyi learned scriptures just by hearing them. This indicates that  he is not expert at poetry. The word *ksama-patih* suggests his pride and materialism. Jayadeva alone and no one else knows excellent poetry endowed with qualities and ornaments (*sandarbha-suddhim*) in its expression (*giram*), since his work describes the qualities of the Lord. It is said in Bhagavatam:

*na yad vacas citra-padam harer yaso*  
*jagat-pavitram pragrnita karhicit \|*  
*tad vayasam tirtham usanti manasa*  
*na yatra hamsa niramanty usik-ksayah \|\|10\|\|*

**Works whose attractive verses do not sing at all the glories of the Lord, which purify not only the author but the whole world, are considered by the devotees who have captured the mind of the Lord to be the place for crows. In such a place, swans which live in pleasant lakes, take no enjoyment at all.**

*tad-vag-visargo janatagha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-slokam abaddhavaty api \|*  
*namany anantasya yaso 'nkitani yac*  
*chrnvanti gayanti grnanti sadhavah \|\|11\|\|*

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotees hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people. (SB 1.5.10-11)**

Thus Jayadeva shows the greatness of his composition since it is endowed with excellent sound and meaning. That is, it is filled with *rasa* because it is endowed with excellent ornaments and devoid of faulty subject matter. It is said:

*nirdosam gunavat kavyam alankarair alankrtam \|*  
*rasanvitam kavih kurvan kirtim bhaktim ca vindati \|\|*

**A poet attains fame and devotion by writing poetry that is enriched with good qualities, devoid of faults (devoid of faulty subject matter), properly ornamented and filled with** ***rasa*****. (*****Sarasvati-kanthabharana*** **1.2)**

*Visva* says that *pallava* means a bud, a bush, extensive or strong. *Amara-kosa* says *drutam* means light or fast. *Uttaram* means an answer.

**\|\| Song One \|\|**

*malava-gauda-ragena rupaka-talena glyate \| astapadi \|*

**In the** ***raga*** **of** ***malava-gauda*** **and in the**  ***tala*** **of** ***rupaka*****, the poem is sung in eight verses to each song.**

Epigraph to the song:

**Reasons for writing song one**

It was previously said “May the pastimes of Radha and Krsna \[who have *parakiya* relationship\] shine eternally!” (verse 1.1). How is it possible to attain *rasa* by *parakiya* relationship, since that becomes filled with fear and condemnation, and is considered vulgar? How can such a relationship shine eternally?

*asvargyam ayasasyam ca phalgu krcchram bhayavaham*  
*jugupsitam ca sarvatra hy aupapatyam kula-striyah*

**For a woman from a respectable family, petty adulterous affairs are always condemned. They bar her from heaven, ruin her reputation and bring her difficulty and fear. (SB 10.29.26)**

The author wants to remove this fault and establish *parakiya* in spiritual world as highest one. He does this by saying that the *parakiya* relationship is filled with eternal bliss (and not a material  one). He conveys this through invoking auspiciousness \[in the following song\] wherein he offers respects to Krsna \[who is the  Supreme Lord\], who is thus fully capable of removing all obstacles..  In this song he establishes the supreme position of the lover Krsna as  possessor of qualities of the *avataras*, as possessor of all astonishing  powers, and as the controller of all beings. His possessing of all  astonishing powers proves that he’s the controller of all beings.  That Krsna has the form of an uncontrolled young cowherd boy  endowed with pastimes. His form gives an experience of spiritual  *rasa* .- In confirmation \[of the fact that *parakiya* relationship is eternal and spiritual\], it is said:

*gopinam tat-patinam ca sarvesam eva dehinam*  
*yo ’ntas carati so 'dhyaksah kridaneneha deha-bhak \|\|35\|\|*

**Krsna, appearing directly in this world, moves among the** ***gopis*****, among their apparent husbands, and among other inhabitants of Vraja, in his eternal spiritual body performing his pastimes.**

*anugrahaya bhaktanam manusam deham asthitah*  
*bhajate tadrsih krida yah srutva tat-paro bhavet \|\|36\|\|*

**When the Lord assumes a humanlike body to show mercy to his devotees, he engages in such pastimes. Hearing about those pastimes a person becomes dedicated to him. (SB 10.33.35-36)**

The Lord speaks in glorification of the *gopis*.

*evam mad-arthojjhita-loka-veda*  
*svanam hi vo mayy anuvrttaye ’balah*  
*mayaparoksam bhajata tirohitam*  
*masuyitum marhatha tat priyam priyah \|\|21\|\|*

**“My dear girls, understanding that simply for my sake you had rejected the authority of worldly opinion, of the Vedas and of your relatives, I acted as I did only to increase your attachment to me. Even when I removed myself from your sight by suddenly disappearing, I never stopped loving you. Therefore, my beloved** ***gopis*****, please do not harbor any bad feelings toward me, your beloved.”**

*na paraye ’ham niravadya-samyujam*  
*sva-sadhu-krtyam vibudhayusapi vah*  
*ya mabhajan durjara-geha-srnkhalah*  
*samvrscya tad vah pratiyatu sadhuna \|\|22\|\|*

**“I am not able to repay my debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahma. Your connection with me is beyond reproach. You have worshiped me, cutting off all domestic ties, which are difficult to break. Therefore, please let your own glorious deeds be your compensation.” (SB 10.32.21-22)**

It is said here that this lover is the supreme Lord and the *gopis* are eternally his consorts who are the highest forms of *prema-rasa*. The conclusion proved here is that this *parakiya* relationship is the shelter of unlimited *rasa* and is the highest excellence. The Lord condemns the worldly *parakiya* relationship:

*asvargyam ayasasyam ca phalgu krcchram bhayavaham*  
*jugupsitam ca sarvatra hy aupapatyam kula-striya*

**For a woman from a respectable family, petty adulterous affairs are always condemned. They bar her from heaven, ruin her reputation and bring her difficulty and fear. (SB 10.29.26)**

However this verse from Bhagavata concerns material relationships. Or it is stated to test the love of the *gopis* by using crooked words in order to increase *rasa*.

The characteristics of *malava-raga* are as follows:

*nitambini-cumbita-vaktra-padmah*  
*suka-dyutih kundalavan pramattah \|*  
*sangita-salam pravisan pradose*  
*maladharo malava-raga-rajah \|\|*

**The king named** ***malava-raga*** **has a lotus face kissed by a slender waisted woman. He has the complexion of a parrot, wears earrings and a garland and is lascivious. He enters the music room in the twilight.**

\[*Rupaka tala* is defined as follows\]

*rupake syad drutam laghu*

*Rupaka tala* is 2 (*drutam*) + 4 (*laghu*) = 6 beats.

### Text 1.5

*pralaya-payodhijale dhrtavan asi vedam*  
*vihita-vahitra-caritram akhedam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-mina-sarira*  
*jaya jagadisa hare \|\|5\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pralaya* - Of destruction; *payodhijale* - in the waters of ocean; *dhrtavan asi* - you protect; *vedam* - the Vedas; *vihita-vahitra* -is made \[like\] a boat; *caritram* - whose behavior; *akhedam* - easily; *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *mina-sarlra* - by whom the form of a fish! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds (or О controller of \[all fourteen\] worlds); *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You easily protect the Vedas in the waters of destruction, while acting as a boat. О Kesava, who assumed the form of a fish! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

**Meaning of last two lines**

О Kesava! Victory to you! Kesava means ‘the Lord who controls (yas) Brahma (*ka*) and Siva (*isa*).’ May you remain with all excellence (*jaya*), by causing bliss to your devotees!

\[But Kesava may say\] “How is it possible to create happiness for my devotees?”

(To bring out the deeper meanings, the commentator uses the dialogue form here, and in several other places)

You are the controller of all fourteen worlds (*jagadisa*)! How is it not possible for the controller of the fourteen worlds to create that happiness?

There is another reason why it is possible. You remove the sufferings of the devotees (*hare*). The nature of the Lord to act for the devotees, is then shown by the description of each *avatara*, though the Lord should not assume such forms (such as fish). For instance the Lord assumes the form of a fish. The Lord appears (*dhrta*) in that form by his free will. The chorus (last two lines) is repeated with each verse.

A verse has four parts:

*udgraho prathamo bhagas tato melapakah smrtah \|*  
*dhruvatvac ca dhruvah pascad abhogas catisobhanah \|*

**The introductory portion is called** ***udgraha*** **and then next portion is called** ***melapaka*** **(bringing together). The third part is called** ***dhruva*** **because it is fixed. The last portion is called** ***abhoga*** **(expansion), which is most splendid. (For more details, please refer to papers on structure of** ***prabandhas*****)**

Though the words describing the particular *avatara* vary in each chorus (*dhruva*) of the verses in this song, the words are identical.

**Explaining rest of the verse**

“Why did I appear in the form of a fish?” This is explained in the rest of the verse.

Assuming the body of a fish, you acted as a boat (*vahitra*) and held up the Vedas (*vedam*) which had sunk in the water of the ocean of *pralaya*, and you did this without distress (*akhedam*) since you are the source of the highest bliss.

Or you supported knowledge (*vedam*) by giving (*vahitra*) the Vedas to Satyavrata. Because of love of your devotees you give no regard for hardships (*akhedam*).

The word *payodhi* (ocean) indicates water. *Jale* (water) repeats this. This repetition is employed to indicate that there was no place to take rest. There were no islands in that vast expanse of water. Still, you held up the Vedas. You did this by assuming the body of a fish. This indicates the Lord’s inclination to act on behalf of his devotee. *Mina* means fish according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 1.6

*ksitir ativipulatare tava tisthati prsthe*  
*dharani-dharana-kina-cakra-garisthe \|*  
*kesava dhrta-kacchapa-rupa*  
*jaya jagadisa hare \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ksitih* - The Earth; *ativipulatare* - very wide; *tava* - your; *tisthati* - stands; *prsthe* - on \[your\] back; *dharani-dharana* - of holding up the earth; *kina-cakra* - because of the round scar; *garisthe* -venerable; *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *kacchapa-rupa* - by whom the form of a turtle! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**The Earth remains on your wide back, and your back is worshipped because of the round scar created by holding up the earth. О Kesava, who has assumed the form of turtle! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

Victory, О Kesava, who assumed the form of a turtle! The reason for assuming such a form is explained: the Earth is situated on your back. The present tense (earth is situated) indicates that even now the Earth is situated there. Hence the act is remarkable.

“How can the Earth which is 500 million *yojanas* in breadth be situated on my back?”

“Your back is wider than the Earth (*ativipulatare*).”

Your back is most venerable (*garisthe*) since it was marked with a circular scar from holding up the Earth. Or your back is marked with a huge circular scar from holding up the earth.

*Kacchapa* means turtle according to *Amara-kosa*. *Kina* means a scar according to *Haravali*. The Lord assumes a burden such as holding up the earth because of his devotees.

### Text 1.7

*vasati dasana-sikhare dharani tava lagna sasini kalanka-kaleva nimagna \| kesava dhrta-sukara-rupa jaya jagadisa hare \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vasati* - Remains; *dasana-sikhare* - to the tip of your tusk; *dharani* -the Earth; *tava* - your; *lagna* - stuck; *sasini -* to the moon; *kalanka-kaleva* - like a small spot; *nimagna* - attached; *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *sukara-rupa* - by whom the form of a boar! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**The earth remains stuck to the tip of your tusk, like a small spot embedded on the crescent moon. О Kesava, who assumes the form of a boar! Victory to you! О protector of the Worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who assumed the form of a boar! The purpose in appearing as a boar is described here. The earth, which supports all people, remains stuck to the tip of your tusks, like a small spot attached (*nimagna*) to the moon (moon is compared to his tusks). The phrase (like a spot attached to moon) suggests the shape of young waxing moon (crescent moon), which is long and thin. The phrase is thus not useless in the verse. Also the earth is nourished by contact with the tusks. *Dasana* means tooth according to *Amara-kosa*. *Sikhara* means the tip or the peak of a mountain. According to *Visva*, *kala* means a small portion or one of the sixteen phases of the moon.

### Text 1.8

*tava kara-kamala-vare nakham adbhuta-srngam*  
*dalita-hiranyakasipu-tanu-bhrngam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-nara-hari-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|8\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tava* - Your; *kara-kamala-vare* - on \[your\] hands, which are the best lotuses; *nakham* - there are claws; *adbhuta-srngam* - with astonishing tips; *dalita* - have torn apart; *hiranyakasipu* - \[demon\] Hiranyakasipu; *tanu-bhrngam* - wasp-like body, *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *nara-hari-rupa* - by whom the form of a manlion! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* — О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**On your lotus hands, you have claws with astonishing tips, which have torn apart the wasp-like body of Hiranyakasipu. О Kesava, who has assumed the form of a man-lion! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who assumed the form of Narasimha! The purpose of the *avatara* is explained here. In your hands, which are the best lotuses, there are claws whose tips (*srngam*) are astonishing. Why are they astonishing? Because they tore up the wasp-like body (*tanu-bhrngam*) of the demon Hiranyakasipu. The wasp is inclined to tear up flower petals. However, the claws of Narasimha, which were like flower petals, tore up the wasp of Hiranyakasipu. This is astonishing. By describing the demon’s huge body as a wasp, the extreme softness of Narasimha’s lotus hands is indicated. In order to frighten Hiranyakasipu, who tormented devotees, the Lord assumed the body of a lion.

*Hari* means a lion according to *Amara-hosa*. *Srnga* means tip  according to *Visva*. *Nakha* means nails and *tanu* means body  according to *Amara-kosa*. *Bhrnga* means a wasp or bee.

### Text 1.9

*chalayasi vikramane balim adbhuta-vamana*  
*pada-nakha-nira-janita-jana-pavana \|*  
*kesava dhrta-vamana-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*chalayasi* - You cheat; *vikramane* - in \[your\] steps; *balim* - \[King\] Bali; *adbhuta-vamana* - О astonishing dwarf; *pada-nakha* - on \[your\] toenail; *nira* - with the water; *janita* - created; *jana-pavana* - who purifies the people! *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *vamana-rupa* - by whom the form of a dwarf! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**О astonishing dwarf! You have cheated Bali by taking huge steps. О the one who purifies all the people by the water that touched your toenail! О Kesava, who has assumed the form a dwarf! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who assumed the form of a dwarf! Victory to you! The reason for this appearance is given here. О astonishing dwarf (*adbhuta-vamana*)! You cheated Bali, the king of the demons, by taking steps. Upendra (another name of Vamana which means younger brother of Indra), taking the form of Vamana - the dwarf, captured Bali, since Bali could not fulfill the promise as there was no more land to offer for the third step. Upendra, then gave the three worlds to Indra. This astonishing act is well known in the Puranas. It is also astonishing that the Lord helped his devotee Indra by using deception since he is controlled by his devotee.

О Lord, who purifies people by the Ganga water created by your toe nail! Expanding himself with the three steps, the Lord pierced the earthly shell of the universe with the tip of the nail of his big toe and then washed his lotus feet by that method. The water from the second layer of the universal shell and the water used by Brahma as *arghya*, eventually came to the earth as the Ganga and flows over people. This is famous in the Puranas.

Expanding himself while standing on earth, by trickery, Vamana was able to purify all the worlds by the water which touched his toe nail, without using water within the universe (this is referring to the second line of the verse). That is astonishing. The Lord’s great power is astonishing. Though he cheated Bali in the past, at present he remains near Bali. Thus the present tense (you cheat) is used in the verse. The ability to purify is a good quality in a hero. *Nira* means water according to *Amara-kosa*. *Bali* refers to the demon according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.10

*ksatriya-rudhira-maye jagad apagata-papam*  
*snapayasi payasi samita-bhava-tapam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-bhrgu-pati-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ksatnya-rudhira-maye* - Full of blood of the Ksatriyas; *jagad* -world; *apagata papam* - whose sin is \[thereby\] destroyed; *snapayasi* - you cause to bathe; *payasi* - in the lake; *samita-bhava-tapam* - \[and\] whose heat of samsara is thereby dispelled; *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *bhrgu-pati-ruра* - by whom the form of Bhrgupati (Parasurama)! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You have made the world bathe in the lake full of blood of the Ksatriyas and destroyed their sins, thus the world is free from the heat of** ***samsara*****. О Kesava, assuming the form of Parasurama! Victory to you! О controller of the worlds! О destroyer of Suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who took the form of Parasurama! Victory to you! The reason for his appearance is described here. You make the people bathe, so all sins are destroyed (*apagata-papam*), in the lake (*payasi*) at Kuruksetra produced from *ksatriyas*’ blood. By killing the *ksatriyas* twenty-one times, Parasurama made five lakes at Kuruksetra from their blood and then performed *tarpana*. Since that time, the place has been a great place of pilgrimage. This is famous in the Puranas. The earth became free of heat of *samsara* (*bhava*). By bathing at this site, living beings become free of all sins. For one who is repentant, all his sufferings are destroyed by bathing in the water. *Rudhira* means blood and *bhava* means samsara according to *Visva*. This action of the Lord shows that he acts for the devotees by destroying unlawful *ksatriyas* in order to maintain *dharma*. Also, just by bathing in the lake made of blood (which is usually considered impure), the Lord created the purity that is usually achieved only after millions of efforts, and thus the Lord’s strength and his ability to purify are also shown.

### Text 1.11

*vitarasi diksu rane dik-pati-kamaniyam*  
*dasa-mukha-mauli-bali ramaniyam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-rama-sarira*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vitarasi* - You bestow; *diksu* - to the \[ten\] directions; *rane* - on the battlefield; *dik-pati-kamaniyam* - as desired by the devatas of the directions; *dasa-mukha-mauli* - the heads of ten-headed (Ravana); *bali* - as an offering; *ramaniyam* - gives relief \[to the world\]. *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *rama-sarira* - by whom the form of Rama! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadlsa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You have offered the ten heads of Ravana to the ten directions on the battlefield, as desired by the devatas of directions, and it has given relief to the world. О Kesava, who assumes the form of Rama! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who assumes the form of Rama! Victory to you! The activities of Rama are described here. On the battlefield, you distribute the heads of Ravana to the ten directions as desired (*kamarniyam*) by the devatas of the directions, since his death gave relief to the world (*ramaniyam*). Ten heads are mentioned to indicate each of them individually. *Mauli* means head. This action shows that the Lord is inclined to act for his devotees and protect the public even though he is served by them. This action also shows his astonishing heroism and skill, since he offered each head individually to each direction.

### Text 1.12

*vahasi vapusi visade vasanam jaladabham*  
*hala-hati-bhiti-milita-yamunabham \|*  
*kesava dhrta-hala-dhara-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vahasi* - You wear; *vapusi visade* - on \[your\| white body; *vasanam* - a garment; *jaladabham* - blue like the raincloud; *hala-hati* - being injured by your plough; *bhiti milita* - afflicted by the fear of; *yamunabham* - shines like that Yamuna! *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *hala-dhara-rupa* - by whom the form of Haladhara (Balarama)! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**On your white body, you wear garment blue like the raincloud, and it shines like the Yamuna that has been afflicted by the fear of being injured by your plough. О Kesava, who takes the form of Balarama! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who takes the form of Balarama! Victory to you! His  pastime is explained here. You wear garment which is blue like the color of a rain cloud (*jaladabham*) on your white (*visade*) body. That garment shone like (*abham*) the Yamuna when overcome (*milita*) with fear of being killed by your plough (*hala-hati-bhiti*). The garment fluttering in the Malaya breeze (breeze coming from Malaya hills) is compared to the Yamuna when overcome with fear. Those in fear tremble. One time Balarama drank Varuni in Vrndavana with the *gopis* and called out to the Yamuna to play in the water. Yamuna, thinking Balarama was babbling because of intoxication, did not come to him out of pride. He became angry and pulled her towards him with his plough. The story is famous in the Puranas. In this *avatara*, his attachment to the *gopis* is seen in this pastime of pulling the Yamuna to enjoy with his dear *gopis*. The word *Haladhara* (*hala* - plow, *dhara* - holder) also suggests that he holds the plough to punish the wicked. Thus he engages in actions for the devotees. This is the purpose of this *avatara*. *Vapu* means body according to *Amara-kosa*. *Visada* means white. *Vasanam* means garment. *Bhiti* means trembling in fear according to Visva.

### Text 1.13

*nindasi yajna-vidher ahaha sruti-jatam*  
*sadaya-hrdaya darsita-pasu-ghatam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-buddha-sarira*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nindasi* - You criticize; *yajna-vidheh* - because it enjoins sacrifices; *ahaha* - alas; *sruti-jatam* - those portions of the Vedas; *sadaya-hrdaya* - having a compassionate heart; *darsita-pasu-ghatam* - in which the killing of animals is explained, *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *buddha-sarira* - by whom the form of Buddha! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You criticize those portions of the Vedas which teach sacrifice of animals. О Lord with a compassionate heart! О Kesava, who assumes the form of Buddha! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who accepts the form of Buddha! Victory to you!

“How can one praise a person who criticizes the Vedas?”

In this form, you do not criticize all the Vedas, but only those statements in the Vedas (*sruti-jatam*) concerned with sacrifices (*yajna-vidheh*).

“Why is that part \[concered with sacrifices\] criticized?”

Oh what a disaster (*ahaha*)! That part explained (*darsita*) the killing of animals (*pasu-ghatam*). You criticize that portion of the Vedas because it teaches killing of animals.

“But, why do you criticize?”

Because you are the Lord with a compassionate heart!

The meaning is this. Seeing uncontrolled attachment \[of people\], he considered those sacrifices not beneficial for people even though they follow the path of the Vedas. Because, they prescribed killing many innocent animals to attain Svarga after death, to obtain \[mere\] temporary enjoyment. The statements in the Vedas advised killing animals while promising rewards of Svarga. He found those statements senseless and then propounded other statements on the path of renunciation such as *na himsyat* *sarva-bhutani* (one should not harm any animal), for attaining the highest goal in life. Thus, as Buddha, you criticized the portions of the Veda which taught sacrifice, but not all the Vedas. Not understanding this intention, unfortunate people lost faith in the Vedas completely. You accepted this *avatara* form to bewilder those people \[who lost faith in the Vedas\]. The purpose of this *avatara* is to fool those who are opposed to the Vedas which are endowed with the highest benefit, for the benefit of your devotees. The word *ahaha* also can indicate astonishment according to Visva. *Sruti* refers to the Vedas according to *Amara-kosa*. *Jatam* means “coming from” according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.14

*mleccha-nivaha-nidhane kalayasi karavalam*  
*dhuma-ketum iva kim api karalam \|*  
*kesava dhrta-kalki-sarira*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mleccha-nivaha* - All the mlecchas (sinful people); *nidhane* - to destroy; *kalayasi* - you wield; *karavalam* - a sword; *dhuma-ketum* - a comet; *iva* - like; *kim api* - very; *karalam* - fearful, *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - was assumed; *kalki-sarira* - by whom the form of Kalki! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadlsa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**You wield a terrifying sword resembling a comet to destroy all the sinful people. О Kesava, who assumes the form of Kalki! Victory to you! О protector of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering!**

**Комментарий:**

О Kesava, who assumes the form of Kalki! Victory to you! His activities are described here. You wield a sword (*karavalam*) to destroy (*nidhane*) all the mlecchas (*mleccha-nivaha*).

*dhuma-ketum iva kim api karalam* - Lord Kalki’s sword is terrifying (*karalam*) like a comet (*dhuma-ketum*). When the living entities see a comet, it indicates disaster for them \[and it terrifies them\], similarly, the mlecchas, when they see the sword on the battlefield, it’s terrifying for them since it destroys them. Or, *dhuma-ketum* means fire, whose sign is smoke (*dhuma* - smoke, *ketum* - sign). In that case, the blade in the Lord Kalki’s sword is dark like smoke and the hilt, which is made of gold, shines like fire.

*Mleccha* means a sinful person according to Visva. *Nivaha* means “all” according to *Amara-kosa*. *Nidhanam* means destruction according to *Visva*. *Karavala* is a sword according to *Amara-kosa*. *Dhumaketu* is a comet according to *Visva*. *Karala* means frightful according to *Visva*. The present tense is used in all these descriptions of *avataras* to indicate the eternal nature of the Lord’s pastimes. After praising the Lord by showing the qualities in his *avataras*, now the author will praise the source of the *avataras* \[in the next verses\].

### Text 1.15

*sri-jayadeva-kaver idam uditam udaram*  
*srnu sukhadam subhadam bhava-saram \|*  
*kesava dhrta-dasa-vidha-rupa*  
*jaya jagad-isa hare \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-kaveh* - By the poet Sri Jayadeva; *idam* - this \[composition\]; *uditam* -spoken; *udaram* - delivers the highest goal (or great); *srnu* - listen; *sukhadam* - which gives bliss; *subhadam* - which gives auspiciousness; *bhava-saram* - \[and\] delivers \[everyone\] from material life (or \[which describes\] the essence of \[his\] birth), *kesava* - О Kesava; *dhrta* - were assumed; *dasa-vidha-rupa -* by whom ten forms! *jaya* - Victory to you! *jagadisa* - О protector of the worlds! *hare* - О destroyer of suffering!

**Перевод:**

**Please listen to this composition spoken by the poet Jayadeva which gives bliss, delivers the highest goal, spreads auspiciousness and delivers everyone from material life. О Kesava, who assumes ten forms! Victory to you! О controller of the worlds! О destroyer of suffering! (END OF SONG ONE)**

**Комментарий:**

**Meaning of last two lines - О Kesava, who assumes ten forms! Victory to you! Kesava means one who resides (sete) with unlimited bliss (ke). Though you have unlimited bliss you assume ten forms. By these actions on behalf of your devotees, you announce your glories. Thus, I take great pleasure in worshipping you. Victory to you for giving happiness to your devotees!**

**Explaining rest of the verse**

Hear this composition (*idam*) spoken (*uditam*) by the poet Jayadeva, which gives satisfaction (*sukhadam*) to the ear and mind and gives auspiciousness (*subhadam*).

This work gives satisfaction and auspiciousness, by explaining the secret or real meaning (*saram*) of his birth (*bhava*). Or the work is the best (*saram*) in one’s life since it makes one’s birth (*bhava*) successful by bestowing the highest human goal.

It is also most generous since it gives the highest goal (*udaram*) or since it is the greatest, or since it quickly gives results. These modifiers are employed to glorify Krsna and include his devotees as well (glorification of devotees who have similar qualities). *Uditam* means spoken. *Sara* means the best according to Visva. After praising the *avataras* who are portions of Krsna, in the next verse the author offers respects directly to Krsna, the crown jewel of all heroes, the perfect form worthy of description in this work.

### Text 1.16

*vedan uddharate jaganti vahate bhugolam udbibhrate*  
*daityam darayate balim chalayate ksatra-ksayam kurvate \|*  
*paulastyam jayate halam kalayate karunyam atanvate*  
*mlecchan murcchayate dasakrti-krte krsnaya tubhyam namah \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vedan* - The Vedas; *uddharate* - he saves; *jaganti* - The Worlds; *vahate* - he supports; *bhugolam* - The Earth; *udbibhrate* - he lifts; *daityam* - Daitya (Hiranyakasipu); *darayate* - he tears apart; *balim* - Bali; *chalayate* - he cheats; *ksatra-ksayam* - The destruction of the *ksatriyas*; *kurvate* - he brings about; *paulastyam* - descendant of Pulatsya (Ravana); *jayate* - he conquers; *halam* - The plough; *halayate* - he wields; *karunyam* - Mercy; *atanvate* - he distributes; *mlecchan* - The sinful; *murcchayate* - he prevails against; *dasakrti* - \[Your\] ten forms; *krte* - for the sake of; *krsnaya* - to Krsna; *tubhyam* - to you; *namah* - my respects.

**Перевод:**

**You save the Vedas, support the worlds, lift up the earth, tear apart Hiranyakasipu, cheat Bali, destroy the** ***ksatriyas*****, conquer Ravana, wield the plough, distribute mercy and destroy the sinful. I offer respects to you, Krsna, who assume these ten forms.**

**Комментарий:**

**krsnaya tubhyam namah**

Krsna is the full embodiment of the highest powers:

*krsir bhu-vacakah sabdo nas ca nirvrtivacakah \|*  
*tayor aikyam param brahma krsna ity abhidhiyate \|\|*

***Krs*** **means “existence” and** ***na*** **means “bliss.” Combined together, the two roots indicate that Krsna is the supreme form of God.(*****Mahabharata - Udyoga-parva*** **71.4)**

Krsna also means “he who attracts his devotees by his supreme sweetness.”

So, the meaning of *'krsnaya tubhyam namah’* is as follows: I offer my respects to Krsna, the form of the highest bliss which is unlimited and most intense, \[he is\] the giver of enjoyment to the devotees, \[he is\] Syamasundara, \[and he is also\] the complete form of *srngara-rasa.*

*Sruti* says *aksarat paratah parah:* the Lord is superior to the Brahman - (*Mundaka Upanisad*). *Parasya saktir vividhaiva sruyate svabhaviki jnana-bala-kriya ca:* the Lord has various superior, intrinsic energies of knowledge, strength and action - (*Svetasvatara Upanisad*). Bhagavad-glta says *aksarad api cottamah:* he is superior to the Brahman (BG 15.18). *Narakrti param brahma sac cidananda vigrahah*: the Lord is the supreme brahman in human form, complete with eternity, knowledge and bliss (*Padma Parana*).

*anandacinmayarasapratibhavitabhis*  
*tabhir ya eva nijarupataya kalabhih \|*  
*goloka eva nivasaty akhilatmabhuto*  
*govindam adi-purusam tam aham bhajami \|\|*

**“I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who, though regarding all the inhabitants as his very self, resides in Goloka exclusively with the young** ***gopis*****, who are embodiments of** ***madhurya-rasa*****. He accepts them as his wives, while they respond to his conjugal affection reciprocally.” (*****Brahma-samhita*** **5.37)**

*Gita-govinda* will also say *srngarah sakhi murtiman iva madhau mugdho harih kridati*: innocent Krsna plays in the spring as if he were the embodiment of Cupid (GG 1.48). Sukadeva says *saksan manmatha-mamathah*: Krsna is the bewilderer of Cupid (SB 10.32.2).

**dasakrti-krte**

“Lord Kesava is the source of all *avataras*. *Kesava* means one who lies down on the water (*kesava*—*ke* (on the water) + *sete* (lies), using the rule *prosodaradini* *yathopadistani* (*Panini* 6.3.109). This refers to Narayana. It is said *etan nanavataranam nidhanam bijam avyayam*: Narayana is the indestructible source of various *avataras* (SB 1.3.5). Then, why do you praise me, the young cowherd boy who plays in the forest, since the scriptures state that Visnu is supreme?”

This verse answers: I offer respects to you (Krsna) who accept the ten forms starting with the fish. The meaning is this. Narayana who is the Lord of the ten *avataras*, is your *amsa*. Brahma says *narayanas tvam*: you are the source of Narayana (SB 10.14.14). Sukadeva says in Bhagavatam:

*stuvatas tasya bhagavan darsayitva jale vapuh*  
*bhuyah samaharat krsno nato natyam ivatmanah*

**While Akrura was still offering prayers, Krsna withdrew his \[Narayana\] form that he had revealed in the water, just as an actor winds up his performance. (SB 10.41.1)**

In describing the *avataras* it is said *ete camsa kala pumsah krsnas tu bhagavan svayam*: these are all expansions and Krsna is the  complete form (SB 1.3.28). *Visnur mahan sa ihayasya kala-visesah*:  Mahavisnu is his special expansion (*Brahma-samhita* 5.48). Thus the meaning of the verse is “I praise you since you are the supreme  form of the Lord.”

Having come to this conclusion, the author again summarizes by reciting the activity of each *avatara*. The Lord saves the Vedas in the  form of fish. He supports the worlds (*jaganti*) in the form of turtle. He lifts up (*udbibhrate*) the earth (*bhu-golam*) in the form of boar.  He tears apart the demon Hiranyakasipu in the form of man-lion.  He cheats Bali in the form of dwarf. He destroys the sinful *ksatriyas*  in the form of Parasurama. He conquers Ravana (*paulastyam*) in  the form of Rama. He holds the plow, the instrument to curb the wicked, in the form of Balarama. He spreads mercy in the form of  Buddha. He destroys the sinful in the form of Kalki. This verse indicates that Krsna is the complete form, endowed with all powers, who performs these actions through his *avataras*. *Akrti* means ‘form’ according to *Visva*.

**\|\| Song Two \|\|**

*gurjari-raga-nihsara-talabhyam giyate \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***gurjari*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***nihsara*****.**

Epigraph to the song: Previously \[in song one\], the poet described the qualities of Krsna through his *avataras*, and \[in verse 1.1, he\] indicated in brief the pastimes of the crest jewel of all heroes - the Supreme Lord, by using the words *rahah kelayah* (private pastimes). Now, the poet describes his qualities such as *dhira-lalita* (will be described in next paragraphs) in *srngara-rasa*, by describing the independent woman (*svadhina-bhartrka*) through the words of a *sakhi*. In this way the poet produces another invocation of auspiciousness.

Though the Lord is the possessor of all powers, his *sakti* - full of all blissful pastimes and *rasa* - is described \[in song two\]. The *sakti* becomes prominent. Elsewhere, other *gopis* become prominent to nourish the *sakti*. The Lord appears as Hero in many forms. The order is as follows: (*dhiroddhata* - haughty, *dhirodatta* - elevated, *dhirasanta* - gentle, *dhira-lalita* - playful)

The Lord is *dhiroddhata* as in Vamana and Parasurama.

*matsarya-darpa-bhuyisthah chadmahankaravan bale \|*  
*cando vikatthamanas ca dhiroddhata udahrtah \|\|*

**The wise cal the person who shows great envy and pride, deceit, anger, forcefulness and boastful nature as** ***dhiroddhata*** **(haughty).**

The *dhiroddhata* is also fond of fame, is dexterous and attached to *dharma*.

The *dhirodatta* (elevated) is described as follows:

*kriya-dhanapati-gambhiro vinitas ca vikatthanah \|*  
*dhirodattah sa vijneyo ramo dasarathir yatha \|\|*

**The** ***dhirodatta*** **hides his actions and wealth, is modest and is without boasting. Rama is an example.**

The *dhirasanta* (gentle) is peaceful, modest and intelligent. Matsya and Buddha are examples. They are famous, fond of *dharma* and skillful.

*Dhira-lalita* (playful) is described as follows:

*vidagdho nava-tarunyah parihasa-visaradah \|*  
*niscinto dhira-lalitah syat prayah preyasi-vasah \|\|*

**He who is clever, endowed with fresh youth (end of** ***kaisora*** **period), skillful at joking and free of worries is called** ***dhira-lalita*****. He is controlled by his dear devotee. (BRS 2.1.230)**

*srngari nayakas tv anyah pancamah kathyate yatha \|*  
*vilasavan vama-silah subhagah sthira-vak yuva \|*  
*patih sadhairya drstis ca sa-vilasa-smitam vacah II*

**There is another, fifth type of hero, in** ***srngara-rasa*** **-** ***srngari-nayaka*****. He is playful, obedient, prosperous, steady in speech, youthful, married, having firm glance and joking words. (*****Sangita-raja*****)**

In this way the hero, dear to Radha is described in various ways in this song.

(Now the *raga* and tala will be described)

*Gurjan* is the *raga* and *mantha* is the *tala*. The *raga* is described as follows:

*syama sukesi malaya-drumanam*  
*mrd-ullasat-pallava-talpa-madhye \|*  
*sruti-svaranam dadhati vibhagam*  
*tantri-mukha daksina-gurjariyam \|\|*

**She is from southern Gujarat and is dark in complexion. Endowed with a beautiful hair, she sits on a bed made of soft, gleaming shoots amidst sandalwood trees. She holds her fingers in** ***tantri*** **position and intones** ***srutis*** **(quarter tones).**

*Manthaka-tala* is described as follows:

*guru-laghu-dvayam yatra bhrnga-talam sa kathyate \|*  
*mangalo manthake jneyo rase cadbhuta-samjnake \|\|*

**Auspicious** ***bhrnga-tala*****, also known as astonishing** ***manthaka*****, consists of 8 (*****guru*****) + 4 (*****laghu*****) +4 (*****laghu*****) = 16 beats.**

*Manthaka-tala* is called astonishing since it reveals astonishing pastimes.

### Text 1.17

*srita-kamala-kuca-mandala*  
*dhrta-kundala e*  
*kalita-lalita-vana-mala*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|17\|\| \[dhru.\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

*srita* - Is resorted to; *kamala-kuca-mandala* - by whom the circle of the breasts of Kamala (Radha) \[which is like a fortress\]; *dhrta-kundala* - by whom the \[*makara*\] earrings are worn; *e* - interjection; *kalita* - is worn; *lalita-vana-mala* - by whom an attractive forest garland! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]; *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]

**Перевод:**

**\[A sakhi speaks\] “O Krsna who has taken shelter of the breasts of Radha, which are like a fortress! О Krsna who is decorated with makara earrings! О Krsna who is wearing an attractive forest garland!**

**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*jaya jaya deva hare -* О Lord who plays (*deva*) or is attached to pastimes (*deva*) with Radha! О lord who steals the hearts of all the young women in the world (*hare*)! Victory to you (*jaya*)! Victory to you! The word jaya is repeated out of respect. These words are the chorus.

**Srita-kamala-kuca-mandala**

*Srita* means ‘taking shelter.’ *Kamala* is derived from the root *kama* (a male lover) plus the suffix la (to receive). *Kamala* means ‘she who controls her lover by her qualities’ (if she receives a male lover, it implies that she dominates him). Or, *kamala* refers to Radha.

\[The meaning is\] О Krsna! Since you’re fearful, you have taken shelter of Radha’s breasts (*kuca*), which are like a fortress (*mandala*).

This phrase shows the greatest qualities which are present in the crown jewel of all heroes. It also shows the quality of the heroine Radha, in the form of her greatest skill in love which she displays in her pastimes. She is an independent lover (*svadhina-bhartrka*). This quality (*svadhina-bhartrka*) will be explained later (GG 12.1).

**dhrta-kundala e - О wearer of makara earrings! Or, О Krsna who wears Radha’s earrings with her permission! Or, О Krsna who wears flower earrings made and gifted by Radha!**

**kalita-lalita-vana-mala**

О Krsna who is wearing (*kalita*) an attractive (*lalita*) forest garland (*vana-mala*), which reaches to his feet! These two phrases (*‘dhrta-kundala e’* and *‘kalita-lalita-vana-mala*’) indicate his beauty. *Vana* means forest. *Vanamala* can also mean a lotus garland. *Sasvata* says that *vanamala* refers to a garland that reaches to the feet.

The phrase can also mean, О Krsna who places (*kalita*) an attractive (*lalita*) garland of forest flowers or lotuses (*vana-mala*) on his beloved’s throat. Or it can mean, О Krsna who becomes submerged (*kalita*) in the forest which has rows of forest flowers (*vana-mala*), for decorating your beloved. Or it can mean, О Krsna, who is decorated (*kalita*) with attractive (*lalita*) drops of perspiration (*vanamala*) because of ecstasy arising from touching your beloved when you decorate her. Or, О Krsna decorated (*kalita*) with drops of perspiration (*vanamala*) arising from exertion during enjoyment. In this case the perspiration arising from fatigue is a *vyabhicari-bhava*? Or the meaning can be, О Krsna who holds various garlands of forest flowers (*vanamala*) which are made for decorating (*kalita*) the beloved. These various explanations show Krsna’s great knowledge of the arts. *Mandala* means a sphere or district according to *Visva.*

### Text 1.18

*dina-mani-mandala-mandana*  
*bhava-khandana e*  
*muni-jana-manasa-hamsa*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dina-mani-mandala* - \[Shines\] like the disk of the jewel of day (the sun) (Kaustubha); *mandana* - whose ornament; *bhava-khandana* -destroyer of rebirth; *e* - interjection; *muni-jana* - of sages (or Gopis, or Radha); *manasa-hamsa* - the swan engrossed in the heart (or Lake Manasa)! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna decorated with the Kaustubha which is shining like the sun! О destroyer of rebirth! О swan engrossed in the heart of Radha!**

**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*dina-mani-mandala-mandana* - О Krsna, who is decorated with the Kaustubha which is superior to the sun , or it means, О Krsna whose ornaments are like the disk of the sun. This indicates the great wealth of Krsna.

**bhava-khandana e** О Krsna! By taking birth in the Nanda’s family, you destroy (*khandana*) further birth (*bhava*) for the devatas in this world . The devatas also desire to be born in the family of *gopis*. Or the phrase means, О Krsna who defeats (*khandana*) Siva (*bhava*) by musical skill.

*vividha-gopa-caranesu vidagdho*  
*venu-vadya urudha nija-siksah*  
*tava sutah sati yadadhara-bimbe*  
*datta-venur anayat svara-jatih*

*savanasas tad upadharya suresah*  
*sakra-sarva-paramesthi-purogah*  
*kavaya anata-kandhara-cittah*  
*kasmalam yayur aniscita-tattvah*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said\] “O pious mother Yasoda, your son, who is expert in all the arts of herding cows, has invented many new styles of flute-playing. When he takes his flute to his bimba-red lips and sends forth the tones of the harmonic scale in variegated melodies, Brahma, Siva, Indra and other chief** ***devatas*** **listen repeatedly to the sound. Although they are the most learned authorities, even with submissive hearts they cannot ascertain the meaning and forget everything.” (SB 10.35.14-15)**

**Or the phrase means, О Krsna who destroys (khandana) the appearance (bhava) of pain of separation for his dear devotees.**

\[All these different meanings of\] the phrase *‘bhava-khandana e*’ indicates Krsna’s good conduct and his skill in music.

**muni-jana-manasa-hamsa**

О Krsna, the swan (*hamsa*) in the minds (or Manasa Lake) of the silent *gopis* (here *gopis* are called *munijana* since they are silent like the *munis*). Or, О Krsna who - out of great attachment - has become a swan (*hamsa*) trying to fathom the intentions in the mind (*manasa*) of Radha who is silent because of her anger (here Radha is called munijana, since she is silent).

Or, О Krsna who is inclined to contemplation (*munijana*), who is thinking of the qualities of Radha! Or О Krsna who has given up all other places and dwells in the heart of Radha (*manasa-hamsa*)!

This employs the ornament called *paramparita-rupaka* (metaphor using puns such as the word *manasa*).

*niyataropanopayah syad aropah parasya yah \|*  
*tat paramparitam sliste vacake bheda-bhaji va \|\|*

***Rupaka*** **(metaphor) becomes consequential (*****paramparita*****) when one thing is called another object using a pun or distinction. (*****Kavya-prakasa*** **95)**

*Manasam* refers to the lake according to *Visva*. *Harhsa* is a bird.

### Text 1.19

*kaliya-visa-dhara-ganjana*  
*jana-ranjana e*  
*yadukula-nalina-dinesa*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kaliya-visa-dhara* - Of the snake Kaliya; *ganjana* - shamer; *jana-ranjana* - attractor of people; *e* - interjection; *yadukula-nalina* - for the lotus of the cowherd dynasty; *dinesa* - the day-lord \[sun\]! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna who humbled Kaliya! О attractor of all the people! О sun which makes the lotus of cowherd families joyful!**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*kaliya-visa-dhara-ganjana* - О Krsna who treated the snake (*visadhara*) Kaliya with disrespect (*ganjana*). This shows Krsna’s skill in the sixty-four arts (art of dancing is one of the sixty-four arts).

*Jana-ranjana e* - О Krsna who attracts (*ranjana*) everyone (*jana*) with sweet words. This shows his wonderful power of speaking. Or you attract (*ranjana*) all the *sakhis* by treating Radha (*jana*) nicely. Or Krsna who decorates Radha (*jana*) with various colors (*ranjana*).

*yadukula-nalina-dinesa* - О Krsna, the sun (*dinesa*) which makes the lotus (*nalina*) of the cowherd dynasty (*yadukula*) bloom. Yadu refers to the cowherds. *Hari-varhsa* says *yadavanam hitarthaya dhrto girivaro maya*: I held up Govardhana for the benefit of the Yadavas (thus the word Yadus refers to cowherds.) *Visadhara* means snake according to *Amara-kosa*. *Nalina* means lotus. *Ranjana* means ‘producing attachment’ or ‘red sandalwood’ according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.20

*madhu-mura-naraka-vinasana*  
*garudasana e \|*  
*sura-kula-keli-nidana*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madhu-mura-naraka* - Of demons Madhum, Mura, and Naraka; *vinasana* - destroyer; *garudasana* - whose seat is Garuda; *e* -interjection; *sura-kula* - for the lineage of devatas; *keli-nidana* - the cause of play! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O destroyer of Madhu, Mura, and Naraka! О Krsna, who has Garuda as a seat! О cause of play for the lineage of devatas!**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

**Meaning of the first two lines**

О Destroyer of Madhu, Mura and Naraka! О Krsna, who has Garuda as your seat!

Or the meaning of those lines is as follows. *Madhu-mura* means, Krsna who protects (*mura*) *madhurya-rasa* (*madhu*) for his devotees. *Mura* is formed from the verb *mur* by the rule *igupadha* (*Panini* 3.1.135). By this he destroys (*vinasana*) other types of happiness (*ka*) of people (*nara*), which means he makes other types of happiness insignificant. *Svarita-venuna susthu cumbitam itara-raga-vismaranam nrnam*: that nectar is thoroughly relished by your vibrating flute and makes people forget any other attachment (SB 10.31.14). You have an amorous pose using the body. The pose resembles Garuda (*garudasana*). This shows his skill in love affairs.

*sura-kula-keli-nidana* - О Krsna, the cause (*nidana*) of the play of devatas (*sura-kula-keli*). *Deva hi krida-caturah*: the devatas are fond of playing. They have learned the taste for playing from you. This indicates Krsna’s supreme ability to engage in pastimes. *Kulam* means people of a family lineage.

### Text 1.21

*amala-kamala-dala-locana*  
*bhava-mocana e*  
*tribhuvana-bhavana-nidhana*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*amala-kamala-dala* - Are like flawless lotus petals; *locana* - whose eyes; *bhava-mocana* - the liberator of rebirth in the material world; *e* - interjection; *tribhuvana* - within the three worlds; *bhavana-nidhana* - whose residential place is a home \[Vrndavana\]! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O Lord with flawless lotus petal eyes! О Lord, who liberates us from rebirth in the material world! О Krsna, who dwells in a house in Vrndavana in three worlds!**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*amala-kamala-dala-locana* - О Krsna with eyes like flawless lotus petals. This indicates his beauty.

*bhava-mocana e* - О Lord who frees (*mocana*) the surrendered devotees from birth in the material world (*bhava*). This indicates that the devotees worship him (the devotee worships him and this causes freedom from the material world) since he has a form of eternal bliss which he presents to them. Or О Krsna, who has freed (*mocana*) Siva (*bhava*) from the great danger of Vrkasura. Or О Krsna, who has freed Siva from intense vows of yoga by attracting him with the beauty of his lotus eyes \[in Mohini *avatar*\], making Siva favorable.

*tribhuvana-bhavana-nidhana* - О Krsna who has a residential place (*bhavana*), within the three worlds (*tribhuvana*) called Vrndavana. This Vrndavana is a great treasure (*nidhana*), or it’s a house (*nidhana*) \[for you\]! *Nidhanam* means treasure or house. *Bhavana* means house. *Dala* means a petal according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 1.22

*janaka-suta-krta-bhusana*  
*jita-dusana e*  
*samara-samita-dasa-kantha*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|22\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*janaka-suta* - On the daughter of Vrsabhanu (Radha); *krta-bhusana* - by whom the decoration is made; *jita-dusana* - by whom faults are conquered; *e* - interjection; *samara-sam-ita* - who have attained ecstasy in the battle \[of love\]; *dasa-kantha* - on whose neck \[cling\] the ten \[heroines\]! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna, who decorates the limbs of Radha! О destroyer of all faults by your good qualities! О Krsna, on whose neck cling ten heroines who have attained ecstasy in the battle of love!**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*janaka-suta-krta-bhusana* - Janaka means one who gives happiness (*ka*) to the people (*jana*) of Vraja. The main person there is Vrsabhanu. His daughter (*suta*) is Radha. О Krsna who decorates Radha by drawing patterns on her limbs!

*jita-dusana e* - О Krsna who has conquered (*jita*) all faults (*dusana*) by your good qualities! Good qualities indicate the hero’s beautiful form and his talent in all arts.

*samara-samita-dasa-kantha* - О Krsna on whose neck (*kantha*) cling ten heroines who have attained (*ita*) happiness (*sam*) and are murmuring in ecstasy in the battle of love (*samara*). This indicates his skill in love. Or О Krsna on whose throat cling heroines who have attained a state (*dasa*) of murmuring in ecstasy in the battle of love (*samara*).

### Text 1.23

*abhinava-jala-dhara-sundara*  
*dhrta-mandara e*  
*sri-mukha-candra-cakora*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*abhinava-jala-dhara* - As a fresh rain cloud; *sundara* - as beautiful as; *dhrta-mandara* - by whom Mandara \[Govardhana\] was held; *e* - interjection; *sri-mukha-candra* - for the moon-like face of Sri (Radha); *cakora* - the *cakora* bird! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna beautiful as fresh rain cloud! О lifter of Govardhana! О Krsna like a cakora drinking the beautiful moon-like face of Radha!**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

*abhinava-jala-dhara-sundara* - О Krsna who is as beautiful as a fresh rain cloud that shows great beauty! This indicates his great beauty.

*dhrta-mandara e* - О Krsna who lifted Govardhana (*mandara*)! In Gopala-stava, Sankaracarya has said *dhrtaika-hasta-mandara*: Krsna held the mountain (*mandara*) in one hand.

*sri-mukha-candra-cakora* - О Krsna who, like a *cakora*, solely tastes the beautiful face (*sri-mukha*) of Radha which is like the moon. You do that since it gives you joy! Krsna is compared to a cakora bird because he longs to drink the nectar of the lips of Radha’s moonlike face. This shows his skill in love. *Abhinava* means ‘new’ according to *Amara-kosa*. In seven verses (1.17 - 1.23) the qualities of the hero have been described. It is said:

*tyagi kulinah kusalo ratesu*  
*kalpah kalavit taruno dhanadhyah \|*  
*bhavyah ksami sthira-rucih subhago’bhimani*  
*strinam abhistas tv iha nayakah syat I\|\|*

**The hero should be renounced, good mannered, skilful in love, competent, knowledgeable of the arts, young, wealthy, handsome, tolerant, fixed in taste, happy, confident, and desired by women. (*****Srngara-tilaka*****)**

### Text 1.24

*tava carane pranata vayam*  
*iti bhavaya e*  
*kuru kusalam pranatesu*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|24\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tava* - Your; carane - at \[your\] feet; *pranata* - are offering respects;  *vayam* - we; *iti* - thus, *bhavaya* - \[Please\] think \[of us\]; *e* - interjection; *kuru kusalam* - provide auspiciousness; *pranatesu* - to  those who are offering respects \[to you at your feet\]! *jaya jaya* -Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**“Please think of us, who offer you respects. Please remove all obstacles for those surrendered to you.**  
**“Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts  of young women!” (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**
