Having praised Krsna since he is the best of all heroes, the author  now prays as follows.

О Krsna, please think (*bhavaya*) of us, who offer respects (*pranata*) at your feet. Please create auspiciousness (*kuru kusalam*), or in  other words, please remove ail obstacles, for the devotees offering  respects to you.

### Text 1.25

*sri-jayadeva-kaver idam*  
*kurute mudam e*  
*mangalam ujjvala-gitam*  
*jaya jaya deva hare \|\|25\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-kaver* - Of the poet Sri-Jayadeva; *idam* - this \[song\]; *kurute* - creates; *mudam* - joy; *e* - interjection; *mangalam* - auspicious; *ujjvala* - faultless; *gitam* - song! *jaya jaya* - Victory to you (repeated twice)! *deva* - О lord \[of pastimes\]! *hare* - О lord who steals \[the hearts of young women\]!

**Перевод:**

**This song of Jayadeva , faultless and auspicious, creates joy.**  
**Victory to you! О lord of pastimes! О lord who steals the hearts of young women! (chorus (END OF SONG TWO)**

**Комментарий:**

This song of Jayadeva creates joy. The song is without fault (*ujjvala*) and auspicious. The faults of songs are mentioned by Bharata:

*sankitam bhitam udghrstam avyaktam anunasikam \|*  
*kdka-svaram sirah-kampam tatha sthana-vivarjitam \|\|*  
*visvaram virasam caiva vislistam visamahatam \|*  
*vyakulam tala-hinam ca gita-dosan vidur budhah \|\|*

**The wise know a song is faulty when it is sung with uncertainty, with fear, or with unclear intonation, inaudibly, nasalized, with the voice of a crow, while shaking the head, without proper pitch, with dissonantly, unpleasantly, disjointedly, unevenly, confusedly, and without** ***tala*****.**

Or *mangalam* can mean that the poem is a prayer to his Lord, or that it is in *mangala-tala*.

### Text 1.26

*padma-payodhara-tati-parirambha-lagna-*  
*kasmira-mudritam uro madhusudanasya \|*  
*vyaktanuragam iva khelad-ananga-kheda-*  
*svedambu-puram anupurayatu priyam vah \|\|26\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*padma-payodhara-tati* - By the tips of the breasts of Padma (Radha); *parirambha-lagna* - after being closely embraced; *kasmira-mudritam* - imprinted with kumkuma; *urah* - the chest; *madhusudanasya* - of Madhusudana (Krsna); *vyaktanuragam* - by which \[his\] passion is evident; *iva* - as if; *khelad-ananga* - of love play; *kheda* - arising from the fatigue; *svedambu-puram* - filled with streams of perspiration; *anupurayatu* - may it \[the chest\] completely satisfy; *priyam* - in an agreeable manner (or in a satisfying manner); *vah* - to all of you;

**Перевод:**

**The chest of Madhusudana, smeared with the kumkuma from the tips of Radha’s breasts after their embrace, is covered with streams of perspiration, showing the manifestation of his love for  her. The perspiration arises from the fatigue of love play. May this  chest of Madhusudana give full pleasure to all of you.**

**Комментарий:**

After describing the great fortune of the heroine (in song two), the author now gives blessings to the listeners by invoking auspiciousness.

*uro madhustidanasya ... anupurayatu priyam vah* - May the chest of Madhusudana create full satisfaction (*priyam*) for you at every moment. The adjectives (*puram, anuragam, mudritam*) show that he has ability to bestow this blessing since he is eternally attached to the pastimes (He is eternally endowed with pastimes which can give satisfaction, and so he can give this blessing to create  full satisfaction for everyone). The experience of *rasa* of the *gopis*,  which is most desirable, is also hinted \[in statements such as chest  embraced by Radha, etc\]. The word Madhusudana (which means a bee) is used to indicate the attainment of a taste of sweet *rasa* (the reader attains the taste of sweet *rasa*).

Meaning of first two lines - *Padma* refers to a woman who has lotuses, according to the rule *arsa adibhyo’c* (*Panini* 5.2.127). It also indicates a woman with qualities like a lotus. The woman who has the fragrance of million lotuses, is Radha, and thus *Padma* means Radha, a *padmini* woman (the definition of *padmini* woman is given later in this commentary). The fragrance also indicates her youthful beauty. That chest of Krsna has a mark of *kumkuma* (*kasmira*) from the tip of Radha’s breasts because he embraced her. She has put the *kumkuma* from the tip of her breast on his chest to announce that his heart belongs to her and no one else. By embraces which touched only the tips of her breasts her skill in making amorous gestures is made known, announcing her *vama* (obstinate) nature.

Meaning of third line (*vyaktanuragam ... svedambu puram*) - His heart is covered with streams of perspiration arising from fatigue after love play. The perspiration combined with the *kumkuma* produces the marks on his chest and indicates their intense enjoyment. This is like visible affection. It seems that because of the intense affection for her internally, his chest has become colored externally, on the pretext of *kumkuma* stains.

*Mudrita* means “imprinted”. *Pura* means stream. *Payodhara* means breast. *Kandarpa* means Cupid.

The characteristics of a *padmini* woman are as follows:

*kamala-mukula-mrdvi phulla-rajiva-gandhih*  
*surata-payasi yasyah saurabham divyam ange \|*  
*cakita-mrga-drsabhe prantarakte ca netre kuca-*  
*yugalam anarghyam sri-phala-sri-vidambi \|\|*

**She is soft as a lotus bud, scented like a blossoming lotus. She has a divine fragrance in her limbs which enjoy amorous delights. She has darting eyes red at the tips, resembling those of a deer. She has priceless breasts, conquering the beauty of** ***bilva*** **fruits.**

*tila-kusuma-samanam bibhrati nasikam ca*  
*dvija-guru-sura-pujam sraddadhana sadaiva \|*  
*kuvalaya-dala-kantih kapi campeya-gauri*  
*vikaca-kamala-kosakara-kamatapatra \|\|*

**She has a nose like a sesame flower and is devoted to worshipping** ***brahmanas*****, elders and** ***devatas*****. She has the complexion of a water lily and is sometimes golden like a** ***campaka*** **flower. She has a lovely umbrella resembling an open lotus bud.**

*vrajati mrdu sa-lilam rdaahamsiva tanvi*  
*trivali-kalita-madhya hamsa-vani suvesa \|*  
*mrdu suci laghu bhunkte manini gadha-lajja*  
*dhavala-kusuma-vaso vallabha padmini syat \|\|*

**With slender body she walks gently and playfully like a swan. Her waist has three folds and she wears fine clothing. She speaks like a swan. She is highly honored and intensely shy. She eats lightly, cleanly, and gently. She smells like the** ***dhavala*** **flower and is loved by all.**

Since it is impossible to describe Radha’s extraordinary qualities, the above qualities are listed so that people can understand to some degree.

Up to this point the poet invoked auspicious verses which show the suitable qualities of the hero which are necessary in describing the expressed goal of the work. Now, he desires to describe the pastimes of Krsna which are predominated with *srngara-rasa*. So, citing a pastime in brief in this verse, he begins to describe the heroine who is the highest embodiment of *srngara-rasa.* While describing the heroine, he focuses on separation (*vipralambha*) in *srngara-rasa*, in order to nourish the rasa of union of Radha and Mohana who are most attached to each other, who do not want separation at any moment and who desire to begin special pastimes by this additional taste.

### Text 1.27

*vasante vasanti-kusuma-sukumarair avayavair*  
*bhramantim kantare bahu-vihita-krsnanusaranam \|*  
*amandam kandarpa-jvara-janita-cintakulataya*  
*valad-badham radham sarasam idam uсе sahacari \|\|27\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vasante* - In spring season; *vasanti-kusuma-sukumaraih* - as tender as vernal flowers (madhavi flower); *avayavaih* - with limbs; *bhramantim* - who was wandering; *kantare* - in the vast forest; *bahu-vihita* - many times; *krsnanusaranam* - searching for Krsna; *amandam* - intensely; *kandarpa-jvara-janita* - arising from the fever of love; *cintakulataya* - because of the confusion of thoughts; *valad-badham* - whose pain was increasing; *radham* - to Radha; *sarasam* - so as to invoke *rasa;* *idam* - the following \[words\]; *uce* - spoke; *sahacari* - a fellow-friend \[*sakhi*\]

**Перевод:**

**To invoke rasa, a sakhi spoke the following words to Radha, as Radha whose limbs were as tender as the flowers of a madhavi creeper, repeatedly searched for Krsna in the vast forest in spring season and experienced great pain while overcome with thoughts arising from the fever of love.**

**Комментарий:**

It is said in the scriptures that separation occurs to produce excellence of union.

*na vina vipralambhena sambhogah pustim asnute \|*  
*kasayite hi vastradau bhuyan evabhivardhate \|\|*

***Sambhoga*** **(union) is not strong without** ***vipralambha*** **(separation). It increases in strength just as cloth increases in beauty by repeatedly dying with red color. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **15.3)**

The Lord desires the experience of *srngara* with a mixture of *rasas*. He is eternally endowed with desire, knowledge, and ability to act. His independence is not destroyed since he is the eternal possessor of unlimited powers. He is the controller of *rasa* by his eternal desire.

*evam sasankamsu-virajita nisah*  
*sa satya-kamo ’nuratabala-ganah*

**With** ***gopis*** **firmly attached to him with** ***anuraga*****, Krsna, whose desires are always fulfilled, who had fixed enjoyment with them in his mind, served the nights lit by the rays of the moon. (SB 10.33.25)**

Thus by his eternal desire, the influence of time (such as obstructions of season, etc) is eradicated. The meaning of the verse is as follows.

**vasante vasanti-kusuma-sukumarair avayavair & radham sarasam idam uce sahacari**

One day in the spring season a *sakhi* (*sahacari*) with similar desires (conjugal desires) spoke to Radha the following words (song three) in order to invoke rasa (*sarasam*). Radha’s limbs were as tender as the flowers of a *madhavi* creeper. *Mardavam komalasyapi samsparsasahatocyate*: when one cannot tolerate even the softest touch, it is called *mardavam*. (*Ujjvala-nilamani* 10.38). \[Radha’s body is described as *uttama mardavam* in (*Ujjvala-nilamani* as follows.\]

*abhinava-nava-malikam ayam sa*  
*sayana-varam nisi radhikadhisisye \|*  
*na kusuma-patalam darapi jaglau*  
*tad-anubhavat tanur eva sa-vranasit \|\|39\|\|*

**\[Rupa-manjari speaks to Rati-manjari\] “Radha slept last night on a bed made of freshest jasmine flowers. Not a single flower was even slightly wilted. From this we understand how her delicate body became bruised”. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.39)**

The word *avayavaih* (with limbs) is a descriptive term for Radha using the instrumental case as in the phrase *jatabhis tapasah* (austere man with matted hair).

**bhramantim kantare bahu-vihita-krsnanusaranam amandam & valad-badham: She wandered in the vast forest (kantare). The reason is given: she searched many times (bahu-vihita) for Krsna since she experiences increased pain which is due to her being intensely (amandam) overcome with thoughts arising from the fever of love. Kantara means a huge forest and badha means pain.**

The verse describes the state of worry (*cinta*) endowed with desire (*abhilasa*). The characteristics of desire (*abhilasa*) are as follows:

*abhilasah sprha tatra kante sarvanga-sundare \|*  
*drste srute va tatrapi vismayananda-sadhvasah \|\|*

***Abhilasa*** **or desire means that on seeing or hearing about the lover who is beautiful in all limbs, she experiences doubt, bliss and panic. (*****Dasarupaka*** **4.53)**

Following is an example (state of worry endowed with desire):

*katham sa vallabhah prapyah kim kuryam tasya siddhaye \|*  
*katham bhaved asau vasya iti cinta bhaved yatha \|\|*

**The woman thinks, “Will I meet my dear lover? What should I do to attain him? What should I do to control him?” (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.8)**

The excellence of her desire to meet Krsna is shown by the words “wandering in the vast forest with her tender limbs (even though the things in the forest may hurt her delicate body)”

*citrotkirnad api visadharad bhiti-bhajo rajanyam*  
*kim va brumas tvad-abhisarane sahasam madhavasyah \|*  
*dhvante yantya yad atinibhrtam radhayatma-prakasa-*  
*trasat panih pathi phani-phanaratna-rodhi vyadhayi \|\|*

**О Madhava, how can we describe Radha’s great fear of serpent, \[even the one\] engraved on her medallion? Yet as she walked on the path to secretly meet you in the middle of the night, she covered a serpent’s hood with her hand, fearing that the effulgence of the jewel on its hood would light up the darkness \[and reveal her presence\]. (*****Padyavah*** **197)**

*dvitraih keli-saroruham tri-caturair dhammilla-malli-srajam*  
*kanthan mauktika-malikam tad anu ca tyaktva padaih pancamaih \|*  
*krsna-prema-vighurnitantarataya durabhisaratura*  
*tanvangi nirupayam adhvani param sreni-bharam nindati \|\|*

**О Krsna! Her heart agitated with love, Radha walks on the path to the far-away rendezvous. But Radha has become exhausted. \[Still, because of her desire, she forces herself to walk by doing the following.\] After taking two or three steps, she abandons her toy lotus flower. After three or four steps she abandons the garland of jasmine flowers decorating her hair. After five steps, she removes the pearlnecklace from her neck. After this she rebukes her hips for being so heavy, but she cannot abandon them. (*****Padyavali*** **212)**

Or, the phrase *amandam kandarpa-jvara-janita-cintakulataya* can modify *krsnanusaranam* instead of modifying *valad badham*: Radha searched for Krsna many times with thoughts arising from the fever of love.

**\|\| Song Three \|\|**

*madhavotsava-kamalakara*  
*vasanta- raga-yati-talabhyam giyate*

**The festival of Madhava in the form of a lotus. The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***vasanta*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati.***

Epigraph to the song: *Vasanta-raga* is described as follows:

*si khandi-barhoccaya-baddha-cudah*  
*pusnan pikam cuta-latankurena \|*  
*bhraman muda ramam ananga-murtir*  
*marto matangah sa vasanta-ragah \|\|*

***Vasanta-raga*** **has a peacock feather on the top of his head and feeds mango buds to a cuckoo. He’s the embodiment of Cupid; he wanders around in joy, enjoying like an elephant.**

*Yati-tala* is *laghu-drutau*: 4 + 4 + 2 + 2=12

### Text 1.28

*lalita-lavanga-lata-parisilana-komala-malaya-samire \| madhukara-nikara-karambita-kokila-kujita-kunja-kutire \|\|28\|\|*

*viharati harir iha sarasa-vasante*  
*nrtyati yuvatl-janena samam sakhi virahi-janasya durante \|\|dhru.\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*lalita* - Attractive; *lavanga-lata* - clove creepers; *parisilana* - embraces; *komala-malaya-samlre* - \[in the spring\] whose mild Malaya breeze; *madhukara-nikara* - \[with\] a host of bees; *karambita* - is mixed; *kokila-kujita* - with cooing cuckoos; *kunja-kutire* - \[and\] whose cottage in the bower;  
*viharati* - \[He\] searches; *harih* - Krsna; *iha* - here; *sarasa* - filled with *rasa* of love; *vasante* - in spring season; *nrtyati* - \[while they\] dance; *yuvati-jane* - young girls (*gopis*); *na* - \[is there\] no; *samam* - together; *sakhi* - О \[my\] friend; *virahi-janasya* - for the person in separation; *duranta* - \[is there no\] misery?

**Перевод:**

**“In the spring filled with the rasa of love, with the mild Malaya breeze embracing attractive clove creepers, replete with a cottage in a bower filled with cooing cuckoos and a host of humming bees.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together, О my friend, Krsna searches for you, but he is miserable because of separation from you. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi* speaks words (the verse) which are like a great medicine to Radha who is in pain.

“O my friend Radha! Here in Vrndavana, in the spring season which gives rise to *srngara-rasa* (*sarasa*), Krsna plays (*viharati*).” (first line of the chorus)

What else is happening?

\[Second line of the chorus answers \] He plays while young girls dance ‘gracefully’ (*samam*) or ‘together’ (*samam*). There is misery (*durante*) for the person in separation (*virahi janasya*), is there not (*na*)? This phrase gives the reason for Radha’s meeting with him. *Duranta* also means ‘without limit’. Or the phrase may be analyzed as *jane ‘ana-samam durante’* where *ana* means life: “His whole life has become miserable.”

Or the meaning of the chorus can be “Though Krsna dances in the spring at this place with groups of *gopis* intoxicated with his beauty, lured there by great *prema*, he does not play (*na viharati*) with them (*samam*), in the same way that he plays with you, though they desire to dance.” Others take the chorus to mean “Krsna plays with the young women (*yuvati-janena samam*) in the spring season in Vrndavana and dances with them.” This however is not suitable for Radha’s meeting with Krsna, because the *sakhis* statement would make Radha envious (and therefore she would not go to Krsna.)

Is this an expression of *purva-raga* (attraction prior to meeting)? That cannot be, since other descriptions indicate previous pastimes with Krsna. It will be said later *rase harim iha vihinita-vilasam smarati mano mama krta-parihasam*: I remember playful Krsna making jokes at the *rasa* dance (verse 2.2). Thus the explanation given above is correct.

Returning to the subject, the sakhi says, “He does not play with others in the same way he plays with you, since he is miserable in separation from you (*virahi-janasya durante*). The other *gopis* and the spring season cannot give Krsna great pleasure without your presence. He desires only you.” *Viharati* (wander about) also suggests that he engages in searching for Radha. To dispel any hesitation in meeting Krsna, the *sakhi* indicates that the other young women are ordinary, and she also hints - “Therefore if you do not go after him, you will experience the spring as misery.” *Samam* means “with” according to *Amara-kosa*. This is the \[explanation of\] chorus.

There are twelve qualities to the *sakhi* who acts as a messenger on orders (*nisrstharta-duti*).

*pravrtti-kusala daksa gudha-mantra drdha-priya \|*  
*svatantra vidhava dasi dhrsta pravrajita sakhi \|\|*  
*malakaradi-nari ca karya dutista-siddhaye \|\|*

**The messenger should be skilful in conduct, expert, secretive, faithful, independent, single, having service attitude, bold, renounced, friendly, and skilful in making garlands.**

Her characteristics are mentioned:

*kalocitodyamabhijna suvesa drdha-mitrata \|*  
*dhrsta ista susila ca daksa capalya-varjita \|*  
*aksudra capy anudvigna prasasya dvadasaiva tu \|\|*

**The messenger knows what to do at a particular time and circumstance, is well dressed, has strong friendship, is bold, affectionate, of good character, expert, steady, not vulgar, undisturbed, and praiseworthy.**

*vilasinos ca svantastham artham jnatva yateta ya \|*  
*anurupav imau ceti hy akalayyanukula-krt \|*  
*nsrstartheti sa jneya kautuhala-samanvita \|\|*

**The designated messenger should make efforts as an intermediary between the two lovers, understanding the goal. And considering the couple ideal for each other, the messenger should work favorably for their meeting, and should be most anxious for their union.**

Twenty-seven actions of the messenger are as follows:

1\.    To create enthusiasm

2\.    To show the goal

3\.    To speak about the qualities of the couple

4\.    To instill faith

5\.    To make known the love of one for the other

6\.    To remind the heroine to act coyly

7\.    To show heroism

8\.    Being friendly to many persons

9\.    To dress nicely

10\.    To tolerate grief

11\.    To speak sweet words

12\.    To give strong advice

13\.    To be friendly

14\.    To spontaneously follow

15\.    To be knowledgeable of methods

16\.    To know aspects of time

17\.    To consider according to place

18\.    To know people’s natures

19\.    To be skilful in all actions

20\.    To cover up faults in the heroine

21\.    To discern good qualities

22\.    To praise beauty

23\.    To avoid treacherous words

24\.    To speak mildly

25\.    To speak practically

26\.    To praise efforts

27\.    To know other philosophies

Exhibiting her qualities such as knowledge of time, enthusiasm, and showing the goal, the *sakhi* speaks of the *rasa* in spring season. In the spring, the soft breeze from Malaya Mountain embraces the attractive (*lalita*) clove creepers. The breeze here is described with these three qualities. The breeze acts as an *uddipana* (stimulus), with the clove creeper representing the heroine. The *sakhi* also  shows other *uddipanas*. What are the other qualities of spring? In  the spring, the small cottage in the bower is filled with the cooing  of cuckoos mixed with the humming of hoards of bees.

*Silana* means embracing according to *Visva*. *Samira* means wind according to *Amara-kosa*. *Madhukara* means a bee. *Nikara* means all. *Karambita* means mixed. *Kutira* means a cottage.

### Text 1.29

*unmada-madana-manoratha-pathika-vadhu-jana-janita-vilape \|*  
*ali-kula-sankula-kusuma-samuha-nirakula-bakula-kalape \|\|29\|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*unmada-madana-manoratha* - \[Is filled\] with desires \[enflamed\] by their intense passion; *pathika-vadhu-jana-janita* - arising from the wives of travelers; *vilape*- \[in spring\] whose lamentation; *ali-kula-sankula* - \[are\] thick with hoards of bees; *kusuma-samuha* - with heaps of flowers; n*irakula-bakula-kalape* - whose unlimited groves of bakula trees.

**Перевод:**

**“In that spring, there is the sound of lamentation arising from travelers’ wives, filled with desires enflamed by their intense passion, and unlimited groves of bakula trees are filled with flowers and spread with hoards of bees.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...**

**Комментарий:**

In that spring there is lamentation arising from wives of travelling husbands. The lamentation is permeated with desire which is filled with ‘intense passion’ or ‘madness and passion’ (*unmada-madana*).

As well all the unlimited groves of bakula trees (*nirakula bakula kalape*) are filled with abundant flowers and hoards of bees. Or *Nirakula bakula kalape* means the spring is ornamented (*kalape*) with unlimited bakula trees. Or it means the spring has quivers (*kalapa*) of unlimited bakula trees to shoot Cupid’s arrows. *Sankula* means spread according to *Visva*. *Bakula* is the *kesara* plant. *Kalapa* means a group.

### Text 1.30

*mrga-mada-saurahha-rabhasa-vasamvada-nava-dala-mala-tamale \|*  
*yuva-jana-hrdaya-vidarana-manasija-nakha-ruci-kimsuka-jale \|\|30\|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mrga-mada-saurabha* - Of fragrant musk; *rabhasa-vasamvada* -\[are\] submissive to the streams; *nava-dala-mala* - with rows of new leaves; *tamale* - \[in spring\] on *tamala* trees; *yuva-jana-hrdaya-vidarana* - that tear apart the hearts of young couples; *manasija* - of Manasija (the Cupid); *nakha-ruci* - glow like the \[scarlet\] nails; *kimsukajale* - whose groups of *palasa* buds

**Перевод:**

**“In that spring, the rows of new leaves on the tamala trees submit themselves to the streams of fragrant musk, and the palasa buds resemble the \[scarlet\] glow of Cupid’s nails tearing apart the hearts of young couples.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...**

**Комментарий:**

How is the spring further described? There are *tamala* trees with rows of new leaves which announce their submission to streams of fragrant musk. Furthermore, there are ‘groups’ or ‘buds’ (*jale*) of *palasa* flowers (which are scarlet) resembling Cupid’s shining nails of love, which tear the hearts of young women. This indicates the beauty of \[Krsna’s\] nails, which resemble those of Cupid’s which are covered with blood when he tears open the hearts of young people. The *palasa* buds are curved and red in color.

*Mrgamada* is musk according to *Amara-kosa*. *Rahhasa* means streams according to *Visva*. *Ruci* means light. *Kimsuka* is the *palasa* flower according to *Amara-kosa*. *Jala* means a group of flower buds according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.31

*madana-mahipati-kanaka-danda-ruci-kesara-kusuma-vikase \|*  
*milita-sili-mukha-patala-patala-krta-smara-tuna-vilase \|\|31 \|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madana-mahipati* - Of Lord Madana (the Cupid); *kanaka-danda-ruci* - glows like the golden umbrella; *kesara-kusuma-vikase* - \[in spring\] whos exhibition of *naga-kesara* flowers; *milita-sili-mukha* -covered with bees \[which resemble arrows\]; *patala-patala* - which is made from a cluster of *patala* flowers; *smara-tuna* - of the quiver of Smara (the Cupid); *vilase* - of whom \[there are\] activities.

**Перевод:**

**“The spring season blossoms with naga-kesara flowers resembling the gleam of lord Cupid’s golden umbrella. The spring is active with Cupid’s quiver made from clusters of patala flowers which are covered with bees resembling the arrows.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...**

**Комментарий:**

The spring season blossoms with *nagakesara* flowers and these flowers resemble the gleam of lord Cupid’s golden umbrella. Or they resemble the gleam of lord Cupid’s umbrella with its golden handle. The umbrella is inferred because of the inclusion of the word *mahipati* (king). A king is accompanied by an umbrella. Or *danda* means rod which is to be used to inflict pain on those who live in separation.

The spring is endowed with the activities of Cupid’s quiver, produced by clusters (*patala*) of *patala* flowers covered with bees which resemble arrows. The bees are compared to arrows (*silimukha*) since they pierce others and cause pain.

*Kesara* means the *nagakesara* according to *Amara-kosa*. *Silmukha* means a bee or an arrow. *Patala* means a group. *Tuna* is a quiver.

### Text 1.32

*vigalita-lajjita-jagad-avalokana-taruna-karuna-krta-hase \|*  
*virahi-nikrntana-kunta-mukhakrti-ketaka-danturitase \|\|32\|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vigalita-lajjita* - From which shame has disappeared; *jagad-avalokana* - at the sight of \[people of \] the world; *taruna-karuna-krta* - \[is\] made by the young karuna flowers; *hase* - \[in spring\] whose smiling; *virahi-nikrntana* - for piercing \[lovers\] in separation; *kunta-mukhakrti* - resembling the tips of spears; ketaka-danturitase - whose directions \[are\] spread with ketaka flowers.

**Перевод:**

**“In the spring, the young karuna flowers smile \[in amusement\] on seeing that people have given up their shame. The directions are filled with ketaka flowers resembling spear tips ready to pierce lovers in separation.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...**

**Комментарий:**

**Meaning of the first line:**

The spring smiles by having flowers of the young karuna trees break into blossom on seeing that living beings have given up their (*vigalita*) *shame* (because of their desires). By mentioning blossoming flowers, white color is hinted. White color represents *hasya rasa*.. Bharata Muni says *sveto hasyah prakirtitah*: the color of *hasya-rasa* is white. The young trees are mentioned since in Vrndavana all trees are eternally young since they are dear to the Lord.

Or, \[the meaning of the first line is\] in spring, young couples who are not separated (*taruna*), have smiles mixed with *karuna-rasa* (compassion) on seeing people of the world (*jagat*) who are separated and have given up (*vigalita*) shame. They have compassion because they lament, “O unfortunate lovers who are in separation! You undergo such difficulty!” They smile because they think “Those in separation have given up modesty and are lamenting.” The reason for mentioning *taruna* (young) is that those who are in their youth will smile and laugh, whereas the infants do not know *rasa* (and so they don’t laugh) and old people suppress laughter in front of strangers. Hence they are described as youthful (*taruna*). *Lajjita* (shame) is taken in the neuter gender to express a state.

Or, *vigalita* can also mean that the cuckoos (*vi*) stumble (*galita*) on the ground. They are therefore ashamed (*lajjita*).

*Jagat* can also mean travelers - entities who move around. \[Then the meaning of the first line is\] the travelers (separated from their lovers) are stumbling in shame on hearing the cuckoos’ warble, and upon seeing this, the youthful couples who are united, smile with compassion (*karuna*)

Spring is further described \[in the second line of the verse\]. The directions are spread (*danturita*) or made painful (*danturita*) with *ketaki* flowers (*keora*) resembling the tips of spears for piercing lovers in separation. Sometimes *ketaki-danturitase* \[instead of *ketaka-danturitase*\] is seen. (Both *ketaki-danturitase* and *ketaka-danturitase* mean the same thing)

*Ketaki* stands for *ketaki* (both *ketaki* and *ketaki* mean the same, but *ketaki* is the correct form). *Nikrntana* comes from the root *kr*: to injure. *Karuna* means compassion according to *Visva*. *Kunta* is a type of weapon according to *Amara-kosa*. *Dantura* means spread out according to *Dharani*. *Asa* means directions according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.33

*madhavika-parimala-lalite nava-malati-jati-sugandhau\|*  
*muni-manasam api mohana-karini taruna-karana-bandhau \|\|33\|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madhavika-parimala* - With the fragrance of madhavi creepers; *lalite* - it \[the spring\] is attractive; *nava-malati-jati* - with types of fresh jasmine; *sugandhau* - it is fragrant; *muni-manasam* - \[in\] the minds of sages; *api* - even; *mohana-karini* - it causes bewilderment; *taruna* - in young \[couples\]; *karana-bandhau* - \[and\] it causes the highest bliss.

**Перевод:**

**“The season is attractive with the fragrance of madhavi creepers and is fragrant with all types of fresh jasmine. It bewilders even the minds of sages and causes the highest bliss in young couples.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...**

**Комментарий:**

The season is attractive (*lalite*) with the fragrance of *madhavi* creepers, or it is attractive with the fragrance of a consort - Radha (*madhavika*); the fragrance is caused due to Radha being crushed by her lover. It is said:

*alingyaiva priyam yati kama-nirjita-cetasa \|*  
*sphurati pratyukti-racana madhavi nayiketi sa \|\|*

**The heroine madhavi, whose heart is conquered by love, goes and embraces her lover while giving some response.**

The word *madhava* (suggested by the word *madhavika*) means spring and the hero. Spring makes her hurry to meet Krsna. Spring acts as a strong *uddipana* (stimulant for invoking love). Spring is most fragrant with fresh jasmine flowers. Spring causes bewilderment even to the minds of sages, what to speak of others. Also it is suggested that spring bewilders even the minds of proud women (having *mana*). Spring causes spontaneous friendship or the highest bliss among young people. Instead of *nava-malati-jati* sometime *nava-malika-jati* is seen. *Nava-malika-jati* means fragrance of fresh flower garlands and jasmine. *Parimala* means fragrance arising from crushing, according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 1.34

*sphurad-atimukta-lata-parirambhana-mukulita-pulakita-cute \|*  
*vrndavana-vipine parisara-parigata-yamuna-jala-pute \|\|34\|\|*

*viharati harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sphurad-atimukta-lata* - Of the splendid atimukta (*vasanti*) creeper; *parirambhana* - because of the embrace; *mukulita-pulakita* - \[are\] budding and bristling; *cute* - \[in Spring\] whose mango trees; *vrndavana-vipine* - in the vrndavana forest; *parisara-parigata* -spread everywhere around the area; *yamuna-jala-pute* - purified by the waters of the Yamuna \[river\],

**Перевод:**

**“In the spring the hairs of budding mango trees are standing on end because of the embrace of the splendid vasanti creeper. The Vrndavana forests are surrounded completely by the purified waters of the Yamuna.**

**“\[In such season,\] while other gopis dance together...”**

**Комментарий:**

The mango trees have hairs standing on end because of the embrace of splendid or desirable vasanti creeper (*atimukta*). The *vasanti* creeper is trembling with *sattvika-bhavas* (involuntary external transformations) due to meeting her lover, and by the embrace of it, the mango trees’ hairs stand on end. Even plants develop symptoms of love.

The word *atimukta* also suggests that passion to embrace must develop even in the most detached person (*atimukta*) during spring. Or some detached persons, attracted to the intense sweetness of Krsna, took the form of creepers in Vrndavana to worship him. This suggests that persons in Vaikuntha (*atimukta*) desire to become creepers in Vrndavana because of the great sweetness. It is said:

*asam aho carana-renu-jusam aham syam*  
*vrndavane kim api gulma-latausadhinam*

**\[Uddhava sang\] “Oh, let me be fortunate enough to be one of the bushes, creepers or herbs in Vrndavana, because the** ***gopis*** **bless them with the dust of their lotus feet.” (SB 10.47.61)**

Up till this point, the descriptions of spring indicate rasa and misery. Now \[in the second half of this verse\] details of the place for pastimes in spring are described as follows. Krsna plays (*harir viharati* from the chorus) in the forest called Vrndavana (the forests of Vrnda). The forest is described as follows. It is purified by the water of the Yamuna which is spread everywhere (*parigata*) around the area (*parisara*). Or Vrndavana is surrounded everywhere by the pure water of the Yamuna. The pure Yamuna water becomes even purer at this place. How pure then Vrndavana must be! It is said:

*rasoyah paramadharah sac-cid-ananda-laksanah \|*  
*brahmety upanisad-gitah sa eva yamuna svayam \|\|*

**Krsna, who is Brahman praised in the Upanisads, who is** ***rasa*** **itself, and supreme basis, characterized by eternity, knowledge and bliss is personally the Yamuna. (Padma Purana)**

Thus it should be concluded that Vrndavana is desired by the residents of Vaikuntha since it is purified by the Yamuna, which is greatly praised. Vrndavana is especially purifying because it doesn’t just destroy sins, but also gives experience of the excellent *rasas* of Krsna. Vrndavana (*vrndam* + *avati*) means one who protects (*avail*) the *campaka* and other trees (*vrndam*) or the groups of *gopis* (*vrndam*), since the forest produces happiness in the Lord’s pastimes (here protecting means it produces happiness). *Atimukta* means a *madhavi* or *vasanti* creeper. *Parisara* means the land at the perimeter. The author then summarizes the meaning of the song by speaking the excellence of his composition \[in the next verse\].

### Text 1.35

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam idam udayati hari-carana-smrti-saram \|*  
*sarasa-vasanta-samaya-vana-varnanam anugata-madana-vikaram \|\|35\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam* - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; *idam* - this \[work\]; *udayati* - shines; *hari-carana* - of the feet of Krsna; *smrti-saram* -\[because\] its essence is the remembrance; *sarasa-vasanta-samaya* - in the spring season full of \[*srngara*\] *rasa*; *vana-varnanam* - its description is the forest; *anugata-madana* - consists of the love \[of Radha\]; *vikaram* - \[and\] its emotion.

**Перевод:**

**This work spoken by the poet Jayadeva is the best since it gives remembrance of the feet of Krsna. This work describes the forest in the spring season full of srngara-rasa and describes the strong emotions of Radha’s love. (END OF SONG THREE)**

**Комментарий:**

The author then summarizes the meaning of the song by speaking the excellence of his composition.

\[Meaning of the first line of the verse is as follows.\] This work spoken by the poet Jayadeva shines (*udayati*) as the best object (*saram*) because it gives remembrance of Krsna’s feet. Or \[this work shines because\] it has the essence (*saram*) of remembrance of Krsna’s feet.

How else can the work be described? It contains descriptions of the forest in the spring season that has a predominance of Srngara-rasa (*sarasam*), containing strong emotions of love. These descriptions are according to the words of a *sakhi* speaking to Radha which I (Jayadeva) have recorded. Or, *anugata madana vikaram* means the work produces emotions related to love in the heart of the hearer. The phrase *anugata-madana-vikaram* can also modify *sarasa-vasanta-samaya-vana-varnanam*: the work contains descriptions of the forest in spring full of rasa and those descriptions cause emotions of love.

### Text 1.36

*dara-vidalita-malli-valli-cancat-paraga-*  
*prakatita-pata-vasair vasayan kananani \|*  
*iha hi dahati cetah ketaki-gandha-bandhuh*  
*prasarad-asama-bana-pranavad gandhavahah \|\|36\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dara-vidalita* - Slightly opened; *malli-valli* - from jasmine creepers; *cancat* - emanating; *pardga* - as pollen; *prakatita* - manifested; *pata-vdsaih* - with perfumed dust; *vasayan* - perfuming; *kdnanani* - the forests; *iha* - now (in the spring); *hi* - indeed; *dahati* - \[it\] burns; *cetah* - the hearts \[of separated lovers\]; *ketaki-gandha* - of the *ketaki* fragrance; *bandhuh* - the friend; *prasarad* - which spread \[everywhere\] (or developing desire); *asama-bana* - of he who has an odd number of (i.e. five) arrows (i.e. the Cupid); *pranavat* - like the life-breath; *gandhavahah* - bearer of fragrance (the breeze)

**Перевод:**

**In the spring, the breeze, the friend of the ketaki fragrance, scenting the forests with pollen emanating from slightly blossoming jasmine creepers, burns the hearts of separated lovers and gives life to the five arrows of Cupid which are spreading everywhere.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse speaks of the spring breeze causing pain for the lover in separation. In the spring (*iha*) the wind (*gandha-vahah*) burns the heart of separated lovers. It perfumes (*vasayan*) the forests with fragrant dust (*pata-vdsaih*) in the form of pollen emanating from clusters of slightly-opened jasmine creepers. The breeze is the friend of the *ketaki* flowers’ fragrance.

\[Meaning of the last line of the verse is as follows.\] The breeze is like the life air (*pranavat*) of five arrows (*asama-bana*) of Cupid which are spreading in all directions. Or, the fragrant breeze is endowed with power (*pranavat*) because of the five arrows which are spreading everywhere. Or the hearts of separated lovers (*cetah*), as if brought to life (*pranavat*) by Cupid (*asama-bana*), are developing desire (*prasarat*).

*Dara* means ‘slightly’ according to *Visva*. *Paraga* is pollen. *Pata-vasaka* is fragrant powder according to *Amara-kosa*. *Gandha-vaha* means the wind.

### Text 1.37

*adryotsanga-vasad-bhujanga-kavala-klesad ivesacalam*  
*praleya-plavanecchayanusarati srikhanda-sailanilah \|*  
*kim ca snigdha-rasala-mauli-mukulany alokya harsodayad*  
*unmilanti kuhuh kuhur iti kalottalah pikanam girah \|\|37\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*adryotsanga* - On the lap of mountain; *vasat* - dwelling; *bhujanga* - of the snakes; *kavala* - caused by the biting; *klesad* - because of the pain; *iva* - as if; *isacalam* - Mountain of Siva (the Himalayas) *praleya* - into the snow; *plavanecchaya* - out of the desire to submerge; *anusarati* - pursues; *srikhanda-sailanilah* - the breeze from Srikhanda-saila (Malaya mountain); *kim ca* - also; *snigdha-rasala* - of the charming mango trees; *mauli-mukulani* - the tips of the buds; *alokya* - having seen; *harsodayad* - with joy; *unmilanti* - becomes manifest; *kuhiih-kuhuh-iti -* “coo coo, coo coo” (the warbling of the cuckoos); *kalottalah* - sweet and excellent; *pikanam* - of the cuckoos; *girah* - voices.

**Перевод:**

**The breeze from the Malaya mountain blows towards the Himalayas with a desire to submerge itself in snow because of the pain inflicted by the touch of the mouths of snakes living in the mountain. The excellent, sweet, soft warbling of cuckoos arising out of joy on seeing the tips of buds on charming mango trees also manifests at this time.**

**Комментарий:**

The wind from Malaya mountain (*srikhanda-saila*) pursues the Himalayas, the mountain of Siva, because of pain arising from the biting of snakes residing in the lap of Malaya mountain. Why does the breeze move towards the Himalaya? It desires to become submerged in snow. This indicates that the pain of separated lovers increases from contact with the poison in the wind. \[The wind has poison in it, because\] it has been devoured and then vomited by snakes. Actually, the south wind is cool and pleasant but because of the intolerance of separated lovers, that wind is criticized as being swallowed by poisonous snakes and needing snow to cool it.

Not just the wind is intolerable, but something else is also intolerable in spring - the warbling of cuckoos. It is indistinct, sweet (*kala*) and most excellent (*uttalah*). It becomes manifest (*unmilanti*) because of the appearance of bliss \[in cuckoos\] on seeing the tips of buds on the charming mango trees. Instead of adryotsange sometimes *adyotsanga* (lap of spring) is seen. *Praleya* means snow according to *Amara-kosa*. *Uttala* means best according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.38

*unmilan-madhu-gandha-lubdha-madhupa-vyadhuta-cutankura-*  
*kridat-kokila-kakali-kalakalair udgirna-karna-jvarah \|*  
*niyante pathikaih katham katham api dhyanavadhana-ksana*  
*prapta-prana-sama-samagama-rasollasair ami vasarah \|\|38\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*unmilan-madhu* - Of oozing sap; *gandha* - for the fragrance; *lubdha* - greedy; *madhupa* - by the bees; *vyadhuta* - shaken; *cutankura* -in the buds of the mango trees; *kridat* - playing; *kokila* - of the cuckoos; *kakali-kala-kalaih* - because of the uproars \[caused\] by the sweet voices; *udgirna* - \[is\] caused; *karna-jvarah* - in which pain for the ears; *niyante* - are attained; *pathikaih* - by travelers (who are separated from their lover); *katham katham*- somehow; api - even; *dhyanavadhana* - of intense thinking \[on his wife\]; *ksana* - at that moment; *prapta* - to be obtained; *prana-sama* - equal to his life; *samagama* - for a meeting; *rasollasaih* - in whom desire springs up; *ami-vasarah* - those days \[of spring\]

**Перевод:**

**For the separated lover, the spring days produce pain for the ears on hearing the soft cooing of cuckoos as they play in the mango buds shaken by bees greedy for the fragrance and oozing sap, because at that time the lover develops eagerness for a pleasurable meeting - his very life, when he begins intensely thinking of his beloved at that moment.**

**Комментарий:**

The duration of the days of spring is also without end. The days (*vasarah*) of spring (*ami*) result in great pain for separated lovers (*pathikaih*). The days manifest pain for their ears because there is great disturbance caused by the cooing (*kakali*) of cuckoos frolicking in the mango buds shaken by bees greedy for their fragrance and oozing honey. It is said that in the spring the cuckoos sing the fifth note. This is an uddipana for love. \[However\] the sounds create pain because the separated lovers develop eagerness for the *rasa* of meeting - their life and soul (but they cannot meet their beloved because they’re far away from their beloved). This eagerness to meet is attained at that moment of hearing the cuckoo by intense meditation on their beloved. It is hinted that Krsna spends his days during the spring meditating on his beloved. *Kakali* means a soft, sweet, indistinct sound. *Kalakalaih* means a great noise.

### Text 1.39

*aneka-nan-parirambha-sambhrama-*  
*sphuran-manohari-vilasa-lalasam \|*  
*murarim arad upadarsayanty asau*  
*sakhi samaksam punar aha radhikam \|\|39\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aneka-nari* - Of many women; *parirambha* - for the embrace; *sambhrama* - by \[his\] eagerness; *sphuran* - revealing (or demonstrated or manifested); *manohari* - of the heart stealer; *vilasa* - because of the arousal of love (or coquettish gestures or actions); *lalasam* - longing (or praying or love); *murarim* - the enemy of demon Mura (Krsna); *arat* - at a distance; *upadarsayanti* - while pointing out; *asau-sakhi* - that *sakhi*; *samaksam* - before the very eyes; *punar aha* - again spoke; *radhikam* - to Radha

**Перевод:**

**The sakhi again spoke to Radha while pointing out Krsna at a distance, who was longing for her with arousal of love, manifesting in his zealous embrace of many women.**

**Комментарий:**

The *uddipanas* have been shown \[in previous verses\]. Now what the sakhi perceives is described. The *sakhi* (*asau*) speaks to Radha again. What is the sakhi doing? She points out Krsna who can be seen in the distance (*drat*).

\[How is he described? He’s described in first two lines of the verse as follows.\] He has attractive longing for Radha. His longing is arising from arousal of love (*vilasa*) \[within him\] and this arousal is revealed (*sphuran*) by his zealous embraces of many women. Or he has longing for the attractive coquettish gestures (*vilasa*) of Radha which are expressed (*sphuran*) by her zealous embraces of many women. Or he prays (*lalasam*) to the *sakhis* on remembering (*sphuran*) Radha’s actions (*vilasa*) in which she excitedly glanced at her *sakhis* and embraced them. Or the *sakhi* demonstrates (*sphuran*) Krsna’s symptoms of love (*lalasam*) with attractive actions (*vilasa*) and these actions manifested (*sphuran*) as zealous embracing of many women. The great qualities in Krsna are shown by describing the *gopis'* emotions on meeting him. It will be said later *pina-payodhara*: they had swollen breasts. (verse 1.41)

*Lalasa* means longing according to *Visva*. *Arat* means “in the distance” or “close” according to *Amara-kosa*. By using the word *sambhrama*, it is shown that he was more eager to embrace than the *gopis* were. He was seen at a distance or close up (*drat*). The *sakhi* describes everything in the next song.

**\|\| Song Four \|\|**

*ramakari-raga-yati-talabhyam giyate \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***ramakari*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The characteristics of the *ramakari-raga* are as follows:

*svarna-prabha bhasvara-bhusana ca*  
*nilam nicolam vahanti \|*  
*kante padopantam adhisrite’pi*  
*manonnata rama-kari pradista \|\|*

***Rama-kari*** **is a very proud woman, with golden complexion, shining ornaments, wearing blue cloth on her beautiful body, while her lover falls at her feet.**

*Yati-tala* was described previously.

### Text 1.40

*candana-carcita-nila-kalevara-pita-vasana-vana-mali \|*  
*keli-calan-mani-kundala-mandita-ganda-yuga-smita-sali \|\|40\|\|*

*harir iha.mugdha-vadhu-nikare vilasini vilasati keli-pare \|\|dhru.\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*candana-carcita* - Smeared with sandal paste; *nila-kalevara* - \[and\] with a bluish body; *pita-vasana* - with yellow clothing; *vana-mali* - \[he is\] wearing a forest-garland; *keli-calat* - moving playfully; *mani-kundala* - with jewel earrings; *mandita* - is decorated; *ganda-yuga* - \[and\] the pair of \[his\] cheeks; *smita-sali* - \[he is\] full of smiles; *harih* - Krsna; *iha* - there \[in Vrndavana\]; *mugdha-vadhu* - of young \[unskillful (or beautiful)\] women (*gopis*); *nikare* - in the assembly of; *vilasini* - О mistress (Radha)! *vildaati* - plays; *keli-pare* - О \[Radha\] bent on coquetry!

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi speaks to Radha\] “Krsna wears a forest garland and a yellow cloth. His blue body is smeared with sandalwood. He smiles and his two cheeks are decorated with the playful movement of jewel earrings.**

**“O Radha, fond of coquetry! In Vrndavana, Krsna plays among the assembly of unskillful gopis who are absorbed in the play, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The meaning \[of the chorus\] is this. О Radha, fond of coquetry! In Vrndavana (iha) Krsna plays among an assembly of beautiful (*mugdha*) women, who are absorbed in the play (*keli-pare*). The word *mugdha* also indicates that these women do not know the art of gestures in love (*mugdha* means ignorant as well). They are absorbed in the playing but it is an ordinary play. The word *vilasini* addressed to Radha indicates that she is knowledgeable of dalliance. He, who knows all arts, is waiting for you. *Mugdha* means beautiful or ignorant according to *Visva*. *Vilasa* means excellent dalliance. The characteristics of *vilasa* are as follows:

*yana-sthanasanadinam netra-vaktradi-karmanam \|*  
*utpadyate viseso yah sa vilasah prakirtitah \|\|*

***Vilasa*** **is known as a manifestation of special actions such as making the eyes crooked, preparing a special carriage, preparing a place and making a seat. (*****Natya-sastra*****)**

Or *keli-pare* can also be an address to Radha like vilasini. О Radha who has the most excellent (*pare*) pastimes (*keli*)! *Mugdha-vadhva* can also indicate the type of heroine called *mugdha*. *Harih* means “he who attracts all women by the skill of his pastimes.” He, who is skillful in all arts, desires only you since you alone can complete his desired pastimes since the others are devoid of proper dalliance.

The qualities of the *mugdha* heroine are described in (*Srngara-tilaka*) 1.35:

*mugdha nava-vadhus tatra nava-yauvana-bhusita \|*  
*navananga-rahasyapi lajja-praya-ratir yatha \|\|*

**The** ***mugdha*** **heroine is young, newly ornamented with youth and thus her love is filled with shyness even if the love is secret.**

*krtaparadhe dayite baspa-ruddhavalokana*  
*priyapriyoktau casakta mane ca vimukhi sada*

**The mugdha woman weeps after her lover commits offense, does not reply to the lover with unkind words, and avoids pride. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **5.14)**

*Nava-yauvana* is described:

*sac-capala-netrantam smara-smera-mukhambujam \|*  
*sa-garva-jarajogandam asamagrarunadharam \|\|*

*lavanyodbheda-ramyangam vilasad-bhava-saurabham \|*  
*unmilitankura-kucam asphutangaka-sandhikam \|\|*

*prathamam yauvanam tatra vartamana mrgeksana \|*  
*apeksate mrdu-sparsam sahate noddhatam ratim \|\|*

*sakhi-keli-rata svanga-samskara-kalitadara \|*

*na kopa-harsau bhajate sapatni-darsanadisu \|*  
*natirajyati kantasya sangame kim tu lajjate \|\|*

**She has slightly restless eyes and a lotus face with a smile of love. Her cheeks are colored with pride and her lips are unevenly red. Her attractive limbs sprout with beauty. She has the fragrance of shining** ***bhavas*****. Her breasts are becoming full. Her joints are not clear. She has eyes like a doe. This is the** ***nava yauvana*****. She expects a light touch from the lover and cannot tolerate bold love. She is absorbed in playing with her friends. She carefully keeps her body clean. She does not show anger or joy on seeing cowives. She is not too conspicuous but is shy in uniting with her lover. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **1.263-266)**

In the verse the qualities of a hero who is *daksina* (compliant) are mentioned.

*yo gauravam bhayam prema daksinyam purva-yositi \|*  
*na muncaty anya-citto’pi jneyo’sau khalu daksinah \|\|*

**The** ***daksina*** **(compliant lover) is the person who, though having a new lover, cannot free himself from respect, fear, and affection for his previous lover, caused by softness of his nature. (*****Ujjvala-nllamani*** **1.32)**

Some say that the *daksina* hero is one who shows equal affection to many women (*Ujjvala-nilamani* 1.34), but that does not apply in the verse since Krsna is seen to have excessive attachment to Radha. *Sarva-gopisu saivaika visnor atyanta-vallabha*: Radha alone is the dearest among all the *gopis* (*Padma Purana*). *Bhagavatam* also says *anayaradhito пuпат bhagavan harir isvarah*: certainly this particular *gopi* has perfectly controlled the all-powerful Lord who takes away distress (SB 10.30.28).

### Text 1.41

*pina-payodhara-bhara-bharena harim parirabhya saragam \|*  
*gopa-vadhur anugayali kacid udancita-pancama-ragam \|\|41\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pina-payodhara* - Of \[her\] full breasts; *bhara-bharena* - with the heavy weight; *harim* - Krsna; *parirabhya* - having embraced; *saragam* - enamored \[of Radha\]; *gopa-vadhuh* - a cowherd damsel (*gopi*); *anugayati* - joins \[him\] in \[his\] singing; *kacid* - someone; *udancita* - lifted up; *pancama-raga* - endowed with the fifth note (‘*pa*’)

**Перевод:**

**“One gopi, embracing Krsna with her full, heavy breasts, sang following after his singing. He sang the fifth note (‘pa’ svara most prominently while he was attracted to Radha.**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...**

**Комментарий:**

(Beginning of the commentary on this verse is missing.)

*capalavihata dhairyam cid-vrttir avikatthana \|*  
*priyanuharanam lila madhuranga-vicestitaih \|\|*

**The woman shows fickleness, patience, hiding of emotions, does not boast, imitates the beloved, and makes gestures with her graceful limbs. (Dasarupaka 2.38)**

Since she followed after Krsna’s singing, it is implied that the knowledge of her singing was less than that of Radha. *Rasa* arises when the knowledge \[of the couple\] is equal. For instance it is said:

*kacit samam mukundena svara-jatir amisritah*  
*unninye pujita tena priyata sadhu sadhv iti*  
*tad eva dhruvam unninye tasyai manam ca bahv adat*

**One** ***gopi*****, joining Mukunda in his singing, sang pure melodious tones that rose harmoniously above his voice. Krsna was pleased and showed great appreciation for her performance, saying “Excellent! Excellent!” When another** ***gopi*** **sang in the** ***dhruva*** **style, Krsna praised her even more. (SB 10.33.9)**

The phrase *sa-ragam* describes Krsna. Krsna is endowed with color (*raga*) which has fallen on him when he enjoyed with Radha, or it means he has great attraction for Radha. The reason is given. He sings the fifth note predominantly (*udancita-pancama-ragam*). It is said *madhyam pancama-bhuyistam hasya-srnnaragyor bhavet*: in *hasya* and *srngara rasas* the fifth note in the middle range predominates (*Natya-sastra*). Attachment in *srngara* increases by predominance of the fifth note in singing. It is said:

*nisada-rsabha-gandhara-sadja-madhyama-dhaivatah \|*  
*pancamas cety ami sapta tantri-kanthotthitah svarah \|\|*

**The seven notes in singing and instrumental music are** ***sa*** **(*****sadja*****),** ***re*** **(*****rsabha*****),** ***ga*** **(*****gandhara*****),** ***ma*** **(*****madhyama*****),** ***pa*** **(*****pancama*****),** ***dha*** **(*****dhaivata*****) and** ***ni*** **(*****nisada*****).**

*nisadam hesate vaji gavas tv rsabha-bhasinah \|*  
*ajavikam tu gandharam dhaivatam brmhate gajah \|\|*

*sadjam тауurа bruvate mrga madhyama-bhasinah \|*  
*puspa-sadharane kale kokila rauti paticamam \|\|*

***Ni*** **resembles the horse’s neigh.** ***Re*** **resembles the cow’s lowing.** ***Ga*** **resembles the goat’s bleating.** ***Dha*** **resembles the elephant’s roar.** ***Sa*** **resembles the peacock’s cry.** ***Ma*** **resembles the deer’s cry.** ***Pa*** **resembles the cry of the cuckoo when flowers blossom.**

It is said elsewhere:

*sadjam vaden тауurа rsabham catako vadet \|*  
*aja vadati gandharam kraunco vadati madhyamam \|*  
*puspa-sadharane kale kokilah pancamam vadet \|*  
*dardura dhaivatam caiva nisadam ca vaded gajah \|\|*

***Sa*** **is the cry of the peacock.** ***Re*** **is the cry of the cataka bird.** ***Ga*** **is the bleating of the goat.** ***Ma*** **is the cry of the osprey.** ***Pa*** **is the song of the cuckoo at the time of flowers blooming.** ***Da*** **is the croaking of the frog and** ***Ni*** **is the roar of the elephant.**

*hasya-srngarayor jneyau svarau pancama-madhyamau \|*  
*sadja-rsabhau tatha jneyau vire raudradhhute rase \|\|*

*gandharas ca nisadhas ca kartavyau karune rase \|*  
*dhaivatas caiva kartavyo bibhatse ca bhayanake \|\|*

*bhairavah pancamo megha-mallaro gaudo malavah \|*  
*desakhyas ceti sad ragah procyante loka-visrutah \|\|*

*sapta-svaras trayo grama murchanas caikavimsatih \|*  
*srutinam dvavimsatis caiva tanah pancasad-unakah \|\|*

***Pa*** **and** ***ma*** **indicate** ***hasya*** **and** ***srngar*****a.** ***Sa*** **and** ***re*** **indicate** ***vira*****,** ***raudra*** **and** ***adbhuta rasa*****.** ***Ga*** **and** ***Ni*** **indicate** ***karuna-rasa.*** ***Dha*** **indicates** ***bibhatsa*** **and** ***bhayanaka rasa.*** **The six main** ***ragas*** **are** ***bhairava*****,** ***pancama*****,** ***megha-mallara*****,** ***gauda*****,** ***malava*** **and** ***desa*****. There are seven notes, three gramas and twenty-one murchanas. There are twenty-two** ***srutis*** **and forty-nine** ***tanas*****.**

It is clearly related that this *gopi* was bewildered by his *raga* and became charmed as she sang imitatively while embracing Krsna, though he sang to indicate Radha and that he wanted to enjoy with her. The woman became bewildered by his singing. It is said:

*gopyas tad-gitam akarnya murcchita navidan nrpa*  
*sramsad-dukulam atmanam srasta-kesa-srajam tatah*

**The** ***gopis*** **became stunned when they heard that song. О King, they did not notice that their fine garments were becoming loose and their hair and garlands disheveled. (SB 10.34.24)**

Sruti says:

*tad-rohi-janma te deva abruvan apa yusmad akramin nasman upavartate vi hvayamaha iti brahma-gandharva avadann agayan devah sa devan gayata upavartata \| tasmad gayantam striyah kamayate kamuka etam striyo bhavanti*

**She adopted the form of a deer and ran away from the Gandharvas. The devatas said, “She has run from you; she comes not to us; let us both summon her.” The Gandharvas uttered a spell \[to make her come to them\]. The gods sang \[to attract her toward them\]. She went to the gods as they sang. Therefore, women love one who sings; enamored are women of him who thus knows. (*****Taittiriya-samhita*** **6.1.6.6.4,** ***Yajur-veda*****)**

The singing of that *gopi* in front of Radha, shows that *gopi’*s disrespect \[toward Radha\], *Раncamа* refers to a type of *raga* according to *Visva*.

### Text 1.42

*kapi vilasa-vilola-vilocana-khelana-janita-manojam \| dhyayati mugdha-vadhur adhikam madhusudana-vadana-sarojam \|\|42\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kapi* — A certain; *vilasa* — of pleasure; *vilola-vilocana* — of \[his\] flickering glances; *khelana* - by the play; *janita-manojam* - by which desire is produced; *dhyayati* - contemplates; *mugdha-vadhuh* - the inexperienced damsel (*gopi*); *adhikam* - excessively; *madhusudana* — of Madhusudana (Krsna); *vadana-sarojam* - the lotus face.

**Перевод:**

**“One inexperienced gopi contemplates Krsna’s lotus face which produces desire in her by the play of his flickering glances.**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...**

**Комментарий:**

One innocent, infatuated *gopi* thinks of the lotus face of Krsna excessively (*adhikam*). Krsna’s face produced desire in her, which has arisen by the play of his flickering eyes. *Adhikam* can also modify *vadana-sarojam* (lotus face). His lotus face is incomparable in beauty. His face is compared to a lotus because the lotus has qualities like fragrance. The lotus stimulates desire and attracts a person by the flow of sweet *rasa.* Krsna’s face is similar. The word Madhusudana suggests Krsna’s unsteady love (like a bee) for that particular *gopi*. This *gopi* is called inexperienced (*mugdha*). She is not expert since she meditates even in the actual presence of Krsna.

### Text 1.43

*kapi kapola-tale milita lapitum kim api sruti-mule \|*  
*caru cucumba nitambavati dayitam pulakair anukule \|\|43\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kapi* - A certain (*gopi*); *kapola-tale* - at the base of his forehead; *milita* - having attached herself; *lapitum* -in order to whisper; *kim* - something; *api* - even; *sruti-mule* - to the base of \[his\] ear; *caru* -attractively (or joyfully); *cucumba* - \[she\] kissed \[him\]; *nitambavati* - with fine hips; *dayitam* - the beloved; *pulakaih* - with hairs stood on end; *anukule* - well disposed (indicating his desire for her).

**Перевод:**

**“One gopi with fine hips whispered in his ear and when his hairs stood on end indicating his desire, she kissed him on the forehead.**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...**

**Комментарий:**

One woman with fine hips, kissed her lover attractively at the base of his forehead. How is she described? She attached herself below his ear to whisper about some activity. He indicated his desire for her (*anukule*') by his hairs standing on end. Boldly kissing Krsna caused laughter among her friends. Using trickery, she showed her skill and began to kiss him. It is suggested however that she became unsteady by that first kiss. Her charming hips indicate beauty.

Or, some beautiful *gopi*, most skillful, your *sakhi*, attached herself to the base of his ear to inform him of how to meet you. Then what did she do? She kissed him nicely on the forehead when he favorably developed goose bumps. She joyfully (*caru*) kissed him since he developed *sattvika-bhava* out of love for you. Since he reacts in this way on hearing news of you, it’s fit to be said that he desires to meet you. Thus you must meet him.

### Text 1.44

*keli-kala-kutukena ca kacid amum yamuna-jala-kule \|*  
*manjula-vanjula-kunja-gatam vicakarsa karena dukule \|\|44\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*keli-kala* - For pastimes; *kutukena* - out of desire; *ca* - and; *kacid* — a certain (*gopi*); *amum* - him (Krsna); *yamuna-jala-kule* - on the bank of the Yamuna \[River\]; *manjula-vanjula* - of attractive reeds; *kunja-gatam* - \[though he had\] gone to a grove; *vicakarsa* - \[she\] pulled \[him\]; *karena* - by his hand; *dukule* - to a fine piece of cloth.

**Перевод:**

**“One gopi eager for pastimes pulled Krsna by the hand to a fine piece of cloth, on the bank of the Yamuna, though he had hidden in an attractive grove of reeds.**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...**

**Комментарий:**

One *gopi* pulled Krsna (*amum*) to a cloth (perhaps a piece of cloth for sitting), with playful gestures, though he had entered an attractive grove of reeds. Some say the verse means that using her hand she pulled him by his cloth.

\[What did he do?\] Following your *sakhis'* instruction he had gone there to fool the other *gopis*. To which place she pulled? She pulled him to the bank of Yamuna. She pulled him to the bank to play with him though he had hidden himself in the grove of reeds, hoping that you would come. It is suggested that he was unsettled by the *gopi* pulling his cloth, since he was attracted to another (Radha).

Or she pulled his cloth with her hand when he had gone to the reed grove on the bank of the Yamuna after he fooled the other *gopis*, since he was most eager for pastimes with you (*keli-kala-kutukena*). *Kutuka* means desire according to *Amara-kosa*. *Manjula* means attractive. *Vanjula* means reeds. *Dukula* means fine cloth.

### Text 1.45

*kara-tala-tala-tarala-valayavali-kalita-kala-svana-varhse \|*  
*rasa-rase saha-nrtya-para harina yuvati prasasamse \|\|45\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kara-tala-tala* - \[Accompanied by\] the clapping of the surface of the hands; *tarala-valayavali* - the jingling series of bracelets; *kalita-kala-svana* - by which a soft sound is made; *vamse* - \[and\] the flute; *rasa-rase* - with a taste for the *rasa* dance (or in great astonishment); *saha-nrtya-para* - equal \[to Krsna\] in dancing; *harina* - by Krsna; *yuvati* - a young woman (Radha); *prasasamse* - was praised

**Перевод:**

**“One young gopi, equal to Krsna in dancing with a taste for the rasa dance, is praised by him. \[That dance is\] accompanied by hand clapping, jingling of bells and soft, sweet notes of the flute.**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...**

**Комментарий:**

“How can Krsna be attracted to me if he is playing with other *gopis*? Are you not speaking uselessly?”

The *sakhi* then says, “Listen first hand.”

\[Her explanation is as follows.\] One young woman called Radha is praised by Krsna. How is she described? She dances equally with Krsna with taste for the *rasa* dance (*rasa-rase*) where there are many dancers. Or she dances equally with Krsna in great astonishment (*rasa-rase*) because of the skill in singing. That dance is tasty with the sound of the flute along with sweet indistinct notes mixed with moving rows of bells and clapping of hands.

The intention is this. With attraction for the *rasa* dance, with clapping of hands, jingling of bells and the soft, sweet notes of the flute, Radha, dancing equally to Krsna, is praised by him.

\[What type of attraction to the *rasa* dance?\] At this moment, he praises you by some hidden hints in playing the flute mixed with hand clapping. The other *gopis* are not aware of his message to you. They are inexperienced and ignorant.

*Tala* means clapping the hands according to *Visva*. *Tarala* means fickle. *Vamsa* is a flute. *Rasa* mean playing of the cowherd women. *Rasa-rasa* means the *rasa* dance.

### Text 1.46

*slisyati kam api cumbati kam api kam api ramayati ramam \|*  
*pasyati sa smita-caru param aparam anugacchati vamam \|\|46\|\|*

*harir iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*slisyati* - \[He\] embraces; *kam api* - a certain (*gopi*); *cumbati* - \[he\] kisses; *kam api* - another; *kam api* - and another; *ramayati* - \[he\] plays with; *ramam* - beautiful woman (or a woman who is inclined to play); *pasyati* - \[he\] gazes at; *sa* - he; *smita-caru* - with a pleasant smile; *param* - a final (last *gopi*); *aparam* - and \[yet\] another \[*gopi*\]; *anugacchati* - \[he\] becomes submissive \[to yet another\]; *vamam* -lovely \[or devious\] woman \[*gopi*\].

**Перевод:**

**“Krsna embraces one gopi, kisses another, plays with another and gazes at a last gopi with a pleasant smile. He becomes submissive to yet another gopi who is obstinate.”**

**“In Vrndavana, Krsna plays...”**

**Комментарий:**

Krsna embraces one woman who is inclined to play (*ramam*). He kisses another and plays with another using sweet words. He sees a last *gopi* (*param*) while smiling.

*aparam anugacchati vamam* - He becomes submissive (*anugacchati*) to your *sakhi* - another *gopi* (*aparam*) with attractive nature (*vamam*) or ‘crooked nature’ (*vamam*), who leaves in anger on seeing Krsna embrace the others who approached him submissively. Or, \[the meaning is\] he becomes submissive (*anugacchati*) to you (in this case, Radha is the other *gopi*) who are obstinate in nature (*vamam*) after cheating the others by smiling and glancing at them. Therefore do not delay in meeting with him. *Rama* means a beautiful woman according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 1.47

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam idam adbhuta-kesava-keli-rahasyam \|*  
*vipina-vinoda-kala-valitam vitanotu subhani yasasyam \|\|47\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam* - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; *idam* - this \[song\]; *adbhuta kesava* - of astonishing Kesava (Krsna); *keli-rahasyam* -the expertise in pastimes; *vipina* - in the forest; *vinoda-kala* - with \[his\] skillful amusements; *valitam* - furnished; *vitanotu* - may it \[the song\] spread; *subhani* - auspiciousness; *yasasyam* - conferring fame

**Перевод:**

**May this ultimate song spoken by Jayadeva, filled with the expert pastimes of astonishing Krsna and describing his skillful play in the forest, and which gives fame, spread auspiciousness. (END OF SONG FOUR)**

**Комментарий:**

May this ultimate song (*idam*) spoken by the poet Jayadeva spread auspiciousness. It is filled with the most expert pastimes of astonishing Krsna. He is astonishing because he attracts many attractive *gopis*, though he is attracted by the *rasa* of Radha. The word Kesava indicates Krsna’s beauty. Also the song is endowed with skillfulness of pastimes in the forest. It gives fame. Another version has *vrndavana-vipine lalitam* (instead of *vipina-vinoda-kala-valitam*): this song is beautiful (*lalitam*) or filled with *srngara-rasa* (*lalitam*) and contains the astonishing skillful pastimes of Krsna (*adbhuta-kesava-keli-rahasyam*) in Vrndavana.

In this poem, the heroine goes to meet her lover (*abhisdrika*). The hero is bold, has many lovers (*daksina*), is favorable (*anukula*) and is cunning. His boldness is shown when it is stated that he plays (enjoys) with one *gopi* (*kam api ramayati*).

*abhivyaktanya-taruni-bhoga-laksmapi nirbhayah \|*  
*mithya-vacana-daksas ca dhrsto’yam khalu kathyate \|\|*

**The lover who is fearless though bearing the marks of enjoyment with other women on his body and who is skillful at lying is called** ***dhrsta*****. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **1.40)**

The *daksina* hero was explained in the commentary on GG 1.40. Cunning is described as follows:

*priyam vakti puro’nyatra vipriyam kurute bhrsam \|*  
*jnataparadha-cestas ca kutilo’sau satho yatha \|\|*

**The deceitful hero speaks nicely in front of a woman, but acts against her when she is not present. He is crooked and has another love affair without her knowing. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.27)**

Krsna went to a grove of attractive reeds. This shows his offensive action and secret nature. Krsna was anukula to one gopi. This shows his favorable nature. This will be further explained in Song 16.

He is always favorable to Radha though here he shows *dhirodatta anukula* nature.

*kuvalaya-drsah sanketa-stha drg-ancala-kausalair*  
*manasija-kala-nati-prastavanam abhitanvatam \|*  
*na kila ghatate radha-ranga-prasanga-vidhayita*  
*vrata-vilasite saithilyasya cchatapy agha-vidvisah \|\|*

**\[Lalita speaks to Citra\] “O lotus-eyed women waiting in your bowers! By giving signals with skillful glances, you should display skillful introductory dances of love! But Krsna does not become slack in executing his vow to make Radha become attached to Cupid’s dances.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **1.28)**

Though Krsna is actually a *dhira-lalitanukula* lover, all qualities of the hero manifest in Krsna in order to nourish Radha’s *rasa* by the various *bhavas*.

### Text 1.48

*visvesam anuranjanena janayann anandam indivara-*  
*sreni-syamala-komalair upanayann angair anangotsavam \| svacchandam vraja-sundaribhir abhitah praty-angam alingitah*  
*srngarah sakhi murtiman iva madhau mugdho harih kridati \|\|48\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visvesam* - Of all beings (or of all the *gopis*); *anuranjanena* - by the conciliation; *janayan* - producing; *anandam* - bliss; *indivara-sreni* - like a row of blue lotuses; *syamala-komalaih* - tender and dark; *upanayann* - establishing; *angair* - with limbs; *anangotsavam* - the festival of Cupid; *svacchandam* - freely (or spontaneously); *vraja-sundaribhih* - by the beautiful women of Vraja (*gopis*); *abhitah* - near; *pratyahgam* - in all of his limbs (or all parts); *alingitah* -embraced (or take shelter); *srngarah* - amorous love; *sakhi* - О *sakhi*!; *murtiman* - in bodily form; *iva* - as if; *madhau* - in spring; *mugdhah* - innocent (or attractive); *harih* - Krsna; *kridati* - plays.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “O sakhi! Innocent Krsna plays in the spring as if he were the embodiment of amorous love, while producing bliss in all beings by satisfying them, and establishes a festival of Cupid by all his tender limbs dark like a host of blue lotuses, while the beautiful women of Vraja freely embrace all his limbs.**

**Комментарий:**

Since Krsna knows all arts. Why did he play with *gopis* who were ignorant of proper etiquette in love? This verse answers. О my friend Radha! Innocent Krsna plays in the spring as if he were the personification of love. What does he do? He produces bliss by gratifying all beings (*visvesam*), or all the *gopis* (*visvesam*). What else does he do? He establishes a festival of Cupid by his tender limbs dark like a host of blue lotuses. His limbs include portions of their limbs as well. His limbs are so soft that they cannot tolerate touch. An example was already given. This phrase indicates his beauty.

*anga-pratyangakanam yah sanniveso yathocitam \|*  
*suslista-sandhi-bandhah syat tat saundaryam itiryate \|\|*

**Beauty is defined as proper placement of all limbs and their parts, nicely joined together. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **1.286)**

Krsna is nicely embraced by the beautiful women of Vraja on all his limbs externally (using their limbs) and internally (by consciousness) as if he were the personification of *Srngara-rasa*. He is compared to *srngara-rasa* because he is blackish (*syama*). *Syamo bhavati srngarah sito hasah prakirtitah*; *syama* is the color of *srngara-rasa* and white is the color of *hasya-rasa* (*Natya-sastra* 6.42).

\[The following is another meaning of the verse.\] Attractive (*mugdhah*) *srngara-rasa* performs pastimes everywhere in the spring (*madhau*). It produces bliss in all beings by generating mutual attraction between young lovers (*anuranjanena*) bringing about a festival of Cupid with its parts like *sancari-bhavas* (transitory emotions). *Srngara-rasa* takes place not only by these causes. There are forests of *tamala* trees dark like blue lotuses and soft beds. The tender women of Vraja take shelter of *srngara-rasa* with all its *angas* spontaneously.

Among the *angas* of *srngara-rasa*, *tambula*, garlands, and sandalwood act as *uddipanas* (stimuli). The *anubhavas* (gestures and actions arising out of love, in relation toward the beloved) are smiling and glances. The *sancari-bhavas* are anxiety and envy. The *sattvika-bhavas* (involuntary external transformations) are standing of hairs on end and perspiration. The components or *ahgas* are described by Bharata.

*vibhavair anubhavais ca bhavah sancari-sattvikaih \|*  
*janito bodhitah sphito vrddhah srngara isyate \|\|*

***Srngara-rasa*** **is generated, stimulated, intensified, and increased by** ***vibhavas*** **(causes of** ***rati*** **- object of love, shelter of love, and stimuli),** ***anubhavas*****,** ***sancari-bhavas*** **(*****aka vyabhicari-bhavas*****) and** ***sattvika-bhavas*****.**

The general characteristics of *vibhava* are described in *Rasa-ratna-dipika*:

*bhavayanti ye rasan vai manoharan \|*  
*te vibhavas tu kathyante natya-sastra-visaradaih \|\|*

**Those knowing dramatics say that vibhavas are those persons who produce attractive rasas. (*****Rasa-ratna-dipika*** **3.1)**

The *vibhavas* of *srngara-rasa* are described by Bharata:

*rtu-malyalankaraih priya -jana-gandharva-kavya-sevabhih \|*  
*upavana-gamana-viharaih srngara-rasah samudbhavati \|\|*

***Srngara-rasa*** **arises from seasons, garlands, ornaments, the lover, music, poetry, service, going to a garden and playing. (*****Natya-sastra*** **6.47)**

The general characteristics of *anubhava* are described:

*kriyante vai rasah samyak lokanubhava-gocarah \|*  
*te’nubhavas tu kathyante natya-sastra-visaradaih \|\|*

***Anubhavas*** **are described by the knowers of dramatics as emotions which can be perceived by others. (*****Rasa-ratna-dipika*** **3.10)**

The *anubhavas* of *srhgara-rasa* are as follows:

*nayana-vadana-prasadaih smita-madhura-vaco-dhrti-pramodais ca \|*  
*madhurais canga-viharais tasyabhinayah prayoktavyah \|\|*

**The general indications are joy in the eyes and face, smiling, sweet words, patience joy, and beautiful movements of the limbs. (*****Natya-sastra*** **6.48)**

The general characteristics of *vyabhicari-bhavas* are described in (*Dasa-rupaka*):

*visesad abhimukhyena caranto vyabhicarinah \|*  
*sthayiny unmagna-nirmagnah kallold iva varidhau \|\|*

**The** ***vyabhicari-bhavas*** **move about, are directly visible, with particular emotion, and appear and disappear in the waves of the ocean of the** ***sthayi-bhava.*** **(*****Dasa-rupaka*** **4.7)**

The *vyabhicari-bhavas* of *srngara-rasa* are described in (*Srngara-tilaka*):

*sankasuya bhayam glanir vyadhis cinta smrtir dhrtih \|*  
*autsukyam vismayo harso vridonmado madas tatha \|\|*

*visado jadata nidravahittham capalam smrtih \|*  
*iti bhavah prayoktavyah srngare vyabhicarinah \|\|*

**The** ***vyabhicari-bhavas*** **of** ***srngara-rasa*** **are generally described as anxiety, envy, fear, fatigue, illness, contemplation, remembrance, patience, zeal, disappointment, joy, shame, madness, intoxication, depression, being stunned, sleep, concealment, fickleness and death-like appearance. (*****Srhgara-tilaka*** **1.28-29)**

The *sattvika-bhavas* are described in (*Srngara-tilaka*):

*stambhah svedo’tha romancah svara-bhango'tha vepathuh \|*  
*vaivarnyam asru pralaya ity astau sattvikah smrtah \|\|*

**The eight** ***sattvika-bhavas*** **are being stunned, perspiration, hairs standing on end, breaking of the voice, shivering, change of color, tears, and fainting. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.15)**

Detailed characteristics are not mentioned out of fear of enlarging the work. Though Krsna knows all arts, he plays with the women of Vraja (though they are not so skillful) according to their qualities, since he attracts them. Thus he maintains his quality of being *daksa* (skillful man) in spite of their lack of skill. It was said that Krsna is able to attract all living entities. By mentioning ordinary *prema* in the other *gopis* the outstanding nature of Radha’s prema is indicated.

Another meaning is as follows. О friend! Producing bliss for all us *sakhis* by developing his love for you (*anuranjanena*), he plays. You should not hate him though he enjoys with other women, since he embraces them while remaining independent of them. He has most attraction for embracing you. The rest is the same.

*Srngara* refers to the *srngara rasa* according to *Visva*. *Visvam* means everything. *Indlvara* means blue according to *Amara-kosa*. *Syamala* means black. *Marti* means a body.

### Text 1.49

*rasollasa-bharena vibhrama-bhrtam abhira-vama-bhruvam*  
*abhyarnam parirabhya nirbharam urah premandhaya radhaya \|*  
*sadhu tvad-vadanam sudha-mayam iti vyahrtya gita-stuti-*  
*vyajad udbhata-cumbitah smita-manohari harih patu vah \|\|49\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rasollasa* - In the joy of the *rasa* dance; *bharena* - abounding; *vibhrama-bhrtam* -filled with confusion (blinded by); *abhira-vama-bhruvam* - the wives of the cowherds (*gopis*); *abhyarnam* - near; *parirabhya* - having been embraced; *nirbharam* - tightly; *urah* -\[his\] chest; *premandhaya* - blinded by intense joy; *radhaya* - by Radha; *sadhu* - beautiful; *tvat-vadanam* - “your face; *sudha-mayam iti* - consists in nectar”; *vyahrtya* - is spoken; *gita-stuti* - of praising a song; *vyajad* - on the pretext; *udbhata-cumbitah* - kissed openly; *smita* - with \[his\] smile; *manohari* - who steals the minds \[of the *gopis*\]; *harih* - Krsna; *patu* - may he (Krsna) protect; *vah* - you.

**Перевод:**

**May Krsna, who steals the minds of the gopis with his smile, and is tightly embraced on his chest by Radha blinded by intense joy, and who is then visibly kissed by her in the presence of other gopis who are blinded by their joy in the rasa dance, protect you. Radha praises his face as nectar on the pretext of praising a song.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of the chapter the author gives a blessing. May Krsna protect you. How is he described? He attracts the women with his soft smile. He is clearly kissed by Radha, after she deeply embraces him on the chest in front of other *gopis*. How are the other *gopis* described? They cannot see properly and are confused (*vibhrama-bhrtam*) because of great joy in the *rasa* dance. Let them be without sight.
