But why is he kissed by Radha when she can see the other young women in front of her? She is blinded by extreme joy (*prema*). She sees nothing except Krsna. Embracing him deeply, she kisses him without seeing a second person. On the pretext of praising his song, she indicates, “Your face is filled with nectar. It is beautiful (*sadhu*)!” She shows skill because she simply says “The song is beautiful, filled with nectar.” Then she kisses him.

## Chapter Two. Aklesa-kesavah - Krsna who destroys the gopis’ suffering

### Text 2.1

*viharati vane radha sadharana-pranaye harau*  
*vigalita-nijotkarsad irsya-vasena gata’nyatah \|*  
*kvacid api lata-kunje gunjan-madhu-vrata-mandali-*  
*mukhara-sikhare lina dinapy uvaca rahah sakhlm \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viharati* - \[While Krsna\] was playing; *vane* - in the forest; *radha* -Radha; *sadharana-pranaye* - whose love \[for the other *gopis*\] was equal; *harau* - while Krsna; *vigalita* - had gone away; *nijotkarsad* -because her superior position; *irsya-vasena* - on account of anger; *gata’nyatah* - having gone elsewhere; *kvacit* - somewhere; *api* - even; *lata-kunje* - in a bower of creepers; *gunjat* - humming; *madhu-vrata* - of black bees; *mandali* - with a swarm; *mukhara-sikhare* - at the height of commotion; *lina* - concealed; *dinapy* - miserable; *uvaca* -spoke; *rahah* - privately; *sakhim* - to a sakhi

**Перевод:**

**Radha, having out of anger left that place because her superior position had been thwarted, while Krsna played in the forest with equal love for other gopis, privately spoke to a sakhi. Suffering, she remained hidden in a bower, filled with the loud humming of bees.**

**Комментарий:**

In chapter one, Radha’s state of thinking of Krsna with desire was described. Now the state of remembering him in previous pastimes is described. The characteristics of this condition will be described in GG 7.2 and 8.1. Instigated by a *sakhi’s* words, Radha, unable to tolerate Krsna being with other gopis in her presence, speaks to the sakhi after entering a bower. This verse shows this.

Radha privately spoke to her *sakhi* who was present. How is Radha described? Swayed by anger, unable to tolerate the situation, she had gone elsewhere. The cause of her anger is given. Her superior position had been lost since she had thought, “I alone am very dear to him.” She had lost her superiority over the others. At that same time, Krsna was playing in the forest (*harau vane viharati*). How is he described? He had equal *prema* for the other *gopis* (*sadharana-pranaye*). The phrase can also be taken as *asadharana-pranaye*: he had incomparable love for them. Therefore, she was miserable (*dina*). How else is she described? She remained hidden in the thick bower of creepers, which was at the height of commotion because of the humming of a swarm of bees. The word lata is used to indicate that the bower was very dense with foliage. *Pranaya* means *prema* according to *Visva*. *Irsya* means anger out of pride. Extra emotion is indicated by the sound of the bees. Falling under the control of anger indicates the appearance of a *vyabhicari-bhava*.

**\|\|Song Five \|\|**

*gurjari-raga, yati-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***gurjari*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The song is in *gurjari-raga* and *pratimanthaka-tala*. The characteristics of the *raga* were previously discussed. The *tala* is described as follows:

*taras capy aparas caiva vicarah kandu-samjnakah \|*  
*catvarah kathita hy ete pratimantha vicaksanaih \|\|*

*viramanta-druta-dvandvam gurus caikas tatah param \|*  
*suranga-talah gatavyas talas tu pratimanthakah \|\|*

*gurur eko bhaved yatra sannipatah sa kathyate \|*  
*apara-pratimanto’sau vidvadbhis tena giyate \|\|*

*laghu-dvayam viramdntam tale kanduka-samjnake \|*  
*vicaro glyate tena vilambas callaso bhavet II*

*drutam ekam bhaved yatra sa talo khanda-samjnitah \|*  
*druta-trayena gatavyah kundas ca pratimanthakah \|\|*

**The wise say there are four types of** ***pratimanthaka-tala*****:** ***suranga, sannipata, kanduka*** **and** ***khanda*****.** ***Suranga*****, to be sung, consists of 2 + 1+ 2 + 1 + 8 = 14.** ***Sannipata*** **consists of 8.** ***Kanduka*****, which is sung slowly, consists of 4 + 4 + 2 = 10.** ***Khanda*** **consists of 2. The beats are played in three speeds.**

The *tala* is called *suratiga* or *pratimantha*, because Radha performs wonderful pastimes (*suranga*). The others will be understood in later songs.

### Text 2.2

*sancarad-adhara-sudha-madhura-*  
*dhvani-mukharita-mohana-vamsam \|*  
*calita-drg-ancala-cancala-mauli-*  
*kapola-vilola-vatamsam \|\|2\|\|*

*rase harim iha vihita-vilasam*  
*smarati mano mama krta-parihasam \|\| \[dhru.\]*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sancarad-adhara* - Pressed to \[his\] lips; *sudha-madhura* - as sweet as nectar; *dhvani* - with \[its\] tone; *mukharita* - resonant; *mohana* -bewilders \[others\] (or the flute named Mohana); *vamsam* - whose flute; *calita* - are darting; *drk-ancala* - \[and\] the corners of whose eyes; *cancala* - is swaying; *mauli* - whose crown; *kapola* - on \[his\] cheeks; *vilola* - are quivering; *vatamsam* - \[and\] whose earrings; *rase* - In the rasa dance; *harim* - Krsna ; *iha* - in this forest (or in this bower); *vihita-vilasam* - by whom pastimes were performed \[with me\]; *smarati* - remembers; *mano* - mind; *mama* - my; *krta-parihasam* - \[and\] by whom jokes were made.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha speaks to sakhi\] “My mind dwells on Krsna who performed pastimes with me and joked with me during the rasa dance in the forest, who plays a flute which bewilders everyone with its sweet tones pressed to his sweet lips, who has earrings quivering on his cheeks, a swaying crown and darting glances.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna who performed pastimes with me and joked with me during the rasa dance in the forest, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Explanation of chorus:

*harim smarati mano mama means*, О friend, my mind remembers Krsna who steals my heart (*harim*). She remembers what happened previously. *rase iha vihita-vilasam krta-parihasam* means, he performed pastimes with me while performing the *rasa* dance in the forest (*iha*) and he joked.

Or the meaning is, as I hide in the bower, my mind remembers the stealer of my heart who made me engage in various types of vibhrama (*vihita-vilasam*) so that he could joke with other women (vibhrama is explained next paragraphs).

Or the meaning is, my mind remembers the stealer of my heart who made me insane with attraction (*vihita-vilasam*).

It is said concerning *vibhrama*:

*adhinasyapi sevayam kantasyanabhinandanam \|*  
*vibhramo vamatodrekat syad ity akhyati kascana \|\|*

**Some say that** ***vibhrama*** **takes place when, out of apparent contrariness, the heroine becomes displeased with her lover even though he is submissive and eager to serve her. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.42)**

Here is an example:

*tvam govinda mayasi kim nu kavari-bandhartham abhyarthitah*  
*klesenalam abaddha eva cikura-stomo mudaril dogdhi me \|*  
*vaktrasyapi na majjanam kuru ghanam gharmasu me rocate*  
*naivottamsaya malatir mama sirah khedam bharenapsyati \|\|*

**“O Govinda! Did I ask you to braid my hair? It is too much trouble. The unbound hair gives me joy. Do not wipe the perspiration from my face. It pleases me. Do not garland my head with these jasmine flowers. They give me pain by their weight.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.43)**

According to *Amara-kosa* the word *vibhrama* means pretentious disregard for the lover in *srngara-rasa*. “My mind dwells on Krsna” \[in the chorus\] suggests that she desired the eight types of enjoyment since remembering these eight types is a means (*sadhana*) to fulfill the goal of the lover.

*smaranam klrtanam kelih preksanam guhya-bhasanam \|*  
*sarikalpo’dhyavasayas ca kriya-nivrtstir eva ca \|\|*

*etan maithunam astangam pravadanti manisinah \|*  
*viparitam brahmacaryam etad evasta-laksanam \|\|*

**The wise explain that the eight limbs of connection between a man and a woman are remembrance, glorification, thinking of amorous play, gazing with desire to see, private conversations, thinking how to meet, mental effort, attaining happiness in union. Doing the opposite of these becomes the eight qualities of** ***brahmacarya*****. (*****Vairagya-martanda*** **12.144-145)**

Some commentators take the words *iha rase* to mean “here at the *rasa* dance”. But this is not proper since it becomes meaningless since it is impossible that the *rasa* dance could be taking place at that time. *Bhatta says idamah prayaksa-gatam*: the word *idam* (related to the word *iha*) refers to events which have passed. The *rasa* dance was not visible, since it took place previously and Radha hid herself in the bower. The *rasa* dance is not possible without her. This is the chorus.

The heroine is an *uttama-madhyama*. Her qualities are as follows;

*dosanurupa-kopa yanunita ca prasidati \|*  
*rajyate ca bhrsam nathe guna-haryottameti sa \|\|*

**The uttama woman is bountifully endowed with charming qualities, and though angered at faults \[of the man\], is pacified and is pleased.**

*kante kim kupitasi ? kah para-jane pranesa kopo bhavet ?*  
*ko’yam subhru paras tvam eva dayite ? daso’smi kas te parah ? \|*  
*ity uktva pranatah priyah ksiti-talad utthapya sanandaya*  
*netrambhah-kanikankite stana-tate tanvya samaropitah \|\|*

**\[The woman says,\] “O beloved! Why are you angry? О lord of my life! How can one become angry at another person?О beloved! Who else can there be except you? I am your servant. Who else is there for you?” She says this while offering respects to her man, and the man happily raises her from the earth, and puts her on his chest which becomes stained with her tears. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **89)**

Here is an example of the *uttama*:

*kartum sarma ksanikam api me sadhyam ujjhaty asesam*  
*cittotsange na bhajati may a datta-khedapy asuyam \|*  
*srutva cantarvidalati mrsapy arti-varta-lavam me*  
*radha murdhany akhila-sudrsam rajate sad-gunena \|\|*

**\[Krsna says,\] “Radha will renounce all her attainments, if that will make me happy for a moment. Even if I torment her, she never becomes angry with me deep in her heart. If she hears a rumor that I am slightly suffering, even if it is untrue, her heart cracks. With these good qualities, Radha reigns as the best of all the beautiful-eyed** ***gopis*****.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **5.98)**

The *madhya* is described as follows:

*samana-lajja-madana prodyat-tarunya-salim \|*  
*kincit-pragalbha-vacana mohanta-surata-ksama \|*  
*madhya syat komala kvapi mane kutrapi karkasa \|\|*

**The madhya woman has equal shyness and desire, has full youth, has somewhat bold speech, has the possibility of love affairs after fainting, is mild in anger and is sometimes rough. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **5.27)**

**Explanation of the verse:**

The rest of the verse is as follows. How is Krsna described? He has a flute which bewilders (mohana) others, which is played with a sweet sound by his lips and it produces various tones. Or he has a flute called Mohana which vibrates with sweet sounds by the nectar of his lips pressed to it. *Sancarad-adhura-sudha-madhura-dhvani* acts as an adverbial phrase in the neuter singular.

*ka stry anga te kala-padayata-venu-gita-*  
*sammohitarya-caritan na calet tri-lokyam*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “Dear Krsna, what woman in all the three worlds wouldn’t deviate from religious behavior when bewildered by the sweet, drawn-out melody of your flute?” (SB 10.29.40)**

An example is as follows:

*satyam srnomi sakhi nitya-nava-priyo’sau*  
*gopas tathapi hrdayam madano dunoti \|*  
*yuktya kathancana samam gamite’pi tasmin*  
*mam tasya kala-murali kavalikaroti \|\|*

**“Yes, my friend, 1 have heard that Krsna is a debauchee always searching for new lovers. Still, my heart trembles with love for him. I pacified my heart with many logical arguments and was able to drive away the love from it, but then, in the next moment I found myself devoured by the sweet sound of Krsna’s flute.” (*****Padyavali*** **234)**

Thus the meaning is, my mind, bewildered by the flute, remembers him even though he offended me.

How else is he described? He has ear ornaments quivering on his cheeks, a swaying crown and eyes that glance through the corners \[of his eyes\] to respect me. \[The crown is swaying and ear ornaments are quivering, because he moves his head while playing the flute.\] Movement of his head is not a fault. By moving his earrings and crown he produces colorful music. The phrase *adhara-sudha* (nectar or bliss of his lips) suggests that his lips produce more bliss than the moon which normally produces bliss. *Vatamsa* stands for *avatamsa* and means earrings. According to *Bhaguri,* the ‘a’ in *avatamsa* can be dropped.

### Text 2.3

*candraka-caru-mayura-sikhandaka-mandala-valayita-kesam \|*  
*pracura-purandara-dhanur-anuranjita-medura-mudira-suvesam \|\|3\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*candraka-caru* - With the colorful eyes \[of the peacock feather\]; *mayura* - of peacocks; *sikhandaka* - of the feathers; *mandala* - by a circle; *valayita* - is surrounded; *kesam* - whose hair; *pracura* -many; *purandara-dhanuh* - with Indra’s bow (rainbow); *anuranjita* - decorated; *medura-mudira* - like a dense cloud; *suvesam* - whose form is beautiful.

**Перевод:**

**“His hair is decorated with many peacock feathers having colorful eyes on their tips. He has a beautiful form, brilliant as a cloud decorated with many rainbows.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

“He cannot be forgotten by his absence.” This is conveyed by this verse. His hair is surrounded by a circle of peacock feathers with colorful eyes on the tips. This statement suggests that on seeing peacocks at that place (near her bower) she remembered him. And he has a form brilliant like dense clouds colored by many rainbows. Krsna is compared to a cloud and his various jewel necklaces, crown and bracelets are compared to many rainbows. This is an example of *adbhutopama* since one does not see many rainbows.

*Candraka* means “the eye of the peacock feather” according to *Amara-kosa*. *Sikhanda* is the peacock feather. *Valayitam* means surrounded. *Mudira* means a cloud. *Vesa* means a house (body) or costume.

### Text 2.4

*gopa-kadamba-nitambavati-mukha-cumbana-lambhita-lobham \|*  
*bandhujiva-madhuradhara-pallavam ullasita-smita-sobham \|\|4\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*gopa-kadamba* - Troop of cowherd women; *nitambavati* - of the excellent-hipped; *mukha* - mouth (lips); *cumbana* - for kissing; *lambhita-lobham* - who has desire; *bandhujiva* - like *bandhujiva* flower; *madhura* - is attractive; *adhara* - of a lower lip; *pallavam* - whose bud; *ullasita-smita* - because of his blossoming smile; *sobham* - beautiful.

**Перевод:**

**“He is greedy to kiss the lips of the cowherd women who have excellent hips. His tender lips are attractive like the bandhuji-va flower. He has a blossoming smile.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

He is greedy to kiss all the cowherd women having excellent hips. Or he has desire to kiss me (Radha), among all the cowherd women. The latter means that his love for Radha is natural and his actions related to the other *gopis* is forced by them.

How else is he described? He has bud-like lips, attractive like the red *bandhujiva* flower. Or, he has bud-like lips attractive because they are the source of nectar (*jiva*) for his devotees (*bandhu*), giving them life. The word *madhura* indicates juiciness and *pallava* indicates tenderness and youth.

He is also beautiful with a blossoming smile. The lips \[which reveal his smile\] are compared to the blossoming of a *bandhuka* flower within a blue lotus. The smile is like a blossoming jasmine. Seeing the *bandhuka* flower, she remembers his smile.

The *bandhujiva* (also known as *bandhuka*) flower is red according to *Amara-kosa*. *Madhura* means juicy or attractive.

### Text 2.5

*vipula-pulaka-bhuja-pallava-valayita-ballava-yuvati-sahasram \|*  
*kara-caranorasi-mani-gana-bhusana-kirana-vibhinna-tamisram \|\|5\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vipula-pulaka* - Hairs standing on end all over; *bhuja* - which are his arms; *pallava* - by the shoots; *valayita* - are surrounded; *ballava-yuvati-sahasram* - by whom thousands of young cowherd -women; *kara-caranorasi* - on his hands, feet and chest; *mani-gana* - consisting in the jewels; *bhusana* - from the ornaments; *kirana* - with the rays; *vibhinna* - is destroyed; *tamisram* - \[and\] by whom the darkness.

**Перевод:**

**“He surrounds thousands of young women with his two arms, soft like shoots, with hairs standing on end all over. The rays from the jewels of his hands, feet and chest destroy the darkness.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

He surrounds (*valayita*) thousands of young women with his two arms, soft like shoots, with hairs standing on end all over. The word *valayita* is used to suggest that his arms surround them carelessly, not fervently. He intimately embraces Radha only. And the rays from the jewels of his hands, feet and chest destroy (*vibhinna*) the darkness. *Tamisram* means darkness according to *Amara-kosa*.

Or the meaning is, he spends time with the cowherd women having goose bumps, who were surrounded by his tender arms. What young women would not remember this? And he defeated my competitors (*vibhinna*) by the rays of jeweled ornaments which decorated my hands, feet and chest. These ornaments were given by him to me. The competing women were defeated on seeing me decorated with his ornaments. Instead of *kirana* sometimes *usra* is seen. *Usra* means illuminating.

### Text 2.6

*jalada-patala-valad-indu-vinindaka-candana-tilaka-lalatam \|*  
*pina-payodhara-parisara-mardana-nirdayа-hrdaya-kapatam \|\|6\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jalada-patala* - In a mass of clouds; *valat* - enclosed; *indu-vinindaka* - which surpasses the moon; *candana-tilaka* - is a sandalwood tilaka; *lalatam* - on whose forehead; *pina-payodhara* - full breasts \[of the *gopis*\] ; *parisara* - the adjoining; *mardana* - crushes; *nirdaya-hrdaya* - of whose cruel chest; *kapatam* - \[and\] the door bolt.

**Перевод:**

**“On his forehead is sandalwood tilaka, which defeats the moon in a mass of clouds. The cruel door bolt of his chest crushes the full breasts of the gopis.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

On his forehead is a sandalwood mark which defeats the moon attached to a mass of rain clouds. Another version has *jalada-patala-calad-indu-vinindaka-candana-bindu-lalatam:* on his forehead is a circular mark of sandalwood which defeats the moon passing over a mass of clouds.

### Text 2.7

*mani-maya-makara-manohara-kundala-mandita-gandam udaram \|*  
*pita-vasanam anugata-muni-manuja-surasura-vara-parivaram \|\|7\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mani-maya* - Made of jewels; *makara* - in the shape of *makara*; *manohara-kundala* - with attractive earrings; *mandita* - are decorated; *gandam* - whose cheeks; *udaram* - beautiful; *pita-vasanam* - whose clothing is yellow; *anugata* - who serve him; *muni-manuja-surasura* - of sages, men, devatas and demons; *vara* - consists in the best; *parivaram* - \[and\] whose retinue.

**Перевод:**

**“His beautiful cheeks are decorated with attractive makara earrings made of jewels. He wears yellow cloth, and the best of loyal sages, men, demons and devatas have become his followers.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

His cheeks are decorated with attractive *makara* earrings made of jewels. It is said *bhusana-bhusanangam*: his form enhances the beauty of ornaments - (SB 3.2.12). *Vibhusanam vibhusyam syad yena tad ruрат ucyate*: excellent form is said to be that by which ornaments become worthy of being ornaments - (BRS 2.1.338). Rupam means beauty:

*muktaphalesu chayayas taralatvam ivantara \|*  
*pratibhati yad angesu lavanyam tad ihocyate \|\|*

**When waves of beauty - emanating from within the limbs - cause reflection, as in pearls, it is called** ***lavanya*****. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.28)**

An example is as follows:

*srnu sakhi tava karne varnayamy atra nicair v*  
*iracaya mukha-candram ma vrtharad vivarnam \|*  
*iyam urasi murarer asti nanya mrgaksi*  
*marakata-mukurabhe bimbitasi tvam eva \|\|*

**\[A** ***sakhi*** **consoles Radha,\] “My friend, listen. I shall now whisper something in your ear while I am close to you. Please do not make the moon of your face so pale. There is no other woman with your beauty. You are reflected on Krsna’s chest, a sapphire mirror.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.30)**

Those cheeks are beautiful (*udaram*). Why would I not remember a person with such beautiful cheeks? Or *udaram* can mean generous. *Audaryam vinayam prahuh sarvavasthagatam budhah*: audaryam means having a kind nature in all circumstances - (*Ujjvala-nilamani* 11.23).

Or, \[the meaning of the first line is as follows.\] I remember the person (Krsna) who decorated my cheeks with attractive *makara* earrings made of jewels. He was so generous (*udaram*) to do so. \[He was generous because\] he fulfilled all my desires privately at that moment. Why should I not remember him?

\[The meaning of the second line is as follows.\]

He wears yellow clothing and the best of loyal sages, men, demons and devatas have become his followers (*parivaram*) and have taken shelter of him (*anugata*). Or, he is outstanding (*parivara*) among all the best of surrendered (*anugata*) sages, men, demons and *devatas*. *Visva* says that *parivara* means excellent. The word *vara* (best) applies to all the types of people in the compound *muni-manuja-surasura*. He is continually served even by these people who have achieved their goals through him. Thus his kind nature (*udaram*) is announced. The best of sages are Narada, Sukadeva and others. The best of men are persons like Pariksit. The best of devatas are Brahma, Siva and others. The best of demons are persons like Prahlada.

Or, he has the best followers (*parivara*) in the form of the *gopis* and *gopas* who are superior (*vara*) to surrendered (*anugata*) sages, men, *devatas* and demons. Or, he has the best (*vara*) obedient (*anugata*) attendants (*parivara*) among sages, humans, *devatas* and demons since they are attracted by his great sweetness.

*Divi bhuvi ca rasayam kah striyas tad-durapah*: in heaven, or on earth or in the subterranean sphere, what women are unavailable to him? - (SB 10.47.15). *Arundhanam arundhati-hrdayam apy ardra-smitardra-sriya*: his beauty captures the heart of even Arundhati by the tender beauty of its tender smile - (*Krsna-karnamrta* 87).

Or he causes the retinue of sages, humans, *devatas* and demons to surrender. The hero’s generosity is described as follows:

*danam abhyupapattis ca tatha ca priya-bhasanam \|*  
*svajane ca pare vapi tad audaryam prakirtitam \|\|*

***Audarya*** **or generosity refers to giving gifts, giving protection or saying kind words to known or unknown persons. (*****Natya-sastra*** **22.40)**

### Text 2.8

*visada-kadamba-tale militam kali-kalusa-bhayam samayantam \|*  
*mam api kim api tarala-tarangad-ananga-drsa manasa ramayantam \|\|8\|\|*

*rase harim iha...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*visada-kadamba* - Of a spotless kadamba tree; *tale* - at the base; *militam-kali* - caused by \[our\] quarrels; *kalusa-bhayam* - \[my\] fear and confusion; *samayantam* - pacifying; *mam* - me; *api* - even; *kim api* - somehow; *tarala-tarangad* - is like trembling waves; *ananga* - in which the desire; *drsa* - in my eyes; *manasa* - \[and\] in \[my\] mind; *ramayantam* - and pleasing

**Перевод:**

**“He was sitting at the base of a spotless kadamba tree, and pacified my fears and confusion caused by our quarrels. Indescribably he produces pleasure in my mind and in my eyes, which have developed ever-increasing desire, like trembling waves.**

**“My mind dwells on Krsna...”**

**Комментарий:**

I remember him sitting at the base of a spotless *kadamba* tree. Having come already , expecting me at the base of the *kadamba* tree designated by me, he remained hidden there to joke with me. He thought, “No one should see me.” \[After sometime\] he \[then\] pacified my fears and disturbances caused by quarreling.

The disturbance from quarrelling is as follows. “I came according to the hints. He has not come. As to be expected, he must be obligated to another woman. If he comes, I will not speak to him.” Fear is as follows. “How can I remain here all alone?”

Or the meaning is, being fearful of my anger and quarrels, coming from the base of the *kadamba*, he pacifies me with clever words.

\[The meaning of the second line is as follows\]

Indescribably *(kim api*), he produces pleasure in my mind and eyes, which have desire, increasing like trembling waves.

Or, he causes pleasure in me by a mind which sees (*drsa*) sweet love arising in waves within the universe when he is in front of me. I have become the object of his supreme affection. My mind remembers Krsna who has the above qualities.

*Kali* means quarrel according to *Visva*. *Kalusa* means confusion.

### Text 2.9

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam atisundara-mohana-madhuripu-rupam \|*  
*hari-carana-smaranam prati samprati punyavatam апиruрат \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam* - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; *atisundara-mohana* -which is very beautiful \[and\] bewilders \[the *gopis*\]; *madhuripu-rupam* -\[for\] the form of Krsna; *hari-carana* - of the feet of; *smaranam* - \[and for\] the remembrance; *prati* - for; *samprati* - now; *punyavatam* - among those who have gained merit; *аnurupam* - is suitable for (it generates a taste for)

**Перевод:**

**This composition of Jayadeva gives to those who have gained qualification by acts of previous bhakti a taste for remembering Radha’s actions and is the embodiment of the form of Krsna, who bewitches the gopis. (END OF SONG FIVE)**

**Комментарий:**

May this composition spoken by the poet Jayadeva be suitable to generate taste for similar *bhava* (which is described in the poem) in persons who have gained qualification by acts which generate remembrance of Krsna’s feet (*punyavatam*), so that they can remember Radha’s actions in the *rasa* dance and other pastimes described here (*hari-carana-smaranam*). This composition contains descriptions of Krsna’s form which bewilders the *gopis* (*atisundara*). This is end of the fifth *prabandha* (song), called “The jeweled necklace.”

### Text 2.10

*ganayati guna-gramam bhamam bhramad api nehate*  
*vahati ca paritosam dosam vimuncati duratah \|*  
*yuvatisu valat-trsne krsne viharini mam vina*  
*punar api mano vamam kamarh karoti karomi kim \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ganayati* - \[My mind\] enumerates; *guna-gramam* - the aggregate of his qualities; *bhamam* - anger; *bhramad* - out of illusion; *api* - even; *nehate* - it does not desire; *vahati* - it bears; *ca* - and; *paritosam* -satisfaction; *dosam* - \[his\] faults; *vimuncati* - it pardons; *duratah* - from afar; *yuvatisu* - for the young women (*gopis*); *valat-trsne* - is one whose thirst is increasing; *krsne* - while Krsna; *viharati* — \[and\] plays; *mam vina* — without me; *punar* — again; *api* — even; *mano* — \[my\] mind; *vamam* - unfavorable; *каmаm-karoti* - has desire; *karomi kim* - what can I do?

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha continues speaking,\] “Somehow my mind desires him even now, even though he has increasing thirst for many gopis and is increasingly inclined to play without me. My mind acts against me, because it enumerates all his qualities, and out of illusion it does not desire to become angry. It pardons his faults, because it is satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

“Krsna plays with other *gopis* without you. Why do you remember him?” This verse answers.

My mind has desire for Krsna even now. What can I do? He has increasing thirst for many *gopis* and is increasingly inclined to play without me. My mind is unfavorable to me (*yamam*).

Another reason for the unfavorable mind is given as follows. My mind enumerates all his qualities such as his beauty. Out of illusion, it does not desire to become angry (*bhamam*). It pardons his faults, such as his disrespecting me completely, because it is satisfied.

The ornaments of *vibhavana* and *visesokti* are expressed here. In *Kavya-prakasa* it is said:

*kriyayah pratisedhe’pi phala-vyaktir vibhavana \|*  
*visesohtir akhandesu karanesu phalavacah \|\|*

***Vibhavana*** **means the appearance of results even though negated by actions which oppose it (effect is present even though the circumstance does not support it).** ***Visesokti*** **means not mentioning some effect, when the causes are present (cause is present, but the effect is absent).**

In this verse they’re as expressed as follows.

*Vibhavana*: Attachment for Krsna (result) appears \[in Radha\], in the form of surrender, even without any reason for the attachment. (Also the actions of Krsna, being attached to other *gopis*, negates that attachment of Radha).

*Visesokti*: Unmentioned fault (effect) of Krsna’s attachment to other women (Krsna’s attachment to other women is the effect, but the cause of that attachment is not mentioned in the verse).

*Grama* means a group according to *Visva*. *Bhama* means anger. *Vamam* means enemy. *Kamam* means desire.

**\|\| Song Six \|\|**

*malava-gauda-ragena ekatali-talena ca giyate \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***malava-gauda*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *malava-gauda* and the *tala* is *ekatali.* The followers of *Kumbha* sing it in *bhairavi-raga.* The characteristics of the *raga* were described already. The *tala* is as follows:

*eka-tali tridha prokta glta-sastra-visaradaih \|*  
*rama-candrika tadvad vipulety atha laksanam \|\|*

*drutam ekam bhaved yatra talo’yam khanda-samjnitah \|*  
*ramanenaika-tall tu kirtyate gayanottamaih \|\|*

*guru-dvayam bhaved yatra talo lalita-samjnakah \|*  
*candrika caika-tall syat tena saubhagya-dayini \|\|*

*kokila-priya-tale vai drutam trayam udahrtam \|*  
*vipula caika-tall syat tena gitajna-sammata \|\|*

*khanda-lalita-kokila-priyah iti bhedas tatra jneyah \|\|*

**Knowers of music say that there are three types of** ***ekatali*****:** ***rama, candrika*** **and** ***vipula*****.**

**1.    A** ***tala*** **with one** ***druta*** **(2 beats) is called** ***khanda*****.** ***Ekatali*** **with this is called** ***rama*****.**

**2.    8 + 8 is called** ***lalita*****.** ***Ekatali*** **with this is called** ***candrika*****.**

**3.    2 + 2 +2 is called** ***kokilapriya-tala*****.** ***Ekatali*** **with this is called** ***vipula.***

**Thus the three types are** ***khanda, lalita*** **and** ***kokilapriya*****.**

In Orissa the *ekatali* is described as follows:

*uddama-manmatha-mahajvara-vepamanam*  
*romanca-kantakitam angam udvahantim \|*  
*samveda-vepathu-ghanotkalika-kulangim*  
*utkanthitarn vadati tam bharatah kavindrah \|\|*

***Bharata,*** **king of poets, describes** ***ekatali*** **as full of longing with hairs standing on end, with limbs like buds, with perceivable trembling because of a great fever of passion.**

### Text 2.11

*nibhrta-nikunja-grham gataya*  
*nisi rahasi niliya vasantam \|*  
*cakita-vilokita-sakala-disa*  
*rati-rabhasa-bharena hasantam \|\|11\|\|*

*sakhi he! kesi-mathanam udaram*  
*ramaya maya saha madana-manoratha-*  
*bhavitaya sa-vikaram \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nibhrta-nikunja* - Which is a lonely bower; *grham* - to a dwelling; *gataya* - \[I\] having gone; *nisi* - in the night; *rahasi* - alone; *niliya* - having concealed; *vasantam* - \[he\] remaining; *cakita* - with fear; *vilokita-sakala-disa* - \[I\] by whom all the directions are seen; *rati-rabhasa* - of the agitation of love; *bharena* - with the burden; *hasantam* - \[he\] laughing.

*sakhi he* - О friend; *kesi-mathanam* - the crusher of Kesi (Krsna); *udaram* - kind; *ramaya* - make \[him\] enjoy; *maya saha* - with me; *madana-manoratha* - the desire for love; *bhavitaya* - having developed \[*rati*\]; *sa-vikaram* - with altered feelings.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha explains her desires to sakhi,\] “I have gone to this lonely bower while he has hidden himself alone in the night. I glance about with fear while he, laden with the agitation of love, laughs.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna, the crusher of Kesi, who is showing symptoms of love, enjoy with me, since I have developed rati with desire for love. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Filled with longing and *rasa,* Radha explains her desires to her *sakhi*. The meaning of the song is as follows (the chorus will be explained first). О friend! Make Krsna play with me, since I have developed *rati* endowed with desire for love (*madana*). Or make Krsna play with me since I have developed *rati* by the desire of Cupid (*madana*).

*Bhavitaya* means “attained.”

“Will he come when I ask him?”

“He is very kind (*udarama*). He will definitely grant your request.”

Or *madana* can refer to Krsna, since Krsna agitates Cupid. \[In that case, the meaning is,\] make Krsna play with me since I have developed the desire for enjoyment with him (*madana-manoratha*).

How is his desire to be known? \[The answer is\] he has changes in the mind (*sa-vikaram*). It was said that *srngara-rasa* is nourished by previous attraction between the couple.

*anurago’nuraktayam rasavaha iti sthitih \|*  
*abhave tv anuragasya rasabhasam jagur budhah \|\|*

**The wise say that love is pleasurable because of attractions. Without attraction, there is only a shadow of the** ***rasa*** **of love.**

In this verse, the heroine is *uttama,* with *maha-bhava*. The hero is also *uttama*. His qualities were previously listed. *Sa-vikaram* can also be an adverb: make him enjoy with me, since I, manifesting transformations (*sa-vikaram*), have developed desire for him. *Rasika-sarvasva* says:

*nabhi-mula-kucodara-prakatana-vyajena уad yositam \|*  
*sakanksam muhur iksanam skhalitata nivi-nibandhasya ca \|*  
*kesa-bhramsana-samyamau ca kamitur mitradi-sandarsanaih \|*  
*saubhagyadi-guna-prasasti-kathanais tasam tu rageksitam \|\| iti \|\|*

**Desire in women is manifested by revealing the navel, breasts, and belly, constant gazing, their belts becoming loose, untying and tying the hair. They glance with love which is revealed by speaking of the good qualities of the lover and seeing his friends.**

The words *kesi-mathana* (destroyer of Kesi) are used in order to show Krsna’s abilities. He can arrange of pastimes with no cause of anxiety (since he can destory Kesi which is a difficult task, he can arrange pastimes which would be easy for him.). Or, *kesi-mathana* can mean he who disturbs (*mathana*) the women and has beautiful hair (*kesi*). Or it means Krsna with beautiful hair who agitates the hearts of the women by his beauty. It is said:

*viksyalakavrta-mukham tava kundala-sri*  
*ganda-sthaladhara-sudham hasitavalokam*  
*dattabhayam ca bhuja-danda-yugam vilokya*  
*vaksah sriyaika-ramanam ca bhavama dasyah*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “Seeing your face encircled by curling locks of hair, your cheeks beautified by earrings, your lips full of nectar, and your smiling glance, and also seeing your two imposing arms, which take away our fear, and your chest, which is the only source of pleasure for the goddess of fortune, we must become your maidservants.” (SB 10 29.39)**

Or Kesi can mean the controller (*isa*) of love (*ka*). Kesa becomes *kesi* according to the *rule ajaty-arthe supi-dhator ninih syat tacchilye* - (*Panini* 3.2.78). *Mathana* is derived from the verb *math* (to churn or agitate) according to the *rule matha vilodane nandi-grahi-pacadibhyah* - (*Panini* 3.1.134). Thus *kesi-mathana* means he who controls love and disturbs my mind. *Saksan manmatha-manmathah:* he directly appeared as one who can bewilder the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people. (SB 10.32.2)

Or *kesi-mathana* can mean he who agitates even those who control lust, the yogis, by his beauty. What then to speak of agitating a person who is already attracted to him? *Ka* can mean love according to *Anekartha*. *Bhavitaya* in the first explanation mean “developing *rati*" according to *itac tarakaidbhya*. (*Panini* 5.2.36) In the second explanation, *bhavitaya* means attained according to *Visva*.

The chorus has been explained.

Now the rest of the verse is explained. Radha is described: I have gone to the bower house alone. Krsna is described: He remains hidden in the night, alone. He has hidden himself from me to joke with me, thinking, “Radha should not see me” or “others should not see me.”

Radha is further described as follows. She looks in all directions in fear \[and thinks\] “Where is Krsna hiding?” or “No one should see me as I go to meet Krsna.” Her vision is confused. It is said in *Sahgita-ratnakara*:

*ya tu kvacid avisrantam avisahyam vilokane \|*  
*vistirna-cancalotphulla-tara sa drstir ucyate \|\|*

*vibhranta vibhrame vege sambhrame ca bhaved asau*

**Vision which glances about, tirelessly for something invisible, with enlarged, restless pupils, is called** ***vibhranta*****. This means it is confused and agitated.**

Krsna is further described as follows. He laughs, while filled with *srhgara-rasa* mixed with eagerness (*rabhasa*) for enjoyment of love (*rati*), or mixed with with desire (*rabhasa*) for love (*rati*). Thinking “She does not see me, though I am right in front of her. I’m hiding in order to reveal myself, but just waiting for the proper time for play”, he is laughing. *Nibhrtam* means ‘alone’ according to *Dharani.* *Rabhasa* means ‘eagerness’ according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 2.12

*prathama-samagama-lajjitaya*  
*patu-catu-satair anukulam \|*  
*mrdu-madhura-smita-bhasitaya*  
*sithili-krta-jaghana-dukulam \|\|12\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prathama-samagama* - At our first meeting; *lajjitaya* - \[I\] embarrassed; *patu-catu* - of skillful sweet words; *sataih* - with hundreds; *anukulam* - \[he\] bent on conciliation; *mrdu-madhura-smita* - with a soft and sweet smile; *bhasitaya* - \[I\] was addressed; *sithili-krta* - became loose; *jaghana-dukulam* - \[he by whom\] the garment at my \[my\] hips.

**Перевод:**

**“I was embarrassed at our first meeting. He pacified me by hundreds of sweet skillful words. He spoke to me with a soft, sweet smile. My clothes became loose on my hips.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Radha is described in the verse as follows. I was ashamed at our first meeting. Though he is the eternal enjoyer, at the first meeting she was embarrassed by the intense sweetness of rasa within Krsna, which seemed ever fresh. *Bhagavatam* says:

*yadyapy asau parsva-gato raho-gatas*  
*tathapi tasyanghri-yugam navam navam \|*  
*pade pade ka virameta tat-padac*  
*calapi yac chrir najahati karhicit \|\|*

**Though Krsna’s feet were next to them and in private, those feet appeared more and more beautiful at every moment. Which woman could give up those feet which Laksmi never gives up? (SB 1.11.33)**

Women in love are embarrassed at the first meeting and act reluctantly. Bharata says:

*slistah kanthe kim iti na maya mudhaya prananathas*  
*cumbaty asmin vadana-vidhutih kim krta kim na drstah \|*  
*noktah kasmad iti nava-vadhti-cestitam cintayanti*  
*pascat-tapam vahati taruni premni jate rasajna \|\|*

**“Though innocent, I did embrace the neck of my lover. Why? When he kissed me I shook my head. Why did I do this? Why did I not look at him? I did not speak to him.” Thinking of the behavior of a new bride, the young woman, knowing rasa, feels regret at the time love had arisen. (*****Amaru-sataka*** **58)**

Or the meaning can be “I feel embarrassed as with the first meeting.” (This could be another meeting but embarrassing as if it were a first meeting.) Krsna is described: He consoled me with hundreds of polite statements which are skillful at pleasing women. This indicates the hero’s skillful use of words since it is said that she immediately became pacified. The method of pleasing the woman is described:

*prathamam iha hi deyam puspa-pug-adikam syat*  
*mrdu-madhura-vacobhir yojayet sannidhane \|*  
*haratalam atha gatre bhramayec chasvad asyas*  
*churitam udara-prsthe mandam aslisya dadyat \|\|*

**First flowers should be given. One should meet her with soft, sweet words. One should let the hand slide over her limbs quickly. One should embrace her gently on the back. (*****Srngara-viveka*****)**

He is skillful at removing faults:

*mugdham anunayaty esa mrdupayena santvayan \|*  
*natibhiti-karair bhavair nibandhair bala-bhisakaih \|\|*

**The lover consoles the innocent girl, using soft means, without strong hands, without obstructing emotions \[within him such as harshness\], without great force.**

*sarati saras-tirad esa bhramad bhramaravali*  
*sumukhi vimukhi padme manye tavasya pipasaya \|*  
*iti nigadite kincid bhitya vivartita-kandhara*  
*vadana-kamale bala bhartra ciram paricumbita \|\|*

**With brows like a host of bees, she moves from the bank of the lake of enjoyment. He says, “O woman with a beautiful face, О tarnished lotus! I think of you with thirst for your face.” With head turned away, she moves in fear. The young girl is kissed on her lotus face by her husband. (*****Srhgara-tilaka*** **1.37)**

Radha is further described: I was addressed with a smile and soft, sweet words. This means her shame disappeared by his skillful words.

*srutam rasa-visesena hy abhilasam nayec chrutim \|*  
*yan nayika-mukha-gatam mardavam tad udiritam \|*  
*madhuryam ca bhaved yatra sabde’rthe casphuto rasah \|\|*

**The ear yearns for what is desired to be heard, in a pleasing way. There arises sweetness, when the man produces rasa in the meaning and sound of words, which makes the woman’s face very soft.**

Krsna is further described: He loosens the cloth on my hips, knowing that 1 have been pacified by his smile. The belt spontaneously will loosen on seeing Krsna, the crest jewel of unlimited heroes (the belt spontaneously loosened on seeing Krsna and hence the act of loosening is attributed to Krsna). When that happens, the cloth will then fall from her hips.” In *Rati-rahasya*, it is said:

*osthagram sphuratiksane vicalatah kupodare matsyavat*  
*dhammillah kusumakulo vigalati prapnoti bandham punah \|*  
*pracchannau vrajatah stanau prakatatam sroni-tatam drsyate*  
*nivi ca skhalati sthitapi sudrdha kamehgite yositam \|\|*

**While she moves her eyes like a fish in a pool, the edge of her lips quivers. Her hair full of flowers comes undone. She again ties up her hair. When she moves, her covered breasts become visible. Her waist is visible. Her belt slackens though tied tightly, as an indication of love.**

*kante talpam upagate vigalita nivi svayam bandhanad*  
*vaso vislatha-mekhala-guna-dhrtam kirncin nitambe sthitam \|*  
*etavat sakhi vedmi sampratam aham tasyanga-sange punah*  
*ko’yam kasmi ratam nu va katham iti svalpapi me na smrtih \|\|*

**“When the lover comes to the bed, my belt becomes untied. My cloth is held by a slackened cord around my waist. О friend! By this, I understand that I am in his company. Who is he? Who am I? How did this happen? I do not remember anything at all.” (*****Amaru-sataka*** **101)**

The state prior to rati is described:

*trutaty angam sphurad-vaksah vikasan-mukha-pankajam \|*  
*nimilan-nayanam nivi-nabhi-saithilyam eva ca \|\|*

**She tears her limbs. Her chest trembles. Her lotus mouth opens. She closes her eyes and her belt becomes slackened.**

*Catu* means ‘words pleasing to women’ according to *Ratna-mala*. *Jaghana* means the lower portion of the body below the waist according to *Haravali*.

### Text 2.13

*kisala-sayana-nivesitaya*  
*ciram urasi mamaiva sayanam \|*  
*krta-pari rambhana-cumbanaya*  
*parirabhya krtadhara-panam \|\|13\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kisala-sayana* - A bed of tender shoots; *nivesitaya* - \[I\] was placed upon; *ciram* - for a long time; *urasi* - on \[my\] chest; *mamaiva* - only my; *sayanam* - \[he\] having rested; *krta* - were made; *parirambhana-cumbanaya* - \[I\] to whom an embrace and a kiss; *parirabhya* - having embraced \[me\]; *krta* - was made; *adhara-panam* - \[he\] by whom my lower lip was drunk.

**Перевод:**

**“I was placed on a bed of tender shoots. He rested on my chest for a long time. He embraced and kissed me. He embraced me, drinking the nectar of my lips.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Radha is again described: I was placed on a bed of tender buds. Krsna is described: He lay for a long time on my chest. This indicates a special embrace. In *Panca-sayaka* it is said:

*gatropavistad atha talpa-madhye*  
*samliyate yan mithunam sariram \|*  
*kamabhimanaksata-purna-cestam*  
*tat ksira-nirakhyam idam pradistam \|\|*

**When two bodies clinging together on a bed with all their limbs, with continuous effort while absorbed in passion, it is called an embrace (union of milk and water).**

Radha is again described: I was being embraced and kissed. This means a very tight embrace.

Krsna is described: After embracing me, he drank the nectar of my lips. This is called *lalatikam alinganam*. In *Panca-sayaka* it is said:

*anyonya-samsakta-mukham kapolam*  
*netram lalatam hrdayam ca bahyam \|*  
*sananda-bhava-srama-militaksam*  
*lalatikam tattva-vido vadanti \|\|*

**When the lover’s mouths, cheeks, eyes, foreheads, hearts and arms are in close contact, with eyes closed because of fatigue from blissful love, the wise call this** ***lalatika*****.**

*sanketa-talpe ca sukhopavistam*  
*gadham patih slisyati sapi kantam \|*  
*anyonya-gatre visativa ragad*  
*dvandvam tadakrsta-jalabhimanam \|\|*

**The man deeply embraces the woman while seated comfortably on the designated bed. She also embraces him. It seems as if the two bodies have merged into each other out of passion. The two seem to be joined by attraction like water flowing into water (can’t be distinguished).**

*Kisala* means buds according to *Amara-kosa.*

### Text 2.14

*alasa-nimilita-locanaya*  
*pulakavali-lalita-kapolam \|*  
*srama-jala-sakala-kalevaraya*  
*vara-madana-madad atilolam \|\|14\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*alasa* - By the fatigue of love; *nimilita* - were closed; *locanaya* - \[I\] whose eyes; *pulakavali* - with rows of goose bumps; *lalita* -\[is\] attractive; *kapolam* - \[he\] whose cheeks; *srama* - arising from exertion; *jala* - with sweat; *sakala* - \[is\] beautiful; *kalevaraya* - \[I\] whose body; *vara-madana-madad* - because of the great joy produced by Cupid; *atilolam* - \[he\] unsteady.

**Перевод:**

**“My eyes close from the fatigue of love. He develops goose bumps on his cheeks. My body became beautiful with perspiration arising from exertion. He is unsteady because of great joy produced by Cupid.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Radha is further described: My eyes were closed because of fatigue. This is the symptom of love called *angika*:

*yatra murdhanam arabhya pada-paryantikah kriyah \|*  
*angena vihita yasmat tasmad angikam ucyate \|\|*

**When actions extend from the head to the foot it is called angika, since all the limbs are used. (*****Nayika-laksana*****)**

The eyes close from throbbing of the limbs during the pleasure of union. *Sangita-ratnakara* says:

*sphurat-samslista-paksmagra sukha-visranta-taraka \|*  
*mukula drstir anandair ucchrayoh sparsa-gandhayoh \|\|*

**The eyes close, with pupils fatigued from happiness, and the tips of the eyelids quiver and press together because of bliss of increased stimulation of touch and smell.**

Krsna is described: His cheeks are attractive with goose bumps. This is a *sattvika-bhava* (involuntary physical responses to *rati*). Bharata says:

*smarane sangame caiva priyasyalokane tatha \|*  
*hetu-trayam samasadya sattvikah sampravartate \|\|*

***Sattvika-bhavas*** **arise on remembering the lover, seeing the lover and uniting with the lover.**

Radha is further described: My body is beautified (*sakala*) by perspiration arising from fatigue after efforts in love. Another version has *sakala* (drops). Another version has *srama-jala-sikta*: soaked in perspiration.

Krsna is further described: He is unsteady because of great joy produced by Cupid. Or he is very unsteady because of intoxication from intense love. *Vara* means abundant or best according to *Visva*.

The description of perspiration (*srama-jala*) in this verse, describes the *nirakula-pragalbha* (steady and bold) heroine:

*dhanyas tah sakhi yositah priyatame sarvanga-lagne’pi yah*  
*pragalbhyam prathayanti mohana-vidhav alambya dhairyam mahat \|*  
*asmakam tu tadiya-pani-kamale’py unmocayaty amsukam*  
*ko’yam ka vayam atra kim nu suratam naiva smrtir jayate \|\|*

**“O friend! Those women who, taking support of the methods of love, show boldness and great steadiness even when intimately united with their lover, are most fortunate. He unties my garment with his lotus hand. I have no memory of who he is, who I am and whether we made love.” (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.43)**

### Text 2.15

*kokilakala-rava-kujitaya*  
*jita-manasija-tantra-vicaram \|*  
*slatha-kusumakula-kuntalaya*  
*nakha-likhita-ghana-stana-bharam \|\|15\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kokila* - Like \[those of\] the cuckoo; *kala-rava* - soft and sweet \[sounds\]; *kujitaya*- \[I\] making sounds; *jita* - is mastered; *manasija-tantra-vicaram* - \[he\] by whom the art of love; *slatha* - has come undone; *kusumakula* - covered with flowers; *kuntalaya* - \[I\] whose hair; *nakha-likhita* - is marked with \[his\] nails; *ghana-stana* - of \[my\] firm breasts; -*bharam* - \[he\] by whom the weight.

**Перевод:**

**“I make sounds like the cooing of the cuckoo. Krsna has mastered the art of love. My hair covered with flowers has come undone. Krsna has marked my heavy breasts with his nails.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna...”**

**Комментарий:**

Radha is described: I made soft,'sweet (*kola*) sounds (*rava*) like warbling of the cuckoo (*kokila*). Or 1 made sounds like the pigeon (*kala-rava*) and the cuckoo (*kokila*). This description refers to external experiences of love. *Panca-sayaka* says:

*pika-sikhi-kalahamsa-praya-paksi-vrajanam*  
*svanitam anukaroti hy angana manatharta \|*  
*mukha-dasana-vivarta tat kavindra vadanti*  
*stanitam iti samasac citra-sambhoga-kale \|\|*

**The woman makes sounds like the cuckoo, peacock and swan with changes in mouth and teeth. The poets in this way describe the sound at the time of enjoyment.**

*Rasika-sarvasva* says:

*datyuha-lavaka-mayura-kapota-hamsa-*  
*paravatadi-rutavad dhvanitam ratakhye \|*  
*tan-misra-nihsvasita-sitkrta-hani-nadaih*  
*samyojayen madana-cumbana-maithunadau \|\|*

**Sounds like the gallinule, lavaka, peacock, dove, swan or pigeon, combine with decreased breathing and sighing during love making and kissing.**

It is also said:

*kante tatha katham api prathitam mrgaksya*  
*caturyam uddhata-manobhavaya ratesu \|*  
*tat kujitam mrdu vadadbhir aneka-varam*  
*siksayitam grha-kapota-satair yatha syat \|\|*

**The woman uses skill with her lover during love with increased passion. She makes sounds with soft words repeatedly as if teaching repeatedly hundreds of pigeons to coo.**

The sequence is described by Vatsyayana and others.

*aslesam prathamam kuryad dvitiyam cumbanas tatha \|*  
*trtiyam nakha-danam ca damstraghatam caturthakam \|\|*

*pancamam ksepanam proktam sastham praharanam tatha \|*  
*saptamam kantha-sabdas ca bandhanam castamam ratam \|\|*

**First one embraces, then kisses (second). Then one uses the nails (third) and teeth (fourth). Then stroking (fifth) and then striking (sixth). Then one makes guttural sounds (seventh) and finally one unites with the lover (eigth).**

Applying nails and teeth in the seventh stage (making sounds) would interrupt Krsna’s pleasure.Krsna is then described as follows. He has mastered all the arts according to the scriptures on love. It is said:

*caturdasa-vidha bharya sviya sevya para na tu \|*  
*parakiyam api bhajann uttamam eva kamayet \|\|*

*parapi bala madhya ca praudha vrddha caturvidha \|*  
*bala bala-karaslista madhya slista rati-prada \|\|*

*praudha bala-ksaya-kari vrddha pumstva-vinasinl \|\|*

**There are fourteen types of women to be enjoyed as one’s own while not accepting unmarried women who is not one’s own wife. Even in accepting a woman other than one’s wife one should desire the best. Among those women who are not one’s wives there are four types:** ***bala, madhya, praudha,*** **and** ***vrddha*****. The** ***bala*****, is embraced and gives strength to the man. The** ***madhya*** **is embraced and incites his passion. The** ***praudha*** **reduces one’s strength. The** ***vrddha*** **destroys one’s masculinity.**

Among the fourteen varieties including devatas, gandharvas are considered *madhya* and most pleasant. The qualities of the *gandharva* are described:

*udyana-kanana-rata gita-vadya-rata tatha \|*  
*siva-bhakta ca susnata gandharvi sa prakirtita \|\|*

**The** ***gandharva*** **woman is one who frequents gardens, plays musical instruments, is devoted to Siva and bathes frequently.**

Service to her is as follows:

*snatva sugandha-dravais tu sangita-dhvani-purvakam \|*  
*mrdv-alingana-danena gandharvim kamayed budhah \|\|*

**The wise man pleases the** ***gandharva*** **woman by offering soft embraces after he has bathed using perfumed articles, while singing.**

\[Now returning to the explanation of the second line of the verse,\] Krsna is skillful at love making (*jita-manasija-tantra-vicaram*). Or Krsna has reached the state of bliss. Or, Krsna disregards the conclusions of other men who are skillful at love. It is said *saksan manmatha-manmathah*: Krsna bewilders the mind of Cupid, though Cupid himself bewilders the minds of ordinary people - (SB 10.32.2). *Kaminam darsayan dainyam*: he showed the wretchedness of ordinary lusty men and hardhearted women - (SB 10.30.34). Or, it means, Krsna forgets the conclusions of scriptures on love.

The meaning is this: Since Krsna is master of the sixty-four arts, is the supreme hero of *rasa* and is the Supreme Lord, there is no fault if he does not remember the sequence in love-making, since he becomes absorbed in *rasa*, in the presence of the supreme heroine, who is cooperative. This does not destroy *rasa.* It is said *rati-cakre pravrttasya naiva sastram na ca kramah*: there is no specific sequence of actions and no scripture for one engaged in the whirlpool of love - (*Kama-sutra* 6.2.32).

Radha is again described \[in the third line of the verse\]: Her hair, spread with flowers, has become loosened by grasping her hair and kissing her or, her hair has loosened because the flowers have become undone.

Krsna is further described \[in the fourth line\]: He has profusely marked her heavy breasts with his nails. Using the nails in this way is called *pankaja-patra*. It is said in *Panca-sayaka*:

*atyurdhvam ekam sphurito’rdha-rekham*  
*prahuh ksatam pankaja-patra-samjnam \|*  
*dor-mula-vaksah-stana-nabhi-sandhav*  
*asyopayogam manute mahesah \|\|*

**Inflicting one vertical half-line at the base of the arm, the chest, breast or navel is called** ***pankaja-patra*** **(lotus leaf). Siva considers this proper.**

*Tantra* means scriptures, according to *Visva*. *Kuntala* means hair, according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 2.16

*carana-ranita-mani-nupuraya*  
*paripurita-surata-vitanam \|*  
*mukhara-visrnkhala-mekhalaya*  
*sakaca-graha-cumbana-danam \|\|16\|\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*carana* - On \[my\] feet; *ranita -* jingled; *mani-nupuraya* - \[I\] whose ankle bells; *paripurita* - was made complete; *surata-vitanam* - \[he\] by whom the distribution of love; *mukhara* - jingling; *visrnkhala* - was dismantled; *mekhalaya* - \[I\] whose belt; *sakaca-graha* - and the grasping of the hair; *cumbana-danam* - \[he\] to whom there was given a kiss;

**Перевод:**

**Radha’s ankle bells jingled. Krsna distributed love completely. Her jingling belt was dismantled. He is kissed by her while she grasped his hair.**

**\[Radha says,\] “O friend! Make kind Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Until now, seven external types of love-making have been described in five verses. Now the eighth type of love is described.

Radha’s jeweled ankle bells jingled. This shows that Krsna is holding her in a certain way. Or, it indicates that Radha is taking a reverse position in love, called *bhramaraka*.

Krsna is described next: He distributed love completely. This refers to the male predominating. \[Radha thinks,\] “Recognizing that I am tired from taking the *bhramaraka* position, he should give me enjoyment, as I lie on the bed of new shoots.”

Radha is described next: Her belt which previously jingled, now was broken. This describes the position called *prenkholita* (oscillating).

Krsna is described again: He is kissed while his hair is grasped. This also describes a reverse position. It is said:

*spigare cumbanam karyam pumsalingana-purvakam \|*  
*viparita-grahe naryah sakaca-graha-cumbanam \|\|*

**In love making, the man kisses while embracing the woman. In the reverse position, the woman kisses the man, while holding his hair.**

*Mekhala* refers to a belt according to *Visva*. *Srnkhala* means a belt.

### Text 2.17

*rati-sukha-samaya-rasalasaya*  
*dara-mukulita-nayana-sarojam \|*  
*nihsaha-nipatita-tanu-lataya*  
*madhusudanam udita-manojam \|\|17\|\|*

*sakhi he!...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rati* - Of love; *sukha* - of \[attaining\] the satisfaction; *samaya -* at the time; *rasalasaya* -\[I\] indolent because of *srngara-rasa; dara-mukulita* - were slightly closed; *nayana-sarojam* — \[he\] whose lotus eyes; *nihsaha* - powerless; *nipatita* - fell \[upon a bed of lotus shoots\]; *tanu-lataya* - \[I\] whose creeper-like body; *madhusudanam* - Madhusudana (Krsna); *udita* - was aroused \[again\]; *manojam* -whose passion

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha continues,\] “I became indolent, having attained satisfaction in love. He closed his lotus eyes slightly. Being incapable of movement, my creeper-like body fell upon the bed of shoots. His passion again became aroused.**

**“O friend! Make kind Krsna...”**

**Комментарий:**

Radha is described: I became indolent because of *srngara-rasa*, with the highest experience, at the time of attaining satisfaction in love. Krsna is then described: He closed his lotus eyes slightly. This description indicates that both had attained bliss. It is said in

*Rati-rahasya*:

*murchana milanam caksnos cyuti-kalasya laksanam \|*  
*sarvam etat parijnaya sadhayet tat svayam tatah \|\|*

**Perfection is reached when there is fainting, closing the eyes, with symptoms of loss of time.**

Radha is described again: Being incapable of anything else, my creeper-like body fell upon the bed of shoots. This indicates the time after enjoyment. It is said:

*ange svedah slathatvam ca kesa-vastradi-samvrtih \|*  
*jate cyuti-sukhe narya virameccha ca jayate \|\|*

**There is perspiration on her limbs. She feels languid. She rearranges her hair and cloth. With the end of happiness, the woman develops desire for rest. (Bharata)**

After love in reverse position, it is said:

*svecchaya milita-vallabho’thava*  
*yosid acarati purusayitam \|*  
*adito ghatitam eva yantritam*  
*bhupa-tanu-karanam ca cestate \|\|*

**By her desire, she meets her lover and takes the dominant role. From the beginning, she exerts herself. She acts like a king.**

Here is an example:

*atiprayatnena ratanta-tanta*  
*krsnena talpad avaropita sa \|*  
*alambya tasyaiva karam karena*  
*jyotsna-krtanandam alindam ара \|\|*

**Fatigued after love, because of great exertion with Krsna, she left the bed while taking support of his hand and came to the terrace that is made blissful by the moon light. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **2.14)**

Krsna is then described \[in the last line of the verse\]: Madhusudana means a person greedy to taste *madhurya-rasa*. Madhusudana also means a bee. His love has again been aroused (*udita-manojam*) by Radha’s glance, by her shy smile, and by the touch of the edge of her hips and breasts, as she replaced her necklace and belt which had previously fallen off. It is said:

*avasane’pi ca pritir upacarair upasthata \|*  
*sa-visrambha-katha-yogaih ratim janayate param \|\|*

**After finishing love making, affection appears again with various elements. One can later generate love by using intimate talks.**

The poem describes the sweetness of the two. Madhusudana can also mean “One who causes sweetness to flow.” He causes love because of bliss, (*mudita*) which arises from the great sweetness, produced by his skill in *srngara-rasa*. Some explain Madhusudana as the killer of the demon Madhu, to denote Krsna’s capabilities.

### Text 2.18

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam idam ati-*  
*saya-madhu-ripu-nidhuvana-silam \|*  
*sukham utkanthita-gopa-vadhu-*  
*kathitam vitanotu sa-lilam \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam* - Spoken by Sri Jayadeva; *idam* - this; *ati-saya-madhu-ripu* - of the supreme Madhuripu (Madhu’s enemy - Krsna); *nidhuvana* - amorous acts; *silam* - \[and\] describing; *sukham* - happiness; *utkanthita* - filled with longing; *gopa-vadhu* - \[describing\] the wives of cowherds (Radha and the *gopis*), *kathitam* - composition; *vitanotu* - may it spread; *sa-lilam* - along with the activities arising from *srngara-rasa*

**Перевод:**

**May this composition spoken by Jayadeva, describing Radha and the gopis filled with longing, and with the amorous actions of Krsna along with the activities arising from srngara-rasa, give happiness to you all. (END OF SONG SIX)**

**Комментарий:**

May what is described here by the poet Jayadeva, concerning Radha and the *gopis* who had great longing, produce happiness. Let the readers hear this. It is filled with descriptions of Krsna’s intimate pastimes (*nidhuvana*), along with the activities arising from *srngara-rasa* (*sa-lilam*). *Nidhuvana* means amorous acts according to *Amara-kosa*. *Kathitam* expresses a state.

### Text 2.19

*hasta-srasta-vilasa-vamsam anrju-bhru-vallimad-ballavi-*  
*vrndotsari-drganta-viksitam atisvedardra-ganda-sthalam \|*  
*mam udviksya vilajjitam smita-sudha-mugdhanana-kanane*  
*govindam vraja-sundari-gana-vrtam pasyami hrsyami ca \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hasta* - From his hand; *srasta* - is fallen; *vilasa-vamsam* - whose pastime-flute; *anrju-bhru* - crooked eyebrows; *vallimat* - like a creeper; *ballavi* - of cowherdesses (*gopis*); *vrndotsari* - repel the group; *drganta -* from the corner of his eyes; *viksitam* - whose glance; *atisvedardra* - is very wet with perspiration; *ganda-sthalam* - the surface of whose cheeks; *mam* - me; *udviksya* - having seen; *vilajjitam* - ashamed; *smita-sudha* - because of the brightness of his smile; *mugdhanana* - with an attractive face; *kanane* - in the forest; *govindam* - Govinda (Krsna); *vraja-sundari* - of cowherdesses (*gopis*); *gana* - with a group; *vrtam* - \[and\] surrounded; *pasyami* - I see; *hrsyami* - I rejoice; *ca* - and;

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha speaks to her sakhi,\] “I saw Govinda in the forest surrounded by other gopis, his attractive face with a bright smile and his cheeks covered with perspiration. When he saw me, he was ashamed and he glanced sideways to repel the group of gopis who have crooked, creeper-like brows. The pastime-flute fell from his hand. Thus 1 rejoice.**

**Комментарий:**

When Radha is led by her *sakhi,* she sees Krsna surrounded by a group of *gopis*. Turning back, she speaks to her *sakhi*.

“O friend’. I see Govinda in the forest and have become excited.” The present tense is used because she just saw him. He was surrounded by a group of *gopis*.

“If you saw him with your competitors, why do you rejoice?”

“Seeing me, he became ashamed. He thinks, ‘She saw me with other women.’ He had an attractive face because of his splendid, bright smile. Though surrounded by many women, he understood he had offended me and was afraid of me. Thus I rejoice. Out of fear he was driving away the group of gopis who had crooked eyebrows, by the glance from the edge of his eye, since I had come.”

He is further described: His pastime-flute had fallen from his hand out of fear on seeing me. His cheeks were moist with profuse perspiration, on seeing me everywhere. The phrase ‘*gopis* with creeper-like crooked brows’ indicates their beauty. Another meaning is, by his glance he drove away the group of *gopis* whose creeper brows became crooked on seeing my superiority. Or, with his crooked creeper brow, he glanced to drive away the group of *gopis* - this indicates his beauty, with brows dancing in a pleasing way. Or, he gave a side glance which spread out widely (*utsari*) to the *gopis* with crooked brows, to show them his mood. Or, on seeing my qualities, he gave side glances. But those glances were spreading upwards (*utsan*) (perhaps to avoid the glances of *gopis*), since other *gopis* were showing crooked brows (he was trying to hide his love). On seeing the best heroine endowed with all qualities, *rasa* with its *angas* arose in Krsna.

Radha and Krsna are the *alambana-vibhava* (shelter and object of rati or love). The *sthayi-bhava* (permanent emotion) is *rati* with favorable and unfavorable elements. The forest and things are the *uddipana-vibhava* (stimuli of *rati*). The *anubhavas* (voluntary physical responses to *rati*) are smiling and glancing: his attractive face had a bright smile and he glanced with the edge of his eye. The *sattvika-bhavas* (involuntary physical responses to *rati*) are perspiration and trembling: he perspired profusely. *Suddipta-sattvika-bhava* is shown when his trembling caused the flute to fall from his hand. Alarm is the *vyabhicari-bhava* (transitory emotion). Shame and fear are also *vyabhicari-bhavas*. The great poet has described the king of all *rasas* - *srngara*, along with its angas (*vibhava* etc) through which Krsna responded to Radha - the queen of Vrndavana, endowed with the highest qualities. What more can be said? The hero is *anukula* (favorable). It is said:

*atiraktataya naryam tyaktanya-lalana-sprhah \|*  
*sitayam ramavat so’yam anukulah praklrtitah \|\|*

*radhayam eva krsnasya suprasiddhanukulata \|*  
*tad-aloke kadapy asya nanya-sahgah smrtim vrajet \|\|*

**He who gives up desire for other women and has intense attachment for one woman, like Rama for Sita, is called the** ***anukula*** **or well-disposed lover. Krsna is famous for his** ***anukula*** **attachment to Radha. Whenever he sees her, he forgets other women. (*****Ujjvala-nllamani*** **1.25-26)**

An example is given:

*vaidagdhi-nikuramba-cumbita-dhiyah saudnarya-sarojjvalah*  
*kaminyah kati nadya vallava-pater divyanti gosthantare*  
*radhe punyavati-sikha-manir asi ksamodari tvam vina*  
*prenkhanti na parasu yan madhoripor drstatra drstir maya*

**\[Vrnda - the controller over forests for pastimes, speaks\] “O Radha! So many women whose minds are filled with playfulness, whose bodies shine with the essence of beauty, do not play in the courtyard of Nanda at this time. You are the crest jewel of pious women. О slender-waisted girl! I have never seen the glances of Krsna fall upon other women when he is separated from you.” (*****Ujjvala-nllamani*** **1.27)**

The ornament is called *preyas*. This is explained by Dandi: *priti-prakasanam yat tat preya ity abhidhiyate* - *Preyas* refers to the manifestation of affection - (*Kavyadarsa* 2.297).

### Text 2.20

*duraloka-stoka-stavakamavakasoka-latika-*  
*vikasah kasaropavana-pavano’pi vyathayati \|*  
*api bhramyad-bhrngi-ranita-ramaniya na mukula-*  
*prasutis cutanam sakhi sikhariniyam sukhayati \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*duraloka* - Is seen with pain; *stoka-stavaka* - on which there is a small clusters of flowers; *navakasoka-latika* - of the new \[pitiable\] asoka branch; *vikasah* - the blossoming; *kasaropavana* - from the pond in the garden; *pavana* - the wind; *api* - also; *vyathayati* -gives pain; *api* - \[and\] even; *bhramyad-bhrngi* - of the female bees; *ranita* - with the humming; *ramaniya* - pleasant; *na* - \[does\] not; *mukula-prastitih* - the appearance of buds; *cutanam* - of the mango trees; *sakhi* - О friend!; *sikhariniyam -* on Govardhana (or having beautiful tips); *sukhayati* - give happiness.

**Перевод:**

**“O friend! The small, blossoming clusters of buds on the new, pitiable asoka branch are seen with pain. The wind from the pond in the garden gives pain. The appearance of buds on the mango tree, pleasant with humming of female bees, on Govardhana, does not give happiness.”**

**Комментарий:**

O friend! The opening of the small clusters of buds on the very new, pitiable *asoka* branch is seen with pain (*duraloka*). *Navaka*, meaning very new, is derived from *nava* according to the rule *ke ‘nah* - *Panini* 7.4.13. *Latika* means a pitiable branch according to the rule *anukampayam* - *Panini* 5.3.76. The *pitiable* new *asoka* branch, held in my hand, is seen with pain.

The wind from the pond in the garden gives pain. Mention of the pond indicates that the wind is very cool. Mention of the garden indicates that the wind is fragrant and indolent. The wind is slowed by the obstacles created by the trees \[in the garden\]. Or, the whole first compound (*duraloka-stoka-stavaka-navakasoka-latika-vikasah*) can modify *pavanah*: the wind from the pond in the garden, which makes the small clusters on the new *asoka* branch bloom, gives me pain.

“Come forward and the wind can be avoided by your shawl.”

“The wind is not visible (*duraloka*). It arises suddenly.”

The word *api* (in the second line of the verse) indicates that even the blossoming *asoka* branch gives pain.

The appearance of buds on the mango tree on Govardhana (*sikharini*) does not give joy. Who knows that it is in the state of blossoming? What will it do? Or, the appearance of buds having beautiful tips (*sikharini*) gives pain. The suffix *‘in'* is added to *sikhara* to indicate praise. O*r sikharini* can mean jasmine: the jasmine does not give joy, though they are pleasant with the humming of female bees. Mention of female bees suggests that they are looking for a male bee. Sikharini also indicates a poetic meter. *Stoka* means small according to *Visva.* *Stavaka* means a bunch according to *Amara-kosa*. *Lata* means a branch according to *Visva*. *Kasara* means a pond according to *Amara-kosa*. *Sikharini* means jasmine according to *Visva*.

### Text 2.21

*sakuta-smitam akulakula-galad-dhammillam ullasita-*  
*bhru-vallikam alika-darsita-bhuja-mulardha-hasta-stanam \|*  
*gopinam nibhrtam niriksya gamitakanksas ciram cintayann*  
*antar mugdha-manoharam haratu vah klesam navah kesavah \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakuta-smitam* - The smiling with a purpose; *akulakula* - completely undone; *galat* - \[and\] falling down; *dhammillam* - the ornamented hair; *ullasita-bhru-vallikam -* the raised brows; *alika* - on the pretext \[of yawning\]; *darsita* - revealed; *bhuja-mula* - the shoulders; *ardha-hasta* - the forearms; *stanam* - \[and\] the breasts; *gopinam* - of the \[other\] *gopis;* *nibhrtam* - secretly; *niriksya* - \[and\] having glanced at; *gamita* - is for Radha; *kanksas* - whose desire; *ciram cintayan* - thinking for a long time; *antah* - internally; *mugdha-manoharam* - \[and\] the attracting of the minds of innocent people; *haratu* -may \[he\] destroy; *vah* - your; *klesam* - sufferings; *navah* - youthful; *kesavah* - Kesava (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**May youthful Kesava, with desire for Radha, thinking for a long time and glancing secretly at the gopis who, with \[some\] intention smiled slightly, with hair completely undone, with raised brows, showing their shoulders on the pretext of yawning, which could attract the innocent, destroy your sufferings.**

**Комментарий:**

May Krsna dispel your suffering! He is described as follows. He has desire for Radha (*gamitakanksah*) or on seeing Radha has lost all desire for other gopis. What does he do? He thought for a long time internally while glancing secretly (*nibhrtam*). Or he thought for a long time internally while glancing at the revelation of emotions of the *gopis*. Why did he glance? He glanced at the group of *gopis* who smiled slightly with a purpose (*sakuta-smitam*), whose hair can come completely undone because of intense desire for him, with hairs standing on end. They had raised brows (*ullasita-bhru-vallikam*). They used some pretext like yawning to reveal portions of their upper chest and shoulder. These actions would steal the minds of innocent people.

Krsna is described as having youth (*navah*). Seeing his youth, women develop desire. Or, on seeing Radha he became new (*navah*), as if never seen previously by others. This shows his manifestation of amazing qualities. Navah can also mean “he who is praised with qualities of a hero.” *Navah* can also mean ever fresh. He is experienced as if never experienced before, though he is constantly experienced. An example is given:

*kula-varatanu-dharma-grava-vrndani bhindan*  
*sumukhi nisita-dirghapanga-tanka-cchatabhih*  
*yugapad ayam apurvah kah puro visva-karma*  
*marakata-mani-laksair gostha-kaksam cinoti \|\|*

**\[Radha says to Lalita\] “O beautiful-faced one, who is this creative person standing before us? With the sharp chisels of his loving glances, he is splitting the hard stones of many women’s devotion to their husbands. And with the luster of his body, surpassing the brilliance of countless emeralds, he is simultaneously constructing private meeting places for his pastimes.” (*****Lalita-madhava*** **- Act One)**

If *nibhrtam* means “revealing emotions”, then the four phrases describing the *gopis* expand upon the emotions. They acted attractively to bewilder others (*mugdha-manoharam*).

The title of this chapter *Aklesa-kesava* means “He who destroys the suffering of the *gopis*" or “Because of his joy on seeing Radha, he has his suffering destroyed.”

## Chapter Three. Mugdha-madhusudanah - Attractive Madhusudana

### Text 3.1

*kamsarir api samsara-vasana-bandha-srnkhalam \|*  
*radham adhaya hrdaye tatyaja vraja-sundarih \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kamsarir* - The enemy of Kamsa (Krsna); *api* - even; *samsara* - of *madhurya-rasa* (or of worldly things); *vasana* - with impressions; *bandha* - for binding \[him\]; *srnkhalam* - a chain; *radham* - Radha; *adhaya* - having taken; *hrdaye* - to \[his\] heart; *tatyaja* - abandoned; *vraja-sundarih* - the \[other\] cowherdesses (*gopis*).

**Перевод:**

**Krsna, taking Radha into his heart, because she was a chain binding him with impressions of madhurya-rasa, gave up the other gopis.**

**Комментарий:**

Radha’s longing for Krsna was described in chapter two. Now, Krsna’s longing for Radha is described. Krsna, enemy of Kamsa, gave up the other *gopis* since he had placed Radha in his heart. This indicates Krsna’s intense attraction for Radha. Radha is described as follows. She acted as a chain to bind him with impressions. This binding develops in stages (*samsara*).

This verse indicates the first stage called *abhilasa*. Its characteristics were described previously - (commentary of GG 1.27). It is also said:

*sangamopaya-racita-prarabdha-vyavasayatah \|*  
*sankalpeccha-samudbhutir abhilaso’tra vikriyah \|\|*

*pravesa-nirgamau tusnim tad-drsti-patha-gaminau \|*  
*raga-prakasana-paras cestah svatma-prasadhanam \|*  
*vyajoktayas ca vijane sthitir ity evam adayah \|\|*

**In the stage of** ***abhilasa*****, a person makes a resolve to find a way of meeting. The desire to make a decision manifests. He goes outside and stands silently looking at the path where the lover should appear. He performs actions which reveal his attachment: he dresses up, speaks with double meaning, and remains in a solitary place. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **2.379-380)**

It is understood that such stages (such as going outside, dressing up, speaking with double meaning, etc) occurred previously as well along with their effects, since *anuraga* acts as the first cause of deep conjugal love and produces intense sweetness in *prema*.

Or *samsara* can mean the complete (*sam*) essence (*sara*), which is *madhurya-rasa*, full of bliss since the Lord is its subject. Thus Radha was like a chain for binding him to eternal impressions of *madhurya-rasa*.

*ananda-cinmaya-rasa-pratibhavitabhis*  
*tabhir ya eva nija-rupataya kalabhih \|*  
*goloka eva nivasaty akhilatma-bhuto*  
*govindam adi-purusam tarn aham bhajami \|\|*

**I worship the Supreme Lord Govinda who, though regarding all the inhabitants as his very self, resides in Goloka exclusively with the young** ***gopis*****, who are embodiments of** ***madhurya-rasa*****. He accepts them as his wives, while they respond to his conjugal affection reciprocally. (*****Brahma-samhita*** **5.37)**

Or *samsara* can indicate Radha, the final goal of all his efforts. Thus she was a chain for controlling him by making him continually think (*vasana*) about her (*samsara*). The word *kamsarih* (Kamsa’s enemy) also means “he who distributes (*sarati*) happiness (*kam*).” Since Kamsa feared him, the name also shows Krsna’s abilities. It is said *bhayat kamsah*: out of fear, Karhsa obtained Krsna’s mercy - (SB 7.1.31). *Srnkhala* means chains according to *Visva*.

### Text 3.2

*itas tatas tam anusrtya radhikam*  
*ananga-bana-vrana-khinna-manasah \|*  
*krtanutapah sa kalinda-nandini-*  
*tatanta-kunje visasada madhavah \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*itas tatas* - Then; *tam* - for her; *anusrtya* - having searched; *radhikam* - for Radha; *ananga* - of the Cupid; *bana* - of the arrows; *vrana* -with the wounds; *khinna* - was oppressed; *manasah* - whose mind; *krtanutapah* - by whom remorse was made; *sa* - he; *kalinda-nandini* - of the Yamuna River; *tatanta* - on the edge of the bank; *kunje* - in a grove; *visasada* - lamented; *madhavah* - Madhava (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**After searching here and there for Radha, in a bower (a hut made of creepers on the edge of the bank of the Yamuna), Krsna, remorseful, lamented. His mind was in pain because of the wounds inflicted by arrows of Cupid.**

**Комментарий:**

What else did Krsna do? In a bower on the edge of the bank of Yamuna, in a hut made of creepers, he lamented. Madhava means the husband (*dhava*) of Laksmi (*ma*). This indicates his pain in separation and the great good fortune of Radha. What had he done before this? Here and there he had searched for Radha, fixed in his heart, giving up his associates at their place where they were to meet. His mind was in pain because of the wounds inflicted by arrows of Cupid. And he was repentant. “Why did I not pacify her before this? How is it possible to exist without her now?” The word *ananga* (Cupid) suggests that this could not be avoided. This verse describes the second stage called *cinta* (anxiety). Its characteristics were described previously - (GG 1. 27).

**\|\| Song Seven \|\|**

*gurjari-raga, yati-tala*

**The song is sung in the raga of gurjari and in the tala of yati.**
