Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *gurjari* and the *tala* is *pratimantha*. Both have already been explained.

### Text 3.3

*mam iyam calita vilokya vrtam vadhu-nicayena \| saparadhataya mayapi na varita'tibhayena \|\|3\|\|*

*hari hari hatadarataya gata sa kupiteva \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mam* - Me; *iyam* - she; *calita* - departed; *vilokya* - having seen; *vrtam* - surrounded; *vadhu-nicayena* - with a group of women (*gopis*); *saparadhataya* - Because of \[my\] being an offender; *mayapi* - even by me; *na* - \[she could\] not \[be\]; *varita’* - stopped; *tibhayena* - because of \[my\] great fear.

*hari-hari* - О what misfortune! (or О Krsna! О Krsna!) *hatadarataya* - Because \[she\] has lost \[her\] respect; *gata* - has gone; *sa* - she; *kupiteva* - \[and\] is angered.

**Перевод:**

**\[Krsna speaks,\] “Seeing me surrounded by other gopis, she went away. She could not be stopped, because I was fearful, having committed offense.**

**“Ah! She is angry because she lost great respect, and she left, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The meaning of the song is as follows. “O what misfortune (*hari hari*)! Radha, dearer than millions of my lives, with famous qualities, has become angry because of losing great respect. She had become angry as a quarrel in jest, since there cannot be complete anger within love.” This is the *vyabhicari-bhava* (transitory emotions) called anger. This is the chorus.

“How do you know that she is angry?”

“Seeing me surrounded by all the *gopis*, she went away.”

The verb *vilokya* indicates that right after seeing this, because of this, she left. “Thus I know she is angry.”

“Why did you not stop her from leaving?”

“Because of great fear, I could not stop her. How could I speak in front of her?”

The cause of the fear was his offense. The offense was to participate with other *gopis* in play without Radha.

### Text 3.4

*kim karisyati kim vadisyati sa ciram virahena \|*  
*kith dhanena janena kim mama jivitena grhena \|\|4\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim* - What; *karisyati* - will \[she\] do? *kim* - What; *vadisyati* - will \[she\] say? *sa* - she; *ciram* - for a long time; *virahena* - because of separation; *kim* - What is the use; *dhanena* - of wealth? *janena* - of \[other\] women; *kim* - What is the use? *mama* - my; *jivitena* - \[What is the use of my\] life? *grhena* - \[What is the use of\] a house?

**Перевод:**

**“What will she do in long separation from me? What will she say to me when we meet? What is the use of wealth? What is the use of other women? What is the use of my life? What is the use of a house?**  
**“Ah! She is angry...**

**Комментарий:**

What will she do in separation from me for a long time? By what method, will she alleviate her pain? He disregards his own pain and is overcome by her pain. This shows his great love. For instance it is said:

*yat te sujata-caranamburuham stanesu*  
*bhitah sanaih priya dadhimahi karkasesu*  
*tenatavim atasi tad vyathate na kim svit*  
*kurpadibhir bhramati dhir bhavad-ayusam nah*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “O dearly beloved! Your lotus feet are so soft that we place them gently on our breasts, fearing that your feet will be hurt. Our life rests only in you. Our minds, therefore, are filled with anxiety that your tender feet might be wounded by pebbles as you roam about on the forest path.” (SB 10.31.19)**

Or, knowing that I suffer from separation for a long time, will she perhaps accept me? *Kim* indicates conjecture in this case. In this case, even a moment’s separation appears as a long time. It is said:

*gopinam paramananda asid govinda-darsane*  
*ksanam yuga-satam iva yasam yena vinabhavat*

**The young** ***gopis*** **took the greatest pleasure in seeing Govinda, since for them even a moment without his association seemed like a hundred ages. (SB 10.19.16)**

This is experienced in *mahdbhava*.

What will she say to me? Seeing me falling at her feet to beg forgiveness for my offense, what words of scolding will she give?

Or, will she speak good news to me, with sweet words?

Or, will she speak soothing words to me or point out my faults?

Or, when I meet with her and speak skillfully, will she become angrier? Will she use rough words?

Or, will she give up anger and, being pleased, speak sweet words? Such thoughts are described in *Hari-vamsa* as well. Jayadeva thus describes the many actions of Krsna, the very form of pastimes which yield a taste of *rasa*. One should not think these explanations are imaginary.

“Why be so eager for her? You should change your heart by cultivating other *gopis*."

“Without her, what is the use of other *gopis* (*kim janena*)? What use do I have for all the wealth of my father Nanda, lord of Vraja (*kim dhanena*)? I have no use at all for it. Without her, I reject all faces gorgeously decorated, since it will bring me no satisfaction. She alone is my ornament and my wealth.”

It will be said later *tvam asi mama jivanam*: you are my life (in GG 10.4).

“But can you be happy in the house without wealth?”

“What is the use of a house?”

“Can you not be happy without her?”

“What is the use of my happiness? What is the use of any happiness without her? She is the form of my happiness” or, “what is the use of happiness related to myself since every woman who gives me happiness will only give suffering to me, since I experience her suffering. If she is happy then I am happy.” Instead of *sukhena* (what is the use of my happiness?) sometimes *jivitena* (what is the use of my life?) is seen: What is the use of my living? What is my life, since I cannot tolerate her suffering? My life is worthless.This verse describes the third stage called *smrti* (remembrance). It is described as follows in *Rasarnava-sudhakara*:

*arthanam anubhutanam desa-kalanuvartinam \|*  
*santatyena paramarso manasah syad anusmrtih \|\|*

*tatranubhava nihsvaso dhyanam krtya-vihastata \|*  
*sayyasanadi-vidvesa ity adyah smara-kalpitah \|\|*

**Remembrance of objects and experiences at various places and times, takes place continually in the mind. In that condition, there are experiences, heavy breathing, meditation, giving up duties, and dislike of beds and chairs. This is all produced because of love. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **2.385-386)**

### Text 3.5

*cintayami tad-ananam kutila-bhru kopa-bharena \|*  
*sona-padmam ivopari-bhramatakulam bhramarena \|\|5\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*cintayami* - I think about; *tad-ananam* - her (Radha’s) face; *kutila-bhru* - whose eyebrow is frowning; *kopa-bharena* - because of the intensity of \[her\] anger; *sona-padmam* - the red lotus; *iva* - like; *upari* - above \[it\]; *bhramatakulam bhramarena* - with a swarm of hovering bees.

**Перевод:**

**“I think of Radha with face like a red lotus arising from her intense anger, with a frowning brow like a swarm of bees hovering above.**

**“Ah! She is angry...**

**Комментарий:**

Though he is overcome by her sorrow, he becomes absorbed in thinking solely of her, though she is angry. This shows the excellence of his love.

I remember her face, which is like a red lotus, flushed with intense anger. The redness in her face, due to anger, is compared to a red lotus. Above the lotus were hovering bees. Her eyebrows are compared to bees. This verse represents the fourth stage called *guna-kirtana* (glorifying qualities).

*anga-pratyanga-lilabhir vak-cesta-hasiteksanaih \|*  
*na tasya sadrsah kascid ity-adi-guna-kirtanam \|\|*

***Guna-klrtana*** **consists of saying that nothing can equal to the grace of lover’s limbs, her glances, smile, actions and words.**

*Sona* means the color of the red lotus according to *Amara-kosa*. This shows her infallible beauty since anger can reveal beauty. This is called *madhurya*. It is said:

*madhuryam nama cestanam sarvavasthasu caruta \|\|*

***Madhuryam*** **means beauty of actions in all circumstances. (*****Ujjvala-nilaman*****i 11.19)**

### Text 3.6

*tam aham hrdi sangatam anisam bhrsam ramayami \|*  
*kim vane’nusarami tam iha kim vrtha vilapami \|\|6\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tam* - Her; *aham* - I; *hrdi* - into \[my\] heart; *sangatam* - having come; *anisam* - constantly; *bhrsam* - fervently; *ramayami* - \[I\] enjoy; *kim* - Why \[do\]; *vane* - in the forest; *’nusarami* - I follow; *tam -* her; *iha* -here? *kim* - Why \[do\]; *vrtha* - uselessly; *vilapami* - I lament?

**Перевод:**

**“Why do I follow her in the forest? Why do I uselessly lament? I enjoy constantly with her in my heart.**

**“Ah! She is angry...**

**Комментарий:**

Why do I pursue for her in the forest? Here in the forest why do I uselessly lament? I play fervently (*bhrsam*) with her constantly in my heart. This is a *sphurti* of union during separation. It is a transformation in *mahabhava.*

Or the verse means, “Should I play wiht her constantly in my heart or should I pursue her in the forest? Uselessly I lament.”

### Text 3.7

*tanvi khinnam asuyaya hrdayam tavakalayami \|*  
*tan na vedmi kuto gatasi natena te’nunayami \|\|7\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tanvi* - О slender-waisted woman (Radha)! *khinnam* - \[is\] pained; *asuyaya* - with indignation \[because of finding fault in me\]; *hrdayam* - heart; *tava* - your; *akalayami* - I know; *tan* - Therefore; *na* - \[do\] not; *vedmi* - I know; *kuto* - where; *gatasi* - you have gone; *natena* - by offering respects \[to the ground\]; *te’* - you; *nunayami* - I conciliate.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha with slender waist! I know your heart is pained by finding fault in me. I do not know where you have gone. Therefore I conciliate you by offering respects on the ground.**

**“Ah! She is angry...**

**Комментарий:**

О woman with slender waist! I know (*akalayami*) your heart is pained because of finding fault in me (*asuyaya*). This sentence shows the fifth stage called *udvega* (distress). It is said in (*Srngara-tilaka*):

*yasmin ramyam aramyam va na ca harsaya jayate \|*  
*pradvesah pranitavye’pi sa udvegah smrto yatha \|\|*

***Udvega*** **refers to the state in which both pleasant and unpleasant situations do not give rise to joy since she despises her lover. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.11)**

This state of distress is also inferred in Radha. Therefore he addresses her as slender (*tanvi*), since she has become thin because of mental pain.

\[Meaning of the second line.\] I do not know where you have gone. I conciliate you with obeisances (*natena*). Or, seeing the path you took, by following that (*tena*), I will conciliate you. The word *na* would indicate conjecture. The word *te* as a genitive indicates relationship. This sentence shows the sixth stage called *vilapa* (lamentation). It is said in (*Srngara-tilaka*):

*bambhramiti mano yasmin ratyautsukyad itas tatah \|*  
*vacah priyasrita eva sa pralapah smrto yatha \|\|*

***Pralapa*** **(or** ***vilapa*****) is described as speaking words in which the mind constantly wanders here and there because of eagerness for love. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.12)**

### Text 3.8

*drsyase purato gatagatam eva me vidadhasi \|*  
*kim pureva sa-sambhramam parirambhanam na dadasi \|\|8\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*drsyase* - You are seen; *purato* - in front; g*atagatam* - coming and going; *eva* - only; *me* - of me; *vidadhasi* - You perform, *kim* - Why; *pureva* - as previously; *sa-sambhramam* - with respect; p*arirambhanam* - an embrace; *na* - \[do\] not; *dadasi* - you give?

**Перевод:**

**“I see you in front of me. You come and go. Why do you not respectfully embrace me as you did previously?**

**“Ah! She is angry...**

**Комментарий:**

О beautiful Radha! Why do you not give embraces as previously (*pura iva*), with respect or on a pretense of fright (*sa-sambhramam*)? The word *sa-sambhramam* hints at the *anubhava* called *bibboka*. On embracing the lover, the woman makes a show of indifference. It is said:

*bibboko mana-garvabhyam syad abhiste’py anadarah \|\|*

**Disrespect towards the lover or articles given by the lover out of anger or pride is called** ***bibboka*****. (*****Ujjvala-nllamani*** **11.52)**

Here is an example:

*priyokti-laksena vipaksa-sannidhau*  
*svikaritam pasya sikhanda-maulina \|*  
*syamdtivama hrdayangamam api*  
*srajam daraghraya nirasa helaya \|\|*

**\[Out of pride:\] “Look! Syama has accepted a garland offered by Krsna who speaks to her with many words in the presence of her rivals. Contrary Syama, though pleased with it, smells it slightly, and then throws it away indifferently.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.53)**

Here is another example.

*sphuraty agre tisthan sakhi tava mukha-ksipta-nayanah*  
*pratiksam krtvayam bhavad-avasarasyagha-damanah \|*  
*drsoccair gambhirya-grathita-guru-hela-gahanaya*  
*hasantiva ksive tvam iha vanamalam racayasi \|\|*

**\[Syamala speaks to Radha:\] “Krsna stands before you, gazing at your face, and waiting for an opportunity. О intoxicated girl! You seem to mock him with a glance, like a garland of great neglect strung with flowers of inscrutability, and you continue to string a flower garland with your hands.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.54)**

\[The meaning of the first line:\] I see you before me. You come and go (*gatagatam*). Persons experiencing separation see the beloved everywhere because of association in the heart. An example is given:

*prasade sa disi disi ca sa prsthatah sa*  
*purah sa paryanke sa pathi pathi ca sa tad-viyogaturasya \|*  
*hamho cetah prakrtir apara nasti me kapi sa sa*  
*sa sa sa sa jagati sakale ko’yam advaita-vadah \|\|*

**The person in separation sees his beloved on the terrace, in all directions, behind, in front, on the couch, on the path. “Oh heart! There is no other woman for me in the whole universe. Why \[you\] talk of all being the same? \[She is special for me and so everyone is not same\]” (*****Amaru-sataka*** **102)**

### Text 3.9

*ksamyatam aparam kadapi tavedrsam na karomi \|*  
*dehi sundari darsanam mama manmathena dunomi \|\[9\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ksamyatam* - Forgive \[me\]; *aparam* - Again; *kadapi* - ever; *tava* -against you; *idrsam* - such \[an act\]; *na* - not; *karomi* - I will commit; *dehi* - Give \[me\]; *sundari* - о beautiful woman (Radha); *darsanam* - the sight \[of you\]; *mama* - \[as a mercy\] to me; *manmathena* - From love; *dunomi* - I am suffering.

**Перевод:**

**“Please forgive my offense. I will never again commit such an act. О beautiful Radha! Please reveal yourself as mercy to me. I am suffering from love.**

**“Ah! She is angry...”**

**Комментарий:**

О beautiful Radha, who is pleasing to see (*sundari*)! Please forgive my offense. I will never again commit such offense (*aparam*). This indicates the seventh stage called *unmada* (madness). In *Rasarnava-sudhakara* it is said *atasmims tad iti bhrantir unmade virahodbhavah*: *unmada* arising from separation produces illusion, so that an object appears differently from what it really is.

Then he recovers his senses a little. О beautiful Radha! Please let me see you. *Mama* indicates relationship in the genitive. Show yourself to me as mercy to me (*mama*), though I am unqualified. By addressing her as beautiful, he tries to encourage her. I suffer because of love.

### Text 3.10

*varnitam jayadevakena harer idam pranatena\|*  
*kindubilva-samudra-sambhava-rohini-ramanena \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*varnitarh* - Was described; *jayadevakena* - by Jayadeva; *harer* - of Krsna; *idam* - this \[description\]; *pranatena* - surrendered \[to the lord\]; *kindubilva* - of Kindubilva village; *samudra* - from the ocean; *sambhava* - which arises; *rohini-ramanena* - \[like\] the moon.

**Перевод:**

**Offering respects to the Lord, Jayadeva, like the moon arising from the ocean of Kindubilva village, has written this description of Krsna. (END OF SONG SEVEN)**

**Комментарий:**

*Jayadevaka* means the same as Jayadeva. This story of Krsna is related by Jayadeva who is surrendered to the Lord (*pranatena*). Another version has *pravanena* (by Jayadeva who is dedicated to the Lord). The poet is like the moon (*rohini-ramanena*) which arises from the ocean of Kindubilva, the ancestral town of Jayadeva. As the full moon increases the ocean, Jayadeva has increased the fame of his family. The word Kindubilva indicates Jayadeva’s ancestors. *Rohini-ramana* means the moon according to *Haravali*. The phrase also indicates the excellence of Jayadeva’s wife Padmavati.

### Text 3.11

*hrdi bisa-lata-haro nayam bhujangama-nayakah*  
*kuvalaya-dala-sreni kanthe na sa garala-dyutih \|*  
*malayaja-rajo nedam bhasma priya-rahite mayi*  
*prahara na hara-bhrantya’nanga krudha kim и dhavasi \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hrdi -* On \[my\] chest; *bisa-lata* - of water lilies (water-lily stems); *haro* - \[is\] a necklace; *nayam* - It \[is\] not; *bhujangama -* of the snakes; nayakah - the king \[Vasuki\]; *kuvalaya* - Of the blue lotus; *dala* - of petals; *sreni* - a string; *kanthe* - \[is\] on \[my\] throat; *na sa* - It \[is\] not; *garala* - of poison; *dyutih* - the \[blue\] streak \[in Siva’s throat\]; *malayaja* - Of sandalwood; *rajo* - the dust \[is on my limbs\]; *nedam* - It \[is\] not; *bhasma* - the ashes \[smeared on Siva’s body\]; *priya* - From \[my\] love; *rahite* - separated; *mayi* - me; *prahara* -beat; *na* - \[do\] not; *hara* - for Siva; *bhrantya* - because of mistaking \[me\]; *ananga* - о Cupid; *krudha* - out of anger, *kim u* - Perhaps; *dhavasi* - you pursue \[me\].

**Перевод:**

**\[Krsna speaks,\] “\[O Cupid!\] Separated from my love, do not beat me out of anger. Perhaps you pursue me out of anger, thinking I am Siva. On my chest is a necklace of water lily stems, not the snake Vasuki. On my throat is a string of blue lotuses, not the blue streak of poison which is in Siva’s throat. I have sandalwood dust on my limbs, not the ashes that are smeared on Siva’s body.**

**Комментарий:**

Cupid alone is the cause of my grief. I will scold him. With that intention Krsna speaks.

О Cupid! Do not beat me. Perhaps (*kim u*) you pursue me, out of anger, mistaking me for Siva.

Or, О Cupid (*ananga*)! Do you chase me because you think I am Siva?

I am without my lover (*priya-rahite*). Siva is always in the company of his beloved. Since I am without my lover, it is not proper to mistake me for Siva. And when I am with my beloved even then you should not beat me. Previously, Siva burned up Cupid’s body. Cupid was without limbs (*ananga*). The word *ananga* is used to show the great enmity of Cupid towards Siva.

The root of the mistaken identity is eradicated as follows. This is a necklace of water lily stems on my chest, which 1 wear to relieve myself of the pain of separation. It is not the king of snakes Vasuki. This is a string of blue lotus petals on my throat for cooling my body. It is not Siva’s throat which became blue because of swallowing poison. I wear sandalwood dust to lessen the heat in all my limbs. It is not Siva’s ashes. The sandalwood powder that he smears on his limbs to cool them appears to be dust, because of the heat in his body caused by separation. You think my necklace of water lily stems is Vasuki because it is long and cool. The blue lotuses appear to be Siva’s blue throat because the color is similar. You mistake my sandalwood dust for ashes. It is not correct to think that I am Siva.

This verse has the poetic ornament called *bhrantiman*. Perceiving one object to be another is called *bhrantiman*. *Kim* or *kim uta* indicates conjecture according to *Amara-kosa.* *U* indicates anger according to *Visva*.

### Text 3.12

*panau ma kuru cuta-sayakam amum ma capam aropaya*  
*krida-nirjita-visva murcchita-janaghatena kim paurusam \|*  
*tasya eva mrgi-drso manasija prenkhat-kataksasuga-*  
*sreni-jarjaritam manag api mano nadyapi sandhuksate \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*panau* - In \[your\] hand; *ma* - \[do\] not; *kuru* - put; *cuta* - of mango buds; *sayakam* - the arrow; *amum* - That; *ma* - \[do\] not; *capam* - bow; *aropaya* - string; *krida* - By \[your\] play; *nirjita-visva* - by whom the universe is conquered; *murcchita* - who have fainted; *janaghatena -* because of killing people; *kim* - are \[you\]; *paurusam* - a human being? *tasya* - Of that woman; *eva* - only; *mrgi-drso* - of the doe-eyed woman (Radha); *manasija -* о Cupid (or о one who is born from the mind); *prenkhat -* wandering; *kataksasuga* - of arrows which are the glances; *sreni* - by masses; *jarjaritam* - torn \[to pieces\]; *manag* - at once; *api* - even; *mano* - \[my\] mind; *nadyapi* - no longer; *sandhuksate* - lives.

**Перевод:**

**“O Cupid! Do not put the mango bud arrow in your hand. Do not string that arrow on your bow. О Cupid, the conqueror of the universe by your play! Are you a human being - shooting people who have fainted? My mind, cut to pieces by masses of arrows of Radha’s glances which wander about, can no longer live.**

**Комментарий:**

Cupid is famous for having mango bud arrows. Placing arrows of mango buds on his bow, he shoots. Worried about this, Krsna becomes humble before Cupid.

О Cupid (*manasa-ja*) Do not put that arrow made of the mango flower in your hand. Seeing the blossoming of spring flowers, he speaks. Do not aim your bow.

Or, do not take up your mango flower arrow in your hand. But if you put in your hand, do not string it on your bow. The word *manasa-ja* also indicates “You should not cause me pain, since you are born from my mind.”

О Cupid, who conquers the universe with your play! Are you a human being (*kim paurusam*) if you beat persons like me who have fainted? *Api tu* means *kim api.* Or, are you a vile person (*‘kim-paurusam’* taken as a compound indicating ‘bad person’) in beating people who have fainted? The cause of his fainting is her doe-like eyes. Because of great separation he cannot even utter her name. My mind, cut to pieces by masses of arrows of Radha’s glances which wander about, cannot live. I faint because of the arrows of her glances. Now the injury from your mango flower arrows kills one who is already dead. In this way, playfully you have conquered the universe. You have destroyed your good name among people who have fainted.

*Sayaka* is an arrow according to *Visva*. *Dhanus* means a bow according to *Amara-kosa*. *Asuga* means an arrow. *Kim-paurasam* is a compound indicating “bad person.” This is according to the rule *kim ksepe* - (*Panini* 2.1.64).

### Text 3.13

*bhru-pallavam dhanur apanga-tarangitani*  
*bana gunah sravana-palir iti smarena \|*  
*tasyam ananga-jaya-jangama-devatayam*  
*astrani nirjita-jaganti kim arpitani \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhru* - Of \[her\] brows; *pallavam* - the shoots; *dhanur* - \[are his\] bow; *apanga-tarangitani* - \[her\] shifty side-glances; *bana* - \[are his\] arrows; *gunah* - \[is his\] bow-string; *sravana* - of \[her\] ears; *palir* - \[and\] the edge, *iti* - Thus; *smarena* - by the Cupid; *tasyam* - in her (Radha); *ananga* - to the Cupid; *jaya* - victory; *jangama* - for giving; *devatayam* - the devata-; *astrani* - the weapons; *nirjita-jaganti* - by which the worlds are conquered; *kim* - somehow; *arpitani* - \[are\] established.

**Перевод:**

**“The weapons for conquering the universe have perhaps been established by Cupid in Radha, who is the devata meant for giving victory to Cupid. The shoots of her brows are his bow, her shifty side glances are his arrows, and her ears are his bow string.**

**Комментарий:**

“You feel sorrow on remembering her glance. Why find fault with Cupid?”

“I speculate (*kim*) that Cupid has given these weapons to Radha.”

“But should not such weapons be given to someone’s hand by a warrior and not placed elsewhere?”

“Cupid has established them in Radha, after having conquered the three worlds with those weapons.”

“Why did no one else get them?”

“She is the devata who enables Cupid to be victorious. The weapons have been given to her to conquer the three worlds. For that purpose, they have been established in her.”

It is also hinted that a weapon given by a *devata* cannot be counteracted by anyone. What power of this *devata*! Though Cupid has no limbs, by his mercy the universe is conquered. But Cupid, being conquered by her, rejects his lord, Krsna, and takes shelter of Radha alone.

The weapons are described as follows. She has bows in the form of her eye brows. The movements of her glances are the arrows. Her ears are the bow strings. Her brows are compared to young shoots since they are dark and glossy. Her glances are compared to arrows because they cannot be counterattacked. Her ears are compared to bow strings because they are long. *Apanga* means the edge of the eye according to *Visva*. *Pali* refers to the edge of the ear.

### Text 3.14

*bhru-cape nihitah kataksa-visikho nirmatu marma-vyatham*  
*syamatma kutilah karotu kavan-bharo’pi marodyamam \|*  
*moham tavad ayam ca tanvi tanutam bimbadharo ragavan*  
*sad-vrttah stana-mandalas tava katham pranair mama kridati \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhru* - Of \[your\] brow; *cape* - on the bow; *nihitah* - strung; *kataksa* - of \[your\] glance; *visikho* - the arrow; *nirmatu* - let it inflict; *marma* - to \[my\] body; *vyatham* - pain; *syamatma* - Whose self is dark; *kutilah* - crooked; *karotu* - let it make; *kavari-bharo* - \[your\] bound-up hair; *api* - even; *marodyamam* - an attempt at \[my\] death; *moham* - The loss of \[my\] consciousness; *tavad* - indeed; *ayam* - this; *ca* - and; *tanvi* - о slender woman; *tanutam* - let it cause*; bimbadharo* - \[your\] lips \[like\] the bimba fruit; *ragavan* - red; *sad-vrttah* - Perfectly round; *stana* - of breasts; *mandalas* - the circle; *tava* - your; *katham* - why; *pranair* - with life; *mama* - my; *kridati* - play?

**Перевод:**

**“Let the arrow of your glance, strung on the bow of your brow, inflict pain to my body. Let your dark, crooked bound-up hair attempt to kill me. Let your bimba-red lips, filled with hatred, cause me to lose consciousness. Why do your round breasts play with my life?**

**Комментарий:**

Now the beauty of Radha’s limbs gives me pain. I should scold her for this. With this intention, Krsna ignores Cupid and addresses Radha as if she were in front of him.

Let the arrow of your glance, strung (*nihitah*) on the bow of your eye brow, cause pain to my limbs. Since that is the nature of your glance, let it be so.

Let your bound up hair, black (*syamatma*) and crooked (*kutila*), try to kill (*mаrа*) me. Or, let the hair try to incite passion (*mаrа*) in me. Someone who is crooked (*kutila*), with inner darkness (*syamatma*), naturally tries to kill others.

О slender woman (*tanvi*)! Let your lips, red (*ragavan*) like the bimba fruit, make me faint. Such a person is filled with hate (*ragavan*) and makes others lose consciousness.

Why do your breasts, perfectly round (*sad-vrttah*), play with my life airs, trying to take them away? It is not proper that one of good character (*sad-vrttasya*) should take others’ lives. \[In the last line of the verse,\] the ornament called *virodha* (contradiction) is used. It is actually not proper that a good person takes others’ lives. *Kavya-prakasa* (10.24) says *virodhah so ‘virodh ‘pi viruddhatvena yad vacah*: when there is no contradiction but things are described as contradictory it is called *virodha*.

*Visikhah* means an arrow according to *Visva*. *Syama* means something condemned according to *Dharani*. *Kavari* means bound up hair according to *Amara-kosa*. *Mara* means death and Cupid according to *Visva*. *Bimba* refers to a fruit. *Raga* refers to hatred and being colored. *Vrttam* means character and round according to *Sasvata*. *Atma* means nature according to *Visva*.

### Text 3.15

*tani sparsa-sukhani te ca taralah snigdha drsor vibhramas*  
*tad-vaktrambuja-saurabham sa ca sudha-syandi giram vakrima \|*  
*sa bimbadhara-madhuriti visayasange’pi cen manasam*  
*tasyam lagna-samadhi hanta viraha-vyadhih katham vardhate \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tani* - these; *sparsa* - \[derived from\] touching; *sukhani* - these happinesses; *te* - your \[limbs\]; *ca* - and; *taralah* - playful; *snigdha* - affectionate; *drsor* - of \[your\] eyes; *vibhramas* - movements; *tad-vaktrambuja* - of \[your\] lotus-face; *saurabham* - the fragrance; *sa* - the; *ca* - and; *sudha* - of nectar; syandi - the flow; *giram* - of \[your\] words; *vakrima* - crooked; *sa* - the; *bimbadhara* - of \[your\] bimba-lips; *madhuri -* the sweet taste; *iti -* thus; *visayasange* - with the senses adhering to their objects; *api* - even; *cen* - if; *manasam* - \[my\] mind; *tasyam* - in her (Radha); *lagna-samadhi* - \[is one\] whose absorption is intense; *hanta* - alas; *viraha* - of separation; *vyadhih* -the pain; *katham* - how; *vardhate* - does increase.

**Перевод:**

**“I experience the happiness of touching your limbs, seeing the affectionate movements of the pupils of your eyes, smelling the fragrance of your lotus face, hearing the flow of nectar of your crooked words and tasting the sweetness of your bimba lips. If I experience in this way with my senses while absorbing my mind in you, how can the pain of separation increase?”**

**Комментарий:**

“This condition is not Radha’s fault and is also not Cupid’s fault. Suffering arises because of the temporary nature of that experience. You should think of Radha whom you previously experienced with your senses.”

“The happiness of touching her limbs, which I previously experienced, I am experiencing now.” This is experienced through the sense of touch. “I experienced the flickering pupils of your eyes filled with affection and playfulness.” This is experienced through sight. “I experienced the fragrance of your lotus mouth.” This is attachment arising from the senses of smell. “I experienced the crookedness in the flow of nectar of your words,” by hearing. “Your lips sweet as bimba fruit which I previously experienced, I experience now.” This is experienced through the sense of taste. “If my mind concentrates completely on Radha even (*api*) with each  sense contacting its object (*visayasange*), why does the pain arising  from separation still increase?”

The word *api* (even) indicates the ornament of *virodha*  (contradiction) since it is impossible during *samadhi* to have the  external senses contact the sense objects. *Samadhi* means complete  concentration of the mind while stopping function of the external  senses.

Separation will occur only when there is non-attainment of  the desired objects of the respective senses. Because my senses  are not separated from their objects and my mind thinks of her  constantly, there should be no separation. How can pain, arising  from separation, then occur? It is hinted that in *samadhi* also he  experiences pain. The objects (*visaya*) are form, sound, smell, taste  and touch according to *Amara-kosa*. The conclusion of this verse is that Krsna experiences most astonishing transformations in *prema*.

### Text 3.16

*tiryak-kantha-vilola-mauli-taralottamsasya vamsoccarad-*  
*giti-sthana-krtavadhana-lalana-laksair na samlaksitah \|*  
*sammugdhe madhusudanasya madhure radha-mukhendau sudha-*  
*sare kandalitas ciram dadatu vah ksemam kataksormayah \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tiryak* - \[Is\] bent; *kantha* - whose neck; *vilola* - \[is\] swaying; *mauli* - whose crown; *taralottamsasya* - \[and\] whose earrings \[are\] quivering; *vamsoccarad* - playing on \[his\] flute; *giti* - of a song; *sthana* - to the notes; *krtavadhana -* because \[his\] attention \[is\] directed; *lalana* - of beautiful women \[*gopis*\]; *laksair* - by the groups; *na* - not; *samlaksitah* - noticing; *sammugdhe* - tender (or beautiful); *madhusudanasya* - of Madhusudana (Krsna); *madhure* - \[and\] charming; *radha -* of Radha; *mukhendau* - on the moon-face; *sudha* - of nectar; *sare* - the essence; *kandalitas* - increased; *ciram* - for a long time; *dadatu* - may it bestow; *vah* - \[upon\] you; *ksemam* - auspiciousness; *kataksormayah* - the waves of \[his\] side-glances.

**Перевод:**

**May the side glance of Madhusudana which increases on seeing the tender, charming moon face of Radha - the shelter of sweetness, the essence of nectar - and which does not notice other gopis as he attentively plays melodies on his flute, with bent neck, swaying crown and quivering earrings, bestow auspiciousness on you forever.**

**Комментарий:**

This is a blessing at the end of this chapter. May the waves of Krsna’s glances from the edge of his eyes bring you auspiciousness (*ksemam*). The word Madhusudana (killer of the demon Madhu) is used to indicate that Krsna can give auspiciousness by destroying the sinful. Or it can indicate his fickle nature (he is like a bee). The waves of glances are described; they shoot up like waves in the presence of Radha’s moon-like face, the shelter of sweetness (*madhure*), and they indistinctly quiver (*sammugdhe*) just as the waves of the ocean increase on seeing the moon. There is another version (of last two lines of this verse):

*premna kandalitah sumugdha-madhure radhananendau sudha-*  
*sare vo madhusudanasya dadatu ksemam kataksormayah \|\|*

**In this case** ***sumugdha-madhure*** **means Radha’s face, which is charming (*****madhure*****) and very tender. That face is the essence of nectar (*****sudha-sare*****).**

“Since he sees other *gopis* why do his glances act like waves only for the face of Radha?”

“When he attentively (focusing his mind - *krtvavadhana*) plays arrangements of notes on his flute, those glances do not look at the group of women. All the women, enchanted by the sound of the flute, do not see that Krsna glances only at Radha.”

His appearance in playing the flute is described as follows. His neck is bent, his crown sways and his earrings quiver. This is a *karma-dharaya* compound. *Giti* means song according to *Amara-kosa*. *Uttamsa* means earrings according to *Dharani*. *Kandala* means new sprouts. *Urmi* means wave according to *Visva*.

## Chapter Four. Snigdha-madhusudanah - Tender Krsna

### Text 4.1

*yamuna-tira-vanira-nikunje mandam asthitam \|*  
*praha prema-bharodbhrantam madhavam radhikasakhi \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yamuna* - Of the Yamuna; *tira* - on the bank; *vanira* - of reeds; *nikunje* - in a bower; *mandam* - motionless; *asthitam* - sitting; *praha* - spoke; *prema* - of \[his\] affection; *bhara* - by the weight; affection; *udbhrantam* - overcome; *madhavam* - to Madhava \[Krsna\]; *radhika* - of Radha; *sakhi* - a female friend.

**Перевод:**

**A sakhi spoke to Krsna, overcome by his strong affection for Radha, sitting motionless in a reed bower on the bank of Yamuna.**

**Комментарий:**

In the previous chapter Krsna’s condition in separation was described. Now the *sakhi* describes Radha’s condition to Krsna. The suffix ka in Radhika suggests pity. The *sakhi* of Radha spoke directly (*praha*) to Krsna. He was confused by his strong affection (*prema bhara*) for Radha. Thus he could not move. He was sitting in a reed bower on the bank of the Yamuna in a state of torpor (*mandam*), having given up searching for her. Or the word can be *amandam* (quickly). The *sakhi* spoke quickly, seeing that Krsna was so disturbed. *Manda* means slow according to *Visva*. *Sa prema bheda-rahitam yunor yad bhava-bandhanam: prema* means the bond of love between two young people without interruption -(*Rasarnava-sudhakara* 2.258).

**\|\| Song Eight \|\|**

*karnata-raga, ekatali-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***karnata*** **and the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The qualities of *karnata-raga* are as follows:

*krpana-panir gaja-danta-patram*  
*ekam vahan daksina-karna-рurе \|*  
*samstuyamanah sura-caranaughaih*  
*karnata-ragah sithikantha-nilah \|\|*

***Karnata-raga*****, praised by** ***devatas*** **and** ***caranas*****, is blue like a peacock’s throat, with a sword in his hand and carrying an ivory petal as his right ear ornament. (*****Sangita-darpana*** **2.66)**

*Ekatall-tala* was previously described (in epigraph to song six).

### Text 4.2

*nindati candanam indu-kiranam*  
*anuvindati khedam adhiram \|*  
*vyala-nilaya-milanena garalam*  
*iva kalayati malaya-samiram \|\|2\|\|*

*sa virahe lava dina \|\|*  
*madhava manasija-visikha-bhayad*  
*iva bhavanaya tvayi lina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nindati* - \[She\] condemns; *candanam* - sandalwood; *indu* - of the moon; *kiranam* - \[from\] the ray; *anuvindati* - \[she\] obtains; *khedam* - pain; *adhiram* - intense; *vyala* - of snakes; *nilaya* - with the abode; *milanena* - by \[its\] mixture; *garalam -* poison; *iva* - as if; *kalayati* - \[she\] considers; *malaya* - from the Malaya mountain; *samiram* - the breeze.

*sa* - She; *virahe* - in separation; *tava -* from you; *dina* - miserable; *madhava -* о Madhava \[Krsna\]; *manasija* - of the Cupid; *visikha* - of the arrows; *bhayad* - in fear; *iva* - as if; *bhavanaya* - by meditation; *tvayi* - into you; *lina* - \[she has\] merged.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi speaks to Krsna,\] “She (Radha condemns sandalwood and feels intense pain from the rays of the moon. She takes the Malaya breeze to be poisonous, having mixed with the poison of snakes in the sandalwood trees of Malaya mountain.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation, she merges into you by meditation, as if in fear of Cupid’s arrows, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The heroine has longing in separation.

*naivagatah samucitesv api vasaresu*  
*pranesvarah sva-guru-karya-vasena yasya \|*  
*durdhara-vrtta-viraha-jvara-tapitangim*  
*utkanthitam vadati tam bharatah kavindrah \|\|*

**Bharata describes the woman in longing as one whose limbs are pained by an intolerable fever of separation, whose lover has not come on the appointed days because of obligations to his superiors.**

\[The meaning of the chorus is as follows.\] О Madhava! She suffers in separation from you. The word Madhava (husband of Laksmi) suggests that he does not know suffering since he has other women.

Ha indicates misfortune.

“If she is suffering, how does she pass her time?”

“She merges into you, out of fear of Cupid’s arrows. Since Cupid arises from your mind, when she merges in you, she no longer has fear.”

“How does she merge in me?”

“She remembers (*bhavanaya*) you while criticizing all objects. She criticizes sandalwood. It heats up people in separation. It is useless to be known for cooling. She feels excessive (*adhiram*) pain on seeing the rays of the moon.” *Dhira* means ‘a little’ according to *Visva* (so *adhiram* means excessive). Or, she criticizes with unsteadiness (*adhiram*). Or *adhiram* (*adhika* + *irayati* = agitates excessively) can modify *candanam* (sandalwood) with the meaning “inspiring the arrows of Cupid.”

She thinks of the wind from Malaya Mountain as poison because of its contact with the sandalwood trees growing there, which are the abode of the snakes. \[She considers that\] the Malaya winds, after having been consumed and vomited by the snakes in the trees, blows here. The wind moves from place to place full of poison. The wind is like poison because it makes everyone faint when it contacts their bodies. Causes of happiness for others become causes of sorrow for persons suffering in separation. She has attained a state of remembering (*smarana*) due to longing in separation. It is said:

*dveso yatranya-karyesu tad-ekagram ca manasam \|*  
*svasair manorathais capi cestas tah smaranam yatha \|\|*

***Smarana*** **is that condition in which the mind and activities are concentrated on one’s aspirations and desires, with hatred for other activities.**

*indum nindati padma-kandala-dalais talpam na va manyate*  
*karpuram kirati prayati na ratim praleya-dhara-grhe \|*  
*svasaih kevalam eva khedita-tanur dhyayaty asau balika*  
*yat tat ko’pi yuva dhruvam smara-suhrc cetasy amusyah sthitah \|\|*

**That girl criticizes the moon and had no regard for a bed made of lotus buds and petals. She rejects camphor. She does not have attraction for a house with showers of dew. With pained body while sighing, she meditates. Some youth, a lover, is situated in her heart. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.9)**

*Vyala* means a snake according to *Visva.* *Garalam* means poison.

### Text 4.3

*avirala-nipatita-madana-sarad iva bhavad-avanaya visalam \|*  
*sva-hrdaya-marmani varma karoti sajala-nalinl-dala-jalam \|\|3\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*avirala* - constantly; *nipatita* - falling \[upon her\]; *madana* - of Cupid; *sarad* - from the arrow; *iva* - as if; *bhavad* - of you; *avanaya* - for the protection; *visalam* - extensive; *sva-hrdaya* - of her heart; *marmani* - in the sensitive area; *varma -* \[into\] armor; *karoti* - \[she\] makes; *sajala* - with water; *nalini* - of the blue lotus; *dala* - of the petals; *jalam* - a cover.

**Перевод:**

**“She makes an extensive armor out of blue lotus petals and water to cover the sensitive area of her heart to protect you from Cupid’s arrows, which constantly fall upon her.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation...**

**Комментарий:**

She makes an extensive armor of blue lotus petals and water over the sensitive area of her heart, to protect you from Cupid’s arrows which fall upon her constantly. You always reside in her heart. She thinks, “He should not be afflicted by Cupid’s arrows.”

Another meaning could be: she makes an armor to protect herself from the arrows of Cupid. This protection however seems to be accomplished already (*bhavat*). *Avana* means protection according to *Visva-prakasa*. *Marma* means a place in the body which cannot tolerate pain. *Visalam* means broad. The first meaning indicates her strong affection for him. This shows the *sakhis* skill as a messenger (expertly revealing the feelings of one lover to the other).

### Text 4.4

*kusuma-visikha-sara-talpam analpa-vilasa-kala-kamaniyam \|*  
*vratam iva tava parirambha-sukhaya karoti kusuma-sayaniyam \|\|4\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kusuma* - of flowers; *visikha* - of the \[Cupid’s\] arrows; *sara* - \[made\] of the shafts; *talpam* - a bed; *analpa* - unlimited; *vilasa* - of play; *kala* - for the art; *kamaniyam* - attractive; *vratam* - a vow; *iva* - as if; *tava* - your; *parirambha* - of \[your\] embrace; *sukhaya* - for the purpose of the happiness; *karoti* - \[she\] makes; *kusuma* - of flowers; *sayaniyam* - a bed.

**Перевод:**

**“She makes a bed of flowers attractive for the art of unlimited play, which is like a vow, a bed made of Cupid’s flower arrows, in order that you give her the happiness of your embrace.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation...**

**Комментарий:**

She makes a bed of flowers. This is described. It is most attractive for many arts of playing. In her condition, she remains with eyes on the path on which you will tread, while making the flower bed with intense love, with expectation that you will come. This bed, a resting place for the arrows of Cupid in the form of the flowers, is like a vow (vows are performed for a certain purpose) to attain the happiness of your embrace. *Talpa* means a bed according to *Visva*. These three lines indicate *udvega* (disturbance), the fifth stage. It is hinted that flowers which are pleasant to touch have become like arrows.

Or, she makes a flower bed pleasant for many arts of play, like a vow (doing it with great attention) in the form of lying on a bed of Cupid’s flower arrows to gain the happiness of your embrace.

### Text 4.5

*vahati ca valita-vilocana-jaladharam anana-kamalam udaram \|*  
*vidhum iva vikata-vidhuntuda-danta-dalana-galitamrta-dharam \|\|5\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vahati* - \[She\] carries; *ca* - and; *valita* - spreading \[sideways\] glances; *vilocana* - \[are\] eyes; *jaladharam* - whose clouds; *anana-kamalam* - a lotus-face; *udaram* - beautiful; *vidhum* - the moon; *iva* - as if; *vikata* - terrible; *vidhuntuda* - of Rahu; *danta* - of the teeth; *dalana* - because of the affliction; *galita* - \[are\] trickling; *amrta-dharam* -whose streams of nectar.

**Перевод:**

**“She has a beautiful lotus face, a face like the moon, trickling with sweet tears because of affliction by terrible Rahu’s teeth, with clouds for eyes, spreading sideways glances.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation...**

**Комментарий:**

She carries her beautiful (*udaram*) lotus face. Or *udaram* can refer to generosity in giving tears of sweet *prema.* The face is described as follows. It has two eyes like clouds which shower *rasa*, spreading sideways (*valita*) in order to gaze at your path. Her eyes are compared to clouds since they constantly shower tears. That face is like the moon (*vidhum'*). The face flows with trickling (*galita*) nectar streams (*dharam*) because of affliction from the huge or terrible (*vikata*) teeth of Rahu (*vidhuntuda*). Her face is compared to the moon and her tears are compared to showers of nectar. This is *vakyartha upama*. Change of complexion is also hinted (the face is pale like the moon). *Vikata* means huge according to *Visva*. *Vidhuntuda* means Rahu according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 4.6

*dhyana-layena purah parikalpya bhavantam atlva durapam \|*  
*vilapati hasati visidati roditi cancati muncati tapam \|\|6\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dhyana* - In meditation \[on you\]; *layena* - bу absorption; *purah* - in front \[of herself\]; *parikalpya* - imagining; bhavantam - you; *ativa* - exceedingly; *durapam* - hard to attain; *vilapati* - \[she\] laments; *hasati* - mocks; *visidati* - becomes depressed; *roditi* - weeps; *cancati* - moves about; *muncati* - \[and\] becomes released; *tapam* - \[from\] pain.

**Перевод:**

**“In deep meditation, she brings your form, hard to attain, in front of her. She laments, mocks, becomes depressed, weeps, moves about and becomes released from pain.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation...**

**Комментарий:**

By complete concentration on you, difficult to attain, she makes you appear in front of her. She draws a picture of him. In this case, the description of her painting his image is not imaginary. Or, by complete absorption in you she attempts to make you come to her directly since you are hard to attain by other means such as messengers.

\[Now her actions are described.\]

She laments - *vilapo duhkhajam vacah*: lamentation consists of speaking, out of grief. (*Ujjvala-nilamani* 11.83)

She mocks him, “This deceiver takes so much time. You better go there.”

She becomes depressed, “He came before me, grasped my feet and pacified me.”

She cries, “How can he again be brought under control?” *ruditam uditam astram yositam vigrahesu*: women use crying as a weapon in quarrels of love. (*Magha-kavya*)

Seeing him follow while uttering conciliatory words, she goes away. Then being pacified, embraced by him, she becomes free of suffering, giving up the pain of separation. Or, she cries and then scolds (*muncati*) her conciliating lover out of affectionate anger. Then she attains (*cancati*) suffering (*tapam*).

The stage is *unmada* (seventh stage). It is said:

*svasa-prarodanotkampa-vasudhollekhanair api \|*  
*vyaparo jayate yatra sa unmadah smrto yatha \|\|*

***Unmada*** **refers to the condition in which a person acts with heavy breathing, wailing, and trembling, while scratching the earth. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.12)**

The *bhava* is *kilakincita*. This is described in *Dasa-rupaka* as *krodhasru-harsa-bhityadeh sankarah kilakincitam*: *kilakincita* refers to a mixture of anger, tears, joy and fear. The ornament in this verse is *dipaka.* *Dipaka* is defined as *saiva kriyasu bahvisu karakasyeti dipakam*: *dipaka* occurs when many verbs are connected with one noun. (*Kavya-prakasa* 10.103)

### Text 4.7

*vilikhati rahasi kuranga-madena bhavantam asama-sara-bhutam \|*  
*pranamati makaram adho vinidhaya kare ca saram nava-cutam \|\|7\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vilikhati* - \[She\] draws; *rahasi* - in a solitary place; *kuranga* - of deer; *madena* - with the musk; *bhavantam* - you; asama - of Cupid; *sara-bhutam* - in the form; *pranamati* - \[and she\] offers respects; *makaram* - a *makara*; *adho* - below; *vinidhaya* - placing; *kare* - in \[his\] hand; *ca* - and; *saram* - an arrow; *nava-cutam* - whose mango buds \[are\] fresh.

**Перевод:**

**“She draws your picture, in a solitary place, in the form of Cupid holding arrows of mango buds in his hand, using musk. She draws the form of a makara as the vehicle, and then offers respects to it.**

**“O Madhava! In misery of separation...**

**Комментарий:**

She paints a picture of you as the form of Cupid (*asama-cara-bhutam*) using musk in a solitary place. After drawing a *makara* below as his seat, she offers respects. The meaning of drawing *makara* as Cupid’s vehicle is that she requests to you to pacify her. Drawing the *makara* also indicates “Mounting his vehicle, my lover will come quickly.” This is the action of a person in separation. *Bharata* says:

*devata-pujanam kuryad dadyad balibhuje balim \|*  
*likhet kanta-pratikrtim pathayec chuka-sarikam \|*  
*ganayec cavadhir dinam gitam gayet tad-angakam \|*  
*evam-vidha-vinodena nayet kalam viyogini \|\|*

**The woman in separation worships the devatas, makes offerings to crows, draws images of her lover, teaches the parrot and** ***sarika*** **(a bird) to speak, counts the days and sings songs, and in this way brings the limbs of the lover before her.**

*Mrga-mada* or *kuranga-mada* means musk according to *Haravali*. Even though drawing the image of Cupid should not occur for the woman in separation (since it would increase her desire and cause more pain), Radha drew Krsna with Cupid’s form (since he is actually the *svarupa* of Cupid) because her heart was overcome with desire for Krsna.

### Text 4.8

*prati-padam idam api nigadati madhava tava carane patitaham \|*  
*tvayi vimukhe mayi sapadi sudha-nidhir api tanute tanu-daham \|\|8\|\|*

*sa virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mati-padam* - With every step; *idam* - this; *api* - even; *nigadati* - \[she\] speaks; *madhava* - О Madhava (Krsna); *tava* - your; *carane* - at  \[your\] feet; *patitaham* - I have fallen; *tvayi* - You; *vimukhe* - \[when you are\] separated \[from me\]; *mayi* - to me; *sapadi* - at once; *sudha-* nidhir - the ocean of nectar (the moon); *api* - even; *tanute* - gives;  *tanu* - in \[my\] body; *daham* - pain.

**Перевод:**

**“With every step she speaks. ‘O Madhava! I fall at your feet. In  separation from you, even the moon gives pain to me.’**

**“О Madhava! In misery of separation...”**

**Комментарий:**

She utters words at each step as she moves here and there. “O  Madhava! I have fallen at your feet. I offer you respects.”

When separated from you, the ocean of nectar (the moon) produces pain in my body (*tanu-daham*). Or the sentence can mean, the moon gives pain of love (*atanu-daham*). Or, the moon gives pain to a great degree (*atanu*). Or, the moon gives pain so that my body disappears.

“You are the husband of Laksmi (*madhava*). If you stay close to me, Laksmi, even as a competitor, can do nothing to me. But if you are unfavorable to me, the moon, remembering me as the competitor of his sister (moon is Laksmi’s brother, since they both arose from the ocean), will give pain to my body.” This is the suggestion of the word Madhava. That the moon gives pain is contrary to its character. This is the ornament called *virodha* (contradiction).

### Text 4.9

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam idam adhikam yadi manasa nataniyam \|*  
*hari-virahakula-ballava-yuvati-sakhi-vacanam pathamyam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - by Sri Jayadeva; *bhanitam* - spoken; *idam* - this \[composition\]; *adhikam* - excellent; *yadi* - if; *manasa* - by the mind; *nataniyam* - is enacted; *hari* - from Krsna; *viraha* - in separation; *akula* - suffering; *ballava-yuvati* - of the young cowherdess (Radha); *sakhi* - of the female friend; *vacanam* - \[and\] the words; *pathaniyam* - should be carefully read \[Prabodhananda explains: then it will bestow a similar desire for Krsna\].

**Перевод:**

**If the composition of Jayadeva becomes enacted and relished by the mind, it will bestow a similar desire for Krsna. Therefore carefully read the words of the sakhi sent by Radha, who is suffering in separation concerning her happiness. (END OF SONG EIGHT)**

**Комментарий:**

If this composition of Jayadeva becomes attractive to the mind, and it’s enacted in the mind, it will produce (*dam*) similar desire (*ih*) for Krsna. Therefore the present words of the *sakhi* sent by Radha who is suffering in separation from him, should be read. The words of the *sakhi* are concerned about Radha’s happiness (*adhikam*).

### Text 4.10

*avaso vipinayate priya-sakhi-malapi jalayate*  
*tapo’pi svasitena dava-dahana-jvala-kalapayate \|*  
*sapi tvad-virahena hanta harini-rupayate ha katham*  
*kandarpo’pi yamayate viracayan sardula-vikriditam \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*avaso* - \[Her\] house; *vipinayate* - resembles a forest; *priya-sakhi* - of \[her\] dear friends; *malapi* - even the set; *jalayate* - resembles a net; *tapo’pi* - even \[her\] pain; *svasitena* - because of her breathing; *dava* - forest-fire; *dahana* - of a burning; *jvala* - of flames; *kalapayate* - resembles the collection; *sapi* - even she; *tvad* - from you; *virahena* - because of separation; *hanta* - alas; *harini* - a doe; *rupayate* - resembles; *ha* - Alas; *katham* - how; *kandarpo* - the Cupid; *api* -even; *yamayate* - does resemble Yama \[the god of death\]; *viracayan* - producing; *sardula-vikriditam* - the sport of a tiger \[this is also the name of the verse’s meter\] ?

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “Radha has become pale as a doe. She takes her house to be the forest and her friends are like a net. Her pain of separation is like forest fire, fanned by the wind of her breathing. What a misery! Cupid, becoming a tiger, brings about her death.”**

**Комментарий:**

In separation from you, she has become like a doe (*harini rupayate*). Her complexion has become pale like a doe, due to sickness arising from separation. And the doe, confronted with a forest fire, becomes agitated and in great fear, seeing a hunter’s net spread out in front, glances here and there with flickering eyes. In this way Radha sometimes takes her house to be a forest (*avaso vipinayate*). Her group of *sakhis* becomes the net (*jalayate*). When she desires to give up her life, unable to tolerate her grief in separation, they prevent her. Or the garland given by her *sakhi* to appease her pain is condemned as a net to bind up a deer.

Due to the breathing, which is like the assistance of wind, the pain of separation seems to become a forest fire. The *sakhi* expresses her anguish. “O misfortune (*hanta*)! How can she exist since Cupid seems to play like a tiger to bring about her death (*yamayate*)?" The word *harini* (doe) also indicates that Radha has great affection for Krsna, just as an animal has affection for its master. *Sardula-vikriditam* also indicates the meter. The first part of the verse shows *udvega* (fifth stage) and the second half shows the eighth stage called *vyadhi* (sickness).

*abhista-sangamabhavad vyadhih santapa-laksanah \|*  
*atra santapa-nihsvasau sita-vastu-nisevanam \|\|*

*nottaram bhasate prasne neksate na srnoti ca \|\|*

***Vyadhi*** **is a symptom of pain arising from separation from the beloved. She experiences pain, breathes heavily, and uses cool objects. She does not answer questions, does not see, and does not hear. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **2.398, 2.401)**

**\|\| Song Nine \|\|**

*desakhya-raga, ekatali tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***desakhya*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali*****.**

Epigraph to the song: *Desakhya-raga* is described as follows:

*asphotanaviskrta-romaharso niyuddha-sannaddha-visala-bahuh \|*  
*pramsuh pracanda-dyutir indu-gauro desakhya-ragah kila malla-murtih \|\|*

***Desakhya-raga*** **is a wrestler with tall figure, with huge arms raised for battle, hair standing on end, slapping his arms together, effulgent with complexion like the golden moon.**

*Ekatali* was described previously (in epigraph to song six)

### Text 4.11

*stana-vinihitam api haram udaram \|*  
*sa manute krsa-tanur iva bharam \|\|11\|\|*

*radhika tava virahe kesava \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*stana* - \[her\] breast; *vinihitam* - upon; *api* - even; *haram* - the necklace; *udaram* - great; *sa* - she (Radha); *manute* - considers; *krsa* - \[is\] wasting away; *tanur* - whose body; *iva* - as if; *bharam* - a burden; *radhika* - Radha; *tava* - from you; *virahe* - in separation; *kesava* - о Kesava (Krsna)!

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “O Kesava! In separation from you (chorus), Radha considers the necklace on her breast to be very heavy since her body is wasting away.**

**Комментарий:**

“O Kesava! In separation from you, Radha considers the necklace worn on her breast to be attractive (*udaram*) since it had touched your limbs during embraces, or was given from his neck. Or, she considers the necklace on her breast to be very (*udaram*) heavy. This is because her body has become very thin. You should show affection to her since she cannot even carry on her chest the necklace given by the *sakhis* to protect her life. The thinness of her body indicates *vyadhi* (sickness). Being thin is an *anubhava*.

*Kesava* means, the controller (*isa*) of happiness (*ka*) possessing nectar (*vam*) to revive a dead person. Even though you have this nature, my dear friend suffers because of you. That is not proper. That is the significance of the word Kesava.

### Text 4.12

*sa-rasa-masrnam api malayaja-pankam \|*  
*pasyati visam iva vapusi sa-sankam \|\|12\|\|*

*radhika tava virahe kesava...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-rasa* - \[And\] liquid; *masrnam* - \[it is\] soft; *api* - even though; *malayaja* - of sandalwood; *pankam* - the pulp; *pasyati* - she \[Radha\] sees; *visam* - poison; *iva* - as if; *vapusi* - on \[her\] body; *sa-sankam* -with anxiety.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from you, with anxiety, she sees sandalwood pulp on her body to be poison, though it is soft and liquid.**

**Комментарий:**

She sees sandalwood on her body with anxiety, taking it as poison, even though it is very soft (*sa-rasa-masrnam*). *Masrna* means soft and liquid according to *Visva*. *Sarasa* can also mean “giving rise to *rasa*.”

### Text 4.13

*svasita-pavanam anupama-parinaham \|*  
*madana-dahanam iva vahati sa-daham \|\|13\|\|*

*radhika tava virahe kesava...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*svasita* - \[is\] exhaled \[heavily\]; *pavanam* - by whom breath; *anupama*  - \[is\] incomparable; *parinaham* - \[and\] whose extent; *madana* - of  Cupid; *dahanam* - the fire; *iva* - as if; *vahati* - \[she\] breathes; *sa-daham* - painful.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from, you, she breathes heavily, incomparably slow and painful, as if she were the fire of Cupid.**

**Комментарий:**

“She exhales wind for her breathing” - this is described as follows. It is incomparably drawn out (*parinaham*). It is like the fire of passion. It is painful. Or, she breathes with pain (*sa-daham*).

### Text 4.14

*disi disi kirati sajala-kana-jalam \|*  
*nayana-nalinam iva vigalita-nalam \|\|14\|\|*

*radhika tava virahe kesava...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*disi disi* - In all directions; *kirati* - \[she\] casts; *sajala* - \[is\] watery; *kana* - of tears; *jalam* - whose multitude; *nayana-nalinam* - \[her\] lotus-eyes; *iva* - as if; *vigalita* - \[are\] gone; *nalam* - \[lotuses\] whose stems.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from you, with tearful eyes, she throws glances in all directions with her lotus eyes, as if they were lotuses without stems.**

**Комментарий:**

She casts her lotus eyes in all directions. The eyes are described as follows. They are filled with profuse (*jalam*) drops of tears. Those eyes are like lotuses without stems (*vigalita-nalam*). The lotus sprinkled with water drops, devoid of a stem, is compared to her constant glance outwards from the place of tears. It is also suggested that out of affection, she sprinkles the path, by which you will approach, with her lotus eyes.

### Text 4.15

*nayana-visayam api kisalaya-talpam \|*  
*kalayati vihita-hutasa-vikalpam \|\|15\|\|*

*radhika tava virahe kesava...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nayana* - To \[her\] eyes; *visayam* - \[it is\] visible; *api* - even though; *kisalaya* - of new shoots; *talpam* - the bed; *kalayati* - \[she\] experiences; *vihita-hutasa* - \[that it is\] covered with fire; *vikalpam* - the doubt.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from you, Radha considers the bed of new shoots to be covered with fire, though the bed is visible to her eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

In doubt (*vikalpam*), she considers the bed of new shoots to be made of fire, though it is very attractive (*nayana-visayam*) or though it is perceivable by the eye. It is visible to the eye, what to speak of touching it. It is also suggested that “My friend (Radha) has given up sleeping.” This verse describes the condition called *udvega* (confusion).

### Text 4.16

*tyajati na pani-talena kapolam \|*  
*bala-sasinam iva sayam alolam \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tyajati* - \[She\] does leave; *na* - not; *pani* - of \[her\] hand; *talena* - with the surface; *kapolam* - \[her\] cheek; *bala* - young \[on the second day\]; *sasinam* - the moon; *iva* - as if; *sayam* - in the evening; *alolam* - stationary.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from you, in the evening she keeps her palm on her stationary cheek, which is like the moon on the second day.**

**Комментарий:**

In the evening she does not give up placing her palm on her cheek. Her cheek is described as follows. The cheek is stationary (*alolam*). In the evening she thinks, with her palm on her cheek, “In the day he can be brought, but at night how can I be brought to meet him.”

The cheek is further described: It is like the moon on the second day after the new moon. Her hand covers half of her face, and the rest half of her face is visible. Because of separation the color of her face is pale. Thus it appears to be a thin and motionless moon, rising in the evening. This verse expresses the ninth stage called *jadata* (being stunned). It is said:

*yatra dhyayati nihsamjnam jadata sa prakirtita \|*  
*atra sparsanabhijnatvam vaivarnyam sithilangata \|*  
*akanda-hunkrtih stambho nihsvasa-krsatadayah \|\|*

***Jadata*** **refers to the condition in which a person meditates but seems unconscious. The complexion changes, the limbs become relaxed, and the person is unaware of the sense of touch. The person sighs and utters sounds of disgust. The person is stunned and becomes thin. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*** **2.402)**

### Text 4.17

*harir iti harir iti japati sa-kamam \|*  
*viraha-vihita-maraneva nikamam \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*harir iti* - “O attractor of my mind (or О Hari)!” *harir iti* - “O Attractor of my mind (or О Hari)!” *japati* - she utters; *sa-kamam* -full of desire; *viraha* - by separation \[from you\]; *vihita* - is arranged; *maraneva* - as if one whose death; *nikamam* - \[and\] intently.

**Перевод:**

**“O Kesava! In separation from you, she utters, ‘O attractor of my mind (hari!) Attractor of my mind (hari)!’ intently and full of desire, as if she is dying from separation.”**

**Комментарий:**

She intensely (*nikamam*) utters your name, as if she is about to die from separation, while being full of desire (*sa-kamam*). She desires to attain the tenth stage — *marana* (death). She repeats your name like the mantra of her worshipable deity. That name is full of auspiciousness, containing her all in all. It is the form of cintamani for her (fulfilling her desires), since she desires the nectar of your association in this body, now that she has developed fresh *anuraga*. The word Hari indicates “he whose special qualities attract the mind” or “he who removes all suffering.”

Not attaining you in this life, she fixes her goal to concentrate on her beloved in the next life. This is the tenth stage (*marana* or *death*) in *anuraga*:

*alokalapa-samrudha-samrogakula-cetasoh \|*  
*tayor bhaved asampraptau dasavasthah smaro yatha \|\|*

**The tenth stage of love arises when by non-attainment of the lover both parties become afflicted in consciousness, afflicted with intense disease, while they converse without being able to see each other.**

*abhilaso’tha cinta syat smrtis ca guna-kirtanam \|*  
*udvego’tha pralapah syad unmade vyadhir eva ca \|\|*

*jadata maranam ceti dasami jayate kramat \|*  
*asampraptau bhavantyetas tayor dasa dasa yatha \|\|*

**First is desire (*****abhilasa*****) then anxiety (*****cinta)*****, followed by remembrance (*****smrti*****) and glorifying the lover’s qualities (*****guna-kirtana*****). Then follows confusion (*****udvega*****), then prattling nonsense (*****pralapa*****), then madness (*****unmada*****), then sickness (*****vyadhi*****), then being stunned (*****jadata*****), and finally death (*****marana*****). In this way, when they cannot attain each other, the tenth stage death arrives. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **2.4-6) .**

The ninth stage (*jadata*) of the couple who possess eternal bliss, and who are the forms of the highest *madhurya-rasa*, is approved. To show the height of *anuraga*, only a shadow of the tenth stage death was shown. Sukadeva and others have described this state in some qualified persons, to show the intensity of Krsna’s *madhurya-rasa*. Here nine of the ten states have been shown. The qualities of sickness (*vyadhi*) are:

*santapa-vedana-prayo dirgha-svasa-samakulah \|*  
*tanukrta-tanur vyadhir astamo’yam smrto yatha \|\|*

**The eighth stage called** ***vyadhi*** **or sickness is that in which the body becomes thin, there is prominence of great pain, and long drawn out breathing with affliction. (*****Srngara- tilaka*** **2.14)**

The difference between the last conditions has been shown. Though these conditions appear to be disagreeable manifestations, they arise because of transformations of the *rasa* of bliss. They are types of bliss since they nourish bliss. That is the conclusion. In reality, they are not conditions of suffering.

### Text 4.18

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam iti gitam \|*  
*sukhayatu kesava-padam upanltam \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanitam* - spoken; *iti* - thus; *gitam* - the song; *sukhayatu* - may \[the song\] give happiness; *kesava* - of Kesava \[Krsna\]; *padam* - to the feet; *upanitam* - \[and\] brought \[by the *sakhi*\].

**Перевод:**

**May the song brought by the sakhi recorded by Jayadeva give happiness at the feet of Krsna. (END OF SONG NINE)**

**Комментарий:**

May the song of Radha’s sakhi, recorded by Jayadeva, and brought to Krsna, give happiness to the feet of Sri Krsna, so that he will quickly go and meet her! Or, may the song which the *sakhi* related to Krsna, put to *raga* and *tala* by Jayadeva, give happiness to the singers and hearers. Or, may the song offered to the spiritual abode (*padam*) of Kesava, Vaikuntha-loka, give happiness to all. This means the song is sung even in Vaikuntha. Or, may the song which has arrived, give happiness to the devotee who is protected by Kesava (*kesava-padam*)! Or, may the song give happiness to Krsna’s place. *Padam* means place or protection, according to *Visva*.

### Text 4.19

*sa romancati sitkaroti vilapaty utkampate tamyati*  
*dhyayaty udbhramati pramilati pataty udyati murcchaty api\|*  
*etavaty atanu-jvare vara-tanur jiven na kim te rasat*  
*svar-vaidya-pratima prasidasi yadi tyakto’nyatha hastakah \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa* - She; *romancati* - \[her\] hair stands on end; *sitkaroti* - \[she\] hisses; *vilapaty* - \[she\] laments; *utkampate* - \[she\] trembles; *tamyati* - \[she\] becomes fatigued; *dhyayaty* - \[she\] meditates; *udbhramati* - \[she\] becomes confused; *pramilati* - \[she\] closes \[her\] eyes; *pataty* - \[she\] falls \[on the ground\]; *udyati* - \[she\] rises up; *mtircchaty* - \[and she\] faints; *api* - even, etavaty - In such a state; *atanu* - of the Cupid; *jvare* - in the fever; *vara-tanur* - whose body is best; *jiven* - would \[she\] live; *na* - not; *kim* - not; *te* - your; *rasat* - because of *rasa;* *svar* - of heaven; *vaidya* - of the physician (Danvatari); *pratima* - the incarnation; *prasidasi* - you are pleased; *yadi* - if; *tyakto* - \[Will be\] given up; *anyatha* - otherwise; *hastakah* - \[her\] hand.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “Her hairs stand on end. She hisses, laments, trembles, becomes fatigued, meditates, becomes confused, closes her eyes, falls on the ground, sees you internally and rises up, and faints. If you, the incarnation of the heavenly physician, are pleased, then Radha possessing the supreme body but afflicted by the fever of love, will live, because of your sweet rasa. Otherwise she will give up \[moving\] her hands \[to communicate\].**

**Комментарий:**

Having Krsna hear of Radha’s extreme pain in separation, up to the tenth state, the *sakhi* speaks of her hopeless heart.

О image of the heavenly physician Danvantari! If you are pleased to give a cure, for her sickness of being separated from your form and beauty, will not Radha, possessing the best body (*yara-tanuh*) whose beauty is meant for you, live because of the great life-giving medicine, in the form of the nectar of your form and enjoyment with you in *srngara-rasa* (*te rasat*)? Yes she will live. Otherwise, not attaining your *rasa* (*anyatha*), she will give up moving her hands to communicate (*hastakah*). In a state of swoon, unable to speak, she interacts with the *sakhis* by moving her hands. But in the absence of meeting you, that will be given up, or, otherwise, medical procedures which involve using the hands will be given up. Another version has *tvatto ‘nyathanantaka*: if you are not pleased, then her death will be caused by you.

The actions caused by the fever of love are described as follows. Her hairs stand on end. She makes a hissing sound. Sound arises in her throat when her hair stands on end due to desire. She laments, “The deceiver does not meet me.” Or, she speaks about your qualities with intense longing. She shivers. \[Thinking about\] “How can I endure his unique qualities?”, she becomes exhausted (*tamyati*). This indicates change of complexion, a *sattvika-bhava* (involuntary physical responses to *rati*). She thinks, “How can I attain him?” By intense thought she becomes confused. This indicates *unmada*.

More details are given. She closes her eyes, because of external confusion. She then concentrates internally on you, and develops your form in her heart. She thus rises up. Being thin, she falls to the ground. Not attaining you, she faints. This is the state of *murccha* (unconsciousness).

In such a state (*eva*), she desires only you, the crest jewel of unlimited heroes, and the abode of all good qualities. Her desire is indicated by the appearance of *rasa* in her limbs - by the stages described such as *abhilasa*, by the *vyabhicari-bhavas* (transitory emotions) such as *cinta*, by the *anubhavas* such as hissing and by the *sattvika-bhavas* such as hairs standing on end. You should save her life.

Meeting her, you will taste a festival of astonishing *madhurya-rasa*. Her body is described as thin (*yara-tanuh*) to inform Krsna that he should not tolerate separation from her any longer. In the condition of fever, the characteristics are: hairs standing on end because of coolness, hissing because of pain, lamenting out of bewilderment, speaking, shivering, thinking “How can I live?”, as well as confusion, exhaustion, and fainting. Though she is in this condition, she can live by the perfect *rasa* administered by the great physician. She can live by meeting you. The ornament is *kriya-dipaka* and the poetic meter (*chanda*) is *sardida-vikriditam*.

### Text 4.20

*smaraturam daivata-vaidya-hrdya tvad-anga-sangamrta-matra-sadhyam \|*  
*nivrtta-badham kuruse na radham upendra-vajrad api daruno’si \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smaraturam* - \[Who is\] lovesick; *daivata* - of the gods; *vaidya* - \[than\] the physicians; *hrdya* - \[more\] attractive; *tvad-anga* - of your limbs; *sangamrta* - by the nectar of bliss through association; *matra-sadhyam* - only to be cured; *nivrtta* - is relieved; *badham* - one whose suffering; *kuruse* - you do make; *na* - not; *radham* - Radha; *upendra* - Of Indra; *vajrad* - than the thunderbolt; *api* - even; *daruno* - harder; *'si* - you are.

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna, more attractive than the heavenly physicians! You are harder than Indra’s thunderbolt. You do not relieve Radha of her pain in the forest, though she is suffering because of Cupid. Her life can be maintained only by your association.**

**Комментарий:**

О you who are more attractive than the physicians of the *devatas*, the Asvini-kumaras! Or, О knower of *rasa* who can revive dead lovers! Or, О attractive physician in the form of our worshipable deity - since you rescue us from the sickness of separation!

You do not relieve Radha of her pain (*nivrtta-badham*), who is in the nearby forest (*upa*) suffering from love (*smaraturam*). You are thus harder than Indra’s thunderbolt.

Or, О Upendra, who can perform actions impossible for Indra’s thunderbolt! Thus you are employed by Indra for his protection and thus you are harder than his thunderbolt.

\[For curing,\] one must first consider the proper medicine, and then make it. How can a medicine \[to cure Radha of her pain\] be obtained? “The only cure is the medicine which will give her life. That is the *rasa* of the supreme bliss (*amrta*) related to your body. Or, the only cure which gives life (*amrta*) is contact with your limbs. *Tvad-anga-sanga* can also mean “happiness of your limbs.” *Sanga* can mean happiness or attachment according to *Ratna-kosa*. *Upendra-vajra* is also the poetic meter of the verse.

### Text 4.21

*kandarpa-jvara-samjvaratura-tanor ascaryam asyas ciram*  
*cetas candana-candramah-kamalini-cintasu santamyati \|*  
*kintu ksanti-rasena sitala-tanum tvam ekam eva priyam*  
*dhyayanti rahasi sthita katham api ksina ksanam praniti \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kandarpa* - Of Cupid; *jvara* - of the fever; *samjvaratura* - \[is\] afflicted by the pain; *tanor* - whose body; *ascaryam* - how astonishing! *asyas* - of she; *ciram* - for a long time; *cetas* - the mind; *candana* -of sandalwood; *candramah* - of the moon; *kamalini* - \[and\] of the lotus; *cintasu* - in thoughts; *santamyati* - becomes exhausted; *kintu* -but; *ksanti-rasena* - with tolerance \[or: *klanti-vasena* - with fatigue\]; *sitala* - \[is\] cooling \[the fever\]; *tanum* - whose body; *tvam* - \[on\] you; *ekam* - alone; *eva* - only; *priyam* - \[her\] beloved; *dhyayanti* - meditating; *rahasi* - in a solitary place; *sthita* - situated; *katham* -somehow; *api* - even; *ksina* - weak; *ksanam* - for a moment; *praniti* - \[she\] remains alive.

**Перевод:**

**“How astonishing! With her body afflicted by the pain of Cupid’s fever, her mind has become exhausted for a long time by thinking of sandalwood, the moon and the lotus. Tolerating this, somehow meditating in a solitary place only on you, her beloved, whose body can cool the fever, she maintains her life for a few moments more.**

**Комментарий:**

How astonishing! Her heart becomes exhausted on remembering the lotus, the moon light and the Malaya breeze. This has happened for a long time to Radha whose body is pained by affliction of Cupid’s fever. This is the ornament called *virodha* (contradiction).

“How can she live with this suffering? It is astonishing.” With tolerance (*ksanti-rasena*) she meditates on you, her dear beloved, whose body can cool her fever of love, though it is difficult for her to meditate. Weakened, she will live only for a moment. In that moment you should go to her. If not, you will no longer be able to see her. So, you must hurry. Or, at every moment (*ksanam*) she mediates on you. By that thought, she continues to live. So by seeing you, she will be cured. Astonishment is shown by describing her as exhausted by thinking of cool things and living by gaining Krsna’s cool association. Another version has *klanti-vasena* (being fatigued) instead of *ksanti-rasena* (being tolerant). The poetic meter is *sardula-vikridltam*.

### Text 4.22

*ksanam api virahah pura na sehe*  
*nayana-nimilana-khinnaya yaya te \|*  
*svasiti katham asau rasala-sakham*  
*cira-virahena vilokya puspitagram \|\|22\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ksanam* - For a moment; *api* - even; *virahah* - the separation; *pura* -hitherto; na - not; *sehe* - is tolerated; *nayana* - of \[her\] eyes; *nimilana* - with the blinking; *khinnaya* - weary; *yayd* - by she (Radha) who; *te* - from you; *svasiti* - \[she\] laments; *katham* - how; *asau* - she; *rasala* - of the mango tree; *sakham* - the branch; *cira* - long; *virahena* - in separation; *vilokya* - having seen; *puspitagram* - with a flowering tip?

**Перевод:**

**“For a long time Radha has not been able to tolerate separation even for a moment. She laments, blinking her eyes. How can she remain alive in long separation on seeing the flowering tip of the mango branch?”**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes her feelings of separation in the presence of *uddipanas* (stimuli). Separation cannot be tolerated by Radha (*yayd*) even for a moment. She suffers even from closing her eyes. The creator has made eyes blink so that there is an obstacle to seeing the beloved.

*atati yad bhavan ahni kananam*  
*truti yugayate tvam apasyatam*  
*kutila-kuntalam sri-mukham ca te*  
*jada udiksatam paksma-krd drsam*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “When you go off to the forest during the day, a tiny fraction of a second becomes like a millennium for us because we cannot see you. And even when we can eagerly look upon your beautiful face, so lovely with its adornment of curly locks, we think the creator was foolish in making eyelids.” (SB 10.31.15)**

The last two lines of the verse mean, “How can she live (*svasiti*) in the spring, seeing the tip of the flowering mango branch, in long separation from you?” Or, “can she remain breathing which is endowed with such suffering?” The poetic meter is *puspitagra*.

### Text 4.23

*vrsti-vyakula-gokulavana-rasad uddhrtya govardhanam*  
*bibhrad ballava-vallabhabhir adhikanandac ciram cumbitah \|*  
*darpeneva tad-arpitadhara-tati-sindura-mudrankito*  
*bahur gopa-tanos tanotu bhavatam sreyamsi kamsa-dvisah \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**
