*vrsti* - Of rain; *vyakula* - from the disturbance; *gokulavana* - in the protection of Gokula; *rasad* - out of courage (or out of emotion); *uddhrtya* - having raised \[him\] up; *govardhanam* - Govardhana; *bibhrad* - suppporting; *ballava* - of the cowherds; *vallabhabhir* - by the wives; *adhikanandac* - out of intense bliss; *ciram* - for a long time; *cumbitah* - kissed; *darpena* - out of pride \[in their good fortune\]; *iva* - as if; *tad-arpitadhara-tati* - of their sloping lower-lips; *sindura* - of *sindura* (vermilion); *mudrankito* - \[and\] marked with the stamp; *bahur* - the arm; *gopa* - \[is that\] of a cowherd; *tanos* - whose body; *tanotu* - may \[it\] bestow; *bhavatam* - \[upon\] you; *sreyamsi* - auspiciousness; *kamsa* - of Kamsa; *dvisah* - of the enemy.

**Перевод:**

**May the arm of the killer of Kamsa, the arm of the cowherd, which raised and supported Govardhana to show courage in protecting Gokula from the disturbance of rain, \[the arm\] marked with the sindura from the lips of the gopis when they felt proud of their good fortune, and was kissed for a long time out of intense bliss by the gopis, bestow auspiciousness on you!**

**Комментарий:**

May the arm of Krsna, killer of Kamsa, spread auspiciousness to you, the listeners. People give gifts by using the hand or arm. Krsna is called killer of Kamsa to show his ability. He is described as follows. He has the body of a cowherd, having been born to most magnanimous Nanda, king of the cowherds. His power of giving is illustrated: his arm lifted and carried Govardhana Mountain. Why? He had prowess or passion (*rasa*) to give protection to Gokula, which was suffering because of rain. The arm is described: it was kissed by his beloved *gopis* out of intense bliss. The arm is further described: it is marked, out of pride in their good fortune, with vermilion caused by their lips kissing that arm. Or, it is marked by their lips, and is becoming red like *sindhura*. The poetic meter is *sardula-vikriditam*. Song nine is called the bracelet of *rasa*.

## Chapter Five. Sakanksa-pundarikaksah - Lotus-eyed Krsna full of desire

### Text 5.1

*aham iha nivasami yahi radham*  
*anunaya mad-vacanena canayethah \|*  
*iti madhu-ripuna sakhi niyukta*  
*svayam idam etya punar jagada radham \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aham* - “I; *iha* - here; *nivasami* - will remain, *yahi* - Go; *radham* -to Radha; *anunaya* - Pacify \[her\]; *mad-vacanena* - with my words; *canayethah* - And you should bring \[her to me\].” *iti* - Thus; *madhu-ripuna -* by the enemy of Madhu (Krsna); *sakhi* - a female friend; *niyukta* - \[was\] commanded; *svayam* - She herself; *idam* - as follows; *etya* - having approached \[her\]; *punar* - again; *jagada* - spoke; *radham* - to Radha.

**Перевод:**

**“Go to Radha and pacify her with my words. Bring her here. \[Until then,\] I will stay here.” With these words, Krsna engaged a sakhi. She went and spoke as follows to Radha.**

**Комментарий:**

In the previous chapter, Radha’s actions in prema were described. Now in the fifth chapter, Krsna’s actions are described. Having heard of Radha’s nature in separation, following her, he situates himself in an appointed bower and then personally approaches her, with a heart fearful of his offense, by sending the *sakhi* to her to pacify her, by explaining his suffering during separation.

A *sakhi* ordered in this way by Krsna, went and spoke the following verses to Radha. The name *Madhu-ripu* (enemy of sweetness) indicates his heavy pain (opposite of sweetness). What did he say?

“Go to Radha and pacify her with my words.”

“But she is angry. Should you not go personally to pacify her?”

“Since I have committed an offense, I cannot personally speak. You should pacify her and bring her here (*anayethah*)."

“The person who has fickleness cannot attain her.”

He then speaks with humility, “I am determined to stay here waiting for her for a long time.”

**\|\| Song Ten \|\|**

*desi-varadi-raga, rupaka-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***desi-varadi*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***rupaka*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *varadi-raga* is described as follows:

*vinodayanti dayitam sukesi*  
*sukankana camara-calanena \|*  
*karne dadhana sura-puspa-guccham*  
*varanganeyam kathita varadi \|\|*

***Varadi-raga*** **is said to be a beautiful woman with beautiful hair, beautiful bracelets, wearing sura flowers in her ear, who plays with her lover while waving a** ***camara.***

### Text 5.2

*vahati malaya-samire madanam upanidhaya \|*  
*sphutati kusuma-nikare virahi-hrdaya-dalanaya \|\|2\|\|*

*tava virahe vana-mali sakhi sidati \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vahati* - While \[it\] blows; *malaya* - of Malaya mountain; *samire* - the breeze; *madanam* - passion; *upanidhaya* - inciting; *sphutati* - \[and\] while \[it\] blooms; *kusuma* - of flowers; *nikare* - the multitude; *virahi* - of the separated \[lover\]; *hrdaya* - of the heart; *dalanaya* - for the piercing; *tava* - from you; *virahe* - in separation; *vana* - of the forest; *mali* - wearing a garland; *sakhi* - о friend; *sidati* - \[Krsna\] remains.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi says to Radha\] “While the Malaya breeze blows, inciting passion, while all the flowers bloom, to pierce the heart of the lonely lover...**

**“O friend! Krsna, wearing a flower garland, remains morose, in separation from you. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi* describes Krsna in intense separation, by listing his *uddipanas* (stimuli). О friend Radha! Wearing a forest garland, Krsna is morose in separation from you. He wears a garland which touched your limbs, which was obtained from you after many requests while previously enjoying together. He is inclined to you, and is morose. You should go and meet him. This is suggested (that he is attracted) by mentioning the flower garland. Or, the previous garlands have been given up. All alone he wears another garland in the designated attractive bower and waits there. Therefore you must go there. This is the chorus.

He remains there morose, while the Malaya wind blows, producing intoxication (*madanam*), while the flower clusters blossom, to give pain to his heart. *Madanam* means that which produces intoxication or joy. For others, it produces joy and for him, it produces madness, since he gets pierced by Cupid’s flower arrows. The wind obedient to Cupid, and the arrows sent by Cupid cause injury. Or, while the wind blows, inciting desire, while all the flowers blossom, Krsna sits there morosely.

### Text 5.3

*dahati sisira-mayukhe maranam anukaroti \|*  
*patati madana-visikhe vilapati vikalataro’ti \|\|3\|\|*

*tava virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dahati* - While \[it\] gives pain; *sisira* - of the moon; *mayukhe* - the light; *maranam* - death; *anukaroti* - \[he\] imitates; *patati* - \[and\] while \[it\] falls; *madana* - of Cupid; *visikhe* - the arrow; *vilapati* -\[he\] laments; *vikalataro’ti* - to the extreme.

**Перевод:**

**“While the moon gives him pain, he reaches a death-like condition. While Cupid’s arrows fall upon him, he laments to the extreme.**

**“O friend! Krsna, wearing a flower garland...**

**Комментарий:**

While the cool moon gives him pain, he appears to be dying. He experiences the pain arising from the tenth stage—death. Experiencing your face, he criticizes the moon. Though the moon alleviates everyone’s pain, in separation from you he experiences pain from it. The word *anukaroti* (imitates) indicates that death actually cannot take place. He experiences pain similar to death. He maintains his life with difficulty, with the hope of attaining you. While Cupid’s arrows are hitting him, he laments to the extreme (*vikalatarah*). “O beloved! Where are you? Save me, save me!”

### Text 5.4

*dhvanati madhupa-samuhe sravanam apidadhati \|*  
*manasi kalita-virahe nisi nisi rujam upayati \|\|4\|\|*

*tava virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dhvanati* - When \[it\] hums; *madhupa* - of bees; *samuhe* - the flock; *sravanam* - \[his\] ear; *apidadhati* - \[he\] blocks; *manasi* - \[and\] when \[his\] mind; *kalita* - is produced; *virahe* - \[is one\] in which separation; *nisi nisi* - every night; *rujam* - \[greater\] pain; *upayati* -\[he\] experiences.

**Перевод:**

**“When all the bees hum, he blocks his ears. With his mind afflicted by separation, every night his pain increases.**

**“O friend! Krsna, wearing a flower garland...**

**Комментарий:**

Moreover, while all the bees, the honey drinkers, hum, he covers his ears with his hands. The bees, followers of Cupid, intoxicated from drinking honey, proclaim the victory of Cupid. In terror, he blocks his ears. Fixed only on you, day and night he desires only you, giving up all objects of the senses such as sound, in order to attain you. Or, having drunk the nectar of your most sweet, enchanting words, how can he hear any other sounds?

By day he is disturbed by whatever is seen and heard. Thus he rejects all the objects of the senses.

“But at night can he not be happy?”

With his mind feeling separation from you, every night he experiences even greater pain. This is because night increases the sickness of separation. With such increase of sickness at night, a moment appears like a *yuga*.

### Text 5.5

*vasati vipina-vitane tyajati lalita-dhama \|*  
*luthati dharani-sayane bahu vilapati tava nama \|\|5\|\|*

*tava virahe...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vasati* - \[He\] dwells; *vipina* - of the forest; *vitane* - in the expanse; *tyajati* - \[he\] gives up; *lalita* - attractive; *dhama* - \[his\] house; *luthati* - \[he\] rolls; *dharani* - of earth; *sayane* - on \[his\] bed; *bahu* -repeatedly; *vilapati* - \[and he\] utters; *tava* - your; *nama* - name.

**Перевод:**

**“Giving up his attractive house, he lives in the depths of the forest. He rolls on his earthen bed and repeatedly utters your name.**

**“O friend! Krsna, wearing a flower garland...”**

**Комментарий:**

“He is adored by many people. Can they not make him happy?”

He gives up Nanda’s attractive house and Vraja. He remains in the depth of the forest. Giving up friends, like a yogi, he lives deep in the forest. How can they make him happy? Or, he rejects his beautiful body. Without you, it appears perishable. *Dhama* means a house or a body according to *Visva-prakasa*. This also means he gives up everything else.

“What is the proof that he has such love for me?” This is shown by describing his *anubhavas*. He rolls around on his bed made of the forest soil, sprinkled with the scent of your lotus feet. He intently (*vi*) utters (*lapati*) your name repeatedly. “O dear Radha! О goddess of my life!” You should not criticize him, since he is fixed on you alone, having given up all other methods and goals.

### Text 5.6

*bhanati kavi-jayadeve viraha-vilasitena \|*  
*manasi rabhasa-vibhave harir udayatu sukrtena \|\|6\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhanati* - While \[he\] speaks; *kavi-jayadeve* - the poet Jayadeva; *viraha* - of separation; *vilasitena* - with the pastimes; *manasi* - in the mind \[of the listeners\]; *rabhasa* - \[is\] frenzied love; *vibhave* - whose condition; *harir* - Krsna; *udayatu* - may \[he\] appear; *sukrtena* - with \[a song\] well-composed.

**Перевод:**

**While Jayadeva recites this poem, may Krsna with his pastimes of separation appear in the listener’s mind which is filled with his frenzied love and is qualified by past devotional acts. (END OF SONG TEN)**

**Комментарий:**

While Jayadeva recites this poem, may Krsna manifest himself with his pastimes of separation in the minds of the singers and listeners. \[For Krsna to reveal himself,\] the minds should be filled with his frenzied *prema* (*rabhasa-vibhave*). This will happen because of good acts in the form of worshipping Bhagavan’s form, or because the song written by Jayadeva (*krtena*) is splendid (*su*).

Radha was afflicted in her heart, because of being unable to tolerate hearing the suffering of her beloved. Seeing this, the *sakhi* stopped speaking. This tenth song thus has only five verses (instead of eight).

### Text 5.7

*purvam yatra samam tvaya rati-pater asaditah siddhayas*  
*tasminn eva nikunja-manmatha-maha-tirthe punar madhavah \|*  
*dhyayams tvam anisam japann api tavaivalapa-mantravalim*  
*bhuyas tvat-kuca-kumbha-nirbhara-parirambhamrtam vanchati \|\|7\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*purvam* - Previously; *yatra* - where; *samam tvaya* - \[along with\] you;  *rati-pater* - of Cupid; *asaditah* - were attained; *siddhayas* - the powers;  *tasminn* - that; *eva* - only; *nikunja* - \[which is\] a bower; *manmatha*  - of Cupid; *maha-tirthe* - in \[that\] great tirtha \[holy place\]; *punar* - again; *madhavah* - Madhava (Krsna); *dhyayams* - meditating; *tvam* - \[on\] you; *anisam* - constantly; *japann* - muttering; *api* - even;  *tavaivalapa* - \[which are\] as if your conversations; *mantravalim* - a  host of mantras; *bhuyas* - again; *tvat* - your; *kuca* - with breasts; *kumbha* - pot; *nirbhara* - deep; *parirambhamrtam* - the nectar (bliss) of embrace; *vanchati* - \[he\] desires.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “Krsna again desires the bliss of deeply embracing your pot-like breasts. He meditates on you constantly, while muttering a host of mantras in the form of your conversations, in a bower sanctified as the great tirtha of Cupid, where previously he attained powers along with you.**

**Комментарий:**

Previously it was said that fixed on Radha, he desired only her. That is shown in this verse. О friend! Krsna again (*bhuyas*) desires the great bliss of the deep embrace of your pot-like breasts. \[Krsna considers that\] the great bliss of the embrace is like nectar because of its taste and life-giving qualities. It is suggested that her breasts are like two golden pots filled with nectar. The word Madhava (husband of Laksmi) suggests that Krsna, endowed with the previously mentioned qualities, is controlled by Radha.

“Why does he desire this supreme bliss without worshipping devatas?”

“He meditates continually on you. You are his worshipable deity. And he utters a host (*avalim*) of mantras in the form of your private conversations which shower the greatest sweetness.” Her words are called mantras since he repeats them like sacred incantations in his heart.

“Will not uttering mantras bring quick success, if uttered at the holy place (*tirtha*) of one’s *devata*?”

“He utters the mantras at the great holy place (*maha-tirtha*) of Cupid’s (*manmatha*) bower.” *Manmatha* means one who agitates the mind. A *nikunja* is a hollow area within creepers. Here, the *nikunja* has taken the form of Cupid. Or, he utters mantras in the bower where the pastimes of Cupid are performed. He dwells in that great holy place which quickly produces results for the mantra. The greatness of the place is shown as follows. There, previously Cupid, along with you who is dearer to Krsna than millions of his life airs, attained *siddhis*.

The word *avali* (group) indicates that he uttered whole groups of conversations which were like a garland of mantras. When the mantra reaches success, it is able to bestow results.

*svapna-prapte striya datte mala-mantre tathaiva ca \|*  
*vaidikesu ca sarvesu siddhadin naiva sodhayet \|\|*  
*grha-gostha-vanarama-nadi-naga-suralaye \|*  
*samipe va guroh siddhih sighram syad uttarottara \|\|*

**One should not purify mantras to gain** ***siddhis*** **in the case of any Vedic mantras, mantras attained in dreams, mantras given by a woman, or** ***mala-mantra*****. The** ***siddhis*** **of mantras quickly manifest if chanted in a house, cowshed, forest, garden, river, mountain, temple, or in front of a** ***guru*** **with increasing effect.**

The word eva indicates that Krsna is fixed in the bower. He will remain there (*yasan*) until his chanting succeeds in bringing a result. Meditating on her, uttering the *siddha-mantras* at a *siddha-ksetra*, while being completely steady, he will quickly attain the *siddhi.* This skillful description gives rise to *srngara-rasa* of the couple.

Thus ends the tenth song called *Hari-samudaya-garuda-pada*: Krsna tries to extract the poison of separation, like Garuda destroying snakes.

**\|\| Song Eleven \|\|**

*gurjari-raga, ekatali-tala.*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***gurjari*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *gurjan* and the *tala* is *ekatali*.

### Text 5.8

*rati-sukha-sare gatam abhisare madana-manohara-vesam \|*  
*na kuru nitambini gamana-vilambanam anusara tam hrdayesam \|\|8\|\|*

*dhira-samire yamuna-tire vasati vane vana-mali \|*  
*pina-payodhara-parisara-mardana-cancala-kara-yuga-sali \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rati* - Of love; *sukha* - with the happiness; sure - excellent; *gatam* -having gone; *abhisare* - to the meeting place; *madana* - to Cupid; *manohara* - \[are\] attractive; *vesam* - whose clothes; *na* - \[do\] not; *kuru* - make; *nitambini* - о woman (Radha) with beautiful hips; *gamana* - of \[your\] going; *vilambanam* - a delay; *anusara* - \[and\] go to; *tam* - him \[Krsna\]; *hrdayesam* - the lord of \[your\] heart (or he whose lord is your heart); *dhira* - \[is\] blowing; samire - \[where\] the breeze; *yamuna* - of the Yamuna; *tire* - on the bank; *vasati* - he waits; *vane* - in the glade; *vana* - of the forest; *mali* - wearing a garland; *pina* - mature; *payodhara* - \[your\] breasts; *parisara* - \[which are\] adjacent; *mardana* - for massaging; *cancala* - restless; *kara* - of hands; *yuga* - a pair; *sail* - possessed of.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi says to Radha\] “Please go to Krsna, the lord of your heart and do not be late. О Radha with beautiful hips! Wearing clothing attractive even to Cupid, he has gone to the meeting place, excellent with the happiness of love.**

**“Wearing a forest garland, he waits in a glade on the bank of the Yamuna, where the gentle breeze is blowing. His hands are restless to massage your mature breasts which are next to them. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The meaning is this. О Radha with beautiful hips! Go and meet the lord of your heart, dearer than a million lives. The phrase ‘lord of your heart’ suggests “just as Krsna cannot tolerate a moment’s separation, your heart is also controlled by him.” Or *hrdayesam* can mean “he whose lord is your heart.” This means he is obedient to your heart.

*Nitambini* indicates that because of her wide hips she walks slowly. But now she should not delay. Do not be late (*vilambanam*) for the meeting. Krsna is described as follows. He has gone to the place appointed for the meeting. *Abhisara* means the place in which one takes shelter. That place is described as follows. It has excellence (*sare*) of happiness of enjoyment. Or, it is the highest form (*sare*), since it provides for the happiness of union (*rati-sukha*).

Krsna is described as follows. He is wearing decorations fit for the meeting, which attract the mind of Cupid. How to describe the beauty of his limbs? Or, he wears cloth which attracts the mind and destroys Cupid (*ma-danam*). Or, he is Cupid (*madanam*) and \[has\] the form (*vesa*) which broke the cart (*anohara*). This shows his prowess as a hero. Or, he wears clothing which makes him more attractive than Cupid. Since he strongly attracts your heart, as the Cupid of women of Vraja, you should meet with him quickly.

The meeting place is described as follows. Wearing a forest garland, he waits in a deep glade on the bank of the Yamuna. Cheating countless other women who tried to meet him, wearing another forest garland (perhaps this garland is not given by another *gopi*), he waits in the deep glade hiding himself in order to meet you.

Wearing a flower garland indicates his great desire to embrace her.

The bank of the Yamuna has gentle breezes blowing. The bank of the Yamuna and the forest indicate coolness and fragrance. Thus the place is served by gentle, cool, fragrant breezes. *Dhira* can also mean clever. The wind is careful to serve you with all its qualities. *Dhira* means wise and slow.

### Text 5.9

*nama-sametam krta-sanketam vadayate mrdu venum \|*  
*bahu manute nanu te tanu-sangata-pavana-calitam api renum \|\|9\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nama* - With \[your\] name; *sametam* - furnished; *krta* - \[is\] made; *sanketam* - by which an indication \[for the meeting\]; *vadayate* -\[he\] plays; *mrdu* - softly; *venum* - \[his\] flute; *bahu* - Greatly; *manute* - \[he\] reveres; *nanu* - ah! *te* - your; *tanu* - body; *sangata* - touching; *pavana* - by the wind; *calitam* - carried; *api* - even; *renum* - the dust.

**Перевод:**

**“He softly plays his flute which gives indications for the meeting, while uttering your name. Ah! He greatly reveres the dust carried by the wind which has touched your body.**

**“Wearing a forest garland...**

**Комментарий:**

“Why does he wait for me? Why is he attracted to me?”

Your beloved now is playing his flute softly (*mrdu*) since the fluteplaying is filled with his heart that is melted by singing your name, while not letting other women hear. He is experiencing *anuraga* for you.

The flute also indicates the meeting (*krta-sanketam*) with names (*nama-sametam*) by saying “O Radha! О goddess of my life!” (the music uses names to indicate the meeting) It reveals a hidden meaning through its song. Or, the flute is played where there will be a meeting (*krta-sanketam*). \[Krsna says to *sakhi*\] “When I play the flute, you bring Radha to that place.” Or, the flute proclaims your good fortune by uttering your names: “She is my lover, dearer than a million lives. I am waiting to meet her. She should not bring others. She should not go to another place by mistake.”

*Nanu* is an exclamation. He holds in great reverence the particles of dust which by chance have been carried to him, not on her order, by the wind which touched your limbs or had some contact by touching your cloth. He greatly respects those particles which came by chance. The dust which has been touched by the wind which touched your limbs is most fortunate. \[Krsna says,\] “I am fortunate to have been able to touch that dust touched by the wind which touched you. If that is so, what can one say of the *rasa* arising from directly contacting your limbs?”

Instead of *nanu* sometimes *tanu* is seen. “O Radha with beautiful body!” Or, it can be the verb *tanute*. He respects the dust and then glorifies it (*tanute*) while making gestures. “This dust gives the highest bliss, and is cool.” Or, *tanu* can stand for the *adverb atanu* (not a little).

### Text 5.10

*patati patatre vicalati patre sankita-bhavad-upayanam \|*  
*racayati sayanam sacakita-nayanam pasyati tava panthanam \|\|10\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*patati* - \[While they\] fly; *patatre* - the birds; *vicalati* - \[and while they\] flutter; *patre* - the leaves; *sankita* - \[is\] expected; *bhavad-upayanam* - on which your approach; *racayati* - \[he\] makes; *sayanam* - a bed; *sacakita-nayanam* - with restless eyes; *pasyati* - \[and he\] sees; *tava* - your; *panthanam* - path.

**Перевод:**

**“He makes a flower bed and with restless eyes, gazes at the path that you are expected to take, while birds fly from tree to tree and the leaves flutter.**  
**“Wearing a forest garland...**

**Комментарий:**

Krsna makes a bed for you out of the most tender shoots and flower petals using his two tender hands which are like shoots. The bed is for pastimes of love and is furnished with various colorful, scented pillows. He gazes at the path by which you come, with glances that are restless because of longing, passion and love. This indicates that all his senses, not just his eyes, are absorbed in Radha.

He shows that by gestures. He gazes at the path on which you are supposed (*sankita*) to come (*bhavad-upayanam*). *Bhavati* is in the masculine (*bhavad*) form since pronouns can be masculine in compounds. “He gazes while birds fly from tree to tree and the leaves flutter.” - this reflects the condition of his mind.

### Text 5.11

*mukharam adhiram tyaja manjiram ripum iva keli-sulolam \|*  
*cala sakhi kunjam sa-timira-punjam silaya nila-nicolam \|\|11\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mukharam* - Noisy; *adhiram* - ignorant; *tyaja* - give up; *manjiram* -\[your\] ankle-bells; *ripum* - an enemy; *iva* - as if; *kelisu -* in pastimes; *lolam* - fickle; *cala* - go; *sakhi* - о friend; *kunjam* - to the bower; *sa-timira* - \[is\] covered with darkness; *punjam* - whose mass; *silaya* - \[and\] wear; *nila* - black; *nicolam* - a mantle.

**Перевод:**

**“O friend! Go to the dark bower, wearing a black mantle. Give up your ignorant, noisy ankle bells, since they are a like an enemy, acting fickle when you engage in pastimes.**

**“Wearing a forest garland...**

**Комментарий:**

“Just as Krsna has gone for the meeting, you should \[also\] prepare for the meeting.” \[This statement is the impetus for this verse.\]

О friend! Go to the bower. Wear a black mantle, so you will appear as \[one with\] darkness. Shining with effulgence \[of your body\] will be an obstacle to the meeting. Or, go to the bower which is most dark. The dark bower and the dark cloth are elements which nourish *rasa* by being favorable to the meeting. It is also hinted that by wearing the dark cloth, her golden beauty will be most attractive to Krsna.

Unfavorable elements must be discarded. Give up your ankle bells which are ignorant (*adhiram*) or bold since they are noisy. This is unsuitable for the meeting. Moreover the ankle bells are fickle in playing (*kelisu lolam*). *Keli-sulolam* is another version. They are most fickle when you play. They are like enemies since they are an obstacle for tasting the nectar of clever conversations in the pastimes of enjoyment. They should be given up like enemies. Since they (the ankle bells) are somewhat favorable in *srngara-rasa*, they are worn by persons who are silent.

### Text 5.12

*urasi murarer upahita-hare ghana iva tarala-balake \|*  
*tadid iva pite rati-viparite rajasi sukrta-vipake \|\|12\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*urasi* - On the chest; *murarer* - of Murari (Krsna); *upahita* - is placed; *hare* - on which a necklace; *ghana* - a cloud; *iva* - as if; *tarala* - \[are\] fickle; *balake* - on which women \[or: on which cranes are moving\]; *tadid* - lightning; *iva* - as if; *pite* - golden; *rati* - in lovemaking; *viparite* - \[when you take\] the dominant role; *rajasi* - you \[will\] shine; *sukrta* - of good character; *vipake* - \[as\] the final result.

**Перевод:**

**“O golden Radha! When you take the dominant role in lovemaking, you will shine like fickle lightning on Krsna’s chest, devoid of ornaments, like a cloud with a line of moving cranes, as the final result of his good character.**

**“Wearing a forest garland...**

**Комментарий:**

“Beauty will be lost by giving up ornaments. When the limbs and ornaments meet together, there will be great beauty.” The *sakhi* stimulates her longing with this verse.

О golden limbed Radha (*pite*)! You will shine (*rajasi*) on the chest of Krsna (*murareh*). *Murari* means ‘one who surrounds (*murayati*) with his arms, thirsty for embracing.’ Rajasi, in the present tense, expresses the near future - (*Panini* 3.3.131). This will happen when you take the dominant position in love making (*rati-viparite*).

His chest is described as follows. It is devoid of ornaments which create obstacles to embracing you, when you take the male role. Or, he has placed a pearl necklace on his neck in order to show special beauty when he skillfully embraces. His chest is like a cloud with a line of cranes flying in it. It is suggested that the necklace is also fickle in taking the male role.

Radha is like lightning (*tadit*) which astonishes a person, with its variety. It also suggests her fragrance. This is an example of *adbhutopama* (simile involving wonder): in the sky there is a rain cloud (Krsna’s chest) and above it, a line of flying cranes (pearl necklace) and above that is fickle lightning (Radha), causing astonishment. If she is there, then the rest of the scene manifests.

When that happens, then there is more attraction. The chest becomes the highest astonishing result (*vipake*) of splendid actions (*sukrta*). Or, the chest is the result of excellent pastimes when the couple’s roles are reversed.

Or, *sukrta-vipake* can modify *taraka-balake.* His chest is the residence of fickle (*tarala*) women overcome with desire for enjoyment (*balake*), which becomes golden (*pite*) with minerals from contact the woman who is in the dominant position, which is the final result (Radha’s actions), for Krsna (*sukrta*).

Or, the phrases can be calling out to Radha. О Radha with a necklace gifted by Krsna on the first meeting (*upahita-hare*)! О Radha, fickle girl (*tarala-balake*)! This shows pacification by giving gifts to her. That necklace on your neck will look splendid when you are on his chest. *Tarala-balake* indicates her nature to dominate. Thus she is addressed as “O woman in the dominant role (*rati-viparite*)."

“O ultimate form (*vipake*) for his highest pious acts (*sukrta*)!” - she is addressed in this way since he desires her as his only goal. Or, this phrase can modify *rati-viparite*. “O woman in the dominant role, the form of the highest piety for both of you!” The cause for being the highest form is *tarala-balake* (fickle girl) and *upahita-hare* (wearing a necklace given by him)

*Balaka* means a girl or a woman full of desire according to *Sasvata*. *Vipaka* means something hard to attain according to *Visva*.

### Text 5.13

*vigalita-vasanam parihrta-rasanam*  
*ghataya jaghanam apidhanam \|*  
*kisalaya-sayane pankaja-nayane*  
*nidhim iva harsa-nidhanam \|\|13\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vigalita* - \[Is\] fallen; *vasanam* - from which cloth; *parihrta* - \[is\] removed; rasanam - from which the belt (with bells); *ghataya* -place; *jaghanam* - \[your\] hips; *apidhanam* - without a covering; *kisalaya* - of shoots; *sayane* - on the bed; *pankaja-nayane* - о lotuseyed woman \[Radha\]; *nidhim* - a treasure; *iva* - as if; *harsa* - of bliss; *nidhanam* - the abode.

**Перевод:**

**“O lotus eyed Radha! Place your hips devoid of cloth and bells, the wealth of Krsna and the abode of bliss, on the bed of shoots.**

**“Wearing a forest garland...**

**Комментарий:**

Having described Radha’s dominant role, her actions are described. О lotus eyed Radha! Place your hips on the fresh bed of shoots made by Krsna. The cloth has fallen off your hips, during the exploits of *srngara-rasa* of the most expert person, in the war with your lover, who is more attractive than millions of Cupids. Your hips are now without covering (*apidhanam*). Small bells have been removed by lotus-eyed Krsna who is dear to your heart and is under the influence of Cupid’s arrows. Those hips are the abode of the highest bliss, like a treasure (*nidhim*)—the desired treasure of the crest jewel of heroes.

Or her role as the dominant lover is shown: О friend! Join your hips devoid of cloth and bells with lotus-eyed Krsna, on the bed. Since the sound of the bells is unsuitable on this occasion, it is proper to have the hips devoid of cloth (since the belt with bells holds up the cloth). The rest is the same.

### Text 5.14

*harir abhimani rajanir idanim*  
*iyam api yati viramam \|*  
*kuru mama vacanam satvara-racanam*  
*puraya madhu-ripu-kamam \|\|14\|\|*

*dhira-samire...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*harir* - Krsna; *abhimani* - regards \[you as his beloved\]; *rajanir* -Night; *idanim* - now; *iyam* - this; *api* - even; *yati* - is going; *viramam* - to \[its\] end; *kuru* - Follow; *mama* - my; *vacanam* - orders; *satvara* - \[is\] swift; *racanam* - whose accomplishment; *puraya* - Fulfill; *madhu-ripu* - of Madhuripu (Krsna, the enemy of spring); *kamam* - the desire.

**Перевод:**

**“Krsna accepts you as his beloved. The end of night is coming. Quickly follow my orders. Fulfill the desires of Krsna \[who has been overcome with love\].**

**“Wearing a forest garland.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing Radha delay, the *sakhi* shows the negative results of not going (*bheda*).

The most attractive male (*harih*) regards you as dearest in all ways (*abhimani*) or has the greatest affection for you in *prema*.  The meaning is this: Just as you think of him as the dearest, he also thinks of you, who can satisfy all his desires, as his dearest. Therefore fully satisfy the desires (in the form of enjoyment with  you) of he who is the enemy of spring (*madhu-ripu*). He is called as the enemy of spring, since spring disturbs him. Overcome by the  enemy, spring will fall under his control with your help, on being  made blissful.

“This night is going to end now. Please follow my instructions so that you can go quickly (*satvara-racanam*)."

\[“What’s is the instruction?”\]

“I said, ‘Go to the bower.’ Please follow that instruction, to fulfill the desires of your beloved. If you become indifferent at the end of night because of stubbornness, you will fall ill in the sickroom, with sorrow from causeless separation.” This is *bheda*, showing negative results by not following the instructions.

Some say that *abhimani* means proud. But this is not correct since he is submissive to Radha. Assuming qualities of the *dhrsta-nayaka* (audacious lover) would destroy the *rasa*. Another version has *atimani* instead of *abhimani*. *Harih* then means ‘he who prevents destruction of love.’ He gives great respect (*atimani*) to enjoyment. With the appearance of ingredients to nourish *rasa*, now fulfill Krsna’s desire to enjoy.

### Text 5.15

*sri-jayadeve krta-hari-seve*  
*bhanati parama-ramaniyam \|*  
*pramudita-hrdayam harim atisadayam*  
*namata sukrta-kamaniyam \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeve* - Sri Jayadeva; *krta* - is done; *hari* - to Krsna; *seve* -by whom service; bhanati - \[since he\] recites \[this story\]; *parama-ramaniyam* - most attractive; *pramudita* - \[is\] blissful; *hrdayam* - whose heart; *harim* - Krsna; *atisadayam* - most compassionate; *namata* - offer respects; *sukrta* - with splendid pastimes; *kamaniyam* - \[and\] pleasant

**Перевод:**

**Offer respects to Krsna, whose heart is blissful, who is most compassionate to devotees, since Jayadeva, doing service to Krsna, has recited this most attractive story, pleasant with pastimes. (END OF SONG ELEVEN)**

**Комментарий:**

О devotees! Offer respects to Krsna who has been described. Worship him by cultivating the actions described. His heart is endowed with bliss because of zeal to meet his beloved. He is most compassionate (*atisadayam*) since he is affectionate to his devotees. Or *sadayam* can mean the good fortune (*ayam'*) of devotees (*sad*). Krsna creates good fortune for the devotees by attracting everyone’s minds, through revealing his complete qualities, when he relates to the supreme heroine. Jayadeva thus records his own realization. It is commonly said that Krsna appeared in the house of Jayadeva because of his writing this work.

Why \[one should offer respects to Krsna\] ? One should offer respects since Jayadeva who has done this service for Krsna (*krta-hari-seve*) narrates this story of Radha and Krsna which is most attractive (*parama-ramaniyam*). This story is supreme because it is the form of the highest bliss. It is attractive because it contains *rasa*. It is most desirable (*kamaniyam*) with splendid actions (*sukrta*). Or, it is attained by performing millions of deserving acts—by associating with great devotees through Krsna’s mercy. The words *parama-ramaniyam* can modify *harim*: Krsna is most desirable. *Sukrta-kamaniyam* can also modify *harim*: Krsna is beautiful (*kamaniyam*) with splendid actions (*sukrta*).

This verse describes the heroine preparing to meet her lover (*abhisarika*). The qualities are explained:

*madananala-santapta yabhisarayati priyam \|*  
*jyotsna-tamasvim yana-yogyambara-vibhusana \|\|*

**The women who goes to meet her lover, burns with the fire of passion. She has a vehicle, is dressed and ornamented properly, and she eclipses the moon light. (*****Rasarnava-sudhakara*****)**

### Text 5.16

*vikirati muhuh svasan asah pure muhur iksate*  
*pravisati muhuh kunjam gunjan muhur bahu tamyati \|*  
*racayati muhuh sayyam paryakulam muhur iksate*  
*madana-kadana-klantah kante priyas tava vartate \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vikirati* - \[He\] heaves; *muhuh* - constantly; *svasan* - sighs; *asah* - in every direction; *puro* - in front \[of him\]; *muhur* - every moment; *iksate* - he gazes; *pravisati* - \[he\] enters; *muhuh* - repeatedly; *kunjam* - the bower; *gunjan* - buzzing; *muhur* - constantly; *bahu* -very; *tamyati* - \[he\] becomes tired; *racayati* - \[he\] makes; *muhuh* - repeatedly; *sayyam* - a bed; *paryakulam* - full of consusion; *muhur* - repeatedly; *iksate* - \[he\] gazes; *madana* - \[Inflicted by\] Cupid; *kadana* - from the pains; *klantah* - exhausted; *kante* - о beautiful woman (Radha); *priyas* - lover; *tava* - your; *vartate* - has become;

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues\] “O Radha! Your lover has become exhausted from the pains inflicted by Cupid. He exhales constantly in pain, and gazes in all directions in front of him at every moment. He repeatedly enters the bower filled with the humming of bees, and constantly develops great fatigue. He repeatedly makes a bed and repeatedly looks around in confusion.**

**Комментарий:**

Other activities in going for the meeting are described in this verse. О Radha, you control your lover by your beauty (*kante*)! Your lover is fading away from love’s pain due to separation from you. He constantly exhales sighs of pain by thinking of your arrival. He constantly looks at the directions (*asah*) in front of him. This means he looks at all direction simultaneously, thinking in confusion, “From which direction will she come?”

Not seeing you there, he repeatedly enters the appointed bower. He looks at the path and thinks, “By which path will she enter the bower?” and then enters the bower. This shows his great longing. The bower resounds with bees. He then becomes very (*bahu*) tired. In spite of this condition, he repeatedly makes a bed using astonishing methods, out of *anuraga* for you. There he again looks in all directions.

Another version has *vikirati muhuh svasan nasa-puro muhur iksate, pravisati muhuh kunjam kujan muhur bahu tamyati*: he meditates on you (*nasa-puro muhur iksate*), enters the bower while uttering in pain (*kujan*) “O beloved, goddess of my life!” Then he becomes exhausted. The poetic meter is *harini.* The ornament is *dipaka*.

### Text 5.17

*tvad-vamyena samam samagram adhuna tigmamsur astam gato*  
*govindasya manorathena ca samam praptam tamah sandratam \|*  
*kokanam karuna-svanena sadrsi dirgha-mad-abhyarthana*  
*tan mugdhe viphalam vilambanam asau ramyo’bhisara-ksanah \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvad-vamyena* - with your obstinacy; *samam* - along; *samagram* -completely; *adhuna* - now; *tigmamsur* - the sun; *astam gato* - \[has\] set; *govindasya* - of Govinda (Krsna); *manorathena* - with the desire; *ca* - and; *samam* - along; *praptam* - \[has\] attained the state; *tamah* -the darkness; *sandratam* - of thickness; *kokanam* - Of the cakravaka birds; *karuna* - pitiable; *svanena* - the cry; *sadrsi* - \[is\] like; *dirgha* -long\[-cherished\]; *mad* - my; *abhyarthana* - request; *tan* - Therefore; *mugdhe* - о beautiful woman \[Radha\]; *viphalam* - \[is\] pointless; *vilambanam* - delay; *asau* - This \[is\]; *ramyo* - pleasant; *‘bhisara* - for meeting; *ksanah* - a time.

**Перевод:**

**“O beautiful Radha! Delay is of no value. This is the appropriate and pleasant time for meeting. Along with your obstinacy, the sun has now set completely. Along with Govinda’s desire, the darkness has become intense. My long cherished request is like the pitiable cry of the cakravaka.**

**Комментарий:**

You should go immediately. The reason is given. “O Radha, controlling your lover by your beauty (*mugdhe*)! О beautiful Radha!” Also the word suggests “O Radha, ignorant of performing your actions (*mugdhe*)!" Delaying is useless since it is simply an obstacle to attaining happiness. This is the appropriate time for the meeting, and it is most attractive.

The time is illustrated as follows. Along with (*samam*) your crooked nature, the sun (*tigmamsuh*) has now completely (*samagram*) set. Or, endowed with complete sweetness (*samagra-madhuna*) the harshness of your heart, arising from the pain of separation, has disappeared. The darkness has become thick along with the desire of Govinda. His desire and the darkness have both become intense. His desire has become intense because darkness acts as an *uddipana* or stimulus for love and darkness is a good reason for his beloved to come.

This (don’t delay) has been my request for a long time, equal to the pitiable cry of the *cakravaka* bird. The *cakravaka* cries at night, in separation from its lover and thus its cry is pitiable. It is not proper to delay, since your lover is solely devoted to you and eager, since there is thick darkness, and since you should be merciful to me who is most fallen and crooked.

The poetic ornament is sahokti. This is explained in Kavyadarsa written by Dandl. *sahoktih saha-bhavena kathanam guna-karmayoh*: *sahokti* means describing qualities and actions, accompanied by other things - (*Kavyadarsa* 2.351). An example is *saha dirgha mama svasair imah samprati ratrayah*: now the nights are long, along with my breathing.

### Text 5.18

*aslesad anu cumbanad anu nakho-lekhad anu svantaja-*  
*prodbodhad anu sambhramad anu ratarambhad anu pritayoh \|*  
*anyartham gatayor bhraman militayoh sambhasanair janator*  
*dampatyor iha ko na ho na tamasi vrida-vimisro rasah \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aslesad* - the embrace; *anu* - after; *cumbanad* - the kiss; *anu* - after; *nakho* - of the nails; *lekhad* - the scratch; *anu* - after; *svantaja* - of the Cupid; *prodbodhad* - the appearance; *anu* - after; *sambhramad* - eagerness; *anu* - after; *ratarambhad* - the beginning of union; *anu* - \[and\] after; *pritayoh* - of the two lovers; *anyartham* - for another reason; *gatayor* - having come; *bhraman* - from wandering about; *militayoh* - encountering; *sambhasanair* - by conversations; *janator* - becoming acquainted; *dampatyor* - the couple; *iha* - now; *ko na ko na* - does \[it\] not \[arise\]; *tamasi* - in the darkness; *vrida* - with shyness; *vimisro* - mixed; *rasah* - the *rasa*?

**Перевод:**

**“Does not the couple’s rasa, mixed with shyness, arise in such darkness? They go with other intentions, and after wandering, meet and get to know each other with words. First they embrace, then kiss, then scratch each other, and on arousing Cupid, become most passionate and then unite. The couple then experiences the highest bliss.**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi* described the pleasant moment for meeting in the last  verse. Now she describes the scene after becoming intensely eager for their meeting, by showing the astonishing nature of various *rasas* during the dense darkness.

In this darkness, cannot the *madhurya-rasa* of a couple endowed with shyness not take place? Yes, it can. The couple is described. They have gone, while indicating other intentions. They meet by wandering about. They know each other through talking to each other: “I am her husband”, “He is my husband.” The intention to enjoy is indicated by *sattvika-bhavas* like choked voice and the manifestation of *bhavas*.

Their actions are described; After (*anu*) embracing, they kiss. After that, they mark each other with their nails in order to agitate intense desire. After that, Cupid (*svanta-ja*) bursts forth. After that, there arises eagerness (*sambhrama*) to touch her skirt out of great passion. After that, amorous union begins as described in *kama-sastra*. After that the couple simultaneously experience bliss (*pritayoh*). *Bharata* says:

*aslesa-cumbana-nakha-ksata-kama-bodha-*  
*sighratva-maithunam ananta-sukha-prabodham \|*  
*pritis tato’pi rasa-bhavanam eva karyam*  
*evam nitanta-caturah suciram ramante \|\|*

**With embraces, kissing, scratching with nails, arousal of love, and quickly engaging in lovemaking, unlimited bliss arises. Affection and then a state of** ***rasa*** **arise. Those who are most skillful enjoy in this way for a long time.**

Or, which *rasa* should not arise? All *rasas* should manifest. Thus in the dense darkness out of fear, lamentation is imitated. This is *karuna-rasa*. Scratching with nails indicates enthusiasm in *vira-rasa.* The experience of unprecedented joy indicates *adbhuta-rasa* because of the astonishment. With the arousal of Cupid, *hasya-rasa* is indicated. With the commencement of union (*ratarambha*), the king of all *rasas*, the friend of *rati*, *srngara-rasa*, shines. It is said:

*ratir hasas ca sokas ca krodhotsahau bhayam tatha \|*  
*jugupsa vismayas samah sthayi-bhavah prakirtitah \|\|*

**The** ***sthayi-bhavas*** **(permanent emotions) are celebrated as** ***rati*** **(*****srngara*****),** ***hasa*** **(joking),** ***soka*** **(lamentation),** ***krodha*** **(anger),** ***utsaha*** **(enthusiasm),** ***bhaya*** **(fear),** ***jugupsa*** **(disgust) and** ***vismaya*** **(astonishment) and** ***sama*** **(peace). (*****Natya-sastra*** **6.17)**

Disgust, anger and peacefulness are not described in the poem since they are contrary to *srngara-rasa*. The others should be considered nourishers of *srngara-rasa* and are not independent. It is also hinted that though the couple goes with other intentions, they, the forms of complete *rasa*, should not delay meeting, because the *sakhi* has narrated the elements such as the darkness which give rise to *rasa*.

Others explain the verse differently: A couple go for a meeting, but by mistake they meet a different man and woman. Meeting together thinking “She is my lover, he is my lover” they each associate with another man and woman. After enjoyment they get to know each other by speaking. Then both develop *rasa* mixed with shyness. But that is not a proper explanation since it impossible for a *sakhi* to speak material love affairs in front of the consort of the Lord. The poetic meter is *sardula-vikrditam*.

### Text 5.19

*sa-bhaya-cakitam vinyasyantim drsam timire pathi*  
*pratitaru muhuh sthitva mandam padani vitanvatim \|*  
*katham api rahah praptam angair ananga-tarangibhih*  
*sumukhi subhagah pasyan sa tvam upaitu krtarthatam \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa-bhaya-cakitam* - Trembling fearfully; *vinyasyantim* - placing \[in all directions\]; *drsam* - \[your\] glance \[like a doe\]; *timire* - in the darkness; *pathi* - on the path; *pratitaru* - from tree to tree; *muhuh* - now; *sthitva* - having stood; *mandam* - slowly; *padani* - \[your\] steps; *vitanvatlm* - placing; *katham* - somehow; *api* - even; *rahah* -to a solitary place; *praptam* - coming; *angair* - with limbs; *ananga* - by the Cupid; *tarangibhih* - agitated; *sumukhi* - о beautiful-faced woman (Radha); *subhagah* - the fortunate one (Krsna); *pasyan* -seeing; *sa* - he; *tvam* - you; *upaitu* - may \[he\] obtain; *krtarthatam* - success.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha with beautiful face! May fortunate Krsna attain success on seeing you, as you glance in all directions on the path in the darkness with doe-like eyes, and as you wander from tree to tree with slow steps, in a solitary place with limbs agitated by Cupid.”**

**Комментарий:**

His only goal is to attain you. The *sakhi* explains this.

О Radha with beautiful face! May Krsna (*subhagah*), seeing you, attain success. From seeing you he should attain all good fortune. It is said:

*tabhir vidhuta-sokabhir bhagavan acyuto vrtah*  
*vyarocatadhikam tata purusah saktibhir yatha*

**\[Sukadeva Gosvami said\] “Encircled by the** ***gopis*****, who were now relieved of all distress, Krsna, perfect in himself, without fault, shone forth more splendidly than previously. My dear King, Krsna thus appeared like the Lord encircled by his spiritual potencies.” (SB 10.32.10)**

Radha is described as follows. You glance with eyes of a young doe in all directions, with agitation for the meeting. This shows the beauty of her glance. You stand at each tree repeatedly thinking, “My lover will come here” and walk with a splendid gait, very gracefully. Somehow by good fortune you attain a solitary place with your limbs excited by Cupid. Instrumental case is used (*angaih* - with limbs) to describe her. It is also suggested that her limbs become splendid because of her excellent beauty. The affix *su* in *subhagah* (most fortunate Krsna) and *sumukhi* (Radha with beautiful face) indicate the couple is suitable for mutual affection. Because her face is like the moon, there will be light even in the darkness.

### Text 5.20

*radha-mugdha-mukharavinda-madhupas trailokya-mauli-sthali-*  
*nepathyocita-nila-ratnam avani-bharavatarantakah \|*  
*svacchandam vraja-sundari-jana-manas-tosa-pradosodayah*  
*kamsa-dhvamsana-dhuma-ketur avatu tvam devaki-nandanah \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*radha* - Of Radha; *mugdha* - beautiful; *mukharavinda* - on the lotus-face; *madhupas* - the bee; *trailokya* - of the three worlds; *mauli* - \[which is\] the crest; *sthali* - of the place \[Vrndavana\]; *nepathyocita* - suitable \[to be\] the ornament; *nila* - blue \[sapphire\]; *ratnam* - the jewel; *avani* - for the earth; *bhara* - \[which are\] a burden; *avatara* - who have appeared \[the demons\]; *antakah* - the destroyer; *svacchandam* - spontaneously; *vraja-sundari-jana* - of the cowherdesses (*gopis*); *manas* - of the minds; *tosa* - for the satisfaction; *pradosodayah* - \[and\] the evening; *kamsa* - of Kamsa; *dhvamsana* - for the destruction; *dhuma-ketur* - the comet; *avatu -may* \[he\] protect; *tvam* - you; *devaki-nandanah* - the son of Devaki (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**May the son of Yasoda - the bee on the beautiful lotus face of  Radha, the sapphire jewel suitable to ornament Vrndavana,  the crest jewel of the three worlds, the destroyer of those who  have appeared as a burden for the earth, the comet to destroy  Kamsa and the evening incarnate to satisfy the hearts of the gopis  spontaneously - protect you.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of this chapter, the poet desires to give blessings.

May the son of Devaki (Yasoda) protect you. He is the bee, the  person who protects sweetness (*madhupah*) on the beautiful  (*mugdha*) lotus mouth of Radha. He increases her face’s sweetness by  being eternally inclined to Radha. Or *madhupah* can mean “he who drinks the sweetness of her face with great thirst.” He is the suitable  sapphire to ornament Vrndavana, which is the crest jewel of the  three worlds. It is said *aho madhupuri dhanya vaikunthac ca gariyasi*:  Mathura district is more fortunate and greater than Vaikuntha - (*Padma Purana*). He is the destroyer of demons who are born as  burdens to the earth. He is the malefic planet for destroying Kamsa.  It is suggested that just by Krsna’s birth, Kamsa was destroyed. Or  *dhumaketu* can mean fire, a natural destroyer. This indicates that  Krsna’s quality called *samarthya* (being fully capable) is useful to  produce his spontaneous pastimes. Thus it is said in this verse that by his will he exists as the boundless (*ciram* from the alternate  reading) night for fulfilling the desires in the hearts of the *gopis*.  Or *ciram* can modify the verb *avatu* (may he protect): May Krsna eternally protect you. Some quote the following from the Puranas:

*dve namni nanda-bharyaya yasoda devakity api \|*  
*atah sakhuyam abhut tasyah devakya sauri-jayaya \|\|*

**Nanda’s wife is called by two names: Yasoda and Devaki. Thus Yasoda was friends with Devaki, wife of Vasudeva.**

This fifth chapter, titled *Sakanksa-pundarikaksa* (lotus-eyed Krsna full of desire), in which Krsna’s eyes are overcome by Cupid because of his desire to meet with Radha, increased by her love for him, is like the fifth arrow of Cupid.

## Chapter Six. Sotkantha-vaikunthah - Krsna full of longing

### Text 6.1

*atha tam gantum asaktam ciram anuraktam lata-grhe drstva\|*  
*tac-caritam govinde manasija-mande sakhi praha \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atha* - Then; *tam* - her (Radha); *gantum* - to move; *asaktam* -unable; *ciram* - for a long time; *anuraktam* - attached \[to Krsna\]; *lata* - of creepers; *grhe* - in a bower; *drstva* - having seen; *tac* - of her (Radha); *caritam* - \[about\] the activities; *govinde* - to Govinda (Krsna); *manasija* - by the Cupid; *mande* - pained; *sakhi* - the female friend; *praha* - spoke.

**Перевод:**

**The sakhi spoke to Govinda, pained by Cupid, about the activities of Radha, after seeing her, attached to him for a long time, sitting in a creeper bower and unable to move.**

**Комментарий:**

In the fifth chapter, Krsna’s longing and his departure to the meeting place were described. Now in the sixth chapter, the *sakhi* speaks to Krsna about Radha, who cannot go, since she is suffering from the arrows of Cupid, and is situated in an attractive designated bower doing various actions. The word *atha* is used to indicate auspiciousness or the next event. The *sakhi* spoke to Govinda about the activities of Radha (*tac-caritam*). \[Previously\] Krsna was tortured by Cupid (*manasija*) or he considered Cupid to be insignificant since he himself is a million Cupids. The *sakhi* spoke about the activities of Radha after seeing her being attached to Govinda for a long time \[and sitting\] in the creeper house. Radha knew that he would come there and pacify her. Radha was unable to move because of the actions of Cupid. The *sakhi* saw that Radha was attached to Govinda and unable to go for a long time because of pain.

**\|\| Song Twelve \|\|**

*gunakari-raga, rupaka-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***gunakari*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***rupaka*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is described as follows:

*ragotsuka kanta-pathi pratiksam*  
*apalayanti mrdu-puspa-talpa \|*  
*itas tatah prerita-drstir arta*  
*syama tanur guna-kari prasiddha \|\|*

***Guna-kari*** **is famous as having a blackish body, glancing here and there, being afflicted, having a soft flower bed, gazing towards the path of her lover, and being fond of music.**

The tala was described previously.

### Text 6.2

*pasyati disi disi rahasi bhavantam \|*  
*tad-adhara-madhura-madhuni pibantam \|\|2\|\|*

*natha hare sidati radha vasa-grhe \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pasyati* - \[She\] looks; *disi disi* - in all directions; *rahasi* - alone; *bhavantam* - for you; *tad -* her; *adhara* - of lips; *madhura* - sweet; *madhuni* - the nectar; *pibantam* - drinking, *natha* - О master! *hare* -О Krsna! *sidati* - Lies; *radha* - Radha; *vasa-grhe* - in the bower.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi says to Krsna,\] “Alone, Radha looks in all directions for you, who drink the sweet nectar of her lips.**

**“O master! О Hari! Radha lies morose in the bower, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The meaning is this. О master! О Hari! Radha lies morose in the appointed bower. Hari indicates “one who can remove the suffering of separation.” *Natha* means a person who is requested. Thus *natha* implies “O master, it is not proper not to eliminate the suffering of your devotees, since you are capable of doing so.” This is the chorus.

Now the rest of the verse is explained. She shows the remarkable transformation of *anuraga.* All alone, she looks in all directions for you alone, who drink the delicious nectar of her lips, or who hear the syllables \[from her mouth\] which are sweet like honey since they touched her lips. Out of respect “hearing” is called “drinking.”

Instead of *tad-adhara* sometimes *tvad-adhara* is seen. Some explain that *tad* would mean that he drinks the nectar from the lips of another woman. This is not proper explanation however since that would be contrary to the characteristics of a woman ready to meet her lover. It is said *tribhuvanam api tan-mayam virahe*: in separation, the whole world also feels the same.

### Text 6.3

*tvad-abhisarana-rabhasena valanti \|*  
*patati padani kiyanti calanti \|\|3\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvad* - With you; *abhisarana* - for a meeting; *rabhasena* - out of eagerness; *valanti* - turning; *patati* - \[she\] falls; *padani* - steps; *kiyanti* - a few; *calanti* - moving.

**Перевод:**

**“Turning out of eagerness to meet you, moving a few steps, she falls on the ground.**

**“O master! О Hari...**

**Комментарий:**

With eagerness to meet you she turns, proceeds a few steps, and then falls. She cannot walk since she faints out of separation from you.

### Text 6.4

*vihita-visada-bisa-kisalaya-valaya \|*  
*jivati param iha tava rati-kalaya \|\|4\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihita* - \[Are\] made; *visada* - white; *bisa* - of the water lily; *kisalaya* - of the buds; *valaya* - whose bracelets; jivati - \[she\] lives; *param* -only; *iha* - now; *tava* - your; *rati* - for love; kalaya - out of desire.

**Перевод:**

**“She continues to live only because of desire for your love \[which she previously experienced\]. She wears bracelets made of white water lily buds.**

**“O master! О Hari...**

**Комментарий:**

If it is impossible for you to come, then how can she remain alive?

She continues to live only waiting for you to come, with a desire for your love which she previously experienced, or she lives only because of the increase of skill in your love making. She wears bracelets made of white water lily buds or suitably made by *sakhis* (*vihita*) to relieve her of pain. *Kala* means increase, time or desire according to *Visva*.

### Text 6.5

*muhur avalokita-mandana-lila \|*  
*madhu-ripur aham iti bhavana-sila \|\|5\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*muhur* - Repeatedly; *avalokita* - \[that she has\] seen; mandana - \[is your\] decoration; *lila* - \[she is one\] whose imitation; *madhu-ripur* - “Madhuripu (Krsna); *aham* - I \[am\]”; *iti -* thus; *bhavana* - \[is\] thinking; *sila* - \[and she is one\] whose habit.

**Перевод:**

**“Repeatedly she imitates your decorations that she has seen. She thinks, ‘I am Krsna, the enemy of Madhu.’**

**“O master! О Hari...**

**Комментарий:**

She imitates (*lila*) your form with decorations like a peacock feather crown and *makara* earrings which she has seen previously. She becomes absorbed in thought, thinking, “I am the enemy of Madhu.” Secretly wearing your clothing, she thinks “I am the enemy of Madhu.” *Lila* is a particular gesture. *Vatsyayana* says:

*priya-janasya cesta vanuragatisayena va \|*  
*kriyate’nukrtih seyam lilokta muni-sattamaih \|\|*

**The best of sages say that** ***lila*** **refers to actions of the lover or imitation of the lover because of extreme** ***anuraga*****.**

*gatyanuraga-smita-vibhrameksitair*  
*manoramalapa-vihara-vibhramaih \|*  
*aksipta-cittah pramada rama-pates*  
*tas ta vicesta jagrhus tad-atmikah \|\|*

**As the cowherd women remembered Krsna, their hearts were overwhelmed by his movements and loving smiles, his playful glances and enchanting talks, and by the many other pastimes he would enjoy with them. Thus absorbed in thoughts of Krsna, the Lord of Radha, the** ***gopis*** **began acting out his various pastimes.**

*gati-smita-preksana-bhasanadisu*  
*priyah priyasya pratirudha-murtayah \|*  
*asav aham tv ity abalas tad-atmika*  
*vyavedisuh krsna-vihara-vibhramah \|\|*

**Because the beloved** ***gopis*** **were absorbed in thoughts of their beloved Krsna, their bodies imitated his way of moving and smiling, his glances, his speech and other distinctive features. Deeply identifying with and maddened by remembering his pastimes, they declared to one another, “I am Krsna!” (SB 10.30.2-3)**

*Madhu-ripu* means “he is even the enemy of spring (representing love).” Thinking of his hard heart, the *sakhi* criticizes him indirectly, “He passes time with great determination in his heart.” Or *Madhu-ripu* can mean “He has made himself useless without pastimes with Radha. ”

### Text 6.6

*tvaritam upaiti na katham abhisaram \|*  
*harir iti vadati sakhlm anuvaram \|\|6\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvaritam* - “Quickly; *upaiti* - has he come; *na* - not; *katham* - why; *abhisaram* - to the meeting place; *harir* - Hari?” *iti* - Thus; *vadati* - she says; *sakhim* - to \[her\] female friend; *anuvaram* - repeatedly.

**Перевод:**

**“She repeatedly says to her sakhi, ‘Why has Hari not come quickly to the meeting place?’**

**“O master! О Hari...**

**Комментарий:**

Overcome by love, she expresses pain on meeting. *Hari* means he who removes the pain of separation or one who steals the heart. Repeatedly she says “Why has he not come quickly to the meeting place?”

### Text 6.7

*slisyati cumbati jala-dhara-kalpam \|*  
*harir upagata iti timiram analpam \|\|7\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*slisyati* - \[She\] embraces; *cumbati* - \[and she\] kisses; *jala-dhara* - a cloud; *kalpam* - \[which is\] like; *harir* - “Hari; *upagata* - \[has\] come”; *iti* - \[thinking\] thus; *timiram* - the darkness; *analpam* - many times.

**Перевод:**

**“Thinking you have come, she embraces and kisses the darkness, black as a rain cloud, many times.**

**“O master! О Hari...**

**Комментарий:**

Confused by the madness of love, absorbed in you, she thinks “Hari has come.” She embraces and kisses the great or revered (*analpam*) darkness. *Analpam* can modify the verbs . She embraces and kisses the darkness many times. The darkness is like a rain cloud.

### Text 6.8

*bhavati vilambini vigalita-lajja \|*  
*vilapati roditi vasaka-sajja \|\|8\|\|*

*natha hare...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhavati* - \[She\] is; *vilambini* - \[when you are\] late; *vigalita* - \[is\] given up; *lajja* - \[one\] by whom shyness; *vilapati* - \[she\] laments; *roditi* - \[and she\] weeps; *vasaka-sajja* - the woman decorated to receive her lover.

**Перевод:**

**“Having decorated herself nicely in expectation, she gives up shyness when you are late and begins to lament and weep.**

**“O master! О Hari...”**

**Комментарий:**

Since you are late, she laments with no sense of shame. “O merciless one! Why do you show such indifference to me?” She weeps (*roditi*). She had decorated herself (*vasaka-sajja*) suitably for all pastimes in meeting with her lover in the pastime-bower.

*bharatadyair abhidadhe strinam vasas tu vasakah \|*  
*sva-vasaka-vasat kante samesyati grham prati \|*  
*sajjikaroti catmanam ya sa vasaka-sajjaka \|\|*

**Bharata and others call the clothing of women** ***vasaka*****. The** ***vasaka-sajja*** **is a person who decorates herself expecting that the lover will come to her room because of her decoration.**

She is a *vasaka-sajja*. This is also described in (*Srngara-tilaka*).

*bhaved vdsaka-sajjdsau sajjitdhga-ratdlayd \|*  
*nisitydgamanam bhartur dvareksana-para yathd \|\|*

**The** ***vasaka-sajja*** **decorates her body and the room for pastimes. At night she constantly looks to the door, expecting her lover to come. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.76)**

### Text 6.9

*sri-jayadeva-kaver idam uditam \|*  
*rasika-janam tamtam atimuditam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - Sri Jayadeva; *kaver* - by the poet; *idam* - this \[song\]; *uditam* - recited; *rasika* - who taste *rasa*; *janam* - \[to\] those; *tanutam* - may \[it\] give; *atimuditam* - great bliss.

**Перевод:**

**May this song, recited by Jayadeva, give great bliss to those who taste rasa. (END OF SONG TWELVE)**

**Комментарий:**

May this song narrated by the poet Jayadeva give great bliss to persons whose hearts appreciate *srngara-rasa*. This means that the work should be relished by those with real taste for *bhakti* endowed with *srngara-rasa*.

### Text 6.10

*vipula-pulaka-palih sphita-sit-karam antar-*  
*janita-jadimam kaku-vyakulam vyaharanti \|*  
*tava kitava vidhayamanda-kandarpa-cintam*  
*rasa-jala-nidhi-magna dhyana-lagna mrgaksi \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vipula* - Are abundant; *pulaka* - of whose bristling hairs; *palih* -the tips; *sphita* - loud; *sit-karam* - making hissing noises; *antar* -internally; *janita* - arises; *jadimam* - in whom a stupor; *kaku* - with broken words; *vyakulam* - confusedly; *vyaharanti* - speaking; *tava* -\[of\] your \[form\]; *kitava* - о cheater; *vidhaya* - placing \[in her heart\]; *amanda* - many; *kandarpa* - more beautiful than a million Cupids (or as Cupids); *cintam* - thoughts; *rasa* - of *rasa*; *jala-nidhi* - in the ocean; *magna* - merged; *dhyana* - in meditation; *lagna* - absorbed; *mrgaksi* - the fawn-eyed woman (Radha).

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues,\] “O cheater! Radha with fawn-like eyes is merged in the ocean of srngara-rasa, absorbed in meditation, concentrating on the form more beautiful than a million Cupids. \[Externally\] she is uttering broken words with pitiful sound, making loud hissing noises, \[and also\] she is becoming stunned from internal transformations, while her hairs stand on end.**

**Комментарий:**

О cheater! Radha with fawn-like eyes is submerged in the ocean of *srngara-rasa*, absorbed in meditation, placing (*vidhaya*) in her heart thoughts of your form more attractive than millions of Cupid (*amanada-kandarpa-cintam*) or placing in her heart thoughts of unlimited love for you, or placing in her heart unlimited thoughts of love—such as “When will he, dearer than a million lives, come to me?”

What is she doing? She utters (*vyaharanti*) sounds in a pitiful way (*kaku-vyakulam*) on observing your attachment to touching her breast, drinking the nectar of her lips and loosening her belt when you become agitated with madness by touching her. This describes her in the state called *kuttamita*. She speaks, trying to forbid him, saying angrily, “Let go of me! Let go of me!” *Kakuh striyam vikaro yah soka-bhity-adibhir dhvaneh*: *kaku* refers to a woman’s condition in which she makes sounds of lamentation and fear. *Kuttamita* is described as follows:

*kesadharadi-grahane modamane’pi manase \|*  
*duhkhiteva bahih kupyed yatra kuttamitam hi tat \|\|*

**In the state of** ***kuttamita*** **the woman appears distressed and exhibits outward anger when her lover holds her hair or kisses her, though internally she enjoys it. (*****Rasarvana-sudhakara*** **1.358)**

She also makes loud hissing sounds (*sphita-sitkaram*). She makes loud sounds while scratching him with her nails. Having described external transformations, she now describes internal transformations. She becomes stunned, which arises from internal change—bliss in the heart. This is the *bhava* called *rata*. Because of these *bhavas* she becomes submerged in the ocean of *srngara-rasa*. When the bliss spreads through the body, the body becomes stunned, unable to act. This hints at the ninth stage called *jadata* (being stunned). Though the symptoms are not seen by others, who can describe what type of *rasa* will arise on meeting you? Even others when merged in the ocean of *rasa* become stunned, their hairs stand on end, they making hissing sounds, utter broken words, and are full of thoughts. (So, not only these symptoms, but something more extraordinary will happen when she meets you directly.)

### Text 6.11

*angesv abharanam karoti bahusah patre’pi sancarini*  
*praptam tvam parisankate vitanute sayyam ciram dhyayati \|*  
*ity akalpa-vikalpa-talpa-racana-sankalpa-lila-sata-*  
*vyasaktapi vina tvaya vara-tanur naisa nisam nesyati \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ahgesv* - In \[her\] limbs; *abharanam* - decoration; *karoti* - \[she\] makes; *bahusah* - in many ways; *patre’pi* - even the leaves; *sancarini* - \[when they\] move; *praptam* - \[have\] come; *tvam* - \[that\] you; *parisankate* - she thinks; *vitanute* - \[she\] spreads; *sayyam* - the bed; *ciram* - for a long time; *dhyayati* - \[and she\] meditates; *ity -* thus; *akalpa* - in dressing; *vikalpa* - in speculating \[that you have come\]; *talpa* - the bed; *racana* - in making; *sankalpa* - of thinking; *lila* -ways; *sata* - \[and\] in a hundred; *vyasaktapi* - even absorbed; *vina* - without; *tvaya* - you; *vara* - is beautiful; *tanur* - \[Radha\] whose body; *na* - not; *esa* - she; *nisam* - the night; *nesyati* - will \[be able to\] pass.

**Перевод:**

**“Without you, beautiful Radha who is absorbed in dressing, in speculating that you have come, in making the bed, and in thinking in a hundred ways, will not be able to pass the night. She decorates her limbs in many ways, and when leaves in the trees move, she concludes that you have come. She spreads the bed with pillows and meditates for a long time.”**

**Комментарий:**

“She has already enjoyed with me \[in meditation\]. Why should I go there?”

Though she has enjoyed with you, without your personal presence, it will not be possible for her to pass the night in her present state. О Krsna! Without you, Radha who is endowed with beautiful limbs will not be able to live until the dawn (*na nisam nesyati*). She has decorated herself (*akalpa*), thinking you are coming (*vikalpa*) and she has prepared the bed, while thinking hundreds of thoughts such as making you come by sending a messenger. Desiring to meet you, her mind does not stop thinking.

Her attention to various actions is described as follows. She puts ornaments on her limbs in many ways for your happiness or auspiciousness. This describes her dressing (*akalpa*). When leaves move because of the birds, she thinks (*sankate*) definitely (*pari*) that you have come. This shows her thinking (*vikalpa*).She spreads the bed meant for your amorous play with various colorful pillows and other items. This shows making the bed. She meditates for a long time on your method of coming. This illustrates *sankalpa*. By these actions she spends her time with hope that you will come. This behavior is called *akalpa*.

*akalpa sa tu kathita priyasyagamane sati \|*  
*didrksayobhayos tadvat sa-sambhramam alankrtih \|\| iti \|\|*

***Akalpa*** **means that the woman decorated with ornaments, in confusion, looks with both eyes to see if her lover has come.**

*Akalpa* means dressing up according to *Amara-kosa*. *Vikalpa* means conjecture. *Sankalpa* means activity of the mind.

### Text 6.12

*him visramyasi krsna-bhogi-bhavane bhandira-bhumi-ruhi*  
*bhratar yasi na drsti-gocaram itah sananda-nandaspadam \|*  
*radhaya vacanam tad adhvaga-mukhan nandantike gopato*  
*govindasya jayanti sayam atithi-prasastya-garbha girah \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kim* - Why; *visramyasi* - are you staying; *krsna-bhogi* - of the black  snake (or, of Krsna the enjoyer); *bhavane* - in the house; *bhandira* - Bhandira; *bhumi-ruhi* - at the \[banyan\] tree; *bhratar* - о brother; *yasi* - you go; *na* - not; *drsti* - of view; *gocaram* - in the field \[in the  vicinity or neighborhood\]; *itah* - from there; *sananda* - prosperous;  *nandaspadam* - to the house of Nanda; *radhaya* - of Radha; *vacanam*  - the words; *tad* - then; *adhvaga* - of \[their meeting\]; *mukhan* - the  occasion; *nandantike* - in front of Nanda; *gopato* - keeping secret;  *govindasya* - of Govinda (Krsna); *jayanti* - may \[they\] remain with  excellence; *sayam* - in the evening; *atithi* - of the guest; *prasastya* - the praiseworthiness; *garbha* - containing; *girah* - the words.

**Перевод:**

**The guest said, “O brother! Do not stay at the house of the black snake at the Bhandira banyan tree. Go from there to the prosperous house of Nanda in the neighborhood.” (This is the  message, the guest got from Radha when he was at Radha’s home.  The guest then came to Krsna’s home and simply repeated the  message to him.**

**\[Upon hearing these words from the guest, Govinda praised the guest.\] May the words of Govinda \[uttered\] in the evening, praising the guest (a messenger sent by Radha in front of Nanda but which held a hidden meaning in reply to Radha’s words which were retold by the guest, remain ever excellent.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of this chapter the poet offers blessings.

May the words \[of Govinda\] remain with excellence (*jayanti*) since they have been produced by the desire of senior devotees, with a wealth of enjoyment, with skillful descriptions and truth spoken by the author with great *bhava*. These words were the householder’s praise (words customary for households) uttered to the guest in the evening. Within the words (*garbha*) there is great happiness since they reveal indications of the meeting (such as meeting place) of the lovers. The words are endowed with special intentions within, when Krsna praises the guest. Govinda keeps Radha’s message which is filled with hints of meeting secret, in front of Nanda.

What are the words? “O brother traveler!” The word “brother” indicates gentleness. Do you take rest at the banyan tree? Do not relax there since that is the house of the black snake (the pastime place of Krsna the enjoyer of *srngara-rasa*). In either case you should not stay there. This is advice of the brother (the guest or traveler). This is an example of *slesa* or pun. Go from the banyan tree to the residence of Nanda filled with all prosperity. Give up the empty snake hole and be happy as the house guest in that prosperous house. It can be seen from here. It is close. May the words of Radha (Radha originally spoke the words) remain forever excellent, since Krsna revealed their depth of meaning by guessing the hidden meaning in the traveler’s words.

Radha and Krsna are the *alambanas*, filled with qualities like cleverness. The *svayam-duti* (self-appointed messenger) is the heroine. The king of *rasas*, *srngara*, is described. The *uddipana* (stimulus) is the banyan tree.

In this chapter titled *Dhanya-vaikuntha*, Krsna recovers from fainting (*vaikuntha*) and is fortunate (*dhanya*) because of being the object of Radha’s love.

## Chapter Seven. Nagara-narayanah - Clever Krsna

### Text 7.1

*atrantare ca kulata-kula-vartma-pata-*  
*sanjata-pataka iva sphuta-lanchana-srih \|*  
*vrndavanantaram adipayad amsu-jalair*  
*dik-sundari-vadana-candana-bindur induh \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atrantare* - Then; *ca* - and; *kulata* - of the *gopis*; *kula* - of the group; *vartma* - the path; *pata* - by obstructing; *sanjata* - \[was\] caused; *pataka* - \[that\] by which \[its\] sin; *iva* - as if; *sphuta* - is evident; *lanchana* - of a mark; *srih* - on which the radiance; *vrndavanantaram* - the middle of the Vrndavana forest; *adlpayad* - lights up; *amsu* - of rays; *jalair* - with a mass; *dik* - of the direction; *sundari* - eastern; *vadana* - on the face; *candana* - of sandalwood; *bindur* - a spot; *induh* - the moon.

**Перевод:**

**The moon, like a sandalwood spot on the face of the eastern direction, shines with a mass of rays in the middle of the dense Vrndavana forest. It has an evident blemish, which is like an indicator of its sin caused by obstructing the path of the gopis trying to meet Krsna in that forest.**

**Комментарий:**

Previously the poet described the *anuraga* of the couple. Now in this chapter he describes *vipralambha-srngara-rasa* (separation in *srngara-rasa*) along with its cause, by revealing intense *bhava* with astonishing *rasa* by entering into a different type of *rasa*.

On this occasion, the moon illuminates the middle of Vrndavana forest with all its rays. This moon is extraordinary since it lights up the middle of that forest and nowhere else. Because the forest is very thick in the middle, the light must be very bright (to counteract the great darkness). Or, the moon lights up the limbs of the young man and woman situated within the forest since it is an assistant in their love. The moon acts as an *uddipana* (stimuli) as follows.

It is like a round *tilaka* mark of sandalwood mixed with *kumkuma*, on the face of the beautiful woman known as the eastern direction. This shows Krsna’s desire to put *tilaka* on Radha’s face. The moon is described as a cause of separation. It has a splendid mark very visible (dark spots on the moon). The cause of separation is explained through a detail. This mark is like its indication of its sin, caused by the moon obstructing the path of the women going to meet Krsna (by not being bright enough at that place). It is said:

*kim uttirnah panthah kupita-bhujagi-bhoga-visamo*  
*visodha bhuyasyah kim iti kulapali-katu-girah \|*  
*iti smaram smaram dara-dalita-sita-dyuti-rucau*  
*sarojaksi sonam disi nayana-konam vikirati \|\|*

**“What kind of path is this? It twists like the winding coils of an angry snake. Why must we tolerate this?” Repeatedly remembering the** ***gopi’s*** **harsh, lotus eyed Radha cast an angry glance from the reddish corner of her eyes in the direction of the moon which is very faint. (*****Padyavali*** **196)**

The ornaments are *rupaka* (metaphor) and *utpreksa*.

Some explain that this verse shows that one *sakhi* could not tolerate the separation experienced by Radha and thus described a meeting at the end of the last chapter. In this seventh chapter, she describes astonishing transformations of the *rasa* called *vaicittya-vipralamba*.

The sequence is as follows. In the previous chapter a union took place by means of a messenger. The following eight verses (GG 7.2 - 7.9) describe Radha’s lamentation in that *prema* (during which there is imagined separation). After that, an unnamed *sakhi* describes Radha’s enjoyment in two songs (songs fourteen and fifteen) with feelings of disappointment by using the third person to indicate Radha. In the last verse of this chapter, drinking the nectar of their words of affection, she describes Krsna in front of Radha, with signs of their intimate meeting. In the eighth chapter, Radha yells “Go, go!” though she is conciliated, since Radha is mad with intense emotions. That will be clear later.

This has been supplied to satisfy the intimate devotees. Please tolerate it. By this verse, it is also understood that meetings with other *gopis* do not take place when Krsna plays with Radha. Though the moon developed a blemish by obstructing the other *gopis'* path, it is still splendid by producing affection between the couple. The rest is the same. The rest of the verse has the same meaning as the first explanation.

### Text 7.2

*prasarati sasa-dhara-bimbe vihita-vilambe ca madhave vidhura \|*  
*viracita-vividha-vilapam saparitapam cakaroccaih \|\|2\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*prasarati* - \[While it\] spreads \[its rays\]; *sasa-dhara* - of the rabbitbearer (the moon); *bimbe* - the disk; *vihita* - is made; *vilambe* - \[while he is one\] by whom a delay; *ca* - and; *madhave* - Madhava (Krsna); *vidhura* - the woman (Radha) suffering from separation; *viracita* - performed; *vividha* - in various ways; *vilapam* - lamentations; *saparitapam* - with great pain; *cakara* - makes; *uccaih* - loudly.

**Перевод:**

**Suffering separation, Radha wails loudly in various ways and experiences pain, while the moon spreads its rays and Krsna delays.**

**Комментарий:**

Having shown the cause of separation, now separation itself is described. Radha laments loudly in various ways. “O lover, where are you?
