О deceiver, why do you ignore me?” She experiences great pain (*saparitapam*). She is pained by separation (*vidhura*). At that time the disk of the moon spreads out, while the crest jewel of heroes, Madhava, was late in keeping the appointment. \[Though Madhava was already there in the appointed place\] he was hiding, in agreement with the *sakhis*, to show some fun in *prem*a. The moon is an *uddipana* (stimulus).

**\|\| Song Thirteen \|\|**

*malava-raga yati-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***malava*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* and *tala* were described previously.

### Text 7.3

*kathita-samaye’pi harir ahaha na yayau vanam \|*  
*mama viphalam idam amala-rupam api yauvanam \|\|3\|\|*

*yami he kam iha saranam sakhi-jana-vacana-vancita \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hathita* - Appointed; *samaye* - at the time; *’pi* - even; *harir* - Krsna; *ahaha* - alas; *na* - not; *yayau* - did come; *vanam* - to the bower; *mama* - Of mine; *viphalam* - \[are\] useless; *idam* - this; *amala* - pure; *rupam* - form; *api* - \[and\] even; *yauvanam* - \[my\] youth; *yami* - Should I go; *he* - о \[friend\]; *kam* - to whom; *iha* - now; *saranam* - refuge \[for alleviating my inner grief\]; *sakhi-jana* - of \[my\] friends; *vacana* - by the words; *vancita* - cheated?

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha laments,\] “Alas, Krsna did not come to the bower at the appointed time. My exquisite beauty and astonishing youth are useless.**

**“O friend! At this time to whom should I surrender for alleviating my inner grief, after being cheated by the words of my friends? (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Radha’s lamentation is described in eight verses. The heroine is *vipralabdha* (cheated):

*presya dutim svayam dattva sanketam nagatah priyah \|*  
*yasyas tena vina duhstha vipralabdha tu sa yatha \|\|*

**The** ***vipralabdha*** **woman is unsettled, without her lover who did not come after personally sending a messenger and fixing the \[meeting\] place. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.78)**

О friend! At this time to whom (*kam*) should I surrender for alleviating my inner grief? Or, should I surrender to water (*kam*) to alleviate the pain? Without him I will drown myself in water.

I have been cheated by my friends. I have been cheated by their words of instruction. They are like other people (*jana*). I have been prevented by their various words again and again, discouraging my efforts to see him. *Kam* can mean water according to *Visva*. *Saranam* means a house or protector according to *Amara-kosa*. This is the chorus.

“Have you only been cheated by your friends?”

“No, Krsna also has cheated me.” Hari means “he who takes away all suffering just by remembering him.” At the time which was agreed, he should have come to the place appointed by me. Ah! How unfortunate (*ahaha*)! He has not arrived at that appointed bower. Thus my newly astonishing youth and my pure beauty are both useless. The characteristics of youth are described:

*nitambo vipulo madhyam krsam angam vara-dyuti \|*  
*pinau kucav uru-yugmam rambhabham purna-yauvane \|\|*

**In the fourth stage of** ***kaisora*****, a girl’s hips become broad, her waist becomes slender, her body becomes lustrous, her breasts become large, and her thighs become like the trunks of a banana tree. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.21)**

The sweetness of youth is described:

*na vitrasta ka te pratiyuvatir asin mukha-ruca*  
*dadhara staimityam pranaya-ghana-vrstya tava na ka \|*  
*vraje sisya kabhan na hi tava kalayam iti harer*  
*nikunja-svarajye tvam asi rasike patta-mahisi \|\|*

**\[Padma comforts Candravali who is lamenting at the good fortune of Radha\] “What rival** ***gopi*** **is not afraid of the beauty of your face? What** ***gopi*** **does not become stunned by seeing the showers from the cloud of your love? What girl in Vraja has not become your student in the various arts? О expert at relishing** ***rasa*****! You are the reigning queen of bowers.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.23)**

*bhusanabhusanabhyam hi camatkarena netrayoh \|*  
*paraspara-smrta-mudam amalam tam prakirtitam \|\|*

**Pure beauty is described as joy of the eyes with astonishment, with or without ornaments.**

### Text 7.4

*yad-anugamanaya nisi gahanam api silitam \|*  
*tena mama hrdayam idam asama-sara-kilitam \|\|4\|\|*

*уami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*уad* - Him (Krsna); *anugamanaya* - in order to follow; *nisi* - at night; *gahanam* - the dense forest; *api* - even; silitam - \[is\] experienced; *tena* - Because of him \[Krsna\]; *mama* - of mine; *hrdayam* - heart; *idam* - this; *asama* - of love; *sara* - by the arrow; *kilitam* - \[is\] pierced.

**Перевод:**

**“My heart is pierced by the arrow of love, because of Krsna. In pursuit of him, I must experience this dense forest at night.**

**“O friend! At this time...**

**Комментарий:**

To follow Krsna (*yad*) I must experience the dense forest at night. Or, I must frequent the forest, though that should not be done. Because of him my vitals are pierced by the crooked arrows of love. They are crooked because love follows a crooked path.

### Text 7.5

*mama maranam eva varam ati-vitatha-ketana \|*  
*kim iha visahami virahanalam acetana \|\|5\|\|*

*у ami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mama* - my; *maranam* - death; *eva* - only; *varam* - \[is\] better; *at*i-vitatha - of the truth; *ketana* - \[since I am one in whom there is\] an indication (or, since my body is completely false); *kim* - How; *iha* -here; *visahami* - \[can\] I endure; *virahanalam* - the fire of separation; *acetana* - without knowledge.

**Перевод:**

**“It is better that I die, since the indications of the truth are now evident. How can I, without knowledge in this forest, endure the fire of separation in the forest?**

**“О friend! At this time...**

**Комментарий:**

Because of being cheated, it is better that I die rather than maintain my body.

My body is completely false (*ati-vitatha*), since it does not perform its functions. Or, I now have an indication (*ketana*) of truth, surpassing illusion (*ati-vitatha*), though I was expecting my lover to come. Or, I know the invitation given by my *sakhis* has been completely fruitless (*ati-vitatha*). Or, my bower (*ketana*) decorated with a bed has become completey useless. The reason is given: I am without knowledge in this forest (*iha*). Or, I am conscious of Krsna (*cetana* - conscious; *a* - name of the lord). Or, my intelligence is fixed on Krsna in this way. How can I tolerate the fire of separation? Therefore it is somewhat (*varam*) better if I attain him in the next life.

*Varam* means ‘a little desirable’ according to *Dharani.* *Vitatha* means false according to *Amara-kosa*. *Ketanam* means a house or a mark or invitation according to *Visva*. Another version has *ativitatha-cetana* - All my aspirations are useless. *Cetanam* can mean expectation according to *Visva*.

### Text 7.6

*mam ahaha vidhurayati madhura-madhu-yamini \|*  
*kapi harim anubhavati krta-sukrta-kamini \|\|6\|\|*

*уami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mam* - Me; *ahaha* - ah; *vidhurayati* - causes pain; *madhura* - pleasant; *madhu* - spring; *yamini* - the night; *kapi* - What; *harim* - Krsna; *anubhavati* - experiences; *krta* - performed; *sukrta* - by pious acts; *kamini* - woman whose desires are fulfilled.

**Перевод:**

**“Ah! The pleasant spring night only brings me pain. What woman, whose desires are fulfilled after performing pious acts, can experience Krsna?**

**“O friend! At this time...**

**Комментарий:**

Ah what misfortunate (*ahaha*)! This charming spring night pains me. What woman whose desires are fulfilled (*kamini*) because of performing pious acts, experiences he who takes away her suffering (*harim*) by embraces? *Vidhura* means distressed according to *Visva*. *Madhura* means pleasant or attractive according to *Dharani*.

### Text 7.7

*ahaha kalayami valayadi-mani-bhusanam \|*  
*hari-viraha-dahana-vahanena bahu-dusanam \|\|7\|\|*

*yami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ahaha* - How astonishing; *kalayami* - I see; *valayadi* - such as bracelets; *mani* - on which \[there are\] jewels; *bhusanam* - the ornements; *hari* - from Krsna; *viraha* - of separation; *dahana* - the fire; *vahanena* - because of carrying; *bahu* - much; *dusanam* - \[to be those\] in which \[there is\] fault.

**Перевод:**

**“How astonishing! I see that the jeweled ornaments such as bracelets are filled with faults since they possess the same fire, caused by separation from Krsna.**

**“O friend! At this time...**

**Комментарий:**

How astonishing (*ahaha*)! I regard the ornaments made of jewels or inlaid with jewels to be filled with faults (*bahu-dusanam*) since they carry the same fire, caused by separation from Krsna. By the burning of her body the ornaments have also become hot.

### Text 7.8

*kusuma-sukumara-tanum atanu-sara-lilaya \|*  
*srag api hrdi hanti mam ativisama-silaya \|\|8\|\|*

*у ami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kusuma* - As a flower; *sukumara* - \[is\] as delicate; *tanum* - whose body; *atanu* - of the Cupid; *sara* - of the arrows; *lilaya* - by means of the play; *srag* - the garland; *api* - even; *hrdi* - in the mind; *hanti* - \[it\] beats; *mam* - me; *ativisama* - is most unjust; *silaya* - whose nature.

**Перевод:**

**“The garland beats my mind using the play of Cupid’s arrows, which is most unjust, since I have a body as delicate as a flower.**

**“O friend! At this time...**

**Комментарий:**

The garland beats my mind by its play of Cupid’s arrows. A garland is made of flowers, and flowers are also called “arrows of Cupid.” That play of arrows is most irregular (*ativisama-silaya*) in nature. It is said:

*apurveyam dhanur vidya manmathasya mahatmanah \|*  
*sariram aksatam krtva bhinatty antar-gatam manah \|\|*

**The military skill of the great Cupid is astonishing. Wounding the body, he pierces within the mind.**

The arrows released by one person strike another person. But these arrows held by one person pierce the same person. This is most irregular. Or, by the action of gross arrows, Cupid (*atanu*) pierces the body. This is never seen and cannot be stopped. Radha’s body is more delicate than a flower. A person should be killed by a person of equal status. But in this case, it is not so since she is most tender whereas Cupid is harsh. Her tenderness also indicates that she cannot tolerate the pain. Another version has *kusuma-sukumara-tanuh* (instead of *tanum*), which modifies the \[subject\] garland: the garland is slender (*tanu*) and soft with flowers. Here also there is irregularity since the garland is soft but its arrows are harsh. *Sila* means nature.

### Text 7.9

*aham iha nivasami na-ganita-vana-vetasa \|*  
*smarati madhusudana mam api na cetasa \|\|9\|\|*

*yami he kam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aham* - I; *iha* - here; *nivasami* - will remain; *na* - not; *ganita* - \[are\] regarded; *vana* - of the forest; *vetasa* - by whom the reeds; *smarati* -Does remember; *madhusudano* - Madhusudana \[Krsna\]; *mam* - me; *api* - even; *na* - not; *cetasa* - with \[affectionate\] mind.

**Перевод:**

**“I will remain here in the bower, giving no regard for the thick cane. Krsna does not remember me with affectionate mind.**

**“O friend! At this time...”**

**Комментарий:**

Though suffering, I will stay in this bower, dedicated to him. I will not be aware of the network of forest reeds. This means she will overcome forest obstacles difficult to pass. Or, having counted all the trees in searching for him (*ganita-vana-vetas*a), will I not (*na*) remain in this bower? Yes, I will remain here.

Krsna does not remember me in his heart. The word Madhusudana means “he who destroys spring (*madhu*) or *madhurya-rasa*" and indicates his hard nature and fickleness.

\[The word *smarati (*remembering) is used in the second line of the verse.\] Since remembering (*smarati*) means using the mind, the word *cetasa* \[at the end of the second line\] would then be redundant. Thus the meaning is, he does not remember me with affectionate mind (*cetasa*).

### Text 7.10

*hari-carana-sarana-jayadeva-kavi-bharatl \|*  
*vasatu hrdi yuvatir iva komala-kalavati \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hari* - Of Krsna; *carana* - at the feet; *sarana* - by whom refuge \[was taken\]; *jayadeva* - Jayadeva; *kavi* - of the poet; *bharati* - the speech; *vasatu* - may \[it\] remain; *hrdi* - in \[your\] heart; *yuvatir* - a young woman; *iva* - as if; *komala* - soft spoken; *kalavati* - \[and\] skillful \[in the sixty-four arts\].

**Перевод:**

**May the words of the poet Jayadeva, who has surrendered to the feet of the Lord, which are easy to understand and full of poetic skill, like a soft-spoken woman, skillful in the arts, remain in your heart. (END OF SONG THIRTEEN)**

**Комментарий:**

May the words of Jayadeva who has surrendered at the feet of Krsna remain in the hearts of the devotees of Krsna! Since he has surrendered to Krsna, his words are not ordinary. The words are easily understood (*komala*). *Komala* means having clear meaning or easy to pronounce. The words are endowed with poetic artistry (*kalavati*). The words are like a young woman (*yuvati*) who speaks softly (*komala*) and is skillful at the sixty-four arts (*kalavati*).

Or the verse could mean, О charming listener (*komala*)! О listener surrendered to Krsna’s lotus feet! In this description of quarrel (*kalau*), the words surpass (*ati*) the wealth of liberation (*i*) which means the singer and the listener have no regard for liberation. Or, the words are like a woman who, though young, surpasses Laksmi during quarrels. Or, the words are like a woman who has a great wealth of beauty (*ati i*) when quarrelling (*kalau*).

Or, the verse could mean, О Krsna, tender with affection for devotees! May your devotee’s words describing the actions of Radha remain in your mind like the young girl Radha (*yuvati*). And may she also remain there.

### Text 7.11

*tat kim kam api kaminim abhisrtah kim va kala-kelibhir*  
*baddho bandhubhir andhakarini vanabhyarne kim udbhramyati \|*  
*kantah klanta-mana manag api pathi prasthatum evaksamah*  
*sanketi-krta-manju-vanjula-lata-kunje’pi yan nagatah \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tat* - Then; him - perhaps; *kam api* - some; *kaminim* - lusty woman; *abhisrtah* - being approached by; *kim* - perhaps; *va* - or; *kala-kelibhir* - with skillful playing; *baddho* - obstructed; *bandhubhir* - by \[his\] friends; *andhakarini* - dark; *vanabhyarne* - in the proximity of the forest; *kim* - \[or\] perhaps; *udbhramyati* - \[he\] is lost; *kantah* - the lover; *klanta* - \[is\] tired; *mana* - whose mind; *manag* - for a moment; *api* - even; *pathi* - on the path; *prasthatum* - to proceed; *evaksamah* - only unable; *sanketi-krta* - appointed for the meeting; *manju* - lovely; *vanjula* - of reeds; *lata* - \[and\] of creepers; *kunje* - in the bower; *’pi* - even; *yan*  *nagatah* - \[has\] not arrived.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha continues,\] “Why did the lover not come to the attractive reed bower for the appointment? Perhaps some lusty woman waylaid him? Or perhaps, \[his\] friends obstructed him with skillful playing. Or perhaps, he got lost in the dark forest. He must have become tired in mind and could not proceed on the path for a moment.”**

**Комментарий:**

Having described the cheated woman (*vipralabdha*) in the previous eight verses, the author now describes her longing in order to show her *prema* for Krsna.

Why has the attractive lover (*kantah*) not come to the attractive cane bower, though he made an appointment there? Did he meet with some amorous woman (*kaminim*)? The word *kaminim* (which can mean a woman with lust) suggests that she considers herself more fortunate since Krsna by his nature desires Radha. Did this other woman forcibly take him away?

“How could he give me up and meet with others?” Perhaps he was obstructed by constant friends playing skillfully? That also did not happen. Since he is attracted to pastimes with me, he cannot be obstructed by them. Perhaps (*kim*) coming to meet me and not getting to the appointed place, he is wandering near the appointed place which is dark because of the shade of trees.

“How can the crest jewel of heroes make a mistake about a place known a thousand times?” It must be that, tortured in mind because of sorrow of separation, he is unable to proceed on the path for a moment. The heroine is called *ukta*:

*utka bhavati sa yasyah sanketam nagatah priyah \|*  
*tasyanagamane hetum cintayanty akula yatha \|\|*

**An** ***ukta*** **woman is one who, when the lover does not keep an appointment, becomes agitated and thinks of the cause of the lover not coming. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.75)**

The ornament is called *samsayaksepa*. This is described by Dandi:

*kim ayam sarad-ambhodah him va hamsa-kadambakam \|*  
*rutam nupura-samvadi sruyate tan na toyadah \|*  
*ity evam samsayaksepah samsayo yan nivartate \|*  
*dharmena hamsa-sulabhenasprsta-ghana-jatina \|\|*

**“Is that an autumn cloud or a sound of a group of swans?" On hearing the sound of ankle bells, she concludes that it could not be a cloud. Where doubt arises because the swan was more likely and cloud was a remote possiblity, it is called** ***samsayaksepa*** **(casting doubt). (*****Kavyadarsa*** **1.163-164)**

*Kim* (perhaps) indicates conjecture. *Upanta* (instead of *vanabhyarne*) means ‘near’ according to *Visva*.

### Text 7.12

*athagatam madhavam antarena*  
*sakhim iyam viksya visada-mukam \|*  
*visankamana ramitam kayapi*  
*janardanam drstavad etad aha \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*athagatam* - Then having arrived; *madhavam* - Madhava (Krsna); *antarena* - without; *sakhim* - the female friend; *iyam* - she (Radha); *viksya* - having seen; *visada* - out of sadness; *mukam* - speechless; *visankamana* - suspecting; *ramitam* - \[that he\] was enjoying; *kayapi*  - with some \[other\] woman; *janardanam* - Krsna; *drstavad* - as if seeing \[it\]; *etad* - as follows; *aha* - \[she\] spoke.

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the sakhi, speechless out of sadness, return without Krsna, Radha speculated that he was enjoying with some other woman.**

**As if seeing the incident, she spoke as follows.**

**Комментарий:**

After that (*atha*) Radha spoke of what was happening as if seeing it. She saw a *sakhi* approaching without (*antarena*) Krsna. The *sakhi* was unable to speak (*visada-mukam*) because of great sorrow. And then Radha speculated that Krsna was enjoying with some other woman. Janardana indicates “one whose nature is to give pain (*ardana*) to people (*jana*).” Therefore it is to be expected that he  will cause me suffering. The peotic meter is *upendra-vajra*.

Or \[the verse could mean as follows:\] *sakhi* saw Radha who had not attained Krsna at the appointed bower. Seeing her friend Radha silent because of devastation, the *sakhi* speculated that Krsna was enjoying with another woman.

**\|\| Song Fourteen \|\|**

*vasanta-raga, yati-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***vasant*****, and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *vasanta* and the *tala* is *ekatali*.

### Text 7.13

*smara-samarocita-viracita-vesa \|*  
*galita-kusuma-dara-vilulita-kesa \|\|13\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna vilasati yuvatir adhika-guna \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smara* - lovemaking; *samarocita* - approved for; *viracita* - \[is\] made; *vesa* - whose clothing; *galita* - falling out; *kusuma* - with flowers; *dara* - slightly; *vilulita* - \[is\] disheveled; *kesa* - whose hair; *kapi* - Some; *madhuripuna* - with Madhuripu (Krsna); *vilasati* - is playing; *yuvatir* - young woman; *adhika* - \[are\] great; *guna* - whose qualities.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha or the sakhi speculates,\] “She wears clothing suitable for love making and her hair is slightly disheveled with flowers falling out.**

**“Some young woman with great qualities is playing with Krsna. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Some woman is playing with Krsna. She has many good qualities like beauty. The woman is the initiator of action. This indicates that she takes the dominant role. Thus by saying that she has many good qualities, she (Radha or the *sakhi*) says that this woman has scant qualities since she uses force to enjoy with the man in front of her. *Madhuripuna* (by the enemy of *madhu* or *madhurya-rasa*) is a criticism of Krsna, suggesting he does not know about *madhurya-rasa* and is fickle.

She is dressed suitably for love making. Flowers fall from her slightly disheveled hair because Krsna grasped her breast and kissed her. \[She also kisses him and grasps his hair\] as explained before, since the woman in a dominant role kisses the man while grasping his hair. Another version has *kusuma-bhara*. She has disheveled hair with a mass of flowers falling out.

### Text 7.14

*hari-parirambhana-calita-vikara \|*  
*kuca-kalasopari taralita-hara \|\|14\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*hari* - of Krsna; *parirambhana* - by the embrace; *calita* - \[is\] produced; *vikara* - whose transformation of love; *kuca* - \[which are her\] breasts; *kalasopari* - above the pots; *taralita* - \[is\] swaying; *hara* - whose necklace.

**Перевод:**

**“She develops symptoms of love by the embrace of Krsna. She wears a necklace that sways above her two pot-like breasts.**

**“Some young woman...**

**Комментарий:**

She develops transformation of love by the embrace of Krsna. She wears a necklace that sways above her two pot-like breasts. This description suggests that auspicious pots have been established for carrying out a festival of love-making without obstacles, since Hari means “he who takes away suffering in love.” Or it means “one who takes away worries by his expertise in love making.”

### Text 7.15

*vicalad-alaka-lalitanana-candra \|*  
*tad-adhara-pana-rabhasa-krta-tandra \|\|15\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vicalad* - Moving; *alaka* - with locks; *lalita* - \[is\] attractive; *anana-candra* - whose moon-face; *tad* - his; *adhara* - of lower lips; *pana* - of the drinking; *rabhasa* - from the passion; *krta* - \[is\] produced; *tandra* - whose exhaustion.

**Перевод:**

**“Her moon-like face is attractive with moving locks. She is exhausted from the passion of drinking his lips.**

**“Some young woman...**

**Комментарий:**

She has a moon-like face attractive (*lalita*) with locks of hair swaying because of the play. The face, attractive with locks, suggests a moving moon that has spots. Again she is described as follows. She is exhausted by the passion of drinking Krsna’s lips. She closes her eyes with the experience of that happiness. She becomes dizzy with the intoxication of drinking his lips’ exquisite sweetness.

### Text 7.16

*cancala-kundalalalita-kapola \|*  
*mukharita- rasana-jaghana-gati-lola \|\|16\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*cancala* - Swaying; *kundala* - with earrings; *lalita* - \[are\] attractive;  *kapola* - whose cheeks; *mukharita* - jinging; *rasana* - with bells;  *jaghana* - of her hips; *gati* - with the gait; *lola* - shaking.

**Перевод:**

**“Her cheeks are attractive with swaying earrings. Her hips move  about, jingling with bells.**

**“Some young woman...**

**Комментарий:**

She has cheeks attractive with earrings quivering from the passion of playing. Or the earrings are quivering because she is shaking her head while conversing with Krsna as follows: Krsna says, “I cannot stay a moment without you. You alone are my dear love.” Hearing these conciliatory words, she says, “Cheater! I know about you!”

Another version has *dalita* (crushed) instead of *lalita*. Her cheeks are crushed by the earrings swaying back and forth since the cheeks are very soft. She moves her hips and this makes her bells (*rasana*) jingle. As warriors become agitated because of the various instruments playing during battle, she becomes agitated by her bells jingle during love.

### Text 7.17

*dayita-vilokita-lajjita-hasita \|*  
*bahu-vidha-kujita-rati-rasa-rasita \|\|17\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*dayita* - By \[her\] lover (Krsna); *vilokita* - having been seen; *lajjita* -ashamed; *hasita* - smiling; *bahu*-vidha - of many sorts; *kujita* - \[like\] the cooing \[of birds\]; *rati* - of lovemaking; *rasa* - because of the *rasa*; *rasita* - making sounds.

**Перевод:**

**“She smiles after showing shame, when Krsna glances at her. She makes a variety of sounds in love-making like the sounds of birds.**

**“Some young woman...**

**Комментарий:**

She smiles and becomes ashamed because of Krsna’s glances which show his intentions. She became ashamed on seeing him in the lower position and herself in the higher position, but since shame is unsuitable in love-making, she then smiled in enjoyment. It is said that shame is the highest ornament except during love-making. She makes sounds (*rasita*) because of attachment in love-making (*rati-rasa*), like the various sounds of the cuckoo, peacock, dove and swan. *Rasita* means making sounds.

### Text 7.18

*vipula-pulaka-prthu-vepathu-bhanga \|*  
*svasita-nimilita-vikasad-ananga \|\|18\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vipula* - Extensive; *pulaka* - \[and\] goose-bumps; *prthu* - great; *vepathu* - of shivering; *bhahga* - \[in whom there are\] waves; *svasita* - breathing heavily; *nimilita* - \[and\] having closed the eyes; *vikasad* - \[is\] appearing; *ananga* - \[in whom\] the Cupid appears

**Перевод:**

**“She experiences waves of shivering and extensive goose bumps. She experiences the climax of bliss with heavy breathing and closed eyes.**

**“Some young woman...**

**Комментарий:**

She experiences waves of great shivering and extensive goose bumps. They are compared to waves since they appear repeatedly with greater intensity. *Bhahga* means wave according to *Visva.* “In this experience of happiness, there should be no grief.” She experiences symptoms of insemination (*ananga*), manifested with heavy breathing out of great joy, indicating the approach of the highest bliss and by closing of her eyes because of that bliss.

### Text 7.19

*srama-jala-kana-bhara-subhaga-sarira \|*  
*paripatitorasi rati-rana-dhira \|\|19\|\|*

*kapi madhuripuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*srama* - Of exertion; *jala* - of the water \[perspiration\]; *kana* - of the drops; *bhara* - with the bearing; *subhaga* - \[is\] attractive; *sarira* -whose body; *paripatita* - \[she\] has fallen; *urasi* - on \[his\] chest; *rati* - of love; *rana* - in the battle; *dhira* - expert.

**Перевод:**

**“Her body is attractive with drops of perspiration. Expert in the battle of love, she has fallen on his chest.**

**“Some young woman...”**

**Комментарий:**

By all the drops of perspiration because of efforts in love, her body looks attractive like a pearl necklace. She falls upon Krsna’s chest, covering him with her arms for more enjoyment, fearing he will rise up \[and leave\]. “Falling down” also indicates she is now weak. This indicates the end of love making.

The woman is described: She is skillful or steady in love making (*rati-rane dhira*). *Kana* means a drop. *Subhaga* means beautiful according to *Visva*. In seven verses (7.13 to 7.19), the inferior qualities of the woman, which were mentioned previously, are proved by describing her actions in relation to the hero who even shows indifference to her.

### Text 7.20

*sri-jayadeva-bhanita-hari-ramitam \|*  
*kali-kalusam janayatu parisamitam \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanita* - spoken; *hari* - of Krsna; *ramitam* - the pastimes; *kali* - of the Kaliyuga; *kalusam* - the foulness \[opposition to worshipping the lord\]; *janayatu* - may \[they\] bring; *parisamitam* - to cessation.

**Перевод:**

**May the pastimes of Krsna described by Jayadeva alleviate the atheism of Kaliyuga. (END OF SONG FOURTEEN)**

**Комментарий:**

May the playing of Krsna described by the poet Jayadeva bring about unlimited cessation (*parisamitam*) of sins of Kaliyuga for the singers and listeners. The sins are in the form of opposition to worship of the Lord.

### Text 7.21

*viraha-pandu-murari-mukhambuja-*  
*dyutir ayam tirayann api vedanam \|*  
*vidhur ativa tanoti mano-bhuvah*  
*suhrd aye hrdaye madana-vyatham \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*viraha* - Because of separation; *pandu* - \[which is\] pale; *murari* -of Murari (Krsna); *mukhambuja* - \[is like\] the lotus-face; *dyutir* -whose brightness; *ayam* - this; *tirayann* - makes disappear; *api* -even; *vedanam* - knowledge; *vidhur* - moon; *ativa* - exceedingly; *tanoti* - \[and\] spreads; *mano-bhuvah* - of Cupid \[the mind-stealer\]; *suhrd* - the friend; *aye* - ah; *hrdaye* - in \[my\] heart; *madana* - of Cupid; *vyatham* - the pain.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha laments,\] “Ah! The moon, the friend of Cupid, whose complexion is like lotus face of Krsna, pale because of separation, and which makes my knowledge disappear, gives intense love pains to my heart.**

**Комментарий:**

The moon gives great pain. ‘*Aye*' indicates lamentation. This moon which I now see gives great love-pains to my heart. It has a complexion like lotus-faced Krsna, which is now grayish (*pandu*) because of separation. Though I can somehow tolerate the pain of love, on remembering Krsna’s dull moon-like face, the pain becomes intolerable. What does the moon do? It makes knowledge (*vedanam*) disappear. It uproots my consciousness, even making me forget his offense. The cause of the moon giving pain is explained as follows. The moon is the friend of Cupid (*manobhuvah*). The moon arises from the mind of the Lord (see next paragraph) and Cupid also arises from the \[Lord’s\] mind (or heart). Thus the moon and Cupid are brothers. The moon gives pain, desiring to help Cupid, who is agitated.

*Candrama manaso jatah*: the moon arose from the mind of the Lord - (*Rg Veda* 10.90.13). *Vedana* means knowledge and suffering. *Aye* indicates lamentation. *Tirah* means disappear. *Tirayan* can also mean “to make manifest.”

The ornament is *yamaka* (*suhrd aye hrdaye*). *Dandi* explains this:

*eka-dvi-tri-catuspadair yamakanam vikalpana \|*  
*adi-madhyanta-madhyanta-madhyadyady-anta-sarvatah \|\|*

***Yamaka*** **means using same sounds (but different meaning) in the first, second, third or fourth parts of the verse, with the recurrence in the beginning, middle or end, or middle and end, or middle and beginning, or beginning and end. (*****Kavyadarsa*** **3.2)**

The verse has another meaning. Aye is used to indicate remembrance. О heart (*hrd*)! *Aye* (remembrance)! I remember that I played with Krsna. Though I vanquished my pain (*vedanam*) at that time, now I do not see him. Thus the friend of Cupid (moon) gives me pain.

**\|\| Song Fifteen \|\|**

*gurjari-raga, eka-tali*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***gurjari*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali*****.**

### Text 7.22

*samudita-madane ramani-vadane*  
*cumbana-valitadhare \|*  
*mrga-mada-tilakam likhati sa-pulakam*  
*mrgam iva rajani-kare \|\|22\|\|*

*ramate уamuna-pulina-vane vijayi murarir adhuna \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*samudita* - Is aroused; *madane* - in whom passion; *ramani* - of the woman; *vadane* - on the face; *cumbana* - for kissing; *valita* - \[is\] endowed with; *adhare* - whose lip; *mrga-mada* - of musk; *tilakam* - a *tilaka*; *likhati* - he draws; *sa-pulakam* - with hairs standing on end; *mrgam* - a dear; *iva* - as if; *rajani-kare* - on the moon \[the night-maker\], *ramate* - Plays; *yamuna* - of the Yamuna; *pulina* - on the bank; *vane* - in the forest; *vijayi* - expertly; *murarir* - Murari \[Krsna\]; *adhuna* - now.

**Перевод:**

**\[Using her imagination, Radha continues\] “With hairs standing on end, he draws musk tilaka on the woman’s face, aroused with passion, with lips ready for kissing. The tilaka is like the deer on the moon.**

**“Krsna now plays in the forest on the bank of the Yamuna. (Chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Having ascertained the enjoyment of another woman in the previous song, the independent woman (Radha) now describes Krsna, using her imagination.

*Vijayi* means one who is most expert in skillful dressing, etc. or who is free of fear. Murari (Killer of the demon Mura) indicates that Krsna is without love, since he exhibits rough behavior in killing the demon. Or Murari can mean “he who surrounds (*murayati*) a woman with unlimited play during love.” He is enthusiastic for love. Now he plays in the forest on the sandy bank of the Yamuna. This shows the good qualities of a suitable place for gaining relief from the fatigue of love making.

The playing is described as follows. He draws *tilaka* of great variety using musk on the woman’s face while his hairs stand on end. *Ramani* indicates a woman who gives joy to Krsna. That face \[on which he drew *tilaka*\] has developed desire by the favorable touch of the lover (*samudite madane*). Or, that face produces desire in Krsna. That face is endowed with lips turned towards Krsna and contracted for kissing. Or, the face has lips long-worshipped (*valita*) by kisses. Or, the face has lips which request to be kissed. *Valita* means ‘requested’ according to *Visva*. He draws *tilaka* which looks like the deer in the full moon. On kissing the lips there is sweetness like nectar flowing from the moon. The musk is like the deer and the face is like the moon, surrounded by locks of hair. The description of the face indicates the extreme passion of the woman rather than of the man. This is a criticism of the woman. Like the moon’s beauty (which changes through the lunar phase cycle), her beauty is not stable. Radha’s beauty however is permanent.

### Text 7.23

*ghana-caya-rucire racayati cikure*  
*taralita-tarunanane \|*  
*kurubaka-kusumam capala susamam*  
*rati-pati-mrga-kanane \|\|23\|\|*

*ramate уamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ghana* - Of clouds; *caya -* \[as\] a mass; *rucire* - attractive; *racayati* -\[he\] arranges; *cikure* - in \[her\] hair; *taralita* - \[is\] agitated; *taruna* - of the young men; *anane* - by which the best; *kurubaka* - golden; *kusumam* - flowers; *capala* - \[as\] lightning; *susamam* - beautiful; *rati-pati* - \[who is\] the Cupid; *mrga* - of the deer; *kanane* - the home.

**Перевод:**

**“He arranges unfading golden flowers, beautiful as lightning, in her locks which are as attractive as a mass of clouds, which are the home of the deer known as Cupid and which agitate Krsna, the best of young men.**

**“Krsna now plays...**

**Комментарий:**

He arranges unfading, golden flowers (*kurubaka*) in her locks, which are attractive like a mass of clouds and are the forest home of the deer known as Cupid (*rati-pati*). The locks agitate the best of young men (*taruna anane*) with love. Or, the locks make the mouth of young Krsna glorify her qualities. Or, the phrase *tarunanane* can modify *kanane*. She has locks of hair like a forest. The flowers tied in her hair appears like the flowers of young trees (*taruna*) growing in that forest. The forest is compared to her hair, since both the forest and the hair induce love and both have blooming flowers. The flowers are beautiful like lightning (*capala-susamam*). The ornament is *rupaka* (metaphor). If the verse means that Krsna strings flowers in another woman’s hair, then his hands do not shake. This means he is not attached to her.

*ma garvam udvaha kapola-tale cakasti*  
*krsna-svahasta-likhita nava-manjariti \|*  
*anyapi kim na sakhi bhajanam idrsmam*  
*vairi na ced bhavati vepathur antarayah \|\|*

**“My friend, please do not be proud because Krsna has decorated your forehead with his own hand. It may be that Krsna is yet attracted by some other beautiful girl. I see that the decoration on your forehead is very nicely made, and so it appears that Krsna was not too disturbed in painting it. Otherwise, he could not have painted such exact lines!” (*****Padyavali*** **303)**

*Mrga* means a deer according to *Visva*. *Kanana* means a forest or a house. *Cikuram* means hair. *Susama* means shining brightly. *Cikura* means hair or locks according to *Amara-kosa*.

### Text 7.24

*ghatayati sughane kuca-yuga-gagane*  
*mrga-mada-ruci-rusite \|*  
*mani-saram amalam taraka-patalam*  
*nakha-pada-sasi-bhusite \|\|24\|\|*

*ramate уamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ghatayati* - \[He\] makes; *sughane* - very firm; *kuca* - of \[her\] breasts; *yuga* - of the pair; *gagane* - on the sky; *mrga-mada* - of musk; *ruci* - with the shining; *rusite* - spread; *mani-saram* - a pearl necklace; *amalam* - spotless; *taraka* - of stars; *patalam* - \[into\] a host; *nakha* - of nails; *pada* - \[which are\] the marks; *sasi* - with \[half-\]moons; *bhusite* - ornamented.

**Перевод:**

**“He makes a pearl necklace become a host of stars on the sky of her firm breasts, spread with shining musk, ornamented with nail marks like half moons.**

**“Krsna now plays...**

**Комментарий:**

He makes a spotless pearl necklace become a host of stars in the sky of her two breasts. He produces the beauty in the sky. That sky of her breasts is very firm (*sughane*), and spread with the glow of musk. And it is ornamented with the moon of nail marks. The nail marks are called *ardhendu* (half moon). It is said in *Panca-sayaka*:

*ardhendu-sankasam idam nakha-jnair*  
*ardhendu-samjnam kathitam samasat \|*  
*haksa-nitamba-stana-parsva-kanthe*  
*datavyam etat karajam sadaiva \|\|*

**Marks made by the finger nails which resemble half moons are called ardhendu. These are made on the throat, side, breast, waist or armpit.**

The breasts are compared to the sky which is ‘most extensive (*sughane*)' or ‘which has beautiful clouds’. And there is a host of pure stars ornamented with the half moon. It is a tradition among poets that the sky is blackish. In this way *rupaka* (metaphor) ornament is presented.

*Ghana* means firm or broad according to *Visva*. *Rusita* means strung.

### Text 7.25

*jita-bisa-sakale mrdu-bhuja-yugale*  
*kara-tala-nalini-dale \|*  
*marakata-valayam madhukara-nicayam*  
*vitarati hima-sitale \|\|25\|\|*

*ramate уamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jita* - which have surpassed; *bisa* - of the lotus stalk; *sakale* - pieces of; *mrdu* - soft; *bhuja* - of \[her\] arms; *yugale* - to the pair; *kara* -whose hands; *tala* - \[is\] the surface; *nalini* - of the lotus; *dale* - the petal; *marakata* - of sapphire; *valayam* - bracelets; *madhukara* - of bees; *nicayam* - \[which are\] a swarm; *vitarati* - \[he\] gives; *hima* - as snow; *sitale* - cool.

**Перевод:**

**“He places sapphire bracelets, like a swarm of bees, on her cool arms, which resemble water lily stems, with hands resembling lotus petals.**

**“Krsna now plays...**

**Комментарий:**

He offers sapphire bracelets, a swarm of bees, to her tender arms which surpass water lily stems in softness and whiteness, with hands like lotus petals, very cool, since they remove heat. Though poets normally do not compare hands to lotus petals, it is acceptable here since the arms are compared to water lily stems. The bracelets on her arms are like bees clustered on a water lily stem. *Nalini* is a lotus according to *Dharani.* *Vitara* means “to give” according to *Amara-kosa*. The ornament is *rupaka* (metaphor).

### Text 7.26

*rati-grha-jaghane vipulapaghane*  
*manasija-kanakasane \|*  
*mani-maya-rasanam torana-hasanam*  
*vikirati krta-vasane \|\|26\|\|*

*ramate yamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rati* - of love; *grha* - \[which are\] the abode; *jaghane* - on \[her\] hips; *vipulapaghane* - very fleshy; *manasija* - of Cupid; *kanakasane* - the golden throne; *mani* - in pearls; *maya* - consisting; *rasanam* - a belt; *torana* - an auspicious garland; *hasanam* - deriding; *vikirati* - \[he\] throws; *krta* - \[is\] placed; *vasane* - \[and\] on which cloth.

**Перевод:**

**“He throws a pearl belt which derides an auspicious garland, on her fleshy hips - the abode of love, the golden throne of Cupid -covered with cloth.**

**“Krsna now plays...**

**Комментарий:**

He throws a belt of pearls on her hips, the abode of *srngara-rasa* (*rati-grha*). The word *kirati* (throws) indicates that his trembling hands throw the belt over her hips, since he cannot tie the belt. His hands tremble with fear on touching her hips as he tries to tie the belt. The hips are very fleshy (*vipulapaghane*). Or, they are great (*vipula*), and without hardness (very soft—*apaghana*). Or, the hips are a limb covered with goose bumps (*vipula*). She shows *sattvika-bhavas*. The hips are the golden throne of Cupid. The belt derides (*hasanam*) an auspicious garland (*torana*). When someone sits on a throne, followers place a garland around the throne. On the throne of her hips, an auspicious garland of bells is placed. Garments are also placed on her hips (*krta-vasane*). Or, the hips are perfumed with a special, stimulating incense to bring Krsna under control. Or, the hips make Krsna desire to enjoy on seeing her. Or, the hips have become the abode of Cupid. This is logical since her hips are the throne of Cupid. He constantly resides there.

*Apaghana* means limb or body according to *Amara-kosa*. *Vasana* means desire, cloth or place according to *Visva*. *Pula* means goose bumps. *Rati* means Cupid’s wife, attachment or amorous actions. The ornament is *rupaka* (metaphor).

### Text 7.27

*carana-kisalaye kamala-nilaye*  
*nakha-mani-gana-pujite \|*  
*bahir-apavaranarn yavaka-bharanam*  
*janayati hrdi yojite \|\|27\|\|*

*ramate yamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*carana* - Which are \[her\] feet; *kisalaye* - on the buds; *kamala* - of Kamala (Laksmi); *nilaye* - the abode; *nakha* - \[which are\] nails; *mani* - of jewels; *gana* - with groups; *pujite* - ornamented; *bahir* - external; *apavaranam* - the covering; *yavaka* - of lac; *bharanam* - wearing; *janayati* - \[he\] produces; *brdi* - to \[his\] chest; *yojite* -attached.

**Перевод:**

**“Putting her feet on his chest, he decorates the buds of her feet - which are the abode of Laksmi, ornamented with jewels in the form of her toe nails - with lac, serving only as an external covering.**

**“Krsna now plays...**

**Комментарий:**

He puts lac on the buds of her feet. The feet are compared to buds because they are tender and red. They are the abode of Laksmi and are ornamented (*pujite*) with jewel-like nails. Laksmi, who finds it hard to attain the Lord, has merged into those feet worshipped by the vibhutis in the form of her jewel-like nails, to offer herself to the Lord. The lac is an external covering on her feet (*bahir-apavaranam*). Her feet, by nature reddish, do not need a covering. Those feet are on his chest out of great attachment. Placing her foot on his chest, he decorates it with lac.

Or his chest is the abode of Laksmi, worshipped by jewels in the form of nail scratches. He holds her foot on his chest, even though his chest is endowed with Laksmi. This indicates the woman’s beauty and good fortune.

Or that chest is the abode of the best of women, Radha, and is decorated with her nail marks. The foot is placed on this chest and he decorates it in an ordinary way, but not with great details. That lac is scattered here and there (*bahir-apavaranam*).

Since buds and jewels do not usually go together, the ornament is called *ayukta-rupaka.* This is described by *Dandi*:

*idam ardra-smitam jyotsnam snigdha-netrotpalam mukham \|*  
*iti jyotsnotpalayogat ayuktam пата rupakam \|\|*

**An example of** ***ayukta-rupaka*** **is the following: her face has slight-smile like moonlight with attractive lotuses for eyes. Moon light and lotuses do not exist together. (*****Kavyadarsa*** **2.78)**

*Nakha* means nail according to *Visva.*

### Text 7.28

*ramayati subhrsam kam api sudrsam*  
*khala-haladhara-sodare \|*  
*kim aphalam avasam ciram iha virasam*  
*vada sakhi vitapodare \|\|28\|\|*

*ramate yamuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ramayati* - \[While he\] enjoys; *subhrsam* - profusely; *kam api* - some woman; *sudrsam* - beautiful-eyed; *khala* - \[who is\] a debaucherous person; *haladhara* - of Balarama; *sodare* - the brother (Krsna); *kim* -should; *aphalam* - uselessly; *avasam* - I stay; *ciram* - for a long time; *iha* - here; *virasam* - without *rasa*; *vada* - tell \[me\] ; *sakhi* - о female friend; *vitapodare* - in the bower

**Перевод:**

**“O my sakhi! Please tell me if I should stay a long time in this bower uselessly, without rasa, while the debaucherous brother of Balarama heartily enjoys with some beautiful-eyed woman?**

**“Krsna now plays...”**

**Комментарий:**

О friend! While the debaucherous (*khala*) Krsna, brother of Balarama, enjoys profusely (*subhrsam*) with some beautiful-eyed woman, please tell me, should I stay a long time in this bower uselessly without *rasa*? By addressing Krsna as the brother of Balarama she scolds Krsna, using crude speech. *Khala* means a lusty person according to *Visva*. Or, should I remain in this place in order to be with a rascal (*vitapodare*) for a long time? Locative case can be used to indicate “in order to.”

The previous seven verses (7.22 - 7.28) can also be taken as the words of a *sakhi*. In that case, this verse has the following meaning. Will my stay in the forest be without result and without rasa when the crest jewel, the brother of the person who holds a plough to punish the sinful, enjoys with Radha in the bower? (rhetorical question) It will be with result and full of *rasa*! Mention of Balarama indicates strength to subdue the wicked. It is hinted that Balarama will assist in producing pastimes without fear.

### Text 7.29

*iha rasa-bhanane krta-hari-gunane*  
*madhuripu-pada-sevake \|*  
*kali-yuga-racitam na vasatu duritam*  
*kavi-nrpa-jayadevake \|\|29\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*iha* - In the writer of this poem; *rasa* - *srngara rasa;* *bhanane* -describing; *krta* - is made; *hari* - of Krsna; *gunane* - by whom the description of the qualities; *madhuripu* - of Madhuripu (Krsna); *pada* - at the feet; *sevake* - serving; *kali-yuga* - in Kaliyuga; *racitam* - performed; *na* - not; *vasatu* - may \[it\] dwell; *duritam* - the sin; *kavi* - of poets; *nrpa* - the king, *jayadevake* - in Jayadeva.

**Перевод:**

**May the sins performed in Kali-yuga not dwell in the writer of this poem, Jayadeva, king of poets, who describes srngara-rasa, who makes one think of the Lord, and who serves the feet of Krsna. (END OF SONG FIFTEEN)**

**Комментарий:**

May the sins (*duritam*) performed in Kali-yuga or by Kali-yuga not dwell in the writer of this poem (*iha*), Jayadeva, king of poets, who describes *srngara-rasa*, who makes one think of Krsna (*krta-hari-gunane*), and who serves the feet of Krsna (*madhu-ripu*). The word *madhu-ripu* (enemy of the demon Madhu) indicates that Krsna is capable of destroying the wicked.

### Text 7.30

*nayatah sakhi nirdayo yadi sathas tvam duti him duyase*  
*svacchandam bahu-vallabhah sa ramate kim tatra te dusanam \|*  
*pasyadya priya-sangamaya dayitasyakrsyamanam gunair*  
*utkantharti-bharad iva sphutad idam cetah svayam уasyati \|\|30\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nayatah* - \[Has\] not come; *sakhi* - о female friend; *nirdayo* - heartless; *yadi* - if; *sathas* - the cheater; *tvam* - you; *duti* - messenger; *kith* - why; *duyase* - do grieve? *svacchandam* - Fearlessly; *bahu* - of many \[women\]; *vallabhah* - lover; *sa* - the; *ramate* - enjoys, *kim* - What; *tatra* - with repsect to that; *te* - you; *dusanam* - \[is\] fault? *pasyadya* - Now see; *priya* - with \[my\] beloved; *sangamaya* - for a meeting; *dayitasya* - of \[my\] lover; *akrsyamanam* - attracted; *gunair* - by the qualities; *utkantharti* - of longing and pain; *bharad* - out of the burden; *iva* - as if; *sphutad idam* - this; *cetah* - heart; *svayam* - my own; *yasyati* - goes.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha continues speaking to sakhi,\] “O messenger! If the heartless cheater has not come, why do you grieve? The lover of many women is enjoying fearlessly. That is not your fault. See at this moment! My heart, attracted by the qualities of my lover and leaving the body out of longing and pain, goes to meet him.”**

**Комментарий:**

О *gopi* expert at delivering messages (*duti*)! If the cheater—the great offender (*sathah*), devoid of mercy, has not come, why should you grieve? Not only is he a cheater. He is arrogant. Affectionate to many women, he enjoys without fear (*svacchandam*). This means he is enjoying with some other *gopi*. What fault do you have for that (*tatra*)? You have no fault at all.

\[*Sakhi* speaks to Radha,\] “Recover for a moment. While you do so, I will go to him and bring him here. See something astonishing right now (*adya*).” The phrase may also be taken as *pasya adya-priya-sangamaya* where *adya-priya* refers to Krsna. Then the meaning is as follows \[Radha replies to *sakhi*,\] “Look! My mind (*idam cetah*) will go spontaneously (*svayam*), without conscious effort to meet (*sangamaya*) Krsna (*adya-priya*). My mind is forcibly pulled by the qualities (*gunaih*) of this person dearer than a million lives (*dayitasya*). Others are pulled elsewhere forcibly by ropes (*gunaih*). That mind leaves the body (*sphutam*) because of the extreme pressure of pain and longing. Others, who are overcome with the burden of a host of other objects, have their hearts broken. Since I cannot tolerate the sorrow any longer, remembering his qualities, I will give up my life. Then that mind will go easily to him. So what is the use if you go to him?”

In the first line the hero is called a cheater. His arrogance is conveyed by the word *svacchandam* (fearless). Those qualities are described as follows. *Satho gudhaparadha-krt dhrsto vyaktany-yuvati-bhoga-lahsmapi nirbhayah*: the deceiver commits a grievous offense and the arrogant lover is fearless though he explicitly displays the marks of enjoyment with another woman - (*Rasarnava-sudhakara* 1.81). The words describing Krsna are spoken with angry affection, for ultimately he is most subservient to Radha. This is described later. *Krite dasa ivopasevaita-padmabhoje*: he is the servant of your lotus feet. (GG 11.22) The ornament is *utprehsa*.

**\|\| Song Sixteen \|\|**

*desa-vradi raga rupakta-tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***desa-vradi*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***rupaka*****.**

Epigraph to the song: In eight verses, Radha describes the pain of the woman in separation and the happiness of the woman enjoying with Krsna. The song is in *desakhya-raga*, *rupaka-tala*.

### Text 7.31

*anila-tarala-kuvalaya-nayanena \|*  
*tapati na sa kisalaya-sayanena \|\|31\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*anila* - In the wind; *tarala* - swaying; *kuvalaya* - \[are like\] lotuses; *nayanena* - whose eyes; *tapati* - suffer; *na* - not; *sa* - she; *kisalaya* - of shoots; *sayanena* - on the bed; *sakhi* - о female friend; *ya* - who; *ramita* - is delighted; *vana-malina* - by he who wears a forest-garland \[Krsna\],

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha speaks to sakhi\] “O friend! She who enjoys with Krsna, (chorus) whose eyes are like lotuses swaying in the wind, does not suffer on the bed of shoots**

**Комментарий:**

О friend! She who enjoys with Krsna (*vana-malina*) does not suffer on the bed of new shoots. That bed gives happiness in association with Krsna’s limbs. Or, the woman who enjoys with Krsna suffers on the bed of new shoots. This is said in a plaintive voice while shaking the head.

*dina-pariksaye nila-kuntalair*  
*vanaruhananam bibhrad avrtam*  
*dhana-rajasvalam darsayan muhur*  
*manasi nah smaram vira yacchasi*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “At the end of the day you repeatedly show us your lotus face, covered with dark blue locks of hair and thickly powdered with dust. Thus, О hero, you arouse desires in our minds.” (SB 10.31.12)**

One who has no experience of Krsna does not have to suffer. Or, one who enjoys with Krsna is not satisfied on the bed of shoots because of thirst for more union repeatedly, while trembling out of astonishing affection. It is said:

*calasi уad vrajac carayan pasun*  
*nalina-sundaram natha te padam*  
*sila-trnankuraih sidatiti nah*  
*kalilatam manah kanta gacchati*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “Dear master, dear lover, when you leave the cowherd village to herd the cows, our minds are disturbed with the thought that your feet, more beautiful than a lotus, will be pricked by the spiked husks of grain and the rough grass and plants.” (SB 10.31.11)**

Krsna is described as follows. He has eyes like a lotus moving in the wind. Or, he has eyes beautiful as lotuses and moving like the wind. The word *vana-malina* indicates *srngara-rasa*. *Vana-malina* can also be *vana-ma alina* which means “by the bee, lusty for tasting the *rasa* in the forest of Radha.” The woman who is enjoyed by this lusty bee does not suffer on the bed of shoots since he is compliant.

### Text 7.32

*vikasita-sarasija-lalita-mukhena \|*  
*sphutati na sa manasija-visikhena \|\|32\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vikasita* - blossoming; *sarasija* - as a lotus; *lalita* - \[is as\] attractive; *mukhena* - by he (Krsna) whose face; *sphutati* - is injured; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *manasija* - of Cupid; *visikhena* - by the arrows.

**Перевод:**

**“She who is enjoyed by Krsna whose face is as attractive as a blossoming lotus is not injured by the arrows of Cupid.**

**Комментарий:**

This woman, who is enjoyed by Krsna, is not wounded by the arrows of Cupid. Krsna is described as follows. He has a face attractive like a bossoming lotus. It is astonishing that the pain of love is extinguished by the lotus of his face.

### Text 7.33

*amrta-madhura-mrdutara-vacanena \|*  
*jvalati na sa malayaja-pavanena \|\|33\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*amrta* - Than nectar; *madhura* - \[are\] sweeter; *mrdutara* - \[and\] softer; *vacanena* - by he \[Krsna\] whose words; *jvalati* - does burn; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *malayaja* - born in the Malaya mountain; *pavanena* - because of the wind.

**Перевод:**

**“She, enjoyed by Krsna whose words are sweeter and softer than nectar, does not burn because of the Malaya wind.**

**Комментарий:**

She who is enjoyed by Krsna does not become enflamed by the wind coming from Malaya Mountain. Krsna speaks exquisitely soft, sweet words. It is said:

*madhuraya gira valgu-vakyaya*  
*budha-manojnaya puskareksana*  
*vidhi-karir ima vira muhyatir*  
*adhara-sidhunapyayayasva nah*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “O lotus-eyed one, by your sweet voice and charming words, which attract the minds of the intelligent, we have fainted. Our dear hero, please revive your maidservants with the nectar of your lips.” (SB 10.31.8)**

The fever caused by the wind mixed with poison from the snake in the sandalwood tree is removed by the nectar of his words. This verse illustrates the hero’s quality of speaking sweetly.

### Text 7.34

*sthala-jala-ruha-ruci-kara-caranena \|*  
*luthati na sa himakara-kiranena \|\|34\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sthala* - On land (the hibiscus); *jala-ruha* - like the lotus; *ruci* - \[are\] charming; *kara-caranena* - by he (Krsna) whose hands and feet; *luthati* - does roll on the ground \[in pain\]; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *himakara* - of the moon; *kiranena* - because of the rays.

**Перевод:**

**“She who enjoys with Krsna, whose hands and feet are charming like the hibiscus, does not roll on the ground in pain because of the moon light.**

**Комментарий:**

She who is enjoyed by Krsna does not roll on the ground because of the rays of the moon. Krsna has charming hands and feet like lotuses growing on land (hibiscus), or which are charming like lotuses growing on land.

### Text 7.35

*sajala-jalada-samudaya-rucirena \|*  
*dalati na sa hrdi viraha-bharena \|\|35\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sajala* - Full of water; *jalada* - of clouds; *samudaya* - as the assemblage; *rucirena* - by he (Krsna) attractive; *dalati* - does burn; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *hrdi* - in \[her\] heart; *viraha* - of separation; *bharena* - from the burden.

**Перевод:**

**“She who is enjoyed by Krsna, attractive as a cloud full of rain, does not suffer from intense separation in her heart.**

**Комментарий:**

She who enjoys with Krsna does not burn with intense separation in her heart. Krsna is attractive like a rising cloud full of water. This verse indicates the Lord’s ever-fresh nature, his attractiveness, his ability to destroy pain, his affection, his ability to cause increase \[in everything\], and his nature of being full of *rasa*.

### Text 7.36

*kanaka-nikasa-ruci-suci-vasanena \|*  
*svasiti na sa parijana-hasanena \|\|36\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kanaka* - Of gold; *nikasa* - on a testing stone; *ruci* - as the appearance; suci - \[is\] as effulgent; *vasanena* - by he \[Krsna\] whose clothing; *svasiti* - does sigh; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *parijana* - of \[her\] associates; *hasanena* - because of the mocking.

**Перевод:**

**“She who is enjoyed by Krsna, who wears clean cloth effulgent like golden marks on a testing stone, does not breath in pain because of being mocked by her associates.**

**Комментарий:**

The woman who is enjoyed by Krsna does not release breaths of pain because of laughter from other people around her (*parijana*). Krsna wears pure cloth whose effulgence is like golden letters on a testing stone. *Nikasa* is a stone used for testing gold. This indicates that the cloth is yellow, but never fades due to water or other substances. Or, he has effulgence like a golden stone in a cloth made of *srngara-rasa* (*suci*). Or, he has effulgence like a golden testing stone and wears clean cloth. *Suci* means *srngara* or pure according to *Visva*. *Nikasa* means a testing stone according to *Haravali.*

### Text 7.37

*sakala-bhuvana-jana-vara-tarunena \|*  
*vahati na sa rujam atikarunena \|\|37\|\|*

*sakhi ya ramita vana-malina \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sakala* - Entire; *bhuvana* - in the world; *jana* - of \[all\] people; *vara* - \[is\] the best; *tarunena* - by he (Krsna) whose youth; *vahati -* does bear; *na* - not; *sa* - she \[who is enjoyed\]; *rujam* - pain; *atikarunena* - because of \[his\] great compassion \[to her\].

**Перевод:**

**“The woman enjoyed by Krsna, whose youthful beauty is the best in all the worlds, does not have pain arising from separation, because he shows great compassion to her.”**

**Комментарий:**

The woman enjoyed by Krsna does not have pain arising from separation (*rujam*) because of the great compassion that he displays. Krsna has astonishing beauty endowed with youth that is better than all the youthful people in the world. He is eternally *kaisora*. Or, he has more youthful beauty than the people of the world existing with beauty (*sa-kala*). It is said *sva-murtya loka-lavanya- nirmuktya locanam nrnam*: I steal people’s eyes with my form which makes them reject all other beauty - (SB 11.1.6). Other types of beauty arise from only a particle of his beauty. Or, he has better youth than the Lord (*jana*) of the splendid (*sakala*) world, Vaikuntha, because of his superior beauty. The Lord of Vaikuntha is famous for being ancient. It is said *vismpanam svasya ca*: \[Uddhava said,\] “that form (Krsna’s form) astonishes even the Lord of Vaikuntha” - (SB 3.2.12). *Taruna* refers to the middle part of *kaisora*. This middle age is described as follows:

*urи-dvayasya bahvos ca kapi srir urasas tatha \|*  
*murter madhurimadyam ca kaisore sati madhyame \|\|*

**During the middle period of the kaisora period, Krsna displays indescribable beauty in his two thighs, his two arms and his chest, and sweetness in his whole form. (BRS 2.1.320)**

The sweetness of this age is illustrated:

*navath dhanur ivatanor natad-agha-dvisor bhru-yugam*  
*saralir iva sanita nakhara-rajir agre khara \|*  
*virajati saririni rucira-danta-lekharuna*  
*na ka sakhi samiksanad yuvatir asya vitrasyati \|\|*

**The two eyebrows of the enemy of Agha are dancing like new bows of Cupid. The tips of his rows of fingernails are so sharp that they shine like line of arrows. The glistening rows of his attractive teeth embody the redness of dawn. What young woman would not be afraid of seeing him? (BRS 2.1.318)**

Or, he has beauty superior to that of all the boys of Vraja.

*Atikarunena* can also modify Krsna. Krsna is most affectionate (*ati* - very; *karunena* - merciful) endowed with the highest *rasa.* Or he is without mercy (*ati* - gone beyond).

It was explained at the beginning of this chapter (see commentary of GG 7.1) that there are two meanings. In the first meaning, Radha speaks in four ways (in four songs- thirteen, fourteen, fifteen and sixteen). In the second meaning, a *sakhi* speaks to inform her in seven verses (GG 7.31 - 7.37). “O friend! You are an independent woman. You have enjoyed with him. Your love is by your side. Why should you lament in separation. She who has enjoyed should act in the following way (as described in the above seven verses).” Or “she who has enjoyed” indicates the woman’s victory in love. She who has enjoyed will never be without a taste for him.

### Text 7.38

*sri-jayadeva-bhanita-vacanena \|*  
*pravisatu harir api hrdayam anena \|\|38\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanita* - spoken; *vacanena* - by words \[of Radha\]; *pravisatu* - may \[he\] enter; *harir* - Krsna; *api* - even; *hrdayam* - \[your\] heart; *anena* - those.

**Перевод:**

**May Krsna enter your heart by the words of Radha described by Jayadeva. (END OF SONG SIXTEEN)    .**

**Комментарий:**

By the words of Radha or her *sakhi* (see commentary of GG 7.37) as related by the poet Jayadeva, may Krsna, who steals the heart enter into the hearts of the devotees, or into Radha’s heart.

Or Hari indicates he who has astonishing prowess. May Krsna, though uncontrolled by anyone, be captured. Having entered the trap he cannot get out.

### Text 7.39

*manobhavanandana candananila*  
*prasida re daksina munca vamatam \|*  
*ksanam jagat-prana nidhaya madhavam*  
*puro mama prana-haro bhavisyasi \|\|39\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*manobhava* - For Cupid; *anandana* - bliss; *candananila* - о sandalwood breeze; *praslda* - settle down; *re* - o; *daksina* - gentle \[one\]; *munca* - \[or\] give up; *vamatam* - \[your\] contrary nature; *ksanam* - For a moment; *jagat-prana* - о life of the world; *nidhaya* - having placed; *madhavam* - Madhava (Krsna); *puro* - before \[me\]; *mama* - my; *prana* - of life; *haro* - the taker away; *bhavisyasi* - \[otherwise\] you will become.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha laments\] “O sandalwood breeze, giving bliss to Cupid! О gentle wind! Please give up your uncooperative nature. О life of the universe! \[Otherwise,\] having placed Madhava before me for a moment, you then become responsible for my life (you take away my life.**

**Комментарий:**

О sandalwood breeze! Please be merciful to me. О you with favor to all, inclined to pastimes (*daksina*)! Give up your contrary nature (*vamatam*)\] О breeze! Since you have a compliant nature (*daksina*), it is not proper to act crookedly toward the person in love through your contact with sandalwood. Or, О compliant one, be merciful. But if you are not merciful, then show your contrary nature.

“I do not have a contrary nature.”

О person who gives bliss to Cupid! You act against me. If you do not reject that contrary nature, then, О life of the universe, bring Krsna in front of me for a moment and then take my life. By addressing the wind as “life of the universe” she actually indicates that by not bringing him again, the wind has the power to end her life. This means that because of affection for Krsna whom she cannot give up, she prays for life. By attaining him she can remain living. Or, by putting one who is enjoyed by other women in front of her, she will die in a moment. The word Madhava also suggests one who causes unsteadiness by his attractive nature.

The ornament called *virodha* (metaphor of contradiction) is shown by describing the contrary nature of the compliant wind and its ability to take life, though it is the life of the universe. *Vama* means inimical. *Daksina* means compliant.

### Text 7.40

*ripur iva sakhi-samvaso’yam sikhiva himanilo*  
*visam iva sudha-rasmir yasmin dunoti mano-gate \|*  
*hrdayam adaye tasminn evam punar valate balat*  
*kuvalaya-drsam vamah kamo nikama-nirankusah \|\|40\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*ripur* - \[Is\] an enemy; *iva* - as if; *sakhi* - with a friend; *samvaso* - living; *ayam* - this; *sikhi* - \[is\] a fire; *iva* - as if; *himanilo* - cool breeze; *visam* - \[is\] poison; *iva* - as if; *sudha* - of the moon; *rasmir* - \[and\] the ray; *yasmin* - in him; *dunoti* - \[when\] is absorbed; *mano-gate* - \[my\] thought; *hrdayam* - \[My\] heart; *adaye* - cruel; *tasminn* - in him; *evam* - only; *punar* - again; *valate* - goes; *balat* -with force; *kuvalaya-drsam* - For lotus-eyed women; *vamah* - \[is\] uncooperative; *kamo* - the Cupid; *nikama* - excessively; *nirankusah* - \[and\] uncontrolled.

**Перевод:**

**“When my mind is absorbed in him, living with friends gives pain as if they were enemies. The cool breeze gives pain like fire. The rays of the moon give pain like poison. My heart goes to that cruel person with great force. For lotus-eyed women, Cupid is most uncontrolled and uncooperative.**

**Комментарий:**

Everything is my mistake and no one else’s. With my mind on Krsna (*yasmin mano-gate*), living with friends gives pain like living with enemies. The cool breeze gives pain like fire. The rays of the moon give pain like poison. He is without mercy (*adaye*) since he gives pain to others in this way.

Because of this, he should not be addressed by name. He is addressed as *yasmin* and *tasmin*. Or he is not named because a husband’s name should not be spoken by the wife. The heart—mind, intelligence, ego and *citta*—most forcibly go to him (*valate*), though prevented.

For lotus-eyed women, Cupid is completely uncontrolled and unfavorable (*vamah*). Cupid is attractive for those who have mutual attraction. In this case however, Krsna has no attraction for me. Only my heart runs after him. This makes Cupid unfavorable to me.

Or, when he goes elsewhere (*yasmin gate*), living with friends gives pain to the mind (*mano*) as if they were enemies. Friends are like enemies, the cool breeze is like fire and the rays of the moon are like poison. This is the ornament called *virodha* (metaphor of contradiction). *Yamaka* (repetition of sounds) is also present. *Sikhi* means fire according to *Amara-kosa.*

### Text 7.41

*badham vidhehi malayanila panca-bana*  
*pranan grhana na grham punar asrayisye \|*  
*kim te krtanta-bhagini ksamaya tarangair*  
*ahgani sinca mama samyatu deha-dahah \|\|41\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*badham* - Pain; *vidhehi* - give \[me\]; *malayanila* - о Malaya wind; *panca* - \[Are\] five; *bana* - о he whose arrows (the Cupid); *pranan* - \[my\] life; *grhana* - take, na - Not; *grham* - home; *punar* - again; *asrayisye* - I will go; *kim* - What \[is the purpose of\]; *te* - your; *krtanta* - of death \[Yama\]; *bhagini* - о sister \[Yamuna\]; *ksamaya* - peace; *tarangair* - With waves; *ahgani* - limbs; *sinca* - sprinkle; *mama* - my; *samyatu* - May \[it\] be extinguished; *deha* - in fmy\] body; *dahah* - the fire.

**Перевод:**

**“O Malaya wind! Give me pain. О Cupid holding five arrows! Take my life. I will not go home again. О Yamuna, sister of death! Why be peaceful? Cover my body with your waves. May the fire in my body be thus extinguished!”**

**Комментарий:**

Not able to tolerate the pain of separation she now decorates herself with the Malaya wind. О wind coming from Malaya Mountain! Give me pain. It is hinted that it is not proper to refuse the request of a person who has completely surrendered. О Cupid (*panca-bana*)! Take my five pranas with your five arrows. Cupid is addressed as “holder of five arrows” since it is thus easy for him to take her five life airs.

“Why do you not go home?”

“I will not go again to my house. Without Krsna my house will only give me more pain.

О sister of Yama (Yamuna)! What is the use in being so peaceful (*ksamaya*)! Flood my limbs with waves. May the burning in my body caused by separation be extinguished. Help your brother Yama (*death*) by taking my life airs. Since you are the sister of merciless death, showing compassion to me now is not proper. This is indicated by addressing her as the sister of death. *Bhagim* means sister according to *Amara-kosa*.

*sammohanah ksobhanas ca dahanah sosanas tatha \|*  
*uccatanas ca kamasya banah рапса prakirtitah \|\|*

**The five arrows of Cupid are bewilderment, agitation, burning, drying and ruination.**

### Text 7.42

*pratar nila-nicolam acyutam urah samvita-pitamsukam*  
*radhayas cakitam vilokya hasati svairam sakhi-mandale \|*  
*vrida-cancalam ancalam nayanayor adhaya radhanane*  
*svadu-smera-mukho’yam astu jagad-anandaya nandatmajah \|\|42\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pratar* - In the morning; *nila*- \[is\] blue \[belonging to Radha\]; *nicolam* - whose cloth; *acyutam* - Krsna; *urah* - the breast; *samvita* - \[is\] covering \[her\]; *pita* - yellow; *amsukam* - whose cloth \[belonging to Krsna\]; *radhayas -* of Radha; *cakitam* - frightened; *vilokya* - having seen; *hasati* - \[while it\] laughs; *svairam* - freely; *sakhi* - of female friends; *mandale* - the group; *vrida* - with bashfulness; *caficalam* - restless; *ancalam* - the corner; *nayanayor* - of his eyes; *adhaya* -having placed; *radha* - of Radha; *anane* - on the face; *svadu* - \[is\] sweet; *smera* - \[and\] smiling; *mukho* - whose face; *ayam* - this; *astu* - may \[he\] be; *jagad* - of the world; *anandaya* - for the joy; *nanda* - of Nanda; *atmajah* - the son.

**Перевод:**

**May the son of Nanda, with wondrous smiling face, give joy to the universe as he glances from the corner of his restless, shy eye at Radha’s face, when the group of sakhis laugh freely on seeing frightened Krsna wearing her blue cloth and Radha covering her breast with his yellow cloth in the morning.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of the chapter, the poet offers benedictions. May the son of Nanda give bliss to the universe. His face has a wonderful smile. He has glanced at Radha’s face from the corner of his eye, restless with shyness. It is said:

*yad gatagati-visranti-vaicitryena vivartanam \|*  
*tarakayah kalabhijnas tarn kataksam pracaksate \|\|*

***Kataksa*** **is defined by the knower of arts as astonishing, repeated movement of the pupils towards the object, staying a moment and then withdrawing. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **7.50)**

When he glances, the group of *sakhis* were laughing freely and smiling, after seeing in the morning, Krsna who has eternal pastimes with her (*acyutam*). Or, the name Acyuta indicates that he showed his powers by hiding the pastimes, while absorbed in enjoyment with her. He wore her blue garment and she wore on her chest his yellow garment. The *sakhis* laughed because they had exchanged their clothing.

“The son of Nanda” indicates the *dhira-lalita* hero. It is said:

*gahanad anuragatah pitrbhyam*  
*apanlta-vyavahara-krtya-bharah \|*  
*viharan saha radhaya murarir*  
*yamuna-kula-vanany alamcakara \|\|*

**Because of his unfathomable attachment, his parents avoid giving him responsibilities. Playing with Radha incessantly, Krsna ornaments the forests on the bank of the Yamuna River. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **1.29)**

The poetic meter is *sardula-vikriditam.* The title is *nagara-narayana* (clever Krsna, shelter of women) because, though he was present among the other *gopis*, Radha did not see him.

## Chapter Eight. Vilaksa-laksmipatih - Unashamed Krsna

### Text 8.1

*atha katham api yaminim vinlya*  
*smara-sara-jarjaritapi sa prabhate \|*  
*anunaya-vacanarh vadantam agre*  
*pranatam api priyam aha sabhyasuyam \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atha* - After that; *katham* - somehow; *api* - even; *yaminim* - the night; *viniya* - having passed; *smara* - of Cupid; *sara* - by the arrows; *jarjarita* - torn to pieces; *api* - though; *sa* - she (Radha); *prabhate* - in the morning; *anunaya* - of conciliation; *vacanam* - words; *vadantam* - \[who\] spoke; *agre* - in \[her\] presence; *pranatam* - \[and who\] offered respects; *api* - even; *priyam* - to her lover (Krsna); *aha* - she spoke; *sabhyasuyam* - with anger.

**Перевод:**

**After that, somehow Radha having passed the night, though pained by Cupid’s arrows, in the morning she spoke to Krsna, who offered respects to her and spoke conciliatory words. But she spoke with anger.**

**Комментарий:**

The word *atha* indicates auspiciousness or can mean “after that.” She who is endowed with the greatest good fortune (*sah*), Radha, in the morning, spoke in anger to her beloved. What was she doing? She had passed the night with difficulty (*katham api*). By using the word *yaminim*, indirectly its constituent parts (*yamas*) are indicated. This usage suggests that the night was long, because of separation from her beloved, just as describing a young woman as having a large abdomen suggests she is pregnant. She was pierced with Cupid’s arrows, but in a reverse way. She was pained by seeing the marks of other women’s nails on his body, which was intolerable. Though pained because of this, she spoke. Her lover is described as follows. He spoke conciliatory words—humble words to pacify her anger at his offenses. \[He said\] “I will not act like this again” and bowed to her feet.

Or she began chastising him by finding faults in his character, concluding that he had enjoyed with another woman by the signs, though she was pleased with him. *Yamini* means night according to *Amara-kosa.*

Though full of pain, she spoke to him in anger while he offered respects. The *vamata* (uncooperative nature) displayed here \[by Radha\] indicates extra *rasa* since splendid *rasa* arises through it, whereas *rasabhasa* arises by a woman’s compliant mood. Therefore this action is most praiseworthy for Radha. Those who imagine otherwise are devoid of the excellence of *rasa.*

**\|\| Song Seventeen \|\|**

*bhairavi raga, yati tala*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***bhairavi*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: What Radha said \[in verse 8.1\] is presented in a song here. The characteristics of the *raga* are as follows:

*sarovara-sthe sphatikasya mandape*  
*saroruhaih sankaram arcayanti \|*  
*tala-prayoge pratibandha-gita*  
*gauri-tanur narada bhairaviyam \|\|*

**О Narada, Bhairavi has a golden body, constantly sings songs with** ***tala,*** **and worships Siva with lotuses in a crystal pavilion in a lake.**

### Text 8.2

*rajani-janita-guru-jagara-raga-kasayitam alasa-nimesam \|*  
*vahati nayanam anuragam iva sphutam udita-rasabhinivesam \|\|2\|\|*

*hari hari yahi madhava yahi kesava ma vada kaitava-vadam \|*  
*tam anusara sarasiruha-locana ya tava harati visadam \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*rajani* - during the night; *janita* - produced; *guru-jagara* - by the extended wakefulness; *raga* - red; *kasayitam* - reddened; *alasa* - \[are\] lazy; *nimesam* - whose eyelids; *vahati* - bear; *nayanam* - \[your\] eyes; *anuragam* - redness; *iva* - as if; *sphutam* - clearly; *udita* - \[is\] indicated; *rasa* - for *rasa* \[with some woman\]; *abhinivesam* - that by which \[your\] desire.

*hari* - Alas! *hari* - Alas! *yahi* - Go; *madhava* - о Madhava \[Krsna\]! *yahi* - Go; *kesava* - о Kesava \[Krsna\]! *ma* - Do not; *vada* - speak; *kaitava* - false; *vadam* - words, *tam* - The woman; *anusara* - pursue; *saraslruha* - \[are like\] lotuses; *locana* - whose eyes; *ya* - who; *tava* -your; *harati* - takes away; *visadam* - sorrow.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha speaks,\] “Your eyes seem to be clearly red, indicating attraction to srhgara-rasa with some woman and are reddened by staying awake all night, with heavy eyelids.**

**“Ah! Go away Madhava! Go away Kesava! Do not speak false words. О lotus-eyed Krsna! Pursue the woman who takes away your sorrow, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

“Ah what misfortune (*hari hari*)! О Madhava, О Kesava! Go, go from here.” She repeats her words out of anger. Madhava (husband of fickle Laksmi) indicates that he also must be fickle in love like her. Having criticized him, she is unsatisfied and addresses him as Kesava. Kesava means “person with hair.” This is according to the rule *kesad vo ‘nyatarasyam* - (*Panini* 5.2.109 - *kesa* + *va*). His hair has been ruffled since it was pulled by another woman. He says “I am surrendered only to you.” She says, “Do not speak false words.” *Kaitava* means false according to *Visva*.

“I suffer for you. You only flatter me with sweet words. О lotus-eyed one! Follow that woman who takes away your sorrow. It is proper that the woman for whom you suffer can take away your sorrow. Since I am suffering, how can I take away your suffering?”

His eyes are compared to lotuses since they are reddish and half open at the end of night. Or, she criticizes him for closing his eyes in meditation on his lover and having red eyes because of attachment to that woman.

“But I am attached to you. Why do you think I am attached to someone else?”

“O Krsna! Your eye carries attachment to her.”

*Vahasi* is sometimes seen instead of *vahati*. You have eyes that seem attached to her. Those eyes are described as follows, they are slightly red because of staying awake all night. Your eyelids are lazy. This laziness comes from carrying some other object. Clearly they indicate (*udita*) that you have been absorbed in *srngara-rasa* with some other woman. However the words hint that it is proper for your eyes to be red out of affection for me since they are filled with anger (*raga*) while thinking, “Uselessly I have stayed awake in association with another woman.” *Sphutam* can modify *anuragam*. Your eyes are reddish inside and outside, and speak of your attachment to her (*udita-rasabhinivesam*). ‘Hari’ expresses lamentation. *Kasaya* means reddish.

### Text 8.3

*kajjala-malina-vilocana-cumbana-viracita-nilima-rupam \|*  
*dasana-vasanam arunam tava krsna tanoti tanor апиruрат \|\|3\|\|*

*hari hari...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kajjala* - With mascara; *malina* - dark; *vilocana* - eyes; *cumbana* - by kissing; *viracita* - produced; *nilima* - \[is\] black; *rupam* - whose color; *dasana* - of \[your\] teeth; *vasanam* - the garment (your lips); *arunam* - red; *tava* - your; *krsna* - о Krsna; *tanoti* - possesses; *tanor* - of body; *anurupam* - the corresponding \[color\].

**Перевод:**

**“Your red lips have become the color of your body, blackish because of kissing eyes anointed with mascara.**

**“Ah! Go away Madhava...**

**Комментарий:**

\[Krsna says\] “My eyes are red from staying awake to search for you. Why be afflicted with sorrow?”
