*sphuritam ananga-taranga-vasad iva*  
*sucita-hari-parirambham \|*  
*prccha manohara-hara-vimala-jala-*  
*dharam amum kuca-kumbham \|\|6\|\|*

*mugdhe ! madhu-mathanam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sphuritam* - Trembling; *ananga* - of Cupid; *taranga* - of the waves; *vasad* - by the force; *iva* - as if; *sucita* - \[is\] indicated; *hari* - of Krsna; *parirambham* - by which the embrace; *prccha* - inquire; *manohara* -attractive; *hara* - \[and on which there is\] a necklace; *vimala* - white; *jala-dharam* - \[like\] a cloud; *amum* - this \[whether it is proper to go\]; *kuca-kumbham* - of \[your\] pot-like breasts.

**Перевод:**

**“Inquire from your pot-like breasts trembling as if by waves of Cupid, which are decorated with an attractive pearl necklace like a white cloud, indicating the embrace of Krsna.**  
**“O beautiful Radha...**

**Комментарий:**

“Ask your pot-like breasts if it is proper or improper to go. Those breasts are trembling as if controlled by waves of Cupid.” This suggests that Radha has waves of *rasa*. The ordinary pot, influenced by the waves of the river, begins to tremble. “The pots of your breasts tremble from the influence of Cupid’s waves in you, and thus you have waves of rasa.” The word *iva* (like) suggests an analogy. “Your breasts hold an attractive pearl necklace, like a spotless cloud.” Her breasts are compared to pots and the necklace is a cloud. Her trembling breasts indicate the embrace of Krsna who takes away the pain of separation (*hari*). When a woman’s breasts tremble, it is an indication of meeting the lover. This is the conclusion of those who know omens. *Nityam naryah stane kampah priya-sangama-sucakah*: constant trembling of a woman’s breast indicates meeting her lover. Her pot-like breasts indicate auspiciousness, since seeing a full pot on a journey is auspicious.

### Text 11.7

*adhigatam akhila-sakhibhir idam tava*  
*vapur api rati-rana-sajjam \|*  
*candi rasita-rasana-rava-dindimam*  
*abhisara sarasam alajjam \|\|7\|\|*

*mugdhe! madhu-mathanam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*adhigatam* - \[It is\] known \[that\]; *akhila* - all; *sakhibhir* - by the *sakhis*; *idam* - this; *tava* - your; *vapur* - body; *api* - even; *rati* - of love; *rana* - for the battle; *sajjam* - \[is\] prepared; *candi* - О bold warrior (Radha); *rasita* - jingling; *rasana* - bells (or girdle); *rava* - rattle; *dindimam* - with the drum; *abhisara* - approach \[the appointed place\]; *sarasam* - with *rasa*; *alajjam* - \[and\] without fear.

**Перевод:**

**“O bold warrior! All your sakhis know that you are prepared for the battle of love. Go to the appointed place without fear. \[Approach the place\] with srngara-rasa, accompanied by the drum rattle of the bells on your waist.**

**“O beautiful Radha...**

**Комментарий:**

When Radha becomes embarrassed in front of the *sakhi*, the *sakhi* speaks.

It is known by all your *sakhis* that your body is fully prepared, with proper ornaments, for the battle of love. Or, it is known by all your *sakhis* that your body is prepared in various ways for the battle of love. Why be embarrassed at this time? О Radha, playing rough in the battle arena of love (*candi*)! Approach the place appointed for amorous *rasa* in front of Krsna (*abhisara*), accompanied by the drum rattle of your jingling belt with *srngara-rasa* or with musical *raga* (*sarasam*) or with enthusiasm (*sarasam*) and without fear (*alajjam*). Usually drums beat on the battlefield to encourage the fighting.

Another version has *abhirama* (enjoy) \[instead of abhisara\]: Enjoy love making with Krsna without shame. It is said that shame is not suitable for love making. *Durga-simha* in his commentary approves of *abhirama* with a sense of *parasmaipada* (give enjoyment to Krsna) even though the verb is in the *atmanepada* form. Other examples of alternatives are *calad-badham* (trembling pain) instead of *valad-badham* - (GG 1.27). The other words modify the verb *abhirama.* Thus the meaning is as follows: enjoy with *srngara-rasa*, endowed with *hava, bhava, mottayita* and *kuttamita*, with the sound of a jingling belt as the drum. The woman taking the dominant position is suggested.

\[Radha asks\] “Should I be embarrassed by the *sakhis*?”

\[*Sakhi* says\] “The *sakhis* know already. Therefore do not delay in going.”

### Text 11.8

*smara-sara-subhaga-nakhena sakhim*  
*avalambya karena sa-lilam \|*  
*cala valaya-kvanitair avabodhaya*  
*harim api nija-gati-silam \|\|8\|\|*

*mugdhe ! madhu-mathanam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*smara* - Of Cupid; *sara* - \[as\] the arrows; *subhaga* - \[are as\] beautiful; *nakhena* - whose nails; *sakhim* - a female friend; *avalambya* - having taken; *karena* - by \[your\] hand; *sa-lilam* - gracefully; *cala* - go; *valaya* - of \[your\] bracelets; *kvanitair* - by the jingling; *avabodhaya* - \[and\] having informed \[him\]; *harim* - to Krsna; *api* - even; *nija* -your own; *gati* - of arrival; *silam* - of the nature.

**Перевод:**

**“Taking a sakhi by your hand whose beautiful nails are Cupid’s arrows, go to Krsna gracefully. Inform him through the jingle of your bracelets that you have come.**

**“O beautiful Radha...”**

**Комментарий:**

О friend! Go with grace, taking support of a *sakhi* with your hand which has fingernails beautiful as arrows of Cupid. In the normal battle there are weapons. In the battle of love there are nails, which are the weapons of Cupid. Inform Krsna of the nature or the reality (*silam*) of your coming by the sound of your bracelets. Or inform him that you have found a proper candidate (*silam*). The proper warrior fights after finding an equal opponent. Another version has *nigadita-sllam*: inform him of what was discussed.

### Text 11.9

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam adharikrta-haram udasita-vamam \|*  
*hari-vinihita-manasam adhitisthatu kantha-tatim aviramam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanitam* - narrated; *adharlkrta* - \[isj defeated; *haram* - by which a necklace; *udasita* - \[is\] separated; *vamam* - \[and\] from which crookedness; *hari* - upon Krsna; *vinihita* - \[are\] directed; *manasam* - of those whose minds; *adhitisthatu* -may \[this poem\] remain; *kantha-tatim* - on the throats; *aviramam* - always.

**Перевод:**

**May this poem narrated by Jayadeva, which defeats a pearl necklace, but is devoid of its crooked nature, remain on the throats of persons absorbed in Krsna. (END OF SONG TWENTY)**

**Комментарий:**

May this work narrated by Jayadeva always (*aviramam*) remain on the throats of those absorbing their minds in Krsna. This work defeats a necklace by its qualities (*adharikrta-haram*) and also defeats (*udasita*) by its refined presentation, without crookedness (*vamam*). *Aviramam* (uninterruptedly) can also modify *bhanitam* (narrated). This word is constantly narrated by Jayadeva. A necklace or beautiful object around the neck gives splendor to an ordinary person. The work of Jayadeva, which great souls place on their necks, defeats these two things (necklace and the beautiful object) by its sweet taste.

### Text 11.10

*sa mam draksyati vaksyati smara-katham praty-angam alinganaih*  
*pritim yasyati ramsyate sakhi samagatyeti sancintayan \|*  
*sa tvam pasyati vepate pulakayaty anandati svidyati*  
*pratyudgacchati murcchati sthira-tamah-punje nikunje priyah \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa* - “She; *mam* - me; *draksyati* - will see; *vaksyati* - \[she\] will speak; *smara* - of love; *katham* - words; *praty-ahgam* - in every limb; *alinganaih* - by embracing \[me\]; *pritim* - affection; *yasyati*  - \[she\] will show; *ramsyate* - \[and she\] will indulge in love-play  \[with me\]”; *sakhi* - о female friend; *samagatya* - “having come.”  *iti* - Thus; *sancintayan* - thinking; *sa* - he; *tvam* - you; *pasyati* - sees; *vepate* - \[he\] trembles; *pulakayaty* - \[his hair\] bristles; *anandati* - \[he\] experiences joy; *svidyati* - \[he\] perspires; *pratyudgacchati* - \[he\] rises to follow \[you\|; *murcchati* - \[and he\] faints; *sthira* - \[is\]  firm; *tamah* - of darkness; *puhje* - in which the mass; *nikunje* - in the  bower; *priyah* - \[your\] lover \[Krsna\].

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi tells Radha,\] “He thinks, ‘Having come, she will see me, talk words of love, show affection by deeply embracing me with all limbs, and indulge in love play with me.’ Krsna, waiting  in the dark bower, sees you, trembles, perspires with his hairs standing on end, and experiences joy. After love, when you leave  unwillingly, he rises and follows you, but then faints.**

**Комментарий:**

Speaking of Krsna’s great longing, the *sakhi* tells Radha to hurry.

“O friend! Radha will come and see me. Seeing me, she will speak about love with crooked and sweet words, faltering because of agitation of Cupid. Speaking of love, she will show love by embracing me on each limb. She will affectionately indulge in play with me. Thinking in this way, Krsna sees you in the dark bower. He trembles with fear concerning your arrival. His hairs stand on end. Thinking of your embrace, by the happiness of touch, his hairs stand on end. He perspires with the fatigue in love making. After love, seeing you with longing as you leave unwillingly, rising up, he follows. But not seeing you, he faints. Or, your beloved seeing you (out of longing though you are not actually present), he rises from his seat. Blissful on seeing you, he then shows *sattvika-bhavas* like trembling, hairs standing on end, and perspiration. How then can one describe what will happen when you directly arrive? Thus you should go quickly. The embrace with all limbs is called *vrksadhirudha* (embracing like a creeper embraces a tree). The poetic meter is *sardula-vikriditam*. The ornament is *dipaka*.

### Text 11.11

*aksnor niksipad anjanam sravanayos tapincha-gucchavalim*  
*murdhni syama-saroja-dama kucayoh kasturika-patrakam \|*  
*dhurtanam abhisara-satvara-hrdam visvan nikunje sakhi*  
*dhvantam nlla-nicola-caru sudrsam praty-angam alingati \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*aksnor* - For the eyes; *niksipad* - offering; *anjanam* - mascara; *sravanayos* - for the ears; *tapincha* - of *tamala* shoots; *gucchavalim* - clusters; *murdhni* - for the head; *syama* - blue; *saroja* - of lotuses; *dama* - a garland; *kucayoh* - for the breasts; *kasturika* - of musk; *patrakam* - designs; *dhurtanam* - cheating; *abhisara* - on meetings \[with their lovers\]; *satvara* - \[are\] intent; *hrdam* - whose hearts; *visvan* - \[spreading\] everywhere; *nikunje* - in the bower; *sakhi* - о female friend; *dhvantam* - the darkness; *nila* - blue; *nicola* - \[as\] a cape; *caru* - beautiful; *sudrsam* - of women; *praty-angam* - every limb; *alingati* - embraces.

**Перевод:**

**“O friend! The darkness in the bower, spreading everywhere, beautiful as a blue cape, embraces each limb of cheating women whose hearts are intent on meeting their lovers. It offers mascara for the eyes, clusters of tamala shoots for the ears, a garland of blue lotuses for the head and musk designs for the breasts.**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi*, after describing Krsna’s *prema*, describes the *uddipana* (stimulus) of the intense darkness, suitable of meeting, and again tells Radha to hurry.

“O friend! The darkness in the bower of creepers, all around, embraces every limb of the beautiful eyed women who cheat others, whose hearts are eager for meeting and who are most skillful in pastimes. The darkness offers black mascara for their eyes. It offers clusters of buds of the black *tamala* tree to their ears. If offers a garland of blue lotuses to their heads. It offers patterns of black musk to their breasts. It has the beauty of a dark blue cape. All these things produce darkness in clothing and ornaments, suitable for meeting secretly. It is not proper to delay by putting on regular ornaments and dress, (since darkness has already provided these)” The poetic meter is *sardula-vikriditam*. The neuter gender is used in this verse (*dhvantam* is a neuter word), to suggest that eunuchs are qualified to decorate these women. All ornamentation should be performed only by Krsna. Why waste time in decorating yourself? *Dhurta* means a cheater.

### Text 11.12

*kasmira-gaura-vapusam abhisarikanam*  
*abaddha-rekham abhito ruci-manjaribhih \|*  
*etat tamala-dala-nilatamam tamisram*  
*tat-prema-hema-nikasopalatam tanoti \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kasmira* - \[As\] saffron; *gaura* - \[are\] golden; *vapusam* - whose bodies; *abhisarikanam* - of woman going to meet their lovers; *abaddha* -\[are\] produced; rekham - by which streaks; *abhito* - in all directions; *ruci* - of beauty; *manjaribhih* - with lines; *etat* - that; *tamala* - of *tamala;* *dala* - \[as\] a leaf;  *nila* - dark; *tamam* - black; *tamisram* - the darkness; *tat* - of them; *prema* - \[which is\] *prema*; *hema* - of the gold; *nikasopalatam* - the touchstone; *tanoti* - becomes.

**Перевод:**

**“That darkness is black like a tamala leaf, filled in all directions with lines of beauty in the form of women, whose bodies are golden like saffron. Night is the testing stone for the prema of women going to meetings.”**

**Комментарий:**

“This is a test of love for people like you.”

That darkness produces a line made of rows of beauty everywhere of heroines, going for meetings whose bodies are gold like saffron stamens. The darkness is black like a *tamala* leaf. It is the testing stone with a gold line of *prema* for women going to meetings. The purity of *prema* is tested in going for the meeting with the lover, for one must expect extreme darkness on the way.

### Text 11.13

*haravali-tarala-kancana-kanci-dama-*  
*manjira-kankana-mani-dyuti-dipitasya \|*  
*dvare nikunja-nilayasya harim niriksya*  
*vridavatim atha sakhim iyam ity uvaca \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haravali* - On \[her\] pearl necklace; *tarala* - of the central jewel; *kancana* - golden; *hand* - of \[her\] belt; *dama* - of the cord; *manjira* - on \[her\] anklets; *kankana* - \[and\] bracelets; *mani* - of the gems; *dyuti* - \[and\] by the lights*; dipitasya* - lit; *dvare* - at the door; *nikunja* - \[which is\] a bower; *nilayasya* - of the secret place; *harim* - Krsna; *niriksya* - having seen; *vridavatim* - \[who became\] shy; *atha* - then; *sakhim* - \[her\] female friend; *iyam* - to her (Radha); *ity* - thus; *uvaca* - \[the *sakhi*\] spoke.

**Перевод:**

**The sakhi spoke to Radha, who became shy on seeing Krsna at the door of the secret bower, lit by the light of gems on her bracelets and anklets, her golden belt and the center jewel of her necklace.**

**Комментарий:**

The most skillful *sakhi* bringing Radha near the appointed bower with many words filled with *rasa*, points out to shy Radha how to enter the bower. Then the *sakhi* spoke to Radha which is described in the following song. Radha became shy on seeing Krsna at the door of the secret bower (*nilayasya*') since lovers become shy at the first meeting. The bower is described as follows. It was illuminated by the light from gems on bracelets and anklets, a golden belt and from the center jewel of the necklace. *Tarala* means the center jewel of a necklace according to *Visva*. *Nilaya* is a thiefs house according to *Ratna-kosa*.

**\|\| Song Twenty-one \|\|**

*varadi-ragena mantha-talena ca \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***varadi*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***mantha*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *varadi* and the *tala* is *mantha*.

### Text 11.14

*manjutara-kunja-tala-keli-sadane \|*  
*vilasa rati-rabhasa-hasita-vadane \|\|14\|\|*

*pravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*manjutara* - Attractive; *kunja-tala* - in the forest-bower; *keli* - of pastimes; *sadane* - in the abode; *vilasa* - perform pastimes; *rati* - for love; *rabhasa* - out of eagerness; *hasita* - \[is\] smiling; *vadane* - \[O Radha\] whose face.

*pravisa* - Enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence of; *iha* - now.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi speaks to Radha,\] “O Radha with face smiling out of eagerness for love! Perform pastimes in the abode of pastimes within the attractive bower.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower, and approach Krsna. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

О Radha! Enter and approach Krsna. Perform pastimes in the pastime abode within the attractive bower. Radha is described as follows. О Radha, whose face is smiling because of eagerness for love! Or, Radha - the life (*ane*) of Krsna - smiling (*hasitavad*) with eagerness for love! You are the life of Krsna.

Or enter the bower (*iha*). Enjoy with Madhava, О Radha, with a pastimes house within the attractive bower! Or, enjoy pastimes with Krsna (*iha*) who has a pastime abode in the attractive bower and is smiling out of eagerness for love. The last line is the chorus, repeated with each verse. *Madhava* means the husband (*dhava*) of the abode of beauty (*ma*). The name thus suggests that he manifests unlimited beauty when meeting with Radha.

### Text 11.15

*nava-lasad-asoka-dala-sayana-sare \| p*  
*ravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa kuca-kalasa-tarala-hare \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*nava* - Fresh; lasad - shining; *asoka* - of *asoka*; *data* - of leaves; *sayana* - on the bed; *sare* - best; *pravisa* - enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* -о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence; *iha* - now; *vilasa* - \[and\] enjoy; *kuca-kalasa* - on \[your\] pot-breasts; *tarala* - \[is\] moving; *hare* - whose necklace;

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha, with a necklace moving on your pot-like breasts! Enter the bower and enjoy with Madhava on the best bed made of fresh, shining asoka leaves.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower...**

**Комментарий:**

Enjoy with Madhava in the great treasure for young women, the bed made of asoka leaves shining with freshness. Or, enjoy on the best bed. О Radha, with a moving necklace on your pot-like breasts. In a room for pastimes there is usually a great treasure. Describing Radha with a moving necklace indicates that she is in the dominant position in love. Also mention of pots and necklace indicates auspiciousness at the beginning of amorous enjoyment. The first line can also mean “O Radha, the best on the bed of *asoka* leaves made by Krsna!” Or, “O Radha, having the best bed of *asoka* leaves.”

Or, enjoy with Madhava (*iha*), who has the best (*sare*) bed of *asoka* leaves made for you, or who is the best treasure (*sare*) of women on the bed of *asoka* leaves, who (Madhava) has a moving necklace touching your breasts. This indicates he is in the dominant position. The word *asoka* (without lamentation) indicates destruction of the sorrow from separation and spreading of bliss.

### Text 11.16

*kusuma-caya-racita-suci-vasa-gehe \|*  
*pravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa kusuma-sukumara-dehe \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kusuma* - Of flowers; *caya* - with heaps; *racita* - made; *suci* - new; *vasa-gehe* - pastime room; *pravisa* - enter \[the pastime room\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence; *iha* - here; *vilasa* - \[and\] enjoy; *kusuma* - \[as\] flowers; *sukumara* - \[is\] soft; *dehe* - whose body.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha, with body soft as flowers! Enter this place, this new pastime house made of many flowers, and enjoy with Madhava.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower...**

**Комментарий:**

О Radha! Enter and enjoy here, (*iha*) in the pastime room, not enjoyed by others (*suci*), with all types of flowers and decorations. Or, enjoy in the pastime room, the very form of *srngara-rasa* (*suc*i), suitable for enjoyment or meant for your enjoyment, built by Krsna. О Radha with a body softer than flowers!

Or, enjoy with Madhava (*iha*) who made a fresh pastime house out of flowers, and who has a body more tender than flowers. Or, О Radha having a pastime house made by Krsna out of flowers. *Pravisa radhe* can be analyzed as excellent (*pra*) love (*visara*) with complete (*a*) trembling (*dhe*). Thus the phrase means, “O Radha filled with *sattvika-bhavas* like trembling in their most intense form.” In puns, *sa*, *sa* and *sa* can be interchanged. In making the bed, he has love for you. Enjoy with him, having developed *sattvika-bhavas*. *Dha* means trembling and *visara* means love according to *Visva*.

*Kusuma-sukumara-dehe* may be analyzed as follows. *Kusuma* means Krsna who on earth (*ku*) has great (*su*) glory (*ma*). *Sukumara* means the great (*su*) shelter (*ku*) in the form of Cupid (*mara*). Thus the phrase means, “O Radha, who has a form of Cupid, the great shelter of Krsna who is possessing great glory on earth. О Radha, the form desired by Krsna!”

### Text 11.17

*cala-malaya-vana-pavana-surabhi-slte \|*  
*pravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa rati-valita-lalita-gite \|\|17\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*cala* - Fickle; *malaya* - of Malaya; *vana* - from the forest; *pavana* - with the wind; *surabhi* - in \[the pastime house\] fragrant; *site* - \[and\] cool; *pravisa* - enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samlpam* - in the presence; *iha* - now; *vilasa* - \[and\] enjoy; *rati* - during love; *valita* - \[is\] increasing; *lalita* - attractive (or alluring); *gite* - whose song (or amorous gesture).

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha singing attractive songs and enacting alluring actions which increase during love! Enter the bower and enjoy with Madhava in the pastime house, cool and fragrant with the fickle wind from the Malaya forest.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower...**

**Комментарий:**

О Radha! Go (*cala*) to Madhava. Enter the pastime house, cool and fragrant with wind from Malaya forest and enjoy with him. Or, *cala-malaya-vana pavana* means “fickle Malaya forest wind.” *Cala* means that which agitates the mind. That Malaya wind \[which agitates the mind\] is indolent.

О Radha, who sings and enacts various gestures (*lalita*) which increase during love (*rati-valita*). Or, О Radha who sings pleasant (*lalita*) songs and is attractive in love (*rati-valita*). Another version has *rasa-valita*. О Radha who sings attractive songs combined with *srngara-rasa*. Or, Radha who sings and makes alluring gestures with *rasa*. Or, enjoy with Krsna (*iha*) who sings about your qualities in attractive songs endowed with *prema* for you. *Lalita* is defined as follows:

*nana-vidham anaharyam svabhavena manoharam \|*  
*srngara-cestitam strinam lalitam sampracaksate \|\|*

***Lalita*** **is defined as activities of women in** ***srngara-rasa,*** **which are of various sorts, cannot be stopped, and are attractive by their nature.**

### Text 11.18

*vitata-bahu-valli-nava-pallava-ghane \|*  
*pravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa ciram alasa-pina-jaghane \|\|18\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vitata* - Spread \[everywhere\]; *bahu* - many; *valli* - of creepers; *nava* - new; *pallava* - with shoots; *ghane* - in \[the pastime room\] thick; *pravisa* - enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence; *iha* - now; *vilasa* - \[and\] perform pastimes; *ciram* - for a long time; *alasa* - \[are\] slow-moving; *pina* - \[and\] fleshy; *jaghane* - whose hips.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha with fleshy, slow-moving hips! Enter close to Madhava and enjoy for a long time in the room dense with new shoots of many creepers spread everywhere.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower...**

**Комментарий:**

О Radha! Enter and go near Madhava. Enjoy for a long time in the pastime room, dense with new shoots of many creepers spread everywhere. О Radha with fleshy, slow moving hips! It is hinted that the happiness experienced in love on the bed takes place for a long time, without fear.

### Text 11.19

*madhu-mudita-madhupa-kula-kalita-rave \|*  
*pravisa radhe. madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa madana-rabhasa-rasa-bhave \|\|19\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madhu* - Because of flower-nectar; *mudita* - joyous; *madhupa* - of bees; *hula* - by groups; *kalita* - \[is\] made; *rave* - \[in the bower\] in which the buzzing; *pravisa* - enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence; *iha* -now; *vilasa* - enjoy; *madana* - for Cupid; *rabhasa* - vehement; *rasa* - \[is characterized by\] *srngara-rasa;* *bhave* - whose love.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha, endowed with love, filled passion for srngara-rasa of Cupid! Enter the bower filled with the humming of many bees, joyous because of the flower nectar, and enjoy with Madhava.**  
**“O Radha! Enter the bower...**

**Комментарий:**

The first line in the verse, is describing the play room: It is filled with the humming of many bees which are blissful because of flower nectar. Or, the phrase describes Krsna: Krsna made sounds like bees that are blissful because of flower nectar. Or, the phrase describes Radha: О Radha, making sounds like a bee which is blissful because of flower nectar.

Radha is further described in the verse (*madana-rabhasa-rasa-bhave*). О Radha, whose love is characterized by passion for *srngara-rasa* of Cupid. Or, the phrase describes Krsna: Enjoy with Krsna who has love filled with passion for *srngara-rasa* of Cupid.

The phrase describes “Krsna who made sounds like bees blissful because of flower nectar” would indicate however that Krsna had already become blissful on enjoying with Radha, which had not actually happened. However *madhu* can also mean spring (instead of flower nectar): he was blissful, humming like a bee in the spring.

### Text 11.20

*madhuratara-pika-nikara-ninada-mukhare \|*  
*pravisa radhe madhava-samipam iha*  
*vilasa dasana-ruci-rucira-sikhare \|\|20\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*madhuratara* - Sweet; *pika* - of cuckoos; *nikara* - of flocks; *ninada* - with the chirping; *mukhare* - in \[the play room\] resounding; *pravisa* - enter \[the bower\]; *radhe* - о Radha; *madhava* - of Madhava (Krsna); *samipam* - in the presence; *iha* - now; *vilasa* - enjoy; *dasana* - \[as your\] teeth; *ruci* - \[are\] shiny; *rucira* - attractive; *sikhare* -whose rubies.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha, with attractive rubies shining as teeth! Enjoy near Madhava in the play room resounding with the chirping of many sweet cuckoos.**

**“O Radha! Enter the bower...”**

**Комментарий:**

The first line in the verse describes the play room further, as follows. It resounds with the chirping of the sweetest cuckoos. Or, the phrase describes Radha. О Radha, who makes noises during love like the sweetest cuckoos. She is further described in the last line: “O Radha, who has attractive rubies like the shining of your teeth!” This is an example of *viparyasa upama* (inverted comparison). *Dandi* describes this:

*tavananam ivonnidram aravindam abhud iti \|*  
*sa prasiddhi-viparyasad viparyasopamesyate \|\|*

***Viparyasa upama*** **arises from reversing the roles of the object and the object it is compared to. An example is this: the sleeping lotus was like your face.**

The real meaning is that the beauty of her teeth shines more than rubies. Or, the first and last line can mean, “enjoy with Madhava who makes sounds indicating he is not fully satisfied on meeting you, sounds like those of the sweetest cuckoos. He also has teeth more beautiful than rubies.” Another version has *madhu-tarala* (made fickle by the nectar) instead of *madhuratara* (sweetest). *Sikhara* means a ruby according to *Sasvata*.

### Text 11.21

*vihita-padmavati-sukha-samaje \|*  
*kuru murare mangala-satani \|*  
*bhanati jayadeva-kaviraja-raje \|\|21\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vihita* - \[Has\] supplied; *padmavati* - with \[your\] beloved; *sukha* - happy; *samaje* - by whom a meeting; *kuru* - perform; *murare* - о enemy of Mura (Krsna); *mangala* - of blessings; *satani* - hundreds; *bhanati* - \[and who\] describes \[you\], *jayadeva* - Jayadeva; *kavi* - of poets; *raja* - of the king; *raje* - to the king.

**Перевод:**

**О Krsna, give a hundred blessings to the king of poets Jayadeva, who has described your qualities and supplied a happy meeting with your beloved. (END OF SONG TWENTY-ONE)**

**Комментарий:**

О Murari! Give a hundred blessings to the king of poets Jayadeva, who glorifies your qualities (*bhanati*). He has given all the happiness to the Laksmi of Sri-vrndavana (by supplying a meeting with her beloved). Or, give a hundred blessings while the king of poets Jayadeva sings, producing joyous meeting with the beloved. This is a statement of intense love. Another version has *kuru sudhire mangala-satani*: О Radha (*sudhire*), give a hundred blessings to Jayadeva, who has supplied a joyous meeting with your beloved. Using the phrase *kavi-raj* (king of poets) indicates pride (*praudha*) on the part of the poet. It is said *yathocita-nijotkarsa-vadl praudha itiritam*: when a person proclaims fittingly his own excellence it is called *praudha*. The title is proper since he excels all others in describing the confidential pastimes.

### Text 11.22

*tvam cittena ciram vahann ayam atisranto bhrsam tapitah*  
*kandarpena ca patum icchati sudha-sambadha-bimbadharam \|*  
*asyankam tad alankuru ksanam iha bhru-ksepa-laksmi-lava-*  
*krite dasa ivopasevita-padambhoje kutah sambhramah \|\|22\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvam* - You; *cittena* - in \[his\] heart; *ciram* - for a long time; *vahann* - carrying; *ayam* - he (Krsna); *atisranto* - \[is\] exhausted; *bhrsam* - extremely; *tapitah* - pained; *kandarpena* - by Cupid; *ca* - and; patum - to drink; *icchati* - desires; *sudha* - with nectar; *sambadha* - filled; *bimba* - \[which is\] a bimba fruit; *adharam* - \[your\] lips; *asya* - His; *ankam* - lap; *tad* - then; *alankuru* - decorate; *ksanam* - for a moment; *iha* - Now; *bhru* - of \[your\] brow; *ksepa* - by the movement; *laksmi* - of wealth; *lava* - by a speck; *krite* - bought; *dasa* - a servant; *iva* - as if; *upasevita* - \[are\] worshipped; *padambhoje* - \[since he is one\] by whom \[your\] lotus-feet; *kutah* - why; *sambhramah* - \[are you\] afraid?

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues speaking to Radha,\] “Carrying you in his heart for a long time, he has become exhausted. He is extremely pained by Cupid. He desires to drink your bimba lips filled with nectar. Decorate his lap for a moment. Why should you fear him, since he worships your lotus feet, purchased by the movement of your brow, like a servant purchased by a speck of wealth?”**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi* encourages Radha with various descriptions of Krsna. Krsna is hard to attain by other women, but is controlled by you since you have attained the greatest good fortune. Understand that he is very tired from carrying you in his mind. He is tortured intensely by Cupid. He is exhausted from carrying the weight of your heavy breasts and hips in his delicate heart. When a person accepts a very heavy load, he becomes extremely tired. He desires to drink your bimba-like lips filled with nectar. It is natural that a tired and suffering person has a desire to drink. Therefore act as an ornament on his lap for a moment.

“He is attached to many women. I fear this.”

“Why should you have fear of Krsna (*iha*), who serves your lotus feet?” This means he is always at her side. The reason is given. He has been purchased by a particle of wealth in the form of the movement of your brow. One should not have fear of a servant purchased in this way. *Sambhrama* means fear according to *Visva*.

### Text 11.23

*sa sa-sadhvasa-sanandam govinde lola-locana \|*  
*sinjana manju-manjiram pravivesa nivesanam \|\|23\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sa* - She \[Radha\]; *sa-sadhvasa* - with trepidation; *sanandam* - \[and\] with joy; *govinde* - to Govinda \[Krsna\]; *lola* - \[are\] moving; *locana* - whose eyes; *sinjana* - jingling; *manju* - attractive; *manjiram* - \[her\] anklet; *pravivesa* - entered; *nivesanam* - the abode.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi describes,\] “Radha, with eyes moving to Govinda, while jingling her attractive anklets, entered the pastime house with trepidation and joy.”**

**Комментарий:**

Radha entered the pastime house. What did she do? She was making sounds with her attractive anklets (*manju-manjira-slnjana*). Another version has *sinjana-manju-manjiram*: she entered with attractive anklets making a jingling sound. *Pravivesa* indicates that she entered (*vivesa*) while manifesting (*pra*) hava and other gestures to charm him. She entered with fear and with joy. Her eyes moved to Govinda (*govinde lola-locana*). Her jingling anklets indicate she was trembling with *sattvika-bhava* and thus stumbled as she walked. Joy and fear are *vyabhicari-bhavas*. Her moving eye indicates an *anubhava*, a side glance. Or it indicates *capala*, a *vyabhicari-bhava*.

**\|\| Song Twenty-two \|\|**

*varadi-raga-yati-talabhyam giyate \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***varadi*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***yati*****.**

Epigraph to the song: Having described the entrance into the pastime room, now the *sakhi* speaks about the transformation of *rasa* with a festival for the eyes.

The *raga* is *varadi* and the *tala* is *yati*.

### Text 11.24

*radha-vadana-vilokana-vikasita-vividha-vikara-vibhangam \|*  
*jala-nidhim iva vidhu-mandala-darsana-taralita-tunga-tarangam \|\|24\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam ciram abhilasita-vilasam \|*  
*sa dadarsa guru-harsa-vasamvada-vadanam ananga-nikasam \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*radha* - of Radha; *vadana* - of the face; *vilokana* - at the sight; *vikasita* - \[were\] apparent; *vividha* - various; *vikara* - of joyful transformations; *vibhangam* - \[and\] in whom waves; *jala-nidhim* - the ocean; *iva* - as if; *vidhu* - of the moon; *mandala* - of the disk; *darsana* - at the sight; *taralita* - \[are\] agitated; *tunga* - huge; *tarangam* - whose waves;

*harim* - Krsna; *eka* - \[was\] for \[her\] alone; *rasam* - whose attraction; *ciram* - for a long time; *abhilasita* - \[was\] desired; *vilasam* - by whom play; *sa* - she \[Radha\]; *dadarsa* - saw; *guru* - great; *harsa* - by bliss; *vasamvada* - \[was\] controlled; *vadanam* - whose face; *ananga-nikasam* - \[was\] like Cupid;

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi describes,\] “Krsna had waves of various joyful transformations on seeing Radha’s face. He is like the ocean, whose huge waves become agitated on seeing the moon.**

**“Radha saw Krsna, dedicated to her alone, who had a desire to play for a long time whose face was controlled by great bliss and who was like Cupid. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

The arousal of *rasa* when the couple met, has been described in this verse.

Radha saw Krsna who is endowed with the chief *rasa*, *srngara-rasa* (*eka-rasam*) or who had great attraction (*rasa*) for Radha alone (*eka*) or who had *rasa* similar (*eka*) to Radha’s. They had similar natures. Attachment between persons of similar natures produces friendliness. This nourishes *rasa*.

Krsna is described as follows. He had a desire to play for a long time. His face was submissive to great bliss because of attaining his beloved. This indicates extraordinary transformation. He is described as submissive because he could not prevent the joy. He was like Cupid (*ananga-nikasam*). Another version has *ananga-vikasam* (he was a manifestation of Cupid). His face controlled by bliss indicates that he had realized that his desire for pastimes had been achieved.

*akarair ingatair gatya cestaya bhasitena ca \|*  
*netra-vaktra-vikarabhyam jnayate’ntar-gatam manah \|\|*

**The thoughts in the mind are known by changes in the eyes and face, by speaking, actions, gait, suggestions and general appearance. (*****Panca-tantra*** **2.50)**

That is the chorus. Now the verse is explained. Krsna is further described as follows. He was endowed with special waves in the form of various joyful (*vikasita*) transformations such as yawning, stretching the body and wringing the hands because of seeing the face of Radha. He was like the ocean (*jala-nidhim*), whose high (*tunga*) waves became agitated by seeing the disk of the moon. Krsna is compared to the ocean because he is the shelter of the deep *srngara-rasa*. Radha is compared to the moon since her face produces bliss. The transformations like yawning are compared to waves because they appeared with intensity.

### Text 11.25

*haram amalatara-taram urasi dadhatam parirabhya viduram \|*  
*sphutatara-phena-kadamba-karambitam iva уamuna-jala-рuram \|\|25\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*haram* - A necklace; *amalatara* - \[are\] finest; *taram* - whose pearls; *urasi* - on \[his\] chest; *dadhatam* - wearing; *parirabhya* - having embraced \[it\]; *viduram* - intensely; *sphutatara* - gleaming; *phena* - of foam; *kadamba* - with clumps; *karambitam* - mixed; *iva* - like; *yamuna* - of the Yamuna; *jala* - of the water; *puram* - the waves.

**Перевод:**

**“He wears the finest pearl necklace on his chest, and embraced it intensely. He is like the waves of Yamuna mixed with clumps of gleaming foam.**

**“Radha saw Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

The transformations are described in this verse. He wore a necklace on his chest, closely (*viddram*) embracing it. The necklace had the finest pearls. This was like the waves (*puram*) of the Yamuna mixed with clumps of gleaming (*sphutatara*) foam. Krsna’s beautiful black body is compared with the Yamuna. The white necklace is compared to the foam. His embrace of the necklace is an expression of *mottaya* (showing affection for an object in the absence of the lover), inspired by the fulfillment of his desire. This is described in *Rasarnava-sudhakara*. *Svabhilasa-prakatam mottayatam itiritam*: mottayita refers to visibly manifesting one’s desires.

*Taram* refers to a pure pearl according to *Visva*. Another version has *parilambita-duram*: the necklace was very long. By holding it on his chest he embraced it. The necklace represents contact with Radha.

### Text 11.26

*syamala-mrdula-kalevara-mandalam adhigata-gaura-dukulam \|*  
*nila-nalinam iva pita-paraga-patala-bhara-valayita-mulam \|\|26\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*syamala* - \[Is\] blackish; *mrdula -* \[and\] soft; *kalevara* - of whose body; *mandalam* - the circle; *adhigata* - recognized; *gaura* - golden; *dukulam* - \[and\] by whom a garment; *nila* - blue; *nalinam* - a lotus; *iva* - like; *pita* - yellow; *paraga* - of pollen; *patala* - by a mass; *bhara* - profuse; *valayita* - \[is\] covered; *mulam* - whose base.

**Перевод:**

**“He has a blackish, soft body and particularly wears golden cloth. He is like a blue lotus whose base is covered by a profuse mass of yellow pollen.**

**“Radha saw Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Krsna’s body is soft and dark blue. He wears golden cloth, particularly understood (adhigata) to be similar to the complexion of Radha. This indicates that there will be the greatest beauty when he embraces her, like a golden creeper embracing a tamala tree. It is said in Rati-rahasya:

*priyam anukrta-valli-vibhrama vestayanti*  
*drumam iva saralangi manda-sitkara-dirgham \|*  
*vadanam udita-khelakrandam acumbanartham*  
*namayati vinatangi yal lata-vestitam tat \|\|*

**The woman with soft limbs embraces her lover, mistaking herself for a creeper and her lover for a tree, while making soft hissing sounds. She lowers her face, weeping with the manifestation of play, avoiding his kisses. This is called lata-vestitam, embracing like a creeper.**

He is like a blue lotus whose root is surrounded by a profuse mass of yellow pollen. Krsna, with blue complexion, softness, beauty and charm is compared to a blue lotus. His yellow cloth is compared to yellow pollen stuck to its base. This comparison shows *uddipanas* (stimuli) for love. The ornament is *upama* (simile). There is *adbhuta upameya* (comparison of astonishment) since pollen does not cover the base of the flower outside. Or his body appears to have pollen on it because the body itself has such brilliance.

### Text 11.27

*tarala-drg-ancala-valana-manohara-vadana-janita-rati-ragam \|*  
*sphuta-kamalodara-khelita-khanjana-yugam iva saradi tadagam \|\|27\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tarala* - moving \[to and fro\]; *drg-ancala* - of side-glances; *valana* - by the movement; *manohara* - attractive; *vadana* - by \[his\] face; *janita* - \[is\] produced \[in Radha\] ; *rati* - of love; *ragam* - by whom the passion; *sphuta* - blossoming; *kamala* - of a lotus; *udara* - in the interior; *khelita* - playing; *khanjana* - of wagtails; *yugam* - with a pair; *iva* - like; *saradi* - in Autumn; *tadagam* - a lake.

**Перевод:**

**“He produces passion in Radha by his attractive face with moving side glances. He is like a lake in the autumn with a pair of wagtails playing within a blossoming lotus.**

**“Radha saw Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

He incites Radha to desire enjoyment by his beautiful face with fickle side glances. Or he develops desire for enjoyment by the beautiful face of Radha with her side glances. This describes the glance of the heroine.

*apibantiva drstya va sa-vihasatinirmala \|*  
*sa-bhru-ksepa-kataksa ca kanta manmatha-vardhim \|\|*

**When passion increases, the woman glances while moving her brow or smiles purely as if drinking up her love with her glance.**

He is like a lake where there is a pair of wagtail birds playing within a blooming lotus in the autumn season. Krsna is compared to a lake in autumn because he contains *rasa* and because he becomes agitated to the extreme on seeing Radha, and because, with appearance of youth, he develops clear beauty.

His face is compared to a blooming lotus and his eyes are compared to wagtail birds because of their beauty and restless movement. In autumn, frolicking wagtails are seen in the lotuses on the lake. The image of the birds in the autumn lotus suggests that the desire of the beholder will be fulfilled. Seeing the face of Krsna with its glances will give the happiness of love play that Radha desired. It is said in *Varaha-samhita*:

*hema-samipa-sitambara-kamalotpala-pujitopalabdhesu \|*  
*dadhi-patra-dhanya-kute drstobhistani cestate vihagah \|\| iti*

*abjesu gosu gaja-vaji-mahoragesu*  
*rajya-pradah kusaladah suci-sadvalesu \|\|*

**When a bird is seen near a yogurt pot, grain vessel, gold, white cloth, a lotus, a water lily or a worshiped object, one’s desires are fulfilled. A bird seen with a lotus, cow, elephant, horse, large snake, or clean pastures is auspicious and bestows a kingdom.**

*Khanjana* is a wagtail according to *Amara-kosa*. *Tadaga* is water body according to *Visva*. The ornament is *yukta-rupaka* (combined metaphor).

*smita-puspojjvalam lola-netra-bhrngam idam mukham \|*  
*iti puspa-dvirephanam samgatya yukta-rupakam \|\|*

**An example of** ***yukta-rupaka*** **in which flowers and bees are combined is “a face blossoming with a smile with moving eyes like bees.” (*****Kavyadarsa*** **2.77)**

### Text 11.28

*vadana-kamala-parisilana-milita-mihira-sama-kundala-sobham \|*  
*smita-ruci-rucira-samullasitadhara-pallava-krta-rati-lobham \|\|28\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vadana-kamala* - Of his lotus-face; *parisilana* - for the revealing; *milita* - rising; *mihira* - to \[two\] suns; *sama* - \[is\] equal; *kundala* - of whose earrings; *sobham* - the beauty; *smita* - of \[his\] smile; *ruci* - with the beauty; *rucira* - attractive; samullasita - trembling; *adhara* - of \[his\] lips; *pallava* - by the buds; *krta* - \[is\] who produces; *rati* - for enjoyment in Radha; *lobham* - desire.

**Перевод:**

**“He is beautiful with earrings shining like the suns, in order to reveal his lotus face. He produces desire for enjoyment in Radha by his bud-like lips, attractively smiling and eager to kiss Radha.**

**“Radha saw Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Krsna is further described in this verse. He is made beautiful by two earrings rising there like suns, for giving an experience of or revealing his lotus face. The earrings are compared to suns since they are inlaid with many gems. His face is compared to a lotus that blooms on seeing his beloved. *Abhuta upameya* (comparison of astonishment) is described as follows:

*sarva-padma-prabha-sarah samahrta iva kvacit \|*  
*tvad-ananam vibhatiti tam abhutopamam viduh \|\|*

**An example of** ***abhuta upama*** **is as follows. Your face shines as if the excellent beauty of all lotuses had been gathered together. (*****Kavyadarsa*** **2.38)**

He is further described. He produces desire for amorous enjoyment in Radha by the buds of his lips slightly quivering from desire to drink her lips, attractive with the beauty of a smile.

### Text 11.29

*sasi-kirana-cchuritodara-jaladhara-sundara-sakusuma-kesam \|*  
*timirodita-vidhu-mandala-nirmala-malayaja-tilaka-nivesam \|\|29\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sasi* - Of the moon; *kirana* - with the rays; *cchuritodara* - endowed; *jaladhara* - \[is like\] a cloud; *sundara* - beautiful; *sakusuma* - with flowers; *kesam -* whose hair; *timira* - in the darkness; *udita* - shining; *vidhu* - of the moon; *mandala* - \[is like\] the disk; *nirmala* - pure; *malayaja* - sandalwood; *tilaka* - of whose *tilaka; nivesam* - the mark.

**Перевод:**

**“His hair with beautiful flowers is like a cloud endowed with the rays of the moon. The circular sandalwood tilaka on his forehead is like the full moon shining in the darkness.**

**“Radha saw Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

He is further described in this verse. He has hair decorated with flowers, like a cloud endowed with the rays of the moon. His hair is compared to a cloud because it is black. The flowers are compared to moonlight shining through the cloud. He wears spotless *tilaka* of sandalwood, like the moon appearing in the darkness. The circular shaped sandalwood *tilaka* on his dark forehead is compared to the full moon shining in the darkness. But there should not be darkness when the full moon shines. This is an example *adbhuta upama* (comparison of astonishment).

### Text 11.30

*vipula-pulaka-bhara-danturitam rati-keli-kalabhir adhiram \|*  
*mani-gana-kirana-samuha-samujjvala-bhusana-subhaga-sariram \|\|30\|\|*

*harim eka-rasam...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vipula* - Completely; *pulaka* - of bristling hairs; *bhara* - the weight; *danturitam* - filled with; *rati* - of love; *keli* - of the amorous play; *kalabhir* - because of \[Radha’s\] arts; *adhiram* - unsteady; *mani* - of \[their\] gems; *gana* - from the clusters; *kirana* - of rays; *samuha* - with profuse; *samujjvala* - shining; *bhusana* - from the ornaments; *subhaga* - \[is\] beautiful; *sariram* - whose body.

**Перевод:**

**“His hairs stand on end completely all over his body. He is unsteady because of Radha’s arts of amorous play. His body becomes beautiful from the ornaments shining with profuse rays from all their gems.**

**“Radha saw Krsna... ”**

**Комментарий:**

He is further described in this verse. His hairs stand on end all over his body (*danturitam*). He is unsteady because of her skill in amorous play such as kissing and embracing. His body is beautiful (*subhaga*) because of ornaments like crown, bracelets and anklets shining with profuse rays from many jewels. The *madhyabhisarika* heroine is indicated in these descriptions. *Tulya-lajja-smara madhya mohanta-surata-ksama*: the *madhya* has equal amounts of desire and shyness, and permits the lover to enjoy her after some bewilderment. The *srngara-rasa* of Krsna which is a *sthayi-bhava* (permanent emotion) has been described along with *vibhavas* (causes of *rati*), *anubhavas* (voluntary physical responses to *rati*), *sattvika-bhavas* (involuntary physical responses to *rati*) and *vyabhicari-bhavas* (transitory emotions).

### Text 11.31

*sri-jayadeva-bhanita-vibhava-dvigunikrta-bhusana-bharam \|*  
*pranamata hrdi vinidhaya harim suciram sukrtodaya-saram \|\|31\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanita* - of \[the words\] spoken; *vibhava* - by the power; *dvigunikrta* - \[has\] doubled; *bhusana* - of whose ornaments; *bharam* - the weight; *pranamata* - offer respects; *hrdi* - in \[your\] heart; *vinidhaya* - having placed \[him\]; *harim* - to Krsna; *suciram* - for a long time; *sukrtodaya* - of all good acts; *saram* - the essence.

**Перевод:**

**After sheltering him in your hearts for a long time, offer respects to Krsna, the essence of all good acts, whose ornaments have doubled by the power of Jayadeva’s words. (END OF SONG TWENTY-TWO)**

**Комментарий:**

О people! Offer respects to Krsna, after establishing him in your heart for a long time.

“One should worship other *devatas* since Krsna is hard to attain by sacrifices or worship. How can he be attained easily?”

He is the essence of pious acts. He is the highest result. Piety increases by worshipping him. Simply by remembering his name, all benefits of sacrifices are accomplished. No one can do anything for him (since he has everything). In that sense he is hard to attain. Thus one should simply worship him to attain him. Krsna’s ornaments have doubled in quantity by the power of Jayadeva’s words. He is ornamented with golden and jeweled ornaments but this has been doubled by describing his qualities with literary ornaments.

### Text 11.32

*atikramyapangam sravana-patha-paryanta-gamana-*  
*prayasenevaksnos taralatara-taram patitayoh \|*  
*tadanim radhayah priyatama-samaloka-samaye*  
*papata svedambhah-prasara iva harsasru-nikarah \|\|32\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atikramya* - Having surpasssed; *apahgam* - the corner of \[her\] eyes; *sravana* - of \[her\] ears; *patha* - of the path; *paryanta* - to the edge; *gamana* - of going; *prayasena* - out of the effort; *iva* - as if; *aksnos* - from \[her\] eyes; *taralatara* - restless; *taram* - \[her\] pupils; *patitayoh* - fell \[upon Krsna\]; *tadanim* - Then; *radhayah* - from Radha; *priyatama* - \[her\] beloved; *samaloka* - of seeing; *samaye* - at the instant; *papata* - fell; *svedambhah* - of perspiration; *prasara* - a stream; *iva* - like; *harsa* - of joy; *asru* - of tears; *nikarah* - a multitude.

**Перевод:**

**\[The sakhi continues her description.\] “At the instant of seeing her beloved, abundant tears of joy fell to the ground because Radha’s eyes which had fallen \[upon Krsna\] with restless pupils, after trying to surpass the edge of her eyes to reach her ears. Those tears spread out like drops of perspiration from the endeavor.”**

**Комментарий:**

The *sakhi* now describes the transformations in Radha.

\[The *sakhi* says to another *sakhi*\] “Friend! When Radha saw him, at that instant, bliss fell from her eyes in the form of profuse tears (*harsasru-nikarah*).”

“Why did the tears fall, since they were held in a proper container (i.e. the eyes)?”

“When her eyes fell upon his limbs, the pupils became very fickle because of seeing him. She could not remove her eyes from him since she could not satisfy herself fully with his beauty. When her eyes fell there, the contents (the tears) also fell.”

The cause of falling \[of eyes\] is explained by a metaphor. A person, becoming tired after going a long way, perspires and falls down.

Similarly the eyes as if taking efforts to go beyond the edge of the eye, attempting to reach the ear, perspired and fell. The tears were like the drops of perspiration and the eyes fell upon Krsna (compared to the tired person falling down). This description indicates that her eyes were wide. The internal joy, unable to attain a place further inside, appeared externally and fell as tears. This is the ornament called *utpreksa* (simile)

### Text 11.33

*bhajantyas talpantam krta-kapata-kanduti-pihita-*  
*smitam yate gehad bahir avahitali-parijane \|*  
*priyasyam pasyantyah smara-sarasam akuta-subhagam*  
*salajja lajjapi vyagamad iva duram mrga-drsah \|\|33\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhajantyas* - Betaking \[herself\]; *talpa* - the bed; *antam* - near; *krta* - performed; *kapata* - \[as\] a deceit; *kanduti* - with scratching; *pihita* - covered; *smitam* - with smiles; *yate* - \[as it\] went; *gehad* - from the bower; *bahir* - away; *avahita* - attentive; *ali* - of female friends; *parijane* - the group; *priya* - of her lover (Krsna); *asyam* - at the face; *pasyantyah* - \[and\] looking; *smara* - with love; *sarasam* - impassioned; *akuta* - because of \[his\] intentions; *subhagam* - \[and\] attractive; *salajja* - ashamed; *lajjapi* - even the shame; *vyagamad* - disappeared; *iva* - as if \[it were\]; *duram* - far away; *mrga-drsah* - of the doe-eyed woman (Radha).

**Перевод:**

**When her group of attentive sakhis left the bower while covering their smiles by pretending to scratch themselves, when doe-eyed Radha approached the bed and gazed at Krsna’s face - attractive because of his intentions and impassioned with love - her shyness disappeared completely as if it were ashamed.**

**Комментарий:**

At that time, the shyness of doe-eyed Radha, who had taken shelter near the bed, (*talpantam*) completely disappeared or went far away (*duram*) as if ashamed (*salajja iva*). At this time the attentive *sakhis* went outside the pastime bower (*gehat*), covering their smiles (*pihita-smitam*) by pretending to scratch (*krta-kapata-kandtiti*) their ears or other organs. Radha was looking at the face of her beloved, which was attractive (*subhagam*) because of his intentions to play (*akuta*) and which was impassioned with love (*smara-sarasam*). Another version has *smara-paravasakuta-subhagam*: his face was attractive with intentions controlled by love.

### Text 11.34

*sanandam nanda-sunur disatu mitaparam sammadam manda-mandam*  
*radham adhaya bahvor vivaram anu drdham pidayan priti-yogat \|*  
*tungau tasya urojav atanu-varatanor nirgatau ma sma bhutam*  
*prstham nirbhidya tasmad bahir iti valita-grivam alokayan vah \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sanandam* - Joyfully; *nanda* - of Nanda; *sunur* - the son \[Krsna\]; *disatu* - may \[he\] bestow; *mitapararh* - unlimited bliss; *sammadam* - exhilaration, *manda-mandam* - Slowly; *radham* - Radha; *adhaya* - having taken; *bahvor* - of \[his\] arms; *vivaram* - the breach; *anu* - in; *drdham* - \[and\] firmly; *pidayan* - pressing \[her\]; *priti-yogat* - out of affection; *tungau* - large; *tasya* - of she; *urojav* - the breasts; *atanu* - \[is\] not thin (broad); *varatanor* - whose beautiful body; *nirgatau ma* - not; *sma bhutam* - let \[them\]; *prstham* - back; *nirbhidya* - having pierced; *tasmad* - from it (back); *bahir* - be emerged; *iti* - \[fearing\] thus; *valita* - turned; *grivam* - with \[his\] neck; *alokayan* - looking; *vah* - upon you.

**Перевод:**

**May the son of Nanda bestow unlimited exhilaration and bliss upon you! Taking Radha slowly, joyfully and firmly between his arms, he then squeezed her firmly out of affection, but feared her large breasts would emerge from the other side of his broad, beautiful body, piercing through his back. He thus turned his neck to look.**

### Text 11.35

*jaya-sri-vinyastair mahita iva mandara-kusumaih*  
*svayam sindurena dvipa-rana-muda mudrita iva \|*  
*bhujapida-krida-hata-kuvalayapida-karinah*  
*prakirnasrg-bindur jayati bhuja-dando murajitah \|\|34\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*jaya-sri* - By the goddess of victory; *vinyastair* - placed down; *mahita* - worshipped; *iva* - as if; *mandara-kusumaih* - with *parijata* flowers; *svayam* - personally; *sindurena* - with vermillion; *dvipa* - with the elephant; *rana* - in fighting; *muda* - out of joy; *mudrita* - painted; *iva* - \[and\] as if; *bhuja* - of \[his\] arms; *pida* - by the pressing; *krida* - \[and\] by playing; *hata* - \[was\] killed; *kuvalayapida* - Kuvalayapida; *karinah* - by whom the the elephant; *prakirna* - \[were\] splattered; *asrg* - of blood; *bindur* - on which drops; *jayati* - may \[it\] remain with excellence; *bhuja* - of arms; *dando* - the pair; *murajitah* - of the enemy of Mura \[Krsna\],

**Перевод:**

**The arms of Krsna, who killed the elephant Kuvalayapida while playing at embracing him, remain with excellence. Those arms seem to be worshipped by the goddess of victory with parijata flowers and seem to be marked by him personally with vermillion out of joy in fighting with the elephant, since they were spattered with the elephant’s blood.**

**Комментарий:**

The arms of Krsna, enemy of the demon Mura, remain with all excellence (*jayati*). This means they protect the devotees. He killed the elephant Kuvalayaplda by playing and embracing him with his arms (*bhujapida-krida*). His arms are compared poetically. They are as if worshipped with *parijata* flowers offered specially by the goddess of victory. The arms appear to be personally marked with *sindhura* out of bliss in fighting with the elephant. He gains beauty by the red blood of the elephant on his black body. “Why are these things used for comparison?" His arms were splattered (*prakirna*) with spots of blood. The mention of Krsna’s arms indicates fearless playing of Radha with deep embraces of those arms.

### Text 11.36

*saundaryaika-nidher ananga-lalana-lavanya-lila-juso*  
*radhaya hrdi palvale manasija-kridaika-ranga-sthale \|*  
*ramyoroja-saroja-khelana-rasitvad atmanah khyapayan*  
*dhyatur manasa-raja-hamsa-nibhatam devan mukundo mudam \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*saundarya* - Of beauty; *eka* - highest; *nidher* - a treasure; *ananga-lalana* - of Cupid’s wife; lavanya - the attractive; *lilа* - gestures; *juso* - endowed with; *radhaya* - of Radha; *hrdi* - \[which is\] the heart; *palvale* - \[which is like\] a lake; *manasija* - of Cupid; *krida* - for the play; *eka* - singular; *ranga-sthale* - in the arena; *ramya* - atractive; *oroja* - \[which are\] breasts; *saroja* - in the lotuses; *khelana* - for play; *rasitvad* - out of desire; *atmanah* - himself; *khyapayan* - establishes; *dhyatur* - upon the meditator; *manasa* - in lake Manasa; *raja-hamsa* - to a swan; *nibhatam* - \[as\] being similar; *deyan* - may \[he\] bestow; *mukundo* - Mukunda; *mudam* - bliss.

**Перевод:**

**May Mukunda bestow bliss to the meditator, while, out of desire to play in the lotuses of her attractive breasts, he establishes himself as a Manasa swan in the arena of Cupid- in the lakelike heart of Radha, who is endowed with attractive gestures of Cupid’s wife, and who is the highest treasure of beauty.**

## Chapter Twelve. Suprita-pitambarah - Most Joyful Krsna, wearing yellow cloth

### Text 12.1

*gatavati sakhi-vrnde manda-trapa-bhara-nirbhara-*  
*smara-paravasakuta-sphita-smita-snapitadharam \|*  
*sarasa-manasam drstva radham muhur nava-pallava-*  
*prasava-sayane niksiptaksim uvaca harih priyam \|\|1\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*gatavati* - When \[it\] departed; *sakhi* - of female friends; *vrnde* - the group; *manda* - decreasing; *trapa -* of shyness; *bhara* - because of the burden; *nirbhara* - deep; *smara* - upon love; *paravasa* - dependent; *akuta* - by \[her\] intentions; *sphita* - increasing; *smita* - with a smile; *snapita* - \[was\] spread; *adharam* - whose lower lip; *sarasa* - attraction; *manasam* - with a mind; *drstva* - having seen \[her\]; *radham* - Radha; *muhur* - repeatedly; *nava* - new; *pallava* -\[was\] from shoots; *prasava* - whose preparation; *sayane* - to the bed; *niksipta* - \[were\] thrown; *aksim* - whose eyes; *uvaca* - spoke; *harih* - Krsna; *priyam* - \[his\] beloved.

**Перевод:**

**While the group of sakhis departed, Krsna having seen \[her\] spoke to Radha, whose lips were spread with a smile, which increased by her intentions arising from deep love caused by decrease in her great shyness, as she glanced constantly at the bed prepared from new shoots, with attraction in her mind.**

**Комментарий:**

Krsna, his mind joyful to see her, fulfilling his long-held desire, spoke to Radha (next song). He spoke to Radha, as the group of *sakhis* departed. Her lips were spread with a smile which increased by the intention (*akuta*) dependent on deep love caused by decrease in her great shyness. She had attraction (*sarasa*) in her mind. She repeatedly gazed at the bed made of flowers and new shoots. Seeing her in this state, Krsna spoke in great joy. Looking at the bed indicates her desire for amorous enjoyment. The heroine is portrayed as *svadhina-bhartrkta* in this chapter:

*yasya rati-gunakrstah patih parsvam na muncati \|*  
*vicitra-vibhramasakta svadhina-patika yatha \|\|*

**The** ***svadhina-bhartrika*** **indulges in various amorous gestures so that her husband does not leave her side, being attracted by her amorous qualities. (*****Srngara-tilaka*** **1.74)**

**\|\| Song Twenty-three \|\|**

*vibhasa-ragena ekatali-tdlena ca \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***vibhasa*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***ekatali.***

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *vibhasa*, which is described as follows:

*svacchanda-sammanita-puspa-capah*  
*priya-rasa-svadu-sudhabhitrptah \|*  
*paryanka-madhyasya krtopaveso*  
*bhasah sunidranvita-hema-gaurah \|\|*

**He is a well respected, independent Cupid, gold complexioned, soundly sitting in the middle of a couch, satisfied with the tasty nectar of love with his beloved.**

Some say the *raga* is *ramakari*. The *tala* is *ekatali*.

### Text 12.2

*kisalaya-sayana-tale kuru kamini*  
*carana-nalina-vinivesam \|*  
*tava pada-pallava-vairi-parabhavam*  
*idam anubhavatu suvesam \|\|2\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna narayanam anugatam anusara radhike \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kisalaya* - Of shoots; *sayana* - of the bed; *tale* - on the surface; *kuru* - make; *kamini* - о woman (Radha) filled with desire; *carana* - of \[your\] feet; *nalina* - lotus; *vinivesam* - a placing, *tava* - Your; *pada* - of feet; *pallava* - the buds (tender); *vairi* - \[is\] by \[its\] enemy; *parabhavam* - one whose defeat; *idam* - this \[bed\]; *anubhavatu* - may \[it\] become; *suvesam* - decorated; *ksanam* - who is joyful; *adhuna* - now; *nara* - \[is\] water (an ocean of *prema*); *ayanam* - the refuge; *anugatam* - he who experiences; *anusara* - enjoy \[me\]; *radhike* - о Radha.

**Перевод:**

**\[Krsna speaks to Radha\] “O Radha filled with desire! Place your lotus feet on the bed of new shoots. May the decorated bed be defeated by its enemy, your tender feet.**

**“O Radha, now worship me, an ocean of prema, experiencing the greatest joy on seeing you. (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

\[Krsna says\] “O Radha! О beautiful woman! Now enjoy (*anusara*) with me who is joyful with a festival of seeing you (*ksanam anugatam*)." Or, he says “follow me who am dependent on the sweetness of a festival of love (*ksanam*).”

\[Radha says\] “You say this while you are present. Why should I enjoy with you, who are without affection and \[often\] absent?”

\[Krsna says,\] “But I took shelter of water (*narayanam*), since I was burning in the fire of separation from you.” Or, he says “I have become covered in tears.” Or, he says “I am the ocean of *prema*." Or, *nara* means ‘the pleasure of women’ or ‘belonging to a woman’, according to the rule *tasyedam* - (*Panini* 4.3.12). Thus Narayana means ‘he who is the shelter of things related to women.’ \[Krsna says,\] “Thus give up your worries and worship me.” This is the chorus.

\[Now the verse is explained.\] О desirous woman (*kamini*)! This is what you desired! Place your lotus feet on the bed of new shoots. This hints that she will utilize her feet in various actions at the bed. The bed was ornately decorated or was naturally beautiful (*suvesam*). Let the bed, experience the defeat by the enemy - the shoots of your lotus feet, since the bed’s tender shoots cannot compare with your soft feet. Or, let the bed, the enemy of your feet, be defeated as you place your soft feet there.

### Text 12.3

*kara-kamalena karomi caranam*  
*aham agamitasi viduram \|*  
*ksanam upakuru sayanopari mam iva*  
*nupuram anugati-suram \|\|3\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna....*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kara* - With \[my\] hands; *kamalena* - lotus; *karomi* - massage; *caranam* - \[your\] feet; *aham* - I; *agamitasi* - You have come; *viduram* - from afar; *ksanam* - For a moment; *upakuru* - favor; *sayanopari* - on the bed; *mam* - me; *iva* - like; *nupuram* - an anklet; *anugati* - in following \[you\]; *suram* - valiant \[at being dedicated\].

**Перевод:**

**“I will massage your feet with my lotus hands, since you have come from afar. Favor me for a moment on this bed, since 1 am dedicated to following you, like your anklet.**

**“О Radha, now worship Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

I will massage your feet with my lotus hands. Or, I will worship your feet (*carana-maham*) by massaging them.

“Why?”

You have come from very far away. I will relieve you of your fatigue caused by coming from so far away. Favor me for a moment on this bed, since I am valiant (*suram*) at being obedient to you.

I am like an ankle bell. Just as, by wearing your anklet, dedicated valiantly to serving you, it assists in making your feet most precious by being close, so I will assist in fulfilling your desires by offering myself to your feet. It is suggested that one who is steady in following a person, can help that person. Or, when she forbids him to touch her lotus feet he replies: “Then favor (wear) this anklet which is faithful in serving like me.” He prays to touch her feet by speaking about putting on her anklet, though it is already on her ankle.

### Text 12.4

*vadana-sudha-nidhi-galitam amrtam iva*  
*racaya vacanam anukulam \|*  
*viraham ivapanayami payodhara-*  
*rodhakam urasi dukulam \|\|4\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vadana* - Of \[your\] face; *sudha-nidhi* - from the moon; *galitam* - flowing; *amrtam* - nectar; *iva* - like; *racaya* - produce; vacanam -words; *anukulam* — favorable (sweet), *viraham* - Separation; *iva* - as if; *apanayami* - I remove; *payodhara* - \[your\] breasts; *rodhakam* - obstructing; *urasi* - on \[your\] chest; *dukulam* - the cloth.

**Перевод:**

**“Speak sweet words, like nectar flowing from the moon of your face. I will remove the cloth on your chest obstructing your breasts, as if removing separation.**

**“O Radha, now worship Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

When she does not reply, he speaks gravely with unfathomable intention.

О beloved! Speak words that produce love, that produce a wealth of amorous enjoyment. Speak a host of sentences with sweet words and alliteration. Those words, like nectar, would fall from the moon of your face.

I will remove the cloth from your chest on your order as if I am removing separation (*viraham iva*). I will remove obstacles to embracing your chest, just as by meeting you, separation goes away in fear. Or, how can I speak while suffering from separation.

I will remove the separation like a cloth (*dukulam iva*). The cloth is situated on your chest and is an obstacle blocking your breasts. The word *payodhara* (holding milk) is used to indicate production of milk and thus hint at the production of new *rasa* (presumably *srngara-rasa*). It is not to indicate their size since that would be improper.

### Text 12.5

*priya-parirambhana-rabhasa-valitam iva*  
*pulakitam atiduravapam \|*  
*mad-urasi kuca-kalasam vinivesaya*  
*sosaya manasija-tapam \|\|5\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

priya - Of a lover; *parirambhana* - for the embrace; *rabhasa* - with enthusiasm; valitam - endowed; *iva* - as if; *pulakitam* - with bristling hairs; *atiduravapam* - very rare; *mad* - my; *urasi* - to chest; *kuca* - \[your\] breasts; *kalasam* - pot; *vinivesaya* - offer; *sosaya* - Extinguish; *manasija* - born of the heart (desire); *tapam* - the pain.

**Перевод:**

**“Offer your rare pot-like breasts, endowed with enthusiasm to embrace a lover, with hairs standing on end. Destroy the pain of desire in my heart.**

**“O Radha, now worship Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Perturbed, when she looks at him with a crooked glance, he speaks.

О Radha! Offer (*vinivesaya*) the pots of your breasts, full of *rasa*, to my chest. Those breasts are hard to attain (*duravapam*). They cannot be attained except by your mercy. Their hairs are standing on end. A comparison is given to explain the reason. They are as if endowed with enthusiasm for embracing a lover. If a person is joyous, his hairs stand on end. Destroy pain born of desire by offering those pots to me. The pain will be extinguished by sprinkling the pots over my afflicted chest. This indicates a request for her to take the dominant role. The word *manasija* (born of the heart—desire) is used to indicate that, to extinguish a fire in the house, only a person present there (breasts are also situated on the heart) can do it. Therefore, you alone must extinguish the fire. Extinguish the pain, but not the desire directed to you since that desire produces a taste for the sweetness of pastimes. Here his skill in using alliteration is obvious.

The chorus in this verse can be Radha’s answer as follows:

“O Krsna! Go from here (*sara*)."

“Where shall I go? Because of my sickness I take shelter of you.” Radhike means ‘while you show (*ke*) sickness (*adhi*) because of desire (*ra*).'

“You should take shelter of the water (*narayanam anu*).”

“That is far away.”

“It is attained very near (*gatam*). Go to the Yamuna to eliminate your pain arising from desire.”

### Text 12.6

*adhara-sudha-rasam upanaya bhamini*  
*jivaya mrtam iva dasam \|*  
*tvayi vinihita-manasam virahanaia-*  
*dagdha-vapusam avilasam \|\|6\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*adhara* - Of \[your\] lips; *sudha* - of the nectar; *rasam* - the rasa; *upanaya* - distribute; *bhamini* - о woman skilled in the arts of love (Radha), *jlvaya* - Enliven; *mrtam* - dead; *iva* - as if; *dasam* - the servant; *tvayi* - to you; *vinihita* - \[is\] offered; *manasam* - whose mind; *viraha* - of separation; *anala* - in the fire; *dagdha* - \[is\] burnt; *vapusam* - \[and\] whose body; *avilasam* - devoid of pastimes.

**Перевод:**

**“O Radha skillful in the art of love! Distribute the sweet rasa arising from your lips. Bring to life the servant who is almost dead, who has offered his mind to you, whose body has burned in the fire of separation and who is devoid of amorous pastimes.**

**“O Radha, now worship Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

О lover, skilled in the arts of love (*bhamini*)! Distribute the sweet rasa from your lips. Revive the obedient person (*dasam*) or, the object of your charity, who is almost dead. A dead person is revived by feeding him nectar.

Why give to me? My heart is offered to you. My body burns by the fire of separation from you. I am without the activities of play (*avilasam*). A servant unable to do his duties should be revived by his competent master.

### Text 12.7

*sasimukhi mukharaya mani-rasana-gunam*  
*anuguna-kantha-ninadam \|*  
*sruti-yugale pika-ruta-vikale mama*  
*samaya cirad avasadam \|\|7\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sasimukhi* - О moon-faced woman \[Radha\]; mukharaya - cause to make sound; *mani* - \[endowed\] with gems; *rasana* - of \[your\] girdle; *gunam* - the belt; *anuguna* - \[is\] similar; *kantha* - from \[your\] throat; *ninadam* - to which the sound; *sruti* - Of ears; *yugale* - in the pair; *pika* - of the cuckoo; *ruta* - by the cry; *vikale* - tortured; *mama* - my; *samaya* - alleviate; *cirad* - long-standing; *avasadam* - the pain.

**Перевод:**

**“O moon-faced Radha! Make sounds with your gem-studded belt, imitating the sounds from your throat. Alleviate the long-standing pain in my ears, tortured by the cry of the cuckoo.**

**“O Radha, now worship Krsna...**

**Комментарий:**

Since she is not pleased, he speaks sweet words.

О Radha with moon-like face! Her face is compared to the moon because both are beautiful and flow with nectar. Make sounds with your belt endowed with gems. This suggests “Enjoy with me while taking the dominant position called *hamsa-lila*.” This was explained in song 7.9. The sounds coming from your throat are similar to that. Make sounds in your throat like the sounds of your belt jingling. Or, he speaks to her as she makes sounds: “Make sounds with your belt similar to the sounds in your throat.”

Dissipate the present pain (*avasadam*) in my ears, arising from separation from you for a long time. My ears are pained (*vikale*) by the warbling of the cuckoo. The sound of the cuckoo is intolerable for the separated lover.

### Text 12.8

*mam ativiphala-rusa vikali-krtam*  
*avalokitum adhunedam \|*  
*militam lajjitam iva nayanam tava*  
*virama visrja rati-khedam \|\|8\|\|*

*ksanam adhuna...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mam* - Me; *ativiphala* - useless; *rusa* - by \[your\] anger; *vikali-krtam* - punished; *avalokitum* - to look; *adhuna* - now; idam - this; *militarh* - closing; *lajjitam* - ashamed; *iva* - as if; *nayanam* - eyes; *tava* -your; *virama* - give up; *visrja* - Give up; *rati* - to love; *khedam* - the obstacles.

**Перевод:**

**“Give up closing your eyes as if ashamed on looking at me, because I have been punished unjustly by your useless anger. Give up obstacles to love.**

**“O Radha, now worship Krsna...”**

**Комментарий:**

When she does not look at him, he speaks.

О beloved! Your eyes are closed when looking at me, as if they are ashamed. 1 have been punished by you (*vikali-krtam*) though I have not committed offense, because of your most useless anger. You are ashamed of this great crime. Therefore give up (*virama*) closing your eyes because of that useless anger now. Give up afflictions to enjoyment (*rati-khedam*). Giving up anger, enjoy with me. Or enjoy intensely (*vi-rama*) in order to counteract the shame. Showing a variety of play, give up (*visrja*) sorrow in love. Or produce in a special manner (*vi-srja*) passionate exertion (*khedam*) in love.

### Text 12.9

*sri-jayadeva-bhanitam idam anupada-*  
*nigadita-madhuripu-modam \|*  
*janayatu rasika-janesu manorama-*  
*rati-rasa-bhava-vinodam \|\|9\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - By Sri Jayadeva; *bhanitam* - narrated; *idam* - this \[poem\]; *anupada* - in every verse; *nigadita* - \[is\] described; *madhuripu* - of Madhuripu (Krsna); *madam* - in which the bliss; *janayatu* - may \[it\] produce; *rasika* - who \[appreciate\] *rasa*; *janesu* - for those people; *manorama* - attractive; *rati-rasa* - in the *rasa* of love (*srngara-rasa*); *bhava* - of \[their own\] *bhavas*; *vinodam* - the pleasure.

**Перевод:**

**May this poem narrated by Jayadeva, describing Krsna’s bliss in every verse, produce the pleasure of their attractive bhavas in srhgara-rasa, for those who appreciate this rasa.(END OF SONG TWENTY-THREE)**

**Комментарий:**

May this song narrated by Jayadeva give joy (*vinodam*) to people qualified to experience this *rasa*. This song is pleasing to the heart and concerns the *sattvika-bhavas* (involuntary physical responses to *rati*) of *srngara-rasa* (*rati-rasa-bhava*). Or may the work create astonishment because of the attractive *srngara-rasa*. It was said:

*yadi hari-smarane sarasam mano*  
*yadi vilasa-kalasu kutuhalam*  
*madhura-komala-kanta-padavalim*  
*srnu tada jayadeva-sarasvatim*

**If you have devotion to Krsna in** ***madhurya-rasa*****, if you have eagerness for the playful pastimes, then please hear this collection of verses composed by Jayadeva, which are sweet with rasa, easy to understand and pleasing. (GG 1.3)**

This work in every verse constantly describes Krsna’s bliss. His bliss is expressed in the form of playful use of words.

### Text 12.10

*pratyuhah pulakahkurena nividaslese nimesena ca*  
*kridakuta-vilokite’dhara-sudha-pane katha-narmabhih \|*  
*anandadhigamena manmatha-kala-yuddhe’pi yasminn abhud*  
*udbhutah sa tayor babhuva suratarambhah priyambhavukah \|\|10\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*pratyuhah* - An interruption; *pulakankurena* - by \[their\] bristling hairs; *nivida* - tight; *aslese* - in \[their\] embraces; *nimesena* - by the blinking \[of their eyes\]; *ca* - and; *krida* - for play; *akuta* - of \[their\] desire (various pleasures); *vilokite* - in the seeing; *adhara* - of \[their\] lips; *sudha* - of the nectar; *pane* - in the drinking; *katha* - of \[their\] words; *narmabhih* - by the play; *ananda* - of bliss; *adhigamena* - by the acquisition; *manmatha* - of Cupid; *kala* - of the arts; *yuddhe* - in the battle; *api* - even; *yasminn* - in which; *abhud* - there was; *udbhutah* - remarkable; *sa* - that; *tayor* - of them \[Radha and Krsna\]; *babhuva* - arose; *suratarambhah* - the beginning of love-making; *priyambhavukah* - affection.

**Перевод:**

**Though the goose bumps interrupted their tight embraces, the blinking of their eyes interrupted seeing the various pleasures, their playful words interrupted their kisses, and eruptions of bliss interrupted the battle of Cupid, the beginning of lovemaking produced remarkable affection.**

**Комментарий:**

Having described their word play now the poet describes the commencement of amorous acts.

That long desired or extraordinary (*sah*) beginning of love-making of Radha and Madhava (*tayoh*) produced remarkable (*udbhittah*) affection (*priyambhavukah*). Another version has *lalitarambhah* (attractive beginning). This refers to attractive play in *srngara-rasa*. That prelude of love is described as follows. In that commencement, when they tightly embraced, there was an obstacle (*pratyuhah*) created by hairs standing on end. The hairs standing on end like buds (*ankura*), when they embraced, indicates a state of *uddipta-maha-sattvika-bhava* (most intense). The intolerance of even hairs standing on end when they embrace, is produced by *atirudha-bhava*. This indicates the highest level of *rasa.*

When they gazed at their play with desire, this was interrupted by blinking of the eyes. They could not tolerate blinking when looking at each other with desire and longing.

*kutila-kuntalam sri-mukham ca te*  
*jada udiksatam paksma-krd drsam*

**\[The** ***gopis*** **said,\] “Even when we can eagerly look upon your beautiful face, so lovely with its adornment of curly locks, we think the creator was foolish in making eyelids.” (SB 10.31.15)**

When they tasted each other’s lips, their intimate joking talks became an obstacle. Though they drank each other’s lips, they could not be fully satisfied. Though intoxicated by the nectar, they could not suppress their conversations. Each of the senses and their particular experiences became very strong like waves in the ocean. In the battle using the arts of love making, there is an obstacle created by the manifestation of joy. This describes the intense attractiveness of the couple at that time. They experience that the love-making is insufficient. This indicates the unlimited nature of the actions and bliss. This is indicated by implication rather than direct statement. By indirect expression, the great poet has described, how the *sambhoga* (union) aspect of *srngara-rasa* attains sweetness by the *sthayi-bhava, vibhavas, anubhavas, sattvika-bhavas* and *vyabhicari-bhavas* mixing together.

### Text 12.11

*dorbhyam samyamitah payodhara-bharenaplditah panijair*  
*aviddho dasanaih ksatadhara-putah sroni-tatenahatah \|*  
*hastenanamitah kace’dhara-madhu-syandena sammohitah*  
*kantah kam api trptim apa tad aho kamasya vama gatih \|\|11\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**
