*dorbhyam* - By \[her\] arms; *samyamitah* - \[was\] bound; *payodhara* - of \[her\] breasts; *bharena* - by the weight; *apiditah* - \[was\] pressed; *panijair* - by \[her\] nails; *aviddho* - \[was\] pierced; *dasanaih* - by \[her\] teeth; *ksata* - \[was\] wounded; *adhara* - of \[his\] lip; *putah* - the fold; *sroni* - of \[her\] hips; *tatena* - by the slope; *ahatah* - \[was\] beaten; *hastena* - by \[her\] hands; *anamitah* - \[he was\] grasped; *kace* - in \[his\] hair; *adhara* - of her lower-lip; *madhu-syandena* - by the sweet drops (nectar); *sammohitah* - \[and was\] bewitched; *kantah* - the lover (Krsna); *kam* - what; *api* - but; *trptim* - satisfaction; *apa* - he obtained! *tad* - Therefore; *aho* - how astonishing; *kamasya* - of the god of love; *vama* - contrary; *gatih* - \[is\] the course!

**Перевод:**

**He was bound by her arms, squeezed by the weight of her breasts, pierced by her nails, wounded on the lip by her teeth, beaten by her hips, had his hair grasped by her hands, and was bewitched by drinking the nectar of her lips. But, he attained indescribable satisfaction. Thus, how astonishing is the contrary course of Cupid!**

**Комментарий:**

The reversal of positions is described with colorful words. He was bound by her arms. He was squeezed by the weight of her breasts. He was scratched by her nails. His lip was bitten by her teeth. He was struck by her hips. He was held by his hair with her hands. He was completely bewildered by drinking the nectar of her lips. In this manner he attained indescribable satisfaction. For this reason (*tatah*) how astonishing (*aho*) is the contrary or attractive (*vama*) course of Cupid. Though he was bound up etc. he attains pleasure. In this way it was contrary. This is a description of the reversed position in love. The ornament is *arthantara-nyasa* (corroboration). This is described by *Dandi*:

*jneyah so’rthantara-nyaso vastu nirdisya kincana \|*  
*tat-sadhana-samarthasya vacanam yatra drsyate \|\|*

***Arthantara-nyasa*** **is understood where a relevant principle is corroborated by indicating a particular object.**

*Vtra-srngara* (bold love making) is described by her *vira-anubhavas* such as binding and squeezing. He requested that he be punished for his offenses (GG 10.3 and 10.11). She does that here.

### Text 12.12

*maranke rati-keli-sankula-randrambhe taya sahasa-*  
*prayam kanta-jayaya kimcid upari prarambhi yat sambhramat \|*  
*nispandajaghana-sthali sithilita dor-vallir utkampitam*  
*vakso militam aksi paurusa-rasah strinam kutah sidhyati \|\|12\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*maranke* - Displaying marks of passion; *rati-keli* - with amorous play; sankula - \[which is\] thick; *rana* - of the battle; *arambhe* - in the beginning; *taya* - by her \[Radha\]; *sahasa* - of boldness; *prayam* - full; *kanta* - of \[her\] lover \[Krsna\]; *jayaya* - for the defeat; *kimcid* - somehow; *upari* - taking the dominant role; *prarambhi -* \[it was\] begun; *yat* - since; *sambhramat* - \[she acted\] out of zeal, *nispanda* - \[Became\] motionless; *jaghana* - of \[her\] thighs; *sthali* - \[but\] the tract; *sithilita* - slackened; *dor* - of her arms; *vallir* - the creeper; *utkampitam* - quivered; *vakso* - \[her\] chest; *militam* - closed; *aksi* - \[and her\] eyes, *paurusa-rasah* - Masculinity (masculine role); *strinam* - by women; *kutah* - how; *sidhyati* - is accomplished?

**Перевод:**

**Radha, boldly desiring to defeat Krsna in the passionate battle thick with amorous play, began to fight with zeal. However, after only a brief struggle, her hips became motionless, the creeper of her arms slackened, her chest quivered and her eyes closed. How can women succeed in taking the masculine role?**

**Комментарий:**

At the beginning of the battle of Cupid, which is permeated with love play such as kissing, embraces, and various positions, in which marks of Cupid or death (*maranke*) were inflicted, Radha, with great boldness, took the dominant role in an indescribable manner (*kirhcit*) in order to conquer her lover, out of great passion (*sambhramat*). But her legs became motionless (*nispanda*). The creepers of her arms became limp. Her heart shook and her eyes closed. If a woman takes the man’s role in love with a desire to defeat him, how can that succeed? It cannot. This is the meaning. This verse describes the stage called *kriya-nivrttih* (cessation of action). This happens to the woman after love-making. Actually she was victorious.

### Text 12.13

*tasyah patala-panijankitam uro nidra-kasaye drsau*  
*nirdhauto’dhara-sonima vilulitah srasta-srajo murdha-jah \|*  
*kanci-dama dara-slathancalam iti pratar nikhatair drsor*  
*ebhih kama-sarais tad adbhutam abhut patyurmanah kilitam \|\|13\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tasyah* - Her \[Radha’s\]; *patala* - red; *panija* - with fingernails; ahkitam - \[was\] marked; *uro* - chest; *nidra* - with sleep; *kasaye* - \[were\] red; *drsau* - \[her\] eyes; *nirdhauto* - \[was\] washed away; *adhara* - of \[her\] lips; *sonima* - the redness; *vilulitah* - \[was\] disheveled; *srasta* - \[was\] lost; *srajo* - whose garland; *murdha-jah* - \[her\] hair; *kanci-dama* -\[and\] the string of \[her\] girdle (belt); *dara* - slightly; *slatha* - \[were\] loosened; *ancalam* - \[and\] the fringe of her garment; *iti* - Thus; *pratar* - in the morning; *nikhatair* - \[which had been\] strung; *drsor* - on \[Krsna’s bow-like\] eyes; *ebhih* - these; *kama* - of Cupid; *sarais* - with arrows; *tad* - this; *adbhutam* - \[is\] astonishing; *abhilt* - was; *patyur* - of the husband (Krsna); *manah* - mind; *kilitam* - pierced.

**Перевод:**

**Because of Cupid’s arrows strung on Krsna’s bow-like eyes in the morning, Radha’s chest bore marks of red nails, her eyes were red with sleep, the redness of her lips had been washed away, her disheveled hair lost its garland and her belt and then the fringe of her cloth became slightly loose. These same arrows now pierced his mind. Is this not astonishing?**

**Комментарий:**

It was most astonishing that Krsna’s mind was pierced by Cupid’s arrows which had in the morning been strung on his eyes. Also, in normal circumstances a different person is pierced by the arrows that one places on one’s bow (but here they pierced the wielder). By these arrows Radha’s chest was marked by red nails and her eyes were red because of sleep. The red of her lips was washed away by kisses. Her disheveled hair decorated with flower garlands lost its flowers when he kissed her and touched her breasts. The edge of her cloth became slightly (*dara*) loose because her belt became loose. Or the belt cord became slightly loose at the edge of her cloth. Intense rasa is indicated by the clear manifestations of marks produced by association. These marks are produced out of absorption in *rasa*. Since Krsna was pierced, this indicates that Radha was victorious, since she controlled him.

### Text 12.14

*vyakosah kesa-pasas taralit am alakaih sveda-lolau kapolau*  
*dasta bimbadhara-srih kuca-kalasa-ruca harita hara-yastih \|*  
*kana kancid gatasam stana-jaghana-padam paninacchadya sadyah*  
*pasyantl satrapam mam tad api vilulita-srag-dhareyam dhinoti \|\|14\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*vyakosah* - \[Is\] undone; *kesa-pasas* - \[her\] ornamented hair; *taralitam* - Trembling \[is done\]; *alakaih* - by \[her\] locks; *sveda* -With sweat; *lolau* - \[are\] restless; *kapolau* - \[her\] cheeks; *dasta* - \[Is\] injured; *bimbadhara* - of \[her\] *bimba* lips; *srih* - the beauty; *kuca-kalasa* - Of \[her\] pot-breasts; *ruca* - along with the effulgence; *harita* - \[is\] rejected; *hara-yastih* - \[her\] pearl necklace; *kanci* - \[Her\] belt; *kancid* - somehow; *gata* - has given up; *asam* - hope; *stana* - Of \[her\] breasts; *jaghana* - \[and her\] hips; *padam* - the place; *panina* - with \[her\] hands; *acchadya* - having covered; *sadyah* - suddenly; *pasyanti* - looking; *satrapam* - shyly; *mam* - at me; *tad* - then; *api* -even; *vilulita* - broken; *srag* - a garland; *dhareyam* - wearing; *dhinoti* - \[she\] pleases \[me\].

**Перевод:**

**\[Krsna’s contemplates,\] “Her ornamented hair is undone, and her locks tremble. Perspiration covers her restless cheeks. Her beautiful lips are injured. Her pearl necklace has been rejected along with the effulgence of her pot-like breasts. Her belt has somehow given up hope. With her garland broken, as she looks shyly at me while covering her breasts with her hands, she suddenly pleases me.”**

**Комментарий:**

This verse explains how Krsna’s mind was pierced.

“This Radha with garland broken, pleases me (*dhinoti*).”

“What does she do?”

“She looks at me with shyness, covering her breasts with her hand at that time.”

“Why is she shy?”

Her shyness arose because her hair had come undone. There was trembling of her locks of hair. Her cheeks are covered with perspiration. The beauty of her lips has been diminished. Her pearl necklace has been discarded somewhere along with the luster of her pot-like breasts. Or the beauty of her necklace has been stolen by the beauty of her body. The bells on her belt have given up hope indescribably (*kancit*). This indicates her absorption in enjoyment. But though she was without ornaments, the mind of her husband was attracted by the beauty of her limbs. The sweetness of *rupa* is indicated in this verse.

*angany abhusitany eva kenacid bhusanadina \|*  
*yena bhusitavad bhati tad ruрат iti kathyate \|\|*

**When a girl’s unornamented limbs appear as if decorated with ornaments, such beauty is called** ***rupam*****. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **10.25)**

This is an example:

*nitam te punar uktatam bhramarakaih kasturika-patrakam*  
*netrabhyam viphalikrtam kuvalaya-dvandvam ca karnapitam \|*  
*haras ca smita-kanti-bhangibhir alam pistanupesikrtah*  
*him radhe tava mandanena nitaram angair asi dyotita \|\|*

**\[Krsna spoke,\] “Your curling locks of hair make these** ***tilaka*** **decorations drawn in musk useless. Your eyes are so beautiful that they make the two lotus flowers on your ears useless. The waves of the beauty of your smile have crushed your necklace into dust. О Radha! What is the use of you wearing all these decorations? You are already made splendid by the beauty of your limbs.” (Vidagdha-madhava 7.47)**

Sobha is defined as follows:

*sa sobha rupa-bhogadyair yat syad anga-vibhusanam \|\|*

**The state, when the body is decorated with beauty and amorous enjoyment, is called** ***sobha*****. (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.13)**

An example is given:

*dhrtva raktanguli-kisalayair nipa-sakham visakha*  
*niskramanti vratati-bhavanat pratar udghurnitaksi \|*  
*venim amsopari viluthatim ardha-muktam vahanti*  
*lagna svante mama na hi bahih seyam adyapy ayasit \|\|*

**\[Krsna spoke,\] “Grasping a** ***kadamba*** **branch with the budding reddish twigs of her fingers, Visakha left the cottage of creepers at daybreak. Her eyes were rolling and her braid fell on her shoulder, half undone. Visakha now sticks in my heart and will never leave it.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **11.14)**

The meter is *srag-dhara*, as mentioned in the verse.

### Text 12.15

*milad-drsti milat-kapola-pulakam sitkara dhara-vasad*  
*avyaktakula-keli-kaku-vikasad-dantamsu-dhautadharam \|*  
*syamottunga-payodharopari parisvangi kurangi-drso*  
*harsotkarsa-vimukta-nihsaha-tanor dhanyo dhayaty ananam \|\|15\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*milad* - \[Were\] closing; *drsti* - whose eyes; *milat* - \[was\] developing; *kapola* - on whose cheeks; *pulakam* - the bristling; *sitkara* - of the streams of sighs (prolonged or profuse sighs); *vasad* - by the power; *avyakta* - indistinct; *akula* - \[and\] confused; *keli* - of amorous play; *bahu* - because of the moaning; *vikasad* - opened; *danta* - from \[her\] teeth; *amsu* - by the rays; *dhauta* - illuminated; *adharam* - with lips; *syama* - dark; *uttunga* - \[and\] swollen \[with *rasa*\]; *payodharopari* -on \[her\] breasts; *parisvangi* - embracing \[her\]; *kurangi-drso* - of the doe-eyed woman (Radha); *harsotkarsa* - because of intense bliss; *vimukta* - \[was\] limp; *nihsaha* - \[and\] unable to move; *tanor* - whose body; *dhanyo* - the praiseworthy one (Krsna); *dhayaty* - drinks; *ananam* - the face.

**Перевод:**

**Praiseworthy Krsna, embracing her swollen breasts, drank the face of Radha, whose body was limp, who was unable to move because of intense bliss, as she closed her eyes, developed goose bumps on her cheeks, and illuminated her lips by the rays emanating from her teeth, which were revealed as she indistinctly moaned in confusion because of releasing profuse sighs.**

**Комментарий:**

Praiseworthy (*dhanya*) Krsna drinks without interruption the face of doe-eyed Radha whose body is limp and unable to move (*nihsaha*) because of intense bliss from various amorous experiences. Or, he kisses her face.

He embraced her breasts swollen with *rasa*. This suggests his desire to touch. Her face is described when he looked at her. She closed her eyes and goose bumps appeared on her cheeks. Her lips became washed by the rays from her teeth. The teeth were revealed because she indistinctly moaned in confusion because of her prolonged sighing.

Or the phrases can simply modify her face. On her face her eyes were closed and there were goose bumps on her cheeks. Her lips were washed by rays from her teeth which were revealed when she indistinctly moaned in confusion because of her prolonged sighing.

### Text 12.16

*atha sahasa supritam suratante sa nitanta-khinnangi \|*  
*radhajagada sadaram idam anandena govindam \|\|16\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*atha* - Then; *sahasa* — suddenly; *supritam* - \[who was\] very pleased; *surata* - of lovemaking; *ante* - at the end; *sa* - she; *nitanta* - excessively; *khinna* - \[were\] exhausted; *angi -* whose limbs; *radha* - Radha; *jagada* - spoke; *sadaram* - \[and\] respectful; *idam* - this; *anandena* - with bliss; *govindam* - to Govinda (Krsna).

**Перевод:**

**Then Radha, her body excessively exhausted after love, suddenly spoke blissfully to Govinda, who was most respectful and pleased.**

**Комментарий:**

Atha indicates auspiciousness. Or it means “after that.” Radha, who is famous for controlling her lover (*sa*), spoke in bliss to Govinda as follows (song twenty-four). She is described as follows in this verse. Her body was very tired after love-making. He is described as follows. He remained there in a state of respect (*sadaram*) thinking, “She will order me. Then I will be most fortunate.” His face was charming (*supritam*) in order to attain her.

**\|\| Song Twenty-four \|\|**

*ramakari-raga-rupaka-talabhyam \|*

**The song is sung in the** ***raga*** **of** ***ramakari*** **and in the** ***tala*** **of** ***rupaka*****.**

Epigraph to the song: The *raga* is *ramakari* and the *tala* is *rupaka*.

### Text 12.17

*kuru yadu-nandana candana-sisira-*  
*tarena karena payodhare \|*  
*mrga-mada-patrakam atra mano-bhava-*  
*mangala-kalasa-sahodare \|\|17\|\|*

*nijagada sa yadu-nandane kridati hrdayanandane \|\| dhru. \|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*kuru* - Make; *yadu* - of the cowherds; *nandana* - о delightful \[moon; Krsna\]; *candana* - than sandalwood; *sisiratarena* - colder; *karena* - with \[your\] hand; *payodhare* - on \[my\] breast; *mrga-mada* - of musk; *patrakam* - a pattern; *atra* - here; *mano-bhava* - Cupid; *mangala* - for welcoming; *kalasa* - pots; *sahodare* - resembling,

*nijagada* - Spoke; *sa* - she \[Radha\]; *yadu -* of the cowherds; *nandane* - to the delightful \[moon; Krsna\]; *kridati* - \[who\] was enjoying; *hrdaya* - in \[his\] heart; *anandane* - \[and who had\] bliss.

**Перевод:**

**\[Radha speaks to Krsna,\] “O moon of the cowherds! Please draw musk patterns on my breast, which are like pots for welcoming Cupid, with your hand cooler than sandalwood.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds, who was enjoying (kridati and had developed bliss in his heart, (chorus)**

**Комментарий:**

Radha spoke to Krsna (*yadu-nandana*), who was enjoying and giving bliss to people’s hearts. This is the chorus.

What did she say? О joyful moon of the cowherds (*yadu-nandana*)! Make musk patterns on my breast with your hand. Your hand is cooler than sandalwood. Or, *yadu-nandana* can be taken as part of a compound instead of a vocative, since the refrain already has the vocative *yadu-nandana*: Make patters of musk on my breast with your hand cooler than sandalwood and cooler than the moon.

She skillfully uses the words “hand cooler than sandalwood” to indicate that her desire will again spring up by his touch; his touch is burning with desire. Her breasts are twins of full pots established for welcoming Cupid. Some explain the word *mangala* to mean gold. This is according to *Visva-kosa*.

### Text 12.18

*ali-kula-ganjana-sanjanakam*  
*rati-nayaka-sayaka-mocane \|*  
*tvad-adhara-cumbana-lambita-kajjalam*  
*ujjvalaya priya locane \|\|18\|\|*

*nijagada sa yadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

ali-kula - Of a swarm of bees; *ganjana* - the derision; *sanjanakam* - producing; *rati-nayaka* - of Cupid; *sayaka* - the arrows; *mocane* - releasing; *tvad* - your; *adhara* - of lips; *cumbana* - because of the kisses; *lambita* - \[which has\] fallen (smeared); *kajjalam* - mascara; *ujjvalaya* - cause to shine; *priya* - о beloved; *locane* - on \[my\] eyes.

**Перевод:**

**“O beloved! Make my eyes, which release Cupid’s arrows, bright with mascara which derides a swarm of bees, since it has been smeared by your lips’ kissing.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О beloved! Make my eyes bright with mascara which has perished, by your lips’ kissing. Put mascara on my eyes again. Her eyes are described as follows. Those eyes release Cupid’s arrows. That mascara derides (*ganjana-sanjanakam*) a swarm of bees. *Ujjvala* means bright. *Ganjana* means defeat according to *Ratna-kosa*.

### Text 12.19

*nayana-kuranga-taranga-vikasa-*  
*nirasa-kara-sruti-mandale \|*  
*manasija-pasa-vilasa-dhare*  
*subha-vesa nivesaya kundale \|\|19\|\|*

*nijagada sayadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

nayana-kuranga - Of \[my\] doe-eyes; *taranga* - of the force; *vikasa* - to the increase; *nirdsa* - an impediment; kara - creates; *sruti* -of \[my\] ears; *mandale* - by which the circle; *manasija* - of Cupid; *pasa* - \[as\] nooses; *vilasa* - charmingly; *dhare* - acting; *subha* - \[is\] beautiful; *vesa* - о he (Krsna) whose clothing; *nivesaya* - place \[on my ears\]; *kundale* - \[two\] earrings.

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna dressed so elegantly! Please place these earrings on my ears which impede the increasing force of my doe-like eyes, and which charmingly act as nooses of Cupid to restrict the eyes.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О Krsna dressed so elegantly or О form of auspiciousness! Place my earrings on my ears. These ears restrict the increase of the speed of my doe-like eyes and also possess the charm of acting as a noose of Cupid. If the noose of the hunter Cupid does not reside in the ear, then the doe-like eyes will leap far away. This shows the playful nature of her eyes. Expansion of the doe’s speed indicates the fickleness of her side glance.

### Text 12.20

*bhramara-cayam racayantam upari*  
*ruciram suciram mama sammukhe \|*  
*jita-kamale vimale parikarmaya*  
*narma-janakam alakam mukhe \|\|20\|\|*

*nijagada sayadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*bhramara* - Of bees; *cay am* - a swarm; *racayantam* - producing \[the color of\]; *upari* - above; *ruciram* - attractive; *suciram* - eternally; *mama* - my; *sammukhe* - bright; *jita* - \[is\] surpassed; *kamale* - by which the lotus; *vimale* - \[and\] pure; *parikarmaya* - decorate; *narma* - of laughter; *janakam* - the producer; *alakam* - locks; *mukhe* - \[my\] face.

**Перевод:**

**“Decorate my locks, producers of laughter, which defeat all beauty and are black like a swarm of bees, hovering eternally above my pure bright face, which surpasses a lotus.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О Krsna! Decorate the attractive (*ruciram*) locks on my pure face (*vimale mukhe*). Those locks produce play. My face defeats a lotus (*jita-kamale*) by its beauty and fragrance, and is bright (*sammukhe*) or is in front of you. My locks produce the beauty of a swarm of bees hovering above my face, for a long time. *Sammukhe* (bright) indicates the face’s incomparable beauty. “For a long time (*suciram*)" indicates that the locks give unfailing beauty eternally.

Ruciram (*attractively*) and *suciram* (for a long time) can also modify the verb *parikarmaya* (decorate). Decorate the hair attractively (*ruciram*) for a long time. *Jita-kamale* can also mean ‘face which has subdued’ or ‘is superior to (*jita*) that of Laksmi (*kamale*) by its extraordinary beauty’. *Vimale* can mean “famous.” “Her locks cause laughter” (*narma-janakam*) means that her locks deride everything by their beauty and decoration. Or *narma-jana-kam* can mean that the locks produce happiness (*kam*) in the joking *sakhis* (*narma-jana*). They joke on seeing her disheveled locks. “For a long time” (*suciram*) also indicates that the bees of her locks can never be satisfied in drinking the nectar of her face.

### Text 12.21

*mrgamada-rasa-valitam lalitam*  
*kuru tilakam alika-rajam-kare \|*  
*vihita-kalanka-kalam kamalanana*  
*visramita-srama-sikare \|\|21\|\|*

*nijagada sayadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mrgamada-rasa* - Of liquid musk; *valitam* - made; *lalitam* - attractive; *kuru* - make (decorate); tilakam - a *tilaka*; alika - of \[my\] forehead; *rajani-kare* - on the moon; *vihita* - \[is\] supplied with; *kalanka* - of a mark; *kalam* - beauty; *kamala* - \[is\] a lotus; *anana* - о he \[Krsna\] whose face; *visramita* - arising from efforts; *srama* - of perspiration; *sikare* - whose drops.

**Перевод:**

**“O lotus-faced Krsna! Decorate my forehead, covered with drops of perspiration arising from efforts now ceased, with attractive tilaka of liquid musk, endowed with the beauty of a blemish.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О lotus-faced Krsna! Or, Krsna, having Laksmi in his face (*kamalanana*)! In either case his friendship with the moon is hinted. Make attractive *tilaka* on the moon of my forehead (*alika-rajani-kare)*. The *tilaka* is made (*valitam*) of liquid musk and has a beauty mark in the form of black circle (*kalanka*). The mark can be taken as beautiful because of similarity to his black form.

My forehead has drops of perspiration caused by previous efforts (efforts in love). The beauty of the drops of perspiration arising from her body surpasses that of snowflakes, which are cold. And the drops destroy the moon-like nature of her forehead completely \[therefore Krsna should decorate her forehead with *tilaka* to avoid this destruction). *Alika* means forehead. Or, the forehead produced perspiration arising from efforts, but has now stopped doing so. First wipe away the perspiration, and then apply *tilaka*, since it is impossible to apply *tilaka* to the perspiring face.

### Text 12.22

*mama rucire cikure kuru manada*  
*manasija-dhvaja-camare \|*  
*rati-galite lalite kusumani*  
*sikhandi-sikhandaka-damare \|\|22\|\|*

*nijagada sayadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*mama* - My; *rucire* - attractive; *cikure* - in hair; *kuru* - put (decorate); *manada* - о giver of respect (Krsna); *manasija* - of Cupid; *dhvaja* -which is the banner; *camare* - the *camara*; *rati* - because of love-play; *galite* - undone; *lalite* - \[and\] more attractive; *kusumani* - flowers; *sikhandi* - of a peacock; *sikhandaka* - the plumage; *damare* - defeats

**Перевод:**

**“O giver of respect! Decorate with flowers my attractive hair, Cupid’s camara, undone because of love play, and thus becoming even more attractive, defeating the plumage of a peacock.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О Krsna, who gives great respect, though breaking the pride of the arrogant! Or О destroyer of pride of people who think they are beautiful by showing their beauty!

Put flowers in my attractive (*rucire*) hair (*cikure*) which has come undone because of enjoyment (*rati-galite*) but which is still attractive (*lalite*). This indicates superior beauty of the hair after becoming undone by amorous play. Thus this is not a case of *punar-ukti*, redundant meaning of the words rucire and *lalite* (they indicate attractiveness of hair before and after undone, respectively). That hair puts to shame the peacock’s plumage.

### Text 12.23

*sarasa-ghane jaghane mama sambara-*  
*darana-varana-kandare \|*  
*mani-rasana-vasanabharanani*  
*subhasaya vasaya sundare \|\|23\|\|*

*nijagada sa yadu...*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sarasa* - With *srngara-rasa;* *ghane* - saturated; *jaghane* - on hips; *mama* - my; *sambara-darana* - of Cupid; varana - of the elephant; *kandare* - the abode; *mani* - \[on which there are\] gems; *rasana* - \[and\] bells; *vasana* - clothing; *abharanani* - \[and\] ornaments; *subha* - \[is\] splendid; *asaya* - о he (Krsna) whose heart; *vasaya* - put; *sundare* - beautiful.

**Перевод:**

**“O Krsna with splendid heart! Dress my beautiful hips, the abode of Cupid’s elephant, which are saturated with snigara-rasa, with cloth and ornaments, along with bells and gems.”**

**Radha spoke to the moon of the cowherds...**

**Комментарий:**

О Krsna with splendid heart! Or Krsna with completely (*a*) splendid (*subha*) hands (*saya*). This is a suitable meaning since he uses his hands to decorate her.

Dress my hips with clothing and ornaments, having small bells (*rasana*) and gems. My hips are firm or saturated with *srngara-rasa* (*sarasa*). Those hips are the cave of a strong, intoxicated elephant belonging to Cupid who destroyed the demon Sambara. Wild elephants live in caves in the mountain. *Varana* means elephant. *Khandara* means a cave.

### Text 12.24

*sri-jayadeva vacasijayade sadayam*  
*hrdayam kuru mandane \|*  
*hari-carana-smaranamrta-nirmita-*  
*kali-kalusa-jvara-khandane \|\|24\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-jayadeva* - О Krsna \[he who shines with beauty and victory; or: the poet Sri Jayadeva\]; *vacasi* - with \[this\] song; *jayade* - giving victory; *sadayam* - mercifully; *hrdayam* - \[your\] heart; *kuru* - decorate; *mandane* - an ornament; *hari* - Krsna’s; *carana* - of \[your\] decoration \[of Radha\]; *smarana* - of remembrance; *amrta* - by the nectar; *nirmita* - \[is\] caused; *kali* - of quarrels; *kalusa* - foul; *jvara* -of the fever; *khandane* - whose destroying.

**Перевод:**

**О Krsna, shining with beauty and victory! Out of mercy decorate your heart with these words which give victory to Radha over all other women. And through the nectar of remembering your pastime of decorating Radha, destroy the pain of disturbance caused by quarrels. (END OF SONG TWENTY-FOUR)**

**Комментарий:**

*Sri-jayadeva* means “O Krsna who shines (*deva*) with a wealth of beauty (*sri*), and victory in all the arts (*jaya*).” Carefully make my words an ornament on your heart. These words are most excellent in front of enemies (*jayade*). By bestowing the nectar which drowns the mind in remembrance of Krsna decorating (*carana*) his independent beloved, these words destroy the distress caused by the disturbance of quarrels in proud lovers. These words make one forget the previous anger in the couple.

Or \[the verse can be taken as follows:\] concentrate your heart with compassion on the words of the poet Jayadeva. These words are the ornaments of all devotees (*mandane*) and give victory (*jayade*) by bestowing supreme *bhakti*, since it is superior to all else. These words destroy the pain of disturbance caused by quarrel, through the medicine of the greatest bliss (*amrta*), which is remembering the feet of Krsna. Another version has *krta-kalusa-jvara-saajvara*: these words destroy the fever (*samjvara*) of painful sin (*kalusa-jvara*) which has been committed.

### Text 12.25

*racaya kucayos patram citram kurusva kapolayor*  
*ghataya jaghane kancim аnса sraja kavari-bharam \|*  
*kalaya valaya-srenim panau pade mani-nupurav*  
*iti nigaditah pritah pitambaro’pi tathakarot \|\|25\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*racaya* - “Make; *kucayos* - on \[my\] breasts; *patram* - \[makara\] patterns; *citram* - \[Tilaka\] patterns; *kurusva* - make; *kapolayor* - on \[my\] forehead; *ghataya* - Place; *jaghane* - on \[my\] hips; *kancim* - a belt \[of small bells\]; *anca* - Honor; *sraja* - with a garland; *kavari-bharam* - \[my\] decorated hair; *kalaya* - Put; *valaya* - of bracelets; *srenim* - a series; *panau* - in \[my\] hands; *pade* - on \[my\] feet; *mani-nupurav* - \[and\] anklets.” *iti* - Thus; *nigaditah* - spoken to \[by Radha\]; *pritah* - the beloved (Krsna); *pita* - \[was\] yellow; *ambaro* - whose cloth; *api* - even; *tatha* - as \[she directed\]; *akarot* - did.

**Перевод:**

**“Make makara patterns on my breast. Make tilaka patterns on my forehead. Place a belt of small bells on my hips. Honor my bound hair with a garland of flowers. Place a series of bracelets on my hands. Put anklets on my feet.” When Radha spoke in this way, Krsna, who wears yellow cloth, did as she directed.**

**Комментарий:**

О beloved Krsna! Make *makara* patterns on my breast. Make *tilaka* patterns on my forehead. Offer a belt of small bells to my hips. Worship my bound hair with a garland of flowers (put a garland in my hair). The verb *anca* (worship) is in the second person singular imperative. Offer a series of bracelets to my hands. Put anklets on my feet. When Radha spoke in this way, Krsna, wearing yellow cloth, did as she said. This describes the *santatasrava-vallabha* (Radha who always controls Krsna). An example is given:

*sad-anghribhir arditan kusuma-sancayan acinod*  
*akhandam api radhike bahu-sikhandakam tvad-gira \|*  
*amunca nava-pallava-vrajam udancad arkojjvalam*  
*karotu vasago janah kim ayam anyad ajnapaya \|\|*

**\[Krsna speaks,\] “O Radhika! I have plucked a mass of flowers uncrushed by the bees and have brought many undamaged peacock feathers according to your words. Give up using the fresh leaves, brilliant as the rising sun, for making the garland. A person like me should act under your control. Give me another order.” (*****Ujjvala-nilamani*** **4.48)**

As well it is said:

*aham bhakta-paradhino hy asvatantra iva dvija*  
*sadhubhir grasta-hrdayo bhaktair bhakta-jana-priyah*

**\[The Supreme Personality of Godhead said to Durvasa Muni,\] “O brahmana! I am completely under the control of my devotees. I am not at all independent. My heart is controlled by the pure devotees. What to speak of my devotee, even those who are devotees of my devotee are very dear to me.” (SB 9.4.63)**

*ye yatha mam prapadyante tams tathaiva bhajamy aham \|*

**“I respond to persons according to the manner and mood in which they surrender to me.” (BG 4.11)**

*gopinam paramananda asid govinda-darsane*  
*ksanam yuga-satam iva yasam yena vinabhavat*

**The young** ***gopis*** **took the greatest pleasure in seeing Govinda, since for them even a moment without his association seemed like a hundred ages. (SB 10.19.16)**

In this way he is controlled by the devotees’ unlimited *anuraga.* Krsna himself says:

*na paraye ’ham niravadya-samyujam*  
*sva-sadhu-krtyam vibudhayusapi vah*

**\[Krsna said to** ***gopis*****,\] “I am not able to repay my debt for your spotless service, even within a lifetime of Brahma.” (SB 10.32.22)**

Bhagavan, the supreme male, the form of eternity, knowledge and bliss, the form of unfailing and supreme qualities, greatness, power, and rasa, eternally enjoys with his dear devotees of similar bhava and form in eternal, blissful pastimes. Even *atmarama yogis* worship him for his great qualities.

*atmaramas ca munayo nirgrantha apy urukrame \|*  
*kurvanty ahaituklm bhaktim ittham-bhuta-guno harih \|\|*

**Some sages who are** ***atmaramas*****, beyond the scriptures, false ego, and rules, also practice unmotivated, pure** ***bhakti*** **to the master of pure** ***bhakti*****, Krsna, since he possesses qualities attractive to even them. (SB 1.7.10)**

It is also said:

*na karhicin mat-parah santa-rupe*  
*nanksyanti no me’nimiso ledhi hetih \|*  
*yesam aham priya atma sutas ca*  
*sakha guruh suhrdo daivam istam \|\|*

**\[Lord Kapila said to his mother Devahuti,\] “The devotees in the spiritual world are never deprived of any enjoyment. My wheel of time does not afflict those devotees, for whom I am a lover, the atmd, son, friend, elder, companion or worshipable deity." (SB 3.25.38)**

Sridhara Svami comments on this verse of *Bhagavatam*;

*nanv anyavat madiyanam bhoktr-bhogyanam vinasah syat?*  
*tatraha—he santa-ruре! santam suddham tad-ruре mal-loke va mat-parah kadacid api na nanksyanti bhogya-hina na bhavanti \| animiso me hetih kala-cakram ca no ledhi tan na grasati \| tatra hetuh—yesam iti \| suta iva sneha-visayah, sakheva visvasaspadam, gurur ivopadesta, suhrd iva hita-kari, istam daivam iva pujyah \| evam sarva-bhavena mam ye bhajanti tan madiyam kala-cakram na grasatity arthah \|\|*

**“But will the devotee not suffer destruction of self and objects, like others?”**

**“O pure form (*****santa-rupe*****—addressed to Devahuti)! My devotees are not deprived of enjoyment at any time on my pure planet or near my pure form (*****santa-ruре*** **- alternative use of the phrase). My wheel of time does not devour them, because for them I am the object of affection like a son, the object of trust like a comrade, the instructor like a guru, the benefactor like a friend, and worthy of worship like a family deity. Those who worship me with complete love (in the above manner) are not devoured by my wheel of time.” (Sridhara Svami’s commentary ends here)**

Or \[the meaning of the last two lines of the Bhagavatam verse is as follows:\] “I am served as the husband (*priyah*), as *jiva* (*atma*), as a son (*suta*), as a friend (*sakha*), as a father (*guruh*), as a relative (*suhrt*), and as a worshippable deity.” In these cases, the devotees relate to the Lord who takes the role of a servant (*jiva*), son, friend, relative, father and husband. Those who describe the Lord in his various relationships as material are foolish. The Lord says:

*avyaktam vyaktim apannam manyante mam abuddhayah \|*  
*param bhavam ajananto mamavyayam anuttamam \|\|*

**“The unintelligent think that the impersonal Brahman has manifested an illusory form as myself. They do not know my transcendental nature (consisting of form, qualities and activities), which are eternal and most excellent.” (BG 7.24)**

### Text 12.26

*уad gandharva-kalasu kausalam anudhyanam ca уad vaisnavam*  
*yac chrngara-viveka-tattva-racana-kavyesu lilayitam \|*  
*tat sarvam jayadeva-pandita-kaveh krsnaika-tanatmanah*  
*sanandah parisodhayantu sudhiyah sri-gita-govindatah \|\|26\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*yad* - Which; *gandharva* - of music; *kalasu* - in the arts; *kausalam* - \[is\] skill; *anudhyanam* - \[is\] meditation; *ca* - and; *yad* - which; *vaisnavam* - related to Visnu; *yac* - \[and\] which; *chrngara* - of *srngara-rasa;* *viveka-tattva* - the true delineation; *racana* - for producing; *kavyesu* - in poetic devices; *lilayitam* - \[is\] pastimes; *tat* - that; *sarvam* - all; *jayadeva* - Jayadeva; *pandita* - scholar; *kaveh* - of the poet; *krsna* - to Krsna; *eka-tana* - \[is\] directed only; *atmanah* - whose mind; *sanandah* - those who are blissful \[because of the Lord\]; *parisodhayantu* - should understand; *sudhiyah* - о wise people; *sri-gita-govindatah* - from the Sri Gitagovinda.

**Перевод:**

**О wise people! Those who are blissful because of the Lord, please understand everything - skill in the art of music, constant meditation on the Lord, and descriptions of pastimes using poetic devices to produce the true delineation of srhgara-rasa - from Sri-gita-govinda written by the scholar and poet Jayadeva, whose mind is dedicated wholly to Krsna.**

**Комментарий:**

О wise people (*sudhiyah*)! Those who are blissful because of the Lord’s qualities just described and because of awakening as if from sleep with the manifestation of a conclusion, should know everything completely from Gita-govinda of the wise (*pandita*) poet (*kaveh*) Jayadeva. Though *pandita* and *kavi* mean the same thing, *pandita* here means one who speaks with discernment, suitability, and logic and *kavi* means one who speaks only with sweetness. Everything (*sarvam*) is described. The poem of Jayadeva is skillful in the arts and music. Gandharva refers a person who knows classical and folk styles. The classical style (*marga*) has verses with musical notation (*svara*). *Kala* refers to the different arts explained previously (of which music is one) \[in commentary of GG 1.3\]. It contains constant thinking related to Visnu. It portrays pastimes with poetic devices and real knowledge (*tattva*) which delineate *srngara-rasa* in terms of separation and union. Jayadeva’s mind is totally absorbed in Krsna (*krsnaika-tanatmanah*).

### Text 12.27

*paryanki-krta-naga-nayaka-phana-sreni-maninam gane*  
*sankranta-pratibimba-samvalanaya bibhrad-vibhu-prakriyam \|*  
*padambho-ruha-dhari-varidhi-sutam aksnam didrksuh sataih*  
*kaya-vyuham ivacarann upaciti-bhuto harih patu vah \|\|27\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*paryanki* - As \[his\] bed; *krta* - acting; *naga* - of snakes (Ananta); *nayaka* - of the lord; *phana* - of hoods; sreni - on the multitude; *maninam* - of gems; *gane* - in the array; *sankranta* - transferred; *pratibimba* - \[his\] reflections; *samvalanaya* - by mixing; *bibhrad* -accepting; *vibhu* - of expansion; *prakriyam* - a method; *pada* - \[his\] feet; *ambho-ruha* - lotus; *dhari* - massaging; *varidhi-sutam* - of the daughter of sea (Laksmi); *aksnam* - of eyes; *didrksuh* - wishing to see; *sataih* - with hundreds (thousands); *kaya* - of bodies; *vyuham* - an array; *iva* - as if; *acarann* - taking on; *upaciti* - \[is\] increasing; *bhuto* - whose existence; *harih* - Krsna; *patu* - may \[he\] protect; *vah* - you.

**Перевод:**

**May the Lord who destroys obstacles, who seems to increase himself, taking many forms with a thousand eyes in order to see Laksmi massaging his lotus feet, by accepting a method of expanding himself as his own reflections in the array of gems on the unlimited hoods of Ananta, the Lord’s bed, protect you all!**

**Комментарий:**

At the conclusion of the poem the author gives blessings to the hearers.

May Krsna, the destroyer of all obstacles just by hearing (*harih*) protect you all. The Lord’s dedication to protecting his devotees is shown by explaining his affection for the devotee. He seems to increase (*upaciti-bhutah*), appearing in many forms (*kaya-vytiham iva*). The reason for increase is given. With thousands of eyes, he desires to see Laksmi (*varidhi-sutam*) who holds his lotus feet. How this happens is explained.

He accepts a method of expansion (*vibhu-prakriyam*) by distributing his reflections in the array of gems on the many hoods of Sesa, the Lord’s bed. The Lord takes unlimited bodies because he has reflections in the unlimited jewels on Ananta’s hoods. With unlimited bodies, he has unlimited eyes.

### Text 12.28

*tvam aprapya mayi svayamvara-param ksiroda-tirodare*  
*sanke sundari kalakutam apiban mudho mrdani-patih \|*  
*ittham purva-kathabhir anya-manaso viksipya vakso’ncalam*  
*padmayah stana-korakopari-milan-netro harih patu vah \|\|28\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*tvam* - “You; *aprapya* - having not obtained; mayi - me; *svayamvara* - on choosing \[as your husband\]; *param* - intent; *ksiroda* - of the ocean of milk; *tirodare* - on the shore; *sahke* - I think; *sundari* -о beautiful woman (Radha); *kalakutam* - the *kalakuta* poison; *apiban* - drank; *mudho* - bewildered; *mrdani-patih* - Siva.” *ittham* - \[Speaking\] thus; *purva* - ancient; *kathabhir* - by the story; *anya* - \[was\] distracted; *manaso* - whose mind; *viksipya* - having pulled aside; *vakso* - on \[her\] chest; *ancalam* - the cloth; *padmayah* - oi the woman resembling a lotus (Radha); *stana* - the breasts; *koraka* - bud; *upari* - on; *milan* - \[were\] falling; *netro* - whose eyes; *harih* -Krsna; *patu* - may \[he\] protect; *vah* - you.

**Перевод:**

**“O beautiful Radha, I think that when Siva could not attain you on the shore of the ocean of milk, since you were determined to be mine, bewildered by your beauty, he swallowed the kalakuta poison.” As Radha’s mind was distracted by the ancient story, he pulled aside the cloth on her chest, and cast his eyes upon her bud-like breasts. May this Krsna protect you all.**

**Комментарий:**

May Krsna (*harih*) protect you. His qualities are described in the verse. He said, “O beautiful Radha! I think that bewildered Siva (*mrdani-patih*), unable to attain you, since you belong to me alone (*mayi svayamvara-param*), on the shore of the Milk Ocean, he drank the *kalakuta* poison.” In this way while her mind was distracted by his relating an ancient tale, he pulled the cloth from her chest and gazed at her bud-like breasts.

### Text 12.29

*sadhvi madhvika cinta na bhavati bhavatah sarkare karkarasi*  
*drakse draksyanti ke tvam amrta mrtam asi ksira niram rasas te \|*  
*makanda kranda kantadhara dharani-talam gaccha yacchanti bhavam*  
*yavac chrngara-sarasvatam iha jayadevasya visvag-vacamsi \|\|29\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sadhvi* - Pure; *madhvika -* о liquor; *cinta* - thought; *na* - not; *bhavati* -is; *bhavatah* - your; *sarkare* - О sugar; *karkara* - coarse; *asi* - you are; *drakse* - О grapes; *draksyanti* - will look; *ke* - who; *tvam* - at you? *amrta* - О nectar; *mrtam* - dead; *asi* - you are. *ksira* - О milk; *niram* - \[is that of\] water; *rasas* - taste; *te* - your; *makanda* - О mango; *kranda* - weep; *kanta* - Of a beloved; *adhara* - о lips; *dharani-talam* - to Hell; *gaccha* - go; *yacchanti* - Bestow; *bhavam* - the essence; *yavac* - as long as; *chrngara* - with *srngara-rasa*; *sarasvatam* - eloquent; *iha* - here; *jayadevasya* - of Jayadeva; *visvag* - everywhere in the world; *vacamsi* - the words.

**Перевод:**

**As long as the skillful words of Jayadeva bestow the auspicious essence of srhgara-rasa everywhere in this world, О liquor, your thoughts will be impure. О sugar! You will be too coarse. О grapes! Who will look at you? О nectar! You will remain dead. О milk! Your taste will be like water. О mango! You should simply weep. О lips of a beloved! Go to hell.**

**Комментарий:**

\*The power of hearing, singing, remembering, and enjoying the work is explained. Sukadeva has said:

*vikriditam vraja-vadhubhir idam ca visnoh*  
*sraddhanvito ’nusrnuyad atha varnayed yah*  
*bhaktim param bhagavati pratilabhya kamam*  
*hrd-rogam asv apahinoty acirena dhirah*

**Anyone who faithfully and continually hears or describes the Lord’s playful affairs with the young** ***gopis*** **of Vrndavana will attain the highest type of** ***prema*** **to the Lord, quickly destroy lust, the disease of the heart, and immediately become steady. (SB 10.33.39)**

О wine, as long as Jayadeva’s words present poetic descriptions of *srngara-rasa* everywhere (*visvak*) in the world (*iha*), your thoughts will not be pure. Though sweet, wine is intoxicating. О sugar! You are coarse. Though you do not intoxicate, you are harsh. О grapes! Who will look at you? Though you are soft, you grow in unmentionable places. О nectar! You are death, since you are applied after a person dies. О milk! Your taste is water. Like water, milk must be stirred to make it tasty. О mango! Weep, since you have seeds as well as sweet parts. О lips of a lover! Go to hell. You should not stay here since your name is *adhara* (bestowing lowness). Those who take bliss in tasting *madhurya-bhakti-rasa* described by Jayadeva, will hate these things.

### Text 12.30

*sri-bhojadeva-prabhavasya vamadevi-suta-sri-jayadevakasya \|*  
*parabaradi-priya-varga-kanthe sri-gita-govinda-kavitvam astu \|\|30\|\|*

**Пословный перевод:**

*sri-bhojadeva* - \[Was\] Sri Bhojadeva; *prabhavasya* - whose origin (father); *vamadevi* - of Vamadevi; *suta* - the son; *sri-jayadevakasya* - of Sri Jayadeva; *parasaradi* - such as Parasara; *priya-varga* - of the group of friends; *kanthe* - in the throat; *sri-gita-govinda* - \[called\] Sri Gita Govinda; *kavitvam* - the poem; *astu* - may \[it\] remain.

**Перевод:**

**May the poem called Gita-govinda written by Jayadeva the son of Bhojadeva and Vamadevi, remain around the throats of friends such as Parasara.**

**Комментарий:**

While remembering his father and mother, Jayadeva gives blessings to those who are qualified and endowed with knowledge, such as Parasara. Bhojadeva was the name of his father. Vamadevi was the name of his mother. May the poem called *Gita-govinda* remain like an ornament on the throats of those who are friends of Jayadeva (such as Parasara) because they have similar knowledge of the confidential pastimes of Radha and Madhava. By this it is understood that this work is the essence of everything, agreeing with the speakers of Vedas, itihasa, and Puranas. This work is also most difficult to understand.

The sequence in *Gita-govinda* is as follows. First, the excellence of Krsna is presented in the description of the *avataras* and ending with *vasante vasanti* (GG 1.27). Then there is a description of Radha’s intense longing, ending with *kamsarir api* (GG 3.1). There is also a description of regular pastimes and her longing. Then Krsna’s longing is described ending with *yamuna-tira* (GG 4.1). Then Radha’s longing for Krsna is described ending with *aham iha* (GG 5.1). Then Krsna’s longing for her is described, ending with *purvam yatra* (GG 5.7). Then there is a description of the appointment, ending with *atha tam* (GG 6.1). Then *vasaka-sajja* (decorating for the meeting) is described, ending with *atrantare* (GG 7.1). Then, there is a description of the rising of the moon. Along with it the longing again arises. This description ends with *athagatam* (GG 7.12). Then there is a description of heroine feeling cheated (*vipralabdha*), till *atha katham api* (GG 8.1). Then the heroine in depression (*khandita*) is described till *tarn atha* (GG 9.1). Then her separation after the quarrel (*kalahantara*) is described till *atrantare masrna-rosa* (GG 10.1). Then there is a description of her anger, till *suciram* (GG 11.1). Then, when the moon is covered by clouds, a *sakhi* makes her request. This description ends with *sa sa-sadhvasa* (GG 11.23). Then the couple see each other, ending with *gatavati* (GG 12.1). Then Krsna pleads, ending with *pratyuhah* (GG 12.10). Then they sport in private, ending with *atha sahasa* (GG 12.16). Then the *svadhina-bhartrka* (independent heroine) is described ending with *paryanki-krta*. (GG 12.27).

This is the chapter named *suprita-pitambara* in which Krsna wears yellow cloth, joyful with *sambhoga-rasa* called *samrddhimat* (union), since he prefers to wear the same color as her complexion, as he is controlled by her.
